PART 1 · Fundamentals

Chapter 1: Introduction to Node.js

Q. What is a Runtime Environment?

Concept:
  • A runtime environment (RTE) is a platform that provides everything needed to execute a program written in a specific programming language.
  • It is also known as the execution context, meaning it provides the necessary infrastructure (memory management, input/output handling, APIs) to execute the code easily.
  • Client-Side (Browser Runtime): Browsers like Chrome, Firefox, and Safari provide a runtime for JavaScript, powered by engines like V8, SpiderMonkey, or JavaScriptCore.
  • Server-Side (Node.js Runtime): Extends JavaScript to run outside browsers, directly on the operating system.
मराठी सारांश (Runtime Environment):
  • Runtime Environment म्हणजे काय? आपला code चालवण्यासाठी लागणाऱ्या सर्व गोष्टी पुरवणारा platform.
  • काय पुरवतो? Memory management, input / output (files, network), APIs आणि execution context.
  • उदाहरण: मोबाईल app ला चालण्यासाठी Android phone लागतो. तसेच JS code ला चालण्यासाठी runtime लागतो.
  • Browser (Client-side runtime): Chrome (V8), Firefox (SpiderMonkey), Safari (JavaScriptCore). इथे DOM, window, document मिळतात.
  • Node.js (Server-side runtime): JS ला browser बाहेर, थेट OS वर चालवते. इथे fs, http, os सारखे modules मिळतात.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: Engine (V8) फक्त JS चालवतो. Runtime = Engine + APIs + इतर सुविधा.
Code Example: Same Language, Different Runtimes
// Works in BROWSER runtime only
document.querySelector('h1');   // DOM API
window.alert('Hi');

// Works in NODE.JS runtime only
const fs = require('fs');       // file system API
fs.writeFileSync('a.txt', 'Hello');

// Works in BOTH (pure JavaScript, handled by the engine)
console.log([1, 2, 3].map(n => n * 2));

Q. Why is Node.js called a server-side runtime?

  • 1. Runs JavaScript Outside the Browser:
    • Earlier, JavaScript could run only inside a browser (client side).
    • Node.js is built on Google’s V8 engine and runs JavaScript on a server.
    • So we can now write the backend in JavaScript too.
  • 2. Provides Server-Side Features (Built-in Modules):
    • http / https → create web servers.
    • fs → read, write and delete files.
    • net / dns → networking.
    • child_process → run other programs (subprocesses).
    • Browsers do not have these modules, which is why Node.js is suited to backend work.
  • 3. Runtime Environment Architecture:
    • Node.js is not just an engine. It is a full runtime made of 3 parts:
    • V8 engine → converts JS into machine code.
    • Core libraries → fs, http, os, etc.
    • libuv → the event loop and async, non-blocking I/O.
  • 4. Handles Server Requests Efficiently:
    • Uses event-driven, non-blocking I/O.
    • A single thread can handle thousands of requests at the same time.
    • Ideal for scalable apps such as APIs, chat apps and streaming.
  • 5. Full-Fledged Backend Capabilities: Node.js can:
    • Accept and process HTTP requests.
    • Connect to databases (SQL / NoSQL).
    • Authenticate users (login / signup).
    • Serve dynamic content (a different page for each user).
    • Handle background jobs (emails, reports).
  • 6. No DOM, but System Access:
    • Node.js has no document or window (there is no web page).
    • Instead, it can access system resources such as the file system, OS and databases.
Code Example: Server-Side Features (Not Possible in Browser)
const http = require('http');   // create a web server
const fs = require('fs');       // access the file system

const server = http.createServer((req, res) => {
    // read a file from the server's disk and send it to the user
    fs.readFile('data.txt', 'utf-8', (err, data) => {
        if (err) return res.end('File not found');
        res.end(data);
    });
});

server.listen(3000, () => console.log('Server running on port 3000'));

// console.log(window);  // ❌ ReferenceError: window is not defined (no DOM in Node.js)
In Short:
  • Node.js lets JavaScript run outside the browser using the V8 engine.
  • It provides server-side features such as file handling, networking and HTTP servers.
  • It is built on an event-driven, non-blocking architecture (libuv), so it handles many requests efficiently.
  • It has no DOM or window, but it can access the file system and databases.
  • That is why it is called a server-side runtime.
मराठी सारांश (Server-side Runtime):
  • आधी JavaScript फक्त Browser मध्ये चालायची. Node.js मुळे ती Server वर चालते.
  • Node.js मध्ये http, fs, net सारखे modules आहेत, जे Browser मध्ये नसतात.
  • Node.js = V8 Engine + Core Libraries + libuv.
  • एकाच thread वर हजारो requests handle करू शकते (non-blocking).
  • यात DOM / window नसतो, पण files आणि database access करता येतो.

Q. How Does Node.js Enable JavaScript on the Server Side?

  • Main Reason: The V8 engine. Node.js wraps V8 and adds extra tools to make a complete runtime environment.
  • 1. V8 Engine (Core Execution):
    • Node.js embeds Google’s V8 engine.
    • V8 converts JavaScript into machine code using JIT (Just-In-Time) compilation.
    • So JS runs directly on the operating system, with no browser needed.
  • 2. Runtime Environment (Not a Language):
    • Node.js is NOT a programming language. It is a runtime environment.
    • It provides: the V8 engine (execution) + core modules (http, fs, os, net, child_process) + libuv (async I/O and the event loop).
  • 3. Server-Side Capabilities: With Node.js, JavaScript can:
    • Create servers → http / https.
    • Access the file system → fs.
    • Do networking → net, dns.
    • Perform OS-level operations → os.
    • None of these are available in the browser.
  • 4. Event-Driven, Non-Blocking Model:
    • libuv handles async, non-blocking I/O.
    • Thousands of requests are handled without creating many threads.
    • Perfect for real-time apps: chat, APIs, streaming.
  • 5. Ecosystem with npm:
    • Node.js comes with npm, the largest package manager in the world.
    • Install ready-made frameworks such as Express, Socket.IO and Mongoose to build faster.
  • 6. Cross-Platform:
    • Runs on Linux, Windows, macOS and even small devices such as the Raspberry Pi.
    • Makes JavaScript a universal language (frontend + backend).
Code Example: JavaScript Talking to the OS (Server Side)
// run with: node app.js   (no browser needed)
const os = require('os');

console.log('Platform:', os.platform());      // e.g. 'win32' / 'linux'
console.log('CPU cores:', os.cpus().length);  // e.g. 8
console.log('Free memory:', os.freemem());    // in bytes
In Short:
  • Node.js embeds the V8 engine, so JS runs directly on the OS instead of only in browsers.
  • On top of V8, it adds server modules (http, fs, net) and non-blocking I/O through libuv.
  • With npm and cross-platform support, it turns JavaScript into a full backend runtime.
मराठी सारांश (Server Side JS):
  • मुख्य कारण V8 Engine आहे. ते JS ला machine code मध्ये बदलते.
  • Node.js हि language नाही, तर runtime environment आहे (V8 + Core modules + libuv).
  • Server बनवणे, files वाचणे, networking, OS माहिती हे सर्व Node.js मध्ये शक्य आहे.
  • npm मुळे Express, Socket.IO सारखे packages सहज मिळतात.
  • Linux, Windows, macOS सगळीकडे चालते.

Q. Is Node.js built on the V8 JS engine?

  • Node.js uses Google's V8 JavaScript engine.
  • V8 is the same JavaScript engine used by Chrome and Chromium-based browsers.
  • V8 is responsible for executing JavaScript.
  • V8 is primarily written in C++.
  • V8 contains the components required to execute and optimize JavaScript efficiently.
  • V8 uses JIT (Just-In-Time) compilation techniques to optimize JavaScript execution.
IMPORTANT:
  • V8 is a JavaScript engine.
  • V8 uses JIT compilation techniques to compile JavaScript into optimized machine code at runtime.
  • V8's main responsibility is JavaScript execution.
  • Node.js embeds V8 and adds additional runtime capabilities around it.

Q. What does Node.js add to the V8 engine?

  • V8 mainly provides JavaScript execution.
  • Node.js adds runtime APIs and supporting components that allow JavaScript to interact with the operating system and network.
  • Node.js provides APIs/features for:
    • File System operations
    • HTTP networking
    • TCP networking
    • UDP networking
    • Streams
    • Timers
    • Process management
    • Environment variables
    • Operating-system interaction
  • Node.js embeds Google's V8 JavaScript engine and provides additional runtime APIs and capabilities that allow JavaScript to interact with the operating system, file system, network, etc.
  • Simple example: V8 is a car engine. Node.js is the complete car: the engine plus wheels, steering and fuel tank (fs, http, timers, libuv). An engine alone cannot take you anywhere.
मराठी सारांश (V8 आणि Node.js ने काय जोडले):
  • Node.js V8 वर बनले आहे का? होय. Node.js Google चे V8 engine वापरते. तेच engine Chrome मध्ये आहे.
  • V8 चे काम: फक्त JavaScript execute करणे. ते C++ मध्ये लिहिले आहे आणि JIT compilation ने JS ला machine code मध्ये बदलते.
  • V8 मध्ये काय नसते? File वाचणे, server बनवणे, network, timers यापैकी काहीच नाही.
  • Node.js ने काय जोडले? File System, HTTP / TCP / UDP networking, Streams, Timers, Process management, Environment variables, OS interaction.
  • उदाहरण: V8 = गाडीचे engine. Node.js = पूर्ण गाडी (engine + चाके + steering + fuel tank). फक्त engine ने प्रवास होत नाही.

Q. Node.js JavaScript vs Browser JavaScript

  • JavaScript running inside a normal browser does not have unrestricted access to the user's local file system.
  • This restriction exists because of browser security and sandboxing.
  • For example: A website should NOT be able to freely execute: deleteFile("C:/Users/User/password.txt");
  • If browsers allowed unrestricted file-system access, it would create serious security problems.
  • Node.js is designed as a runtime environment that provides controlled APIs for interacting with the operating system.
  • It is mainly used for building:
    • Server-side applications
    • Backend applications
    • REST APIs
    • Web servers
    • Real-time applications
    • Network applications
Figure 1: Conceptual Architecture Diagram of Node.js
PointBrowser JavaScriptNode.js JavaScript
Runs onInside the browser (sandbox)Directly on the OS (server)
Global objectwindowglobal (both have globalThis)
DOM access✅ document, HTML elements❌ No DOM
File system❌ Not allowed (security)✅ fs module
ModulesES Modules (import)CommonJS (require) + ES Modules
Used forUI, user interactionServers, APIs, CLI tools, real-time apps
मराठी सारांश (Node.js JS vs Browser JS):
  • Browser JS: Sandbox मध्ये चालते. Security साठी त्याला तुमच्या computer च्या files ला हात लावता येत नाही.
  • का? कोणतीही website तुमची password file delete करू शकली असती, तर मोठा धोका झाला असता.
  • Node.js JS: थेट OS वर चालते. fs, http, os सारखे controlled APIs देते.
  • Browser मध्ये window, document (DOM) असतात. Node.js मध्ये ते नसतात, त्याऐवजी global असते.
  • Node.js कशासाठी? Servers, backend, REST APIs, real-time apps (chat), network apps.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: भाषा (JavaScript) एकच, पण environment वेगळे, म्हणून मिळणाऱ्या सुविधा वेगळ्या.

Q. Key Features of Node.js

  • JavaScript Runtime Environment => Executes JavaScript outside the browser.
  • Built on V8 => Uses Google's V8 JavaScript engine.
  • Event-driven => Uses an event-driven programming model.
  • Non-blocking I/O => Handles asynchronous I/O efficiently.
  • Single JavaScript Thread => JavaScript execution primarily happens on one main thread.
  • Highly Scalable - Suitable for handling many concurrent I/O operations.
  • Cross-platform - Runs on Windows, macOS, and Linux.
  • Open-source - Source code is publicly available.
  • Large npm Ecosystem - Provides access to a huge collection of packages.
  • Versatile : Useful for APIs, web servers, real-time applications, microservices, streaming, and network applications.
मराठी सारांश (Node.js ची Key Features):
  • Runtime: JS browser बाहेर चालवते.
  • V8 वर आधारित: Google चे fast engine.
  • Event-driven: घटना (event) घडली की त्याचे function चालते.
  • Non-blocking I/O: File / DB / network ची वाट न पाहता पुढचे काम करते.
  • Single JS thread: JS एकाच main thread वर चालते.
  • Scalable: एकाच वेळी हजारो requests handle करू शकते.
  • Cross-platform: Windows, macOS, Linux सगळीकडे चालते.
  • Open-source + npm: मोफत, आणि लाखो तयार packages उपलब्ध.
  • Versatile: APIs, web servers, chat apps, microservices, streaming साठी उपयोगी.

Q. What is Node.js Architecture?

  • Node.js uses an event-driven, non-blocking I/O model.
  • This architecture allows Node.js to handle many concurrent I/O operations efficiently.
  • Node.js does not need to create a separate JS thread for every incoming request; instead, it uses an event-driven architecture where asynchronous operations can be handled without blocking the main JavaScript thread.
  • This makes Node.js particularly suitable for applications that perform a lot of I/O operations (e.g., REST API, real-time app, chat, streaming, web servers, network apps).
  • Main building blocks: V8 (runs JS) + Node.js core APIs (fs, http) + libuv (event loop and thread pool). See Chapter 18 for the full deep dive.
  • Simple example: one smart cashier (main thread) takes every order, passes cooking to the kitchen (libuv) and serves the next customer instead of waiting.
मराठी सारांश (Node.js Architecture):
  • Node.js चे architecture event-driven आणि non-blocking I/O वर आधारित आहे.
  • प्रत्येक request साठी नवीन thread बनवत नाही. एकाच main thread वर काम चालते.
  • Slow कामे (file, DB) background ला (libuv) दिली जातात, त्यामुळे main thread मोकळा राहतो.
  • घटक: V8 (JS चालवतो) + Core APIs (fs, http) + libuv (event loop + thread pool).
  • उदाहरण: एक हुशार cashier. Order घेतो, kitchen ला देतो, लगेच पुढच्या customer कडे वळतो.
  • कशासाठी योग्य? जास्त I/O असलेल्या apps: REST API, chat, streaming, web servers.

Q. What is meant by Event-driven architecture?

Definition:
  • Event-driven architecture is a programming model where the flow of the application is decided by events.
  • When an event occurs, Node.js runs the matching event handler (callback).
  • The code reacts to things that happen, instead of running in a fixed top-to-bottom order.
  • Event means: simply something that happened (e.g., user click, HTTP request arrive, file reading complete, database query complete, timer finish, socket receive a message).
  • Something Happens => Event occurs => Node.js detects the event => associated handler executes.
  • Node.js provides the EventEmitter from the events module.
  • Note: Node.js follows an event-driven architecture where the application responds to events by executing registered event handlers.
Code Example: Reacting to an Event
const EventEmitter = require('events');
const shop = new EventEmitter();

shop.on('orderPlaced', (item) => console.log(`Preparing ${item}...`)); // handler
shop.emit('orderPlaced', 'Pizza');   // event happens → handler runs
// Output: Preparing Pizza...
मराठी सारांश (Event-driven Architecture):
  • Event म्हणजे घडलेली गोष्ट: request आली, file वाचून झाली, timer संपला, message आला.
  • Event-driven म्हणजे program घटनांवर react करतो. ठराविक क्रमाने वरून खाली चालत नाही.
  • Flow: काहीतरी घडते → event → Node.js ओळखतो → त्याचे handler function चालते.
  • Node.js मध्ये यासाठी events module मधला EventEmitter वापरतात.
  • उदाहरण: Doorbell वाजली (event) → आपण दार उघडतो (handler).

Q. What is Synchronous Programming?

Concept:
  • In synchronous programming, tasks run one after another (sequentially).
  • A task must finish completely before the next one starts.
  • While a task is running, the program waits (is blocked) and cannot do anything else.
  • Simple example: a single ticket counter with one queue. The next person is served only after the current person is completely done.
Key Characteristics:
  • Sequential Execution: Code runs line by line, top to bottom.
  • Blocking Behavior: While one task runs, the whole program is “stuck” on it.
  • Deterministic Flow:
    • Deterministic means predictable and consistent: the same input always gives the same output in the same order.
    • So the code is easy to read, write and debug.
Disadvantages:
  • Blocking: If one task is slow (reading a big file, calling an API), everything else pauses.
  • Poor Performance: The CPU sits idle while waiting for slow tasks (disk, network, database).
  • Scalability Issues: Not suitable for apps with many users at once, such as chat apps, real-time apps and APIs.
Diagram: Synchronous (Blocking) Execution
Code Example: Synchronous (Blocking) Code
const fs = require('fs');

console.log('1. Start');

// readFileSync BLOCKS: nothing below runs until the file is fully read
const data = fs.readFileSync('bigFile.txt', 'utf-8');
console.log('2. File read done, length:', data.length);

console.log('3. End');

// OUTPUT (always in this exact order: deterministic):
// 1. Start
// 2. File read done, length: ...
// 3. End
  • Syntax: fs.readFileSync(path, options)
  • path: The file path to read (string, Buffer or URL).
  • options: An encoding such as 'utf-8' (returns a string), or an object { encoding, flag }. Without an encoding it returns a Buffer.
  • Returns: The file content directly. There is no callback, because the program waits.
  • Problem in a server: If 100 users request a big file, each request waits for the previous one, so the server feels “frozen”.
In Short:
  • Synchronous programming runs tasks one by one, in order.
  • It is easy to understand and predictable.
  • But it blocks execution, causing poor performance and scalability problems in I/O-heavy or multi-user apps.
मराठी सारांश (Synchronous Programming):
  • Synchronous म्हणजे कामे एकामागून एक होतात. पहिले काम पूर्ण झाल्याशिवाय दुसरे सुरू होत नाही.
  • उदाहरण: एकच तिकीट खिडकी. पुढच्या माणसाला मागच्याचे काम संपेपर्यंत थांबावे लागते.
  • फायदा: code सोपा आणि predictable (deterministic) असतो, debug करणे सोपे.
  • तोटा: एखादे काम slow असेल (file, API, database) तर संपूर्ण program थांबतो (blocking).
  • त्यामुळे chat apps, APIs सारख्या जास्त users असलेल्या apps साठी योग्य नाही.

Q. What is non-blocking I/O?

  • I/O means input / output. It refers to operations where your application communicates with something external (e.g., read file, read database, receive network data, receive HTTP request).
  • These operations take time. So what should JavaScript do while waiting? There are 2 approaches:
    • 1. Blocking: It's synchronous (one by one execution). JavaScript waits until the operation completes. JS cannot continue executing the next code until the task is complete.
    • 2. Non-blocking: It's asynchronous. JS does not wait for the file to finish reading. Non-blocking tells us that Node.js doesn't wait for I/O operations. Event-driven tells us how Node.js reacts when those operations are completed.
  • Note: Non-blocking I/O allows Node.js to initiate I/O operations without waiting for them to complete. While the operation is in progress, Node.js can continue executing other tasks.
✔ Non-Blocking = Don't wait while the task is running.
✔ Event-Driven = React when the task is completed.
Code Example: Blocking vs Non-Blocking I/O
const fs = require('fs');

// BLOCKING: the next line waits for the file
const data = fs.readFileSync('a.txt', 'utf8');
console.log('After sync read');

// NON-BLOCKING: the next line runs immediately
fs.readFile('a.txt', 'utf8', (err, data) => console.log('File ready (later)'));
console.log('I run first!');
मराठी सारांश (Non-blocking I/O):
  • I/O म्हणजे काय? Input / Output. App बाहेरच्या गोष्टींशी बोलते: file वाचणे, database, network, HTTP request.
  • ही कामे वेळ घेतात. मग JS ने वाट पाहावी का? याचे दोन मार्ग आहेत.
  • Blocking: काम संपेपर्यंत JS थांबते (synchronous). उदा. readFileSync.
  • Non-blocking: JS थांबत नाही, पुढचे काम करते (asynchronous). उदा. readFile.
  • Non-blocking = काम चालू असताना थांबू नका. Event-driven = काम पूर्ण झाल्यावर callback ने react करा.
  • उदाहरण: वॉशिंग मशीन लावून आपण इतर कामे करतो (non-blocking). मशीन संपल्यावर बीप वाजतो (event), मग कपडे काढतो (callback).

Q. Scalability

  • Node.js is well suited for building scalable network applications.
  • Its event-driven and non-blocking I/O architecture allows a relatively small number of threads to handle many concurrent I/O operations efficiently.
  • This is especially useful for I/O-intensive applications (REST APIs, chat apps, streaming, web servers).
  • Scalability = the ability to handle more users or load without slowing down or crashing.
  • Ways to scale Node.js: the cluster module or PM2 (use all CPU cores), load balancers (several servers), and microservices.
IMPORTANT:
  • "Single-threaded" does NOT mean Node.js can only do one thing at a time.
  • JavaScript execution primarily happens on a single main thread, but Node.js can use the operating system and its runtime components to handle asynchronous I/O operations (e.g. using a thread pool behind the scenes).
मराठी सारांश (Scalability):
  • Scalability म्हणजे users / load वाढले तरी app slow न होता चालू राहणे.
  • Node.js event-driven आणि non-blocking असल्याने थोड्या threads मध्ये हजारो I/O कामे handle करते.
  • REST APIs, chat apps, streaming, web servers साठी उत्तम.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: Single-threaded म्हणजे एका वेळी एकच काम असे नाही. I/O कामे OS / thread pool कडे जातात.
  • अजून scale कसे करायचे? Cluster / PM2 (सर्व CPU cores), load balancer, microservices.

Q. Versatility & NPM Ecosystem

  • Node.js can be used to build many types of applications (REST APIs, Microservices, CLI tools).
  • NPM Ecosystem: npm stands for Node Package Manager. It allows developers to install and use open-source packages and libraries instead of implementing everything from scratch.
  • Example: npm install express
  • npm helps developers reuse packages, speed up development, manage dependencies, and share code.
  • Other uses: desktop apps (Electron, e.g. VS Code), build tools (Vite, Webpack), real-time apps (Socket.io), IoT.
मराठी सारांश (Versatility आणि npm):
  • Node.js ने अनेक प्रकारची apps बनवता येतात: REST APIs, microservices, CLI tools, desktop apps (VS Code Electron वर बनले आहे), real-time chat.
  • npm = Node Package Manager. जगातील सर्वात मोठे package store.
  • सगळे स्वतः लिहिण्याऐवजी तयार packages वापरता येतात. उदा. npm install express.
  • फायदे: code reuse, जलद development, dependencies manage करणे, code share करणे.

Q. Streams & Timers

  • Streams: Node.js provides Streams for handling data gradually instead of loading everything into memory at once. Useful for large files, video streaming, network communication.
  • Timers: Node.js provides timer APIs (setTimeout(), setInterval(), setImmediate()) for scheduling operations. Integrated with the event-driven execution model.
  • Simple example (streams): watching a YouTube video while it loads, chunk by chunk, instead of downloading the full 2 GB file first.
मराठी सारांश (Streams आणि Timers):
  • Streams: मोठा data एकदम memory मध्ये न घेता थोडा-थोडा (chunks मध्ये) process करणे.
  • उदाहरण: YouTube video पूर्ण download न होता चालू होतो. Data तुकड्यांमध्ये येत राहतो.
  • मोठ्या files, video streaming, network communication साठी वापरतात. Memory वाचते.
  • Timers: setTimeout() (एकदा, उशिरा), setInterval() (वारंवार), setImmediate() (I/O नंतर लगेच).
  • Timers event loop सोबत काम करतात (Timers phase आणि Check phase).
Figure 2: Node.js Execution & Ecosystem Diagram

Q. Removed features from Node.js (Compared to Browser)

  • window object: The global window object, which is part of web browsers, is absent in Node.js.
  • DOM manipulation: Node.js does not have a built-in Document Object Model (DOM), as it is not intended to interact with a webpage's content.
  • BOM (Browser Object Model): No direct interaction with things like navigator or screen.
  • Web specific APIs: APIs like LocalStorage, SessionStorage, and browser-based fetch (though Node 18+ has a native fetch API, historically it was absent) are not available.
  • Why removed? Node.js runs on a server, where there is no screen, no web page and no user clicking buttons. Instead, it adds server features (fs, http, process).
मराठी सारांश (Browser मधील कोणत्या गोष्टी Node.js मध्ये नाहीत):
  • window object नाही: Node.js मध्ये त्याऐवजी global आहे.
  • DOM नाही: document, HTML elements नाहीत, कारण server वर web page नसतो.
  • BOM नाही: navigator, screen, alert() नाहीत.
  • Web APIs नाहीत: LocalStorage, SessionStorage नाहीत. (fetch Node 18+ मध्ये आले आहे.)
  • का? Server वर screen किंवा user नसतो. त्याऐवजी Node.js server ची कामे देते: fs, http, process.

Q. Responsibilities of JavaScript on Server

  • Database Management: store, retrieve, and manage data efficiently through CRUD operations.
  • Authentication: verifies user identities to control access to the system.
  • Authorization: Determines what authenticated users are allowed to do (permissions/access controls).
  • Input Validation: Checks incoming data for correctness, completeness, and security to prevent malicious data entry.
  • Session Management: Tracks user activity across various requests to maintain state.
  • API Management: Provides and handles interfaces for applications to interact.
  • Error Handling: Manages and responds to errors effectively.
  • Security Measures: Implements protocols to protect data from unauthorized access (e.g. SQL injection, XSS).
  • Data Encryption: Secures sensitive information.
  • Logging & Monitoring: Keeps records of system activity.
मराठी सारांश (Server वर JavaScript ची जबाबदारी):
  • Database: data save करणे, आणणे, बदलणे, delete करणे (CRUD).
  • Authentication: user कोण आहे ते तपासणे (login).
  • Authorization: user ला काय करायची परवानगी आहे ते ठरवणे (admin / normal user).
  • Input Validation: आलेला data बरोबर आणि सुरक्षित आहे का ते तपासणे.
  • Session Management: user ची activity वेगवेगळ्या requests मध्ये लक्षात ठेवणे.
  • API Management, Error Handling: APIs देणे आणि errors नीट handle करणे.
  • Security, Encryption: SQL injection, XSS पासून संरक्षण आणि passwords सारखा data encrypt करणे.
  • Logging, Monitoring: server वर काय घडले याची नोंद ठेवणे.

Q. Server Architecture with Node.js

  • A Node.js server will:
    1. Create server and listen to incoming requests.
    2. Business Logic: validation, connect to DB, actual processing of data.
    3. Return response (like JSON, etc.).
Code Example: The 3 Steps in Code
const http = require('http');

http.createServer(async (req, res) => {          // 1. listen for requests
    if (req.url === '/users') {
        const users = [{ id: 1, name: 'Vishal' }];  // 2. business logic (DB, validation)
        res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' });
        res.end(JSON.stringify(users));             // 3. return response
    }
}).listen(3000);
मराठी सारांश (Node.js Server Architecture):
  • Step 1 (Listen): Server तयार करून port वर client च्या requests ऐकतो.
  • Step 2 (Business Logic): Data validate करतो, database ला जोडतो, खरे काम (processing) करतो.
  • Step 3 (Response): Result (JSON / HTML) client ला परत पाठवतो.
  • उदाहरण: हॉटेल. Waiter order घेतो (listen) → kitchen मध्ये जेवण बनते (logic + DB) → waiter जेवण आणतो (response).

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. Is Node.js truly single-threaded?
  • Yes and No.
  • The JavaScript execution itself happens on a single main thread (the Event Loop).
  • However, Node.js uses C++ libraries (libuv) behind the scenes, which maintains a thread pool (default 4 threads).
  • This pool is used to handle heavy I/O operations (like file system, DNS resolution, and crypto operations) asynchronously without blocking the main thread.
Q2. What is the difference between blocking and non-blocking I/O?
  • Blocking I/O forces the application execution to wait until the operation finishes (e.g., fs.readFileSync), preventing any other code from running.
  • Non-blocking I/O (e.g., fs.readFile) initiates the request and immediately moves to the next line of code.
  • It handles the result later via a callback, Promise, or async/await once the operation completes.
Q3. Why does Node.js not have a window object?
  • The window object is part of the Browser Object Model (BOM) used to interact with the browser window, DOM, and frontend-specific APIs (like localStorage).
  • Since Node.js runs on a server and has no graphical browser context or DOM, the window object does not exist.
  • Instead, Node.js has a global object for global scope variables.
Q4. Explain Event-Driven architecture in Node.js.
  • It means the flow of the program is determined by events (e.g., an HTTP request arriving, a file finishing reading).
  • Instead of polling or waiting linearly, Node.js registers event listeners/callbacks.
  • When an event occurs, the system adds it to an event queue.
  • The Event Loop eventually picks it up and executes the associated callback function.
Q5. What are the common types of errors in programming?
  • Syntax Error: When you miss any syntax (e.g., missing a parenthesis or comma).
  • Runtime Error: When the code is syntactically correct but throws an error during execution.
  • Logical Error: When the code doesn't crash but behaves incorrectly or gives wrong output.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Interview मध्ये नेहमी Node.js चे Single-threaded असणे, Blocking vs Non-blocking, आणि Event-driven architecture यावर प्रश्न विचारले जातात.
  • Q1 (Single-threaded का?): JS एकाच main thread वर चालते (हो), पण libuv चा thread pool (4 threads) file, DNS, crypto कामे background मध्ये करतो (नाही). म्हणून उत्तर: "हो आणि नाही".
  • Q2 (Blocking vs Non-blocking): Blocking = काम संपेपर्यंत थांबा (readFileSync). Non-blocking = काम सुरू करून पुढे जा (readFile), result नंतर callback / Promise ने.
  • Q3 (window का नाही?): window हा browser चा (BOM) भाग आहे. Server वर browser किंवा DOM नसतो, म्हणून त्याऐवजी global आहे.
  • Q4 (Event-driven): Event घडतो → event queue मध्ये जातो → event loop callback चालवतो.
  • Q5 (Errors): Syntax error (लिहिण्यात चूक, code चालतच नाही), Runtime error (चालताना crash), Logical error (crash नाही पण उत्तर चुकीचे).

Quick Revision Summary

Concept Description
Node.js JS runtime built on Chrome's V8 engine allowing JS execution outside the browser.
V8 Engine Google's engine that compiles JS into machine code (JIT compilation).
Event-Driven Application flow is governed by reacting to events (HTTP requests, timers, I/O completion).
Non-Blocking I/O Operations run asynchronously in the background, allowing the main thread to continue executing code.
Single-Threaded The main JS code runs on one thread, avoiding context-switching overhead, but handles concurrency via the Event Loop and libuv.
सारांश (Summary):
  • Node.js मुळे आपण JavaScript चा उपयोग backend server बनवण्यासाठी करू शकतो.
  • हे V8 engine वापरते आणि Single-threaded असूनही Event-driven व Non-blocking I/O मुळे एकाच वेळी हजारो requests अत्यंत वेगाने handle करू शकते.
  • यामध्ये browser प्रमाणे DOM किंवा window object नसतात, पण File System (fs), Network (http) सारखे server-side APIs असतात.
  • V8 Engine: Google चे engine, JIT compilation ने JS ला machine code मध्ये बदलते, म्हणून fast.
  • Event-Driven: request आली, file वाचली, timer संपला अशा events वर app react करतो.
  • Non-Blocking I/O: slow कामे background (libuv) ला देऊन main thread पुढचे काम करतो.
  • Single-Threaded: JS एकाच thread वर, पण Event Loop आणि libuv thread pool मुळे concurrency मिळते.

Chapter 1.5: Project Setup & Package Management

Q. What is a Package in Node.js?

Concept:
  • A Package is a reusable block of code, often containing multiple modules or files, created to solve a specific problem (e.g., creating a server, connecting to a database).
  • It is bundled together with a package.json file that describes its contents and requirements.
  • Packages can be shared globally via the NPM registry, allowing developers to avoid writing repetitive code from scratch.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Package म्हणजे काय? एखादे विशिष्ट काम करण्यासाठी बनवलेला तयार code चा संच. उदा. server बनवणे (express), password encrypt करणे (bcrypt).
  • एका package मध्ये अनेक files / modules असू शकतात, आणि त्याची माहिती package.json मध्ये असते.
  • Packages npm registry वर जगभर share केले जातात. कोणीही download करून वापरू शकतो.
  • उदाहरण: स्वयंपाकासाठी रोज मसाला दळण्याऐवजी तयार मसाल्याचे पाकीट आणणे. तेच package आहे.
  • Module vs Package: Module = एक file. Package = अनेक modules + package.json.
Code Example: Using a Package
// Terminal: npm install bcrypt
const bcrypt = require('bcrypt');          // use ready-made code

bcrypt.hash('myPassword', 10).then(hash => console.log(hash)); // no need to write hashing yourself

Q. What are npm and Yarn?

Concept:
  • Both npm and Yarn are JavaScript package managers used to install, update, and manage third-party libraries (dependencies) for your Node.js projects.
  • npm (Node Package Manager) is the default package manager. It is installed automatically when you install Node.js.
  • Yarn is an alternative package manager created by Facebook. It was historically faster than npm, but modern npm versions are equally fast. Yarn needs to be installed separately.
Code Example: Basic Installation Commands
// To install a package globally or locally
npm install express      // Installs the express package using npm
yarn add express         // Installs the express package using Yarn
मराठी सारांश:
  • npm आणि Yarn हे दोन्ही Package Managers आहेत.
  • हे आपल्या प्रोजेक्टमध्ये बाहेरचे code/libraries (उदा. express, mongoose) डाउनलोड आणि मॅनेज करण्यासाठी वापरले जातात.
  • npm: Node.js सोबत आपोआप install होतो (default).
  • Yarn: Facebook ने बनवला. आधी npm पेक्षा fast होता, आता दोन्ही जवळपास सारखेच. वेगळा install करावा लागतो.
  • Commands तुलना: npm install express = yarn add express. npm uninstall x = yarn remove x.
  • उदाहरण: Play Store आणि App Store. दोन्ही apps देतात, फक्त पद्धत वेगळी.
  • Rule: एका project मध्ये एकच package manager वापरा (lock files मिसळू नका).
TasknpmYarn
Install allnpm installyarn
Add packagenpm install expressyarn add express
Add dev packagenpm install -D nodemonyarn add -D nodemon
Removenpm uninstall expressyarn remove express
Run scriptnpm run devyarn dev
Lock filepackage-lock.jsonyarn.lock

Q. How to Setup a New Node.js Project (Step-by-Step)?

  • Step 1: Create a Folder — Create an empty folder and open it in VS Code.
  • Step 2: Initialize Project — Generate a package.json file. 
    npm init
    • If you use npm init, it will ask you questions interactively (name, version, description).
    • If you use npm init -y or npm init --yes, it will skip all questions and create the file instantly.
  • Step 3: Install Packages — Install your required dependencies. 
    npm install express
    This automatically creates the node_modules folder and package-lock.json file.
  • Step 4: Create Entry File — Create your main file (e.g., app.js or index.js) and start writing your Node.js code.
  • Step 5: Run It — node app.js (or node --watch app.js to auto-restart on changes).
  • Step 6: Create .gitignore — Add node_modules/ and .env so they are never pushed to GitHub.
Resulting Folder Structure
my-app/
├── node_modules/        ← downloaded packages (never commit)
├── app.js               ← entry file
├── package.json         ← project info + dependency list
├── package-lock.json    ← exact versions (commit this)
└── .gitignore           ← node_modules/, .env
मराठी सारांश (नवीन Node.js Project Setup):
  • Step 1: रिकामा folder बनवा आणि VS Code मध्ये उघडा.
  • Step 2: npm init (प्रश्न विचारतो) किंवा npm init -y (सगळे default, लगेच) चालवा. यातून package.json बनते.
  • Step 3: npm install express. यामुळे node_modules folder आणि package-lock.json आपोआप बनतात.
  • Step 4: app.js / index.js बनवून code लिहा.
  • Step 5: node app.js ने चालवा.
  • Step 6: .gitignore मध्ये node_modules/ आणि .env लिहा.

Q. How to Setup Node.js with TypeScript (Step-by-Step)?

Concept:
  • Dynamically Typed: JavaScript only catches errors at runtime (e.g., when the server crashes).
  • Statically Typed: TypeScript (TS) adds strict static typing to JavaScript.
  • Compile-Time Safety: It catches errors during development, way before running the code.
  • Developer Experience: It provides excellent autocomplete and IntelliSense directly in VS Code.
  • Step 1: Initialize Project — Create the package.json file. 
    npm init -y
  • Step 2: Install TypeScript & Node Types — Install required TS packages. 
    npm install -D typescript tsx @types/node
    • -D (or --save-dev): Installs these packages as development dependencies (they won't be included in the final production build).
    • typescript: The official compiler that converts (compiles) your TypeScript code into standard JavaScript.
    • tsx: A modern and fast execution engine (built on esbuild) that allows you to run TypeScript files directly during development. It completely replaces the older ts-node which often faces issues with ES Modules (like ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND).
    • @types/node: Provides type definitions for core Node.js modules (like fs, path), giving you excellent autocomplete and error-checking in VS Code.
  • Step 3: Generate TS Config File — Define compiler rules. 
    npx tsc --init
    • npx: Node Package Execute. It runs the package directly from the npm registry without needing to install it globally on your computer.
    • tsc: Stands for TypeScript Compiler. It is the tool that actually converts your TypeScript code into JavaScript.
    • --init: A flag that tells the compiler to generate a default tsconfig.json setup file in your project.
    • tsconfig.json: The configuration file where you define compiler rules (e.g., "outDir": "./dist" to save compiled files, and "rootDir": "./src" for source files).
  • Step 4: Create Source Folder — Create a src folder and add an app.ts file inside it.
  • Step 5: Add Scripts — Open package.json and add scripts to run and build your code.
Code: package.json (TS Scripts Setup)
"scripts": {
  "dev": "npx tsx src/app.ts",      // Runs TypeScript directly and perfectly handles ES modules
  "build": "tsc",                   // Compiles TypeScript into standard JavaScript
  "prod": "node dist/app.js"        // Runs the compiled JavaScript in production
}
मराठी सारांश (TS Setup):
  • Node.js मध्ये TypeScript वापरण्यासाठी आपल्याला npm init -y नंतर typescript आणि @types/node इन्स्टॉल करावे लागतात.
  • npx tsc --init मुळे TS config फाईल बनते.
  • आपण डेव्हलपमेंटसाठी tsx वापरतो जे थेट .ts फाईल चालवते आणि जुन्या ts-node पेक्षा अधिक जलद आणि error-free आहे.
  • TypeScript का? JS मध्ये errors code चालवल्यावर (runtime ला) दिसतात. TS मध्ये ते code लिहितानाच दिसतात. Autocomplete पण उत्तम मिळते.
  • -D: हे packages फक्त development साठी (devDependencies).
  • @types/node: fs, path सारख्या Node modules चे types देतो.
  • tsconfig.json: compiler चे नियम. rootDir: ./src (source), outDir: ./dist (compiled JS).
  • Scripts: dev = tsx ने थेट चालवा, build = tsc ने JS बनवा, prod = node ने dist मधील JS चालवा.

Q. Difference between `tsx` and `ts-node` (Why we use `tsx` now?)

Concept:
  • Both tsx and ts-node are engines used to run TypeScript files directly during development without needing a separate compile step.
  • ts-node (The Old Way): It is the traditional engine but it struggles heavily with modern ES Modules (import/export). It often throws the ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND error unless you configure it with complicated flags (like --esm).
  • tsx (The Modern Way): It stands for TypeScript Execute and is the modern replacement for ts-node.
  • Speed: tsx is built on top of esbuild, which makes it incredibly fast compared to ts-node.
  • Zero Config: It flawlessly handles both CommonJS (require) and ES Modules (import) out of the box without requiring any extra setup.
मराठी सारांश:
  • जुना ts-node बऱ्याचदा ES Modules सोबत (import/export) नीट काम करत नाही आणि ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND एरर देतो.
  • त्याऐवजी आपण आता tsx वापरतो. ते esbuild मुळे खूप फास्ट आहे आणि कुठल्याही एक्स्ट्रा सेटिंग शिवाय थेट TypeScript रन करते.
  • दोघांचे काम एकच: development मध्ये .ts file compile न करता थेट चालवणे.
  • tsx = TypeScript Execute. CommonJS (require) आणि ES Modules (import) दोन्ही zero-config चालवते.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: tsx type-check करत नाही. Type errors पाहण्यासाठी tsc --noEmit वापरा.
Pointts-node (Old)tsx (Modern)
SpeedSlower (uses the TS compiler)Very fast (esbuild)
ES ModulesOften fails (ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND)Works out of the box
ConfigNeeds flags like --esmZero config
Type checkingYes (slower)No (use tsc --noEmit separately)
Commandnpx ts-node src/app.tsnpx tsx src/app.ts

Q. What is `package.json`?

Concept:
  • package.json is the main configuration and metadata file for any Node.js project.
  • It acts as the "Identity Card" of the project, containing the project's name, version, author, and description.
  • It is essential for managing dependencies (the external libraries your project needs to run).
  • It is also used to define custom scripts (like npm start) and identify the main entry point of the application.
  • You can generate it automatically by running npm init -y in your terminal.
  • name & version: Crucial for identifying the project if published.
  • main: The entry point file of your application (usually index.js or app.js).
  • scripts: Custom commands you can run using npm run <script-name> (e.g., npm start or npm run dev).
  • dependencies: A list of packages required for the app to run in production (installed via npm install <pkg>).
  • devDependencies: A list of packages only needed for development and testing, like nodemon or typescript (installed via npm install -D <pkg>).
Code Example: A Sample package.json File
{
  "name": "my-node-app",
  "version": "1.0.0",
  "main": "app.js",
  "scripts": {
    "start": "node app.js",
    "dev": "nodemon app.js"
  },
  "dependencies": {
    "express": "^4.18.2"
  },
  "devDependencies": {
    "nodemon": "^3.0.1"
  }
}

Q. What is `package-lock.json`?

Concept:
  • package-lock.json is a file automatically generated by npm when a project's dependencies are installed or updated.
  • While package.json only specifies the minimum required versions of dependencies, package-lock.json records the exact versions of every single package and sub-package installed.
  • This guarantees that the exact same dependency tree is installed on every machine, ensuring perfectly reproducible builds across different environments.
  • Version Locking: It locks the exact version of every installed package and its nested dependencies. This ensures that if another developer clones the project and runs npm install, they get the exact same versions of all packages.
  • Speed: It speeds up the installation process because npm doesn't have to resolve version conflicts or fetch metadata for every package—it just reads the lock file.
  • Rule: You should ALWAYS commit package-lock.json to GitHub along with package.json. Do NOT ignore it.
मराठी सारांश:
  • package.json म्हणजे आपल्या प्रोजेक्टचे Identity Card आहे ज्यामध्ये प्रोजेक्टचे नाव आणि लागणाऱ्या packages ची यादी असते.
  • package.json मधील महत्वाचे भाग: name / version (ओळख), main (entry file), scripts (shortcut commands), dependencies (production साठी), devDependencies (फक्त development साठी).
  • Version मधील ^: ^4.18.2 म्हणजे 4.x.x मधील नवीन minor / patch version चालेल. ~4.18.2 म्हणजे फक्त 4.18.x.
  • तर package-lock.json म्हणजे त्या packages चे exact versions lock करणारी फाईल आहे, जेणेकरून दुसऱ्या developer च्या मशीनवर पण सेम कोड चालेल.
  • Lock file चे फायदे: सगळीकडे same versions, आणि install जलद होते.
  • उदाहरण: package.json = "मला साखर हवी" (साधी यादी). package-lock.json = "XYZ brand, 1 kg, batch नंबर 123" (नेमकी माहिती).
  • या दोन्ही फाईल्स नेहमी GitHub वर upload कराव्यात.

Q. Dependencies vs DevDependencies

  • Dependencies:
    • These are packages required by the application to run in production.
    • Examples: Express, Mongoose.
    • Installed using: npm install <package-name>.
  • DevDependencies:
    • These are packages required ONLY during development.
    • They are NOT needed in production.
    • Examples: Testing tools (Jest) or live-reloading tools (Nodemon).
    • Installed using: npm install --save-dev <package-name> (or -D).
Code Example: Installing Dependencies
// 1. Install regular dependency (production)
npm install react

// 2. Install dev dependency (only for development)
npm install -D nodemon
मराठी सारांश:
  • Dependencies म्हणजे असे packages जे ॲप चालवण्यासाठी आवश्यक आहेत (उदा. Express).
  • आणि DevDependencies म्हणजे असे packages जे फक्त coding करताना मदत करतात, पण फायनल ॲपमध्ये त्यांची गरज नसते (उदा. Nodemon).
  • Install: dependency = npm install express. devDependency = npm install -D nodemon.
  • Production मध्ये: npm install --omit=dev केल्यास devDependencies install होत नाहीत. Server हलका राहतो.
  • उदाहरण: घर बांधताना scaffolding (devDependency) लागते, पण घर तयार झाल्यावर काढून टाकतो. भिंती (dependency) कायम राहतात.
  • Dev examples: nodemon, jest, typescript, eslint, prettier. Prod examples: express, mongoose, bcrypt, dotenv.

Q. NPM Scripts

  • NPM scripts allow you to define custom terminal commands in your package.json file.
  • Instead of typing long commands like node --watch server/app.js, you can define a script and run it using npm run <script-name>.
Code Example: Running NPM Scripts
// Inside package.json:
"scripts": {
  "start": "node server.js",
  "dev": "nodemon server.js"
}

// In terminal, run:
npm start
npm run dev
मराठी सारांश:
  • NPM Scripts म्हणजे स्वतःचे छोटे terminal commands बनवणे.
  • वारंवार टाईप करावे लागणारे मोठे commands आपण package.json मध्ये सेव्ह करून एका छोट्या नावाने रन करू शकतो.
  • Special scripts: start आणि test ला run लागत नाही (npm start, npm test). बाकीच्यांना npm run dev लागतो.
  • फायदा: संपूर्ण team एकाच पद्धतीने project चालवते. Local packages (nodemon) global install न करता वापरता येतात.
  • उदाहरण: मोबाईल मधील speed-dial. पूर्ण नंबर न दाबता "1" दाबले की call.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. Should we upload the node_modules folder to GitHub?
  • No! The node_modules folder is huge and contains thousands of files. Instead, we upload only package.json and package-lock.json. Anyone who downloads the code can run npm install to download everything automatically. (Always add node_modules/ to your .gitignore file).
Q2. What is the difference between npm install and npm ci?
  • npm install updates package-lock.json if there are newer versions available matching the rules in package.json.
  • npm ci (Clean Install) strictly follows package-lock.json and completely deletes the existing node_modules folder before installing. It is used in CI/CD pipelines for perfectly reproducible builds.
Q3. What is npm audit?
  • npm audit scans your project's dependency tree for known security vulnerabilities. You can fix them automatically using npm audit fix.
Q4. What is the nodemon package and how is it used?
  • nodemon is a tool that helps develop Node.js based applications by automatically restarting the node application when file changes in the directory are detected.
  • Usually installed as a dev-dependency: npm install --save-dev nodemon.
मराठी सारांश:
  • इंटरव्यूमध्ये नेहमी विचारतात की node_modules फोल्डर गिटहबवर अपलोड का करत नाहीत?
  • उत्तर: कारण तो खूप मोठा असतो. आपण फक्त package.json देतो, ज्यावरून कुणीही npm install करून सगळे packages पुन्हा मिळवू शकतो.
  • npm install vs npm ci: install lock file update करू शकतो. ci lock file काटेकोरपणे पाळतो, आधी node_modules delete करतो. CI/CD मध्ये वापरतात.
  • npm audit: packages मधील security त्रुटी शोधतो. npm audit fix ने आपोआप fix करतो.
  • nodemon: file save केली की server आपोआप restart करतो. devDependency म्हणून install करतात. (Node 18+ मध्ये node --watch पण आहे.)

Quick Revision Summary

Concept / Command Description
npm init -y Creates a new package.json file with default settings.
npm install <pkg> Installs a package and adds it to dependencies.
npm install -D <pkg> Installs a package and adds it to devDependencies.
package.json Stores project metadata and dependency requirements.
package-lock.json Stores the exact installed versions of all packages for consistency.

Chapter 2: Node.js REPL

Q. What is REPL?

Definition:
  • REPL stands for Read – Evaluate – Print – Loop.
  • It is an interactive command-line environment built into Node.js.
  • You type JavaScript, and it immediately runs the code and shows the result, again and again.
  • Start it by typing node (with no file name) in the terminal. Exit with .exit or Ctrl + D.
  • Simple example: a calculator. You type 2 + 3 and instantly see 5, then type the next thing.
  • R (Read): Read the user input & parse into the JavaScript code or data structure that the JS engine can understand and store in memory.
  • E (Evaluate): Take input and evaluate (the parsed input is executed by the JS engine; if it is a valid expression, the REPL computes the result).
  • P (Print): Print the result of evaluation to the console (so that we can see the output).
  • L (Loop): Loop back to read more user input (the process waits for the next input, and continues until the user exits).
मराठी सारांश (REPL):
  • REPL (Read-Evaluate-Print-Loop) हे एक interactive shell आहे जिथे आपण JavaScript code लिहून लगेच त्याची output पाहू शकतो.
  • R (Read): आपण लिहिलेला code वाचतो आणि समजतो (parse).
  • E (Evaluate): तो code चालवतो (execute).
  • P (Print): result screen वर दाखवतो.
  • L (Loop): पुन्हा पुढच्या code ची वाट पाहतो. .exit करेपर्यंत हे चालू राहते.
  • सुरू कसे करायचे? Terminal मध्ये फक्त node टाइप करा.
  • उदाहरण: Calculator सारखे. 2 + 3 टाइप केले की लगेच 5.
  • हे testing आणि experimentation साठी खूप उपयुक्त आहे.
Example: A REPL Session
$ node
> 2 + 3
5
> const name = 'Vishal'
undefined
> name.toUpperCase()
'VISHAL'
> require('os').platform()
'win32'
> _            // special: holds the last result
'win32'
> .exit
Figure 3: REPL Execution Flow

Q. Important Features of REPL

  • Interactive JS execution.
  • Immediate evaluation of expressions.
  • Access to Node.js runtime API.
  • Multi-line input (... prompt indicates the REPL is waiting for additional input to complete the expression or block).
  • Session saving and loading.
  • Special REPL commands.
  • Quick experimentation.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Interactive: थेट terminal मध्ये JS code चालवता येतो.
  • Immediate result: लिहिताच output मिळतो.
  • Node.js API access: fs, os, path सारखे modules थेट वापरता येतात.
  • Multi-line input: { उघडला की ... prompt येतो, म्हणजे REPL block पूर्ण होण्याची वाट पाहत आहे.
  • Session save / load: .save आणि .load ने code file मध्ये साठवता / आणता येतो.
  • Special commands: .help, .break, .editor, .exit.
  • Tab key: autocomplete. Up arrow: मागील commands.

Q. When to use REPL?

  • Streamline interactive shell: Execute JavaScript code interactively.
  • Quick Testing: Ideal for testing and debugging code snippets on the fly.
  • Built-in help: Offers help commands via .help.
  • Session Management: Supports saving (.save) and loading (.load) code sessions.
  • Node.js API access: Provides direct access to Node.js API for experimentation.
  • Customizable: Allows customization of prompt and behavior settings.
  • Quick calculation: Perform math or string operations instantly.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Quick testing: छोटे code snippets लगेच तपासण्यासाठी. उदा. [1,2,3].map(x => x*2) काय देते?
  • शिकण्यासाठी: Node.js API शिकण्यासाठी. उदा. require('path').join('a','b').
  • Debugging: एखादे function नेमके काय return करते ते पाहण्यासाठी.
  • Quick calculation: math किंवा string operations लगेच करण्यासाठी.
  • Help आणि session: .help ने मदत, .save / .load ने session साठवणे.

Q. When NOT to use REPL?

  • Do not use REPL as the main place for developing a large application.
  • REPL is useful for experimentation and quick testing, but it should not be confused with a full debugger.
  • Code typed in REPL is lost when you exit (unless you use .save). Real code belongs in .js files under version control (Git).
  • For real debugging (breakpoints, step-by-step), use the VS Code debugger or node --inspect.
मराठी सारांश:
  • REPL फक्त छोटे code test करण्यासाठी आहे, मोठे projects बनवण्यासाठी त्याचा वापर करू नये.
  • REPL बंद केल्यावर लिहिलेला code हरवतो (.save केला नसेल तर).
  • खरा code नेहमी .js files मध्ये आणि Git मध्ये ठेवा.
  • REPL हा पूर्ण debugger नाही. Breakpoints साठी VS Code debugger किंवा node --inspect वापरा.
  • उदाहरण: REPL म्हणजे rough पान (कच्चे काम). Project म्हणजे final वही.

Q. Special REPL Commands

REPL Dot Commands
> .help
.break    Sometimes you get stuck, this gets you out
.clear    Alias for .break
.editor   Enter editor mode
.exit     Exit the REPL
.help     Print this help message
.load     Load JS from a file into the REPL session (.load filename.js)
.save     Save all evaluated commands in this REPL session to a file (.save session.js)
मराठी सारांश (REPL Dot Commands):
  • .help: सगळ्या commands ची यादी दाखवतो.
  • .break / .clear: अडकलेले multi-line input रद्द करतो.
  • .editor: editor mode. मोठा code लिहा आणि Ctrl + D ने चालवा.
  • .save file.js: सध्याच्या session मधला सगळा code file मध्ये save करतो.
  • .load file.js: file मधला code REPL मध्ये load करून चालवतो.
  • .exit: REPL बंद करतो (Ctrl + D किंवा दोनदा Ctrl + C पण चालते).

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What happens if you write a multi-line function in REPL?
  • When you open a block (like {), the REPL detects that the statement is incomplete.
  • It will show a ... prompt on the next line instead of >, indicating it is waiting for you to complete the block.
  • Once you close the block with } and press Enter, it evaluates the entire function.
Q2. How can you clear an ongoing command if you made a mistake?
  • You can type .break or .clear.
  • Alternatively, pressing Ctrl + C once will break out of the current multi-line input.
  • Pressing Ctrl + C twice will exit the REPL completely.
Q3. Is REPL available in the browser console?
  • The concept is the same! The browser's DevTools Console is essentially a REPL environment for browser-side JavaScript.
  • However, Node.js REPL runs in the terminal and provides access to backend APIs (like fs, os) that the browser console does not have.
Q4. How do you save your experimentation in REPL to a file?
  • You can type .save filename.js inside the REPL.
  • This saves all the commands and expressions you typed in the current session into that file, which is very useful for prototyping.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Q1 (Multi-line): { उघडला की REPL ओळखतो की statement अपूर्ण आहे. तो ... prompt दाखवतो. } बंद केल्यावर संपूर्ण function चालते.
  • Q2 (चूक झाली तर?): .break / .clear किंवा एकदा Ctrl + C. दोनदा Ctrl + C केल्यास REPL बंद होतो.
  • Q3 (Browser console पण REPL आहे का?): हो, concept तोच. पण Node REPL मध्ये fs, os सारखे backend APIs मिळतात.
  • Q4 (Save कसे करायचे?): .save filename.js. Session मधले सगळे commands file मध्ये जातात.

Quick Revision Summary

Command / Concept Description
REPL Read, Evaluate, Print, Loop - interactive Node.js shell.
.load file.js Executes the JavaScript file inside the current REPL session.
.save file.js Saves the current session history into a file.
.exit Exits the REPL session (Ctrl + D or double Ctrl + C also work).

Chapter 3: Global Object & globalThis

Q. Why Node.js does not have a window object?

  • In Node.js, there is no window object because Node.js runs outside the browser.
  • So it does not deal with DOM, BOM, or browser-specific APIs.
  • Instead, Node.js has a global object.
  • It is equivalent to the window in the browser but designed for a server-side environment.
  • What lives on global: console, setTimeout, setInterval, process, Buffer, URL, fetch (Node 18+).
Code Example: window vs global
// In Node.js:
console.log(typeof window);          // 'undefined'
console.log(typeof global);          // 'object'
console.log(global.setTimeout === setTimeout); // true
मराठी सारांश (Node.js मध्ये window का नाही?):
  • Node.js browser बाहेर (server वर) चालते, त्यामुळे DOM, BOM किंवा browser APIs नसतात.
  • म्हणून window object नाही. त्याऐवजी global object आहे.
  • global वर console, setTimeout, process, Buffer असे globally उपलब्ध objects असतात.
  • उदाहरण: Browser चे घर window, Node.js चे घर global. घर वेगळे, पण दोन्हीकडे "global" वस्तू ठेवल्या जातात.

Q. What is the use of the globalThis keyword?

Concept:
  • globalThis is a standard, cross-environment way to access the global object in JavaScript, introduced in ECMAScript 2020.
  • It provides a consistent way to access the global object regardless of the JavaScript environment (Browser, Node.js, Web Workers).
  • In the past, accessing the global object varied: window in Browser, global in Node.js, and self in Web Workers.
  • globalThis solves this by providing one universal name for all environments.
मराठी सारांश (globalThis):
  • पूर्वी वेगवेगळ्या environment मध्ये global object चे नाव वेगळे असायचे (उदा. browser मध्ये window, Node.js मध्ये global).
  • पण आता globalThis आल्यामुळे सर्व ठिकाणी एकच शब्द वापरता येतो.
  • जुनी नावे: Browser = window, Node.js = global, Web Worker = self.
  • नवीन (ES2020): globalThis सगळीकडे चालतो.
  • फायदा: if (typeof window !== 'undefined') सारखे checks लागत नाहीत. एकच code browser आणि Node दोन्हीत चालतो.
  • उदाहरण: वेगवेगळ्या शहरात "मुख्य बाजार" ला वेगवेगळी नावे. globalThis म्हणजे सगळीकडे चालणारे एक common नाव.
Figure 4: globalThis is Environment-Independent
Example 1: globalThis in Browser vs Node.js
// ─── Browser Environment ───
console.log(globalThis === window);
// → true   (Because in a browser, globalThis points to window)

// ─── Node.js Environment ───
console.log(globalThis === global);
// → true   (Because in Node.js, globalThis points to global)
What happens: globalThis seamlessly adapts to whichever environment the code is running in and points to the respective global object. Output: true in both environments. ✅ Advantage: We no longer need if (typeof window !== "undefined") checks.

Q. globalThis vs Global Scope

  • globalThis refers to the global object.
  • But a variable declared with let, const, or class does NOT automatically become a property of the global object (unlike var declarations in some global contexts).
Example 2: let vs explicit property
let x = 30;
console.log(x);
// → 30

console.log(globalThis.x); 
// → undefined   (Variables declared with let have block scope and don't attach to the global object)

// But if we explicitly set it:
globalThis.x = 10;
console.log(globalThis.x);
// → 10   (Explicitly creates/accesses a property on the global object)
What happens: let variables do not attach to the global object. To store something globally, you must explicitly attach it using globalThis.propertyName.
मराठी सारांश:
  • globalThis म्हणजे global object. Global scope म्हणजे सगळीकडे दिसणारे variables. दोन्ही वेगळे आहेत.
  • let किंवा const ने बनवलेले variables आपोआप global object चा भाग बनत नाहीत.
  • त्यासाठी globalThis.variableName असे स्पष्टपणे लिहावे लागते.
  • Node.js मध्ये विशेष: प्रत्येक file एक module असते. File च्या top ला var लिहिले तरी ते global object वर जात नाही (browser script मध्ये जाते).
  • उदाहरण: let x = 30 → globalThis.x = undefined. globalThis.x = 10 → आता global.

Q. How to access a variable globally without using export and import?

  • Normally, we use export and import to share variables between files.
  • But if we want a value to be globally accessible everywhere, we can attach it to the global object using globalThis or global.
Example 3: Setting a Global Variable
globalThis.username = 'heloo_javascript';
console.log(globalThis.username);
// → 'heloo_javascript'
What happens: You can now access globalThis.username in any other module in your Node.js application without importing it. ⚠ Disadvantage: Overusing this causes polluted global scope and makes tracking state changes hard.
मराठी सारांश:
  • सामान्यपणे files मध्ये data share करण्यासाठी export / import वापरतात.
  • जर तुम्हाला एखादा variable import/export न करता सगळीकडे वापरायचा असेल, तर तुम्ही तो globalThis (किंवा global) ला जोडू शकता.
  • उदाहरण: globalThis.username = 'vishal'. आता कोणत्याही file मध्ये globalThis.username वापरता येईल.
  • महत्वाचे: value वापरण्यापूर्वी ती set करणारी file आधी load (require) झाली पाहिजे.
  • धोका: जास्त वापरल्यास global scope गढूळ होतो, कोणी value बदलली ते शोधणे कठीण. म्हणून कमी वापरा.

Q. Should we use global variables (globalThis) for everything?

  • NO. As a general application design, use them CAREFULLY.
  • Avoid unnecessarily doing things like: globalThis.user = {} or globalThis.database = {}.
  • Problems:
    • Global state can be changed from anywhere (difficult to track).
    • Can create naming conflicts.
    • Makes testing harder and creates hidden dependencies.
  • Prefer: Modules (export/import), functions, classes, or dependency injection.
  • Use Case: Only use globalThis for genuine environment-level configurations.
मराठी सारांश:
  • प्रत्येक गोष्टीसाठी global variables वापरू नयेत.
  • यामुळे code मध्ये bugs येऊ शकतात आणि testing अवघड होते.
  • शक्यतो import/export चाच वापर करा.
  • का नको? global value कुठूनही, कोणीही बदलू शकतो. कोणी बदलली ते शोधणे कठीण होते.
  • Naming conflicts: दोन files नी same global नाव वापरले तर एक दुसऱ्याची value पुसतो.
  • Testing अवघड: एका test ने बदललेली global value दुसऱ्या test वर परिणाम करते (hidden dependency).
  • कधी चालते? फक्त खऱ्या environment-level settings साठी, कमी प्रमाणात.
  • उदाहरण: सोसायटीचा common notice board. कोणीही काहीही लिहू / पुसू शकतो, म्हणून महत्वाची माहिती तिथे ठेवत नाही.

Q. The process Object

Concept:
  • process is a built-in Node.js global object.
  • It provides information and control related to the current running Node.js process.
  • Important point: Global variables are only available within the same Node.js process.
  • They are NOT automatically shared between different Node.js processes (i.e., if you spin up two separate servers, they don't share globals).
  • To share data between multiple processes, use: Redis, Databases, or Message Brokers.
मराठी सारांश:
  • process हा Node.js चा built-in global object आहे. require करावा लागत नाही.
  • तो सध्या चालू असलेल्या Node.js program ची माहिती देतो आणि त्यावर नियंत्रण ठेवतो. उदा. process.pid (ID), process.cwd() (folder path), process.env (environment variables).
  • महत्वाचे: Global variables फक्त एका process पुरतेच मर्यादित असतात.
  • दोन वेगळे servers (processes) चालवले तर ते एकमेकांचे globals share करत नाहीत. प्रत्येकाची memory वेगळी असते.
  • Data share करायचा असेल तर: Redis, Database किंवा Message Broker वापरा.
  • उदाहरण: दोन वेगळी घरे. एका घरातील फ्रिजमधील वस्तू दुसऱ्या घरात आपोआप दिसत नाहीत.
Example 4: Using the process object
console.log(process.pid);
// → 12345   (Returns the Process ID of the currently running Node.js instance)

// ✔ corrected: console.log(process.pwd); is wrong. There is no `pwd` property. Use process.cwd() instead.
console.log(process.cwd());
// → 'D:\IMP\FULL-NODE-JS'   (Returns the Current Working Directory)
✔ `process.pwd` does not exist in Node.js. The correct function to get the current working directory is `process.cwd()`.
✔ मराठी: Node.js मध्ये `process.pwd` नसते. सध्याची directory कोणती आहे हे शोधण्यासाठी `process.cwd()` हे function वापरावे लागते.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What is the difference between global and globalThis in Node.js?
  • In Node.js, both point to the same underlying global object.
  • global is specific only to the Node.js runtime.
  • globalThis is the ECMAScript standard introduced to make code cross-compatible between browser, Node.js, and workers.
Q2. Why is attaching data to globalThis considered a bad practice?
  • It creates global state that any part of the application can modify without restriction.
  • This leads to unpredictable bugs, naming collisions, and makes unit testing very difficult.
  • State should be encapsulated within modules and passed explicitly.
Q3. If I start two terminal windows and run node app.js in both, do they share globalThis.data?
  • No. Each terminal starts its own isolated Node.js process with its own memory heap.
  • Global variables are scoped to a single process, not the operating system.
Q4. How do you find the current directory path where a Node script was executed?
  • You use the process.cwd() method.
  • This returns the absolute path of the current working directory of the Node.js process.
Q5. TRAP QUESTION: Are `__dirname`, `__filename`, and `require` part of the Global Object?
  • NO. This is a very common interview trap!
  • Even though you can use them anywhere without importing, they are not attached to globalThis.
  • Node.js internally wraps every file in a hidden Module Wrapper Function before execution.
  • __dirname, __filename, require, module, and exports are actually passed as local arguments to this hidden wrapper function, making them local to the current file only.
Q6. What is the exact difference between `__dirname` and `process.cwd()`?
  • __dirname: Always returns the path of the folder where the current file is located. (It is fixed).
  • process.cwd(): Always returns the path of the folder from where you ran the `node` command in the terminal. (It is dynamic).
मराठी सारांश (Interview Tips):
  • Interview मध्ये global आणि globalThis मधला फरक विचारला जातो.
  • तसेच Global variables का वापरू नयेत आणि दोन वेगवेगळे Node.js server एकमेकांचे variables share करत नाहीत हे लक्षात ठेवा.
  • सर्वात मोठी चूक (Trap): __dirname आणि require हे Global Object चा भाग नाहीत! ते प्रत्येक फाईलचे local variables असतात.
  • __dirname फाईल कुठे आहे ते सांगते, आणि process.cwd() terminal मध्ये command कुठून चालवली ते सांगते.

Quick Revision Summary

Concept Description
window Global object in the Browser environment.
global Global object in the Node.js environment.
globalThis Standard, environment-independent way to access the global object.
process Node.js global object for interacting with the current running process (e.g., getting process ID, current directory).

Chapter 4: Module System in Node.js

Q. What is a Module System in Node.js?

Concept:
  • A module is a self-contained, reusable, and encapsulated unit of code.
  • The module system is a mechanism that allows us to divide an application into separate, independent files (modules) instead of writing everything in one large file.
  • Each module can contain related variables, functions, classes, objects, constants, and business logic.
  • A module can:
    • Keep its internal code private (Encapsulation).
    • Export functionality that other modules need.
    • Import functionality from other modules.
  • In CommonJS (the default Node.js module system), each JavaScript file is treated as a separate module, and variables/functions inside it are private by default.
  • This makes the application modular, reusable, maintainable, testable, and easier to debug.
मराठी सारांश (Module System):
  • Module म्हणजे एक स्वतंत्र फाईल ज्यामध्ये संबंधित code असतो (functions, variables, classes).
  • Module system म्हणजे संपूर्ण app एकाच मोठ्या file मध्ये न लिहिता छोट्या स्वतंत्र files मध्ये विभागण्याची पद्धत.
  • प्रत्येक module चा code default ने private असतो. बाहेर द्यायचे असेल तर export करावे लागते आणि वापरायचे असेल तर import / require करावे लागते.
  • उदाहरण: पुस्तकातील प्रकरणे (chapters). प्रत्येक प्रकरण एक विषय सांभाळते. सगळे एकाच पानावर लिहिले तर गोंधळ होतो.
  • फायदे: reusable, maintainable, testable, debug करायला सोपे.
Code Example: A Simple Module (CommonJS)
// math.js
const secret = 42;                        // private (not exported)
function add(a, b) { return a + b; }
module.exports = { add };                 // public

// app.js
const { add } = require('./math');
console.log(add(2, 3));                   // 5
// console.log(secret);                   // ❌ ReferenceError (private)

Q. Why do we need Modules?

  • Encapsulation: Keeps internal implementation private. Other modules cannot access internal variables directly unless exported.
  • Reusability: A module (like `math.js`) can be written once and reused in multiple files.
  • Better Code Organization: Related functionality is kept together (e.g., all auth logic in `auth.js`).
  • Separation of Concerns: Each module focuses on a specific responsibility (e.g., database connection vs. user operations).
  • Easier Maintenance: If there is a bug, you only need to check the specific module instead of scanning a massive single file.
  • Prevent Global Scope Pollution: Variables inside a module do not conflict with variables in another module.
  • Module Caching: Node.js caches modules after they are loaded, avoiding repeated evaluation and improving performance.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Encapsulation: आतला code private राहतो. Export केल्याशिवाय बाहेरून वापरता येत नाही.
  • Reusability: एकदा लिहिलेला math.js अनेक files मध्ये वापरता येतो.
  • Organization: संबंधित code एकत्र. उदा. login चा सगळा code auth.js मध्ये.
  • Separation of Concerns: प्रत्येक module चे एकच काम (DB connection वेगळे, user logic वेगळे).
  • Easy Maintenance: bug आला तर फक्त ती file तपासायची, हजारो lines नाही.
  • No Global Pollution: एका file मधले count दुसऱ्या file मधल्या count शी भांडत नाही.
  • Caching: module एकदाच load होतो, पुढे cache मधून मिळतो. Performance चांगला.
Figure 5: Encapsulation (Private Data vs Exposed API)

Q. What are the Types of Modules in Node.js?

  • 1. Core / Built-in Modules: Provided by Node.js itself. No need to install them. (e.g., fs, path, os, http).
  • 2. User-defined Modules: Custom modules created by the developer (e.g., math.js, auth.js).
  • 3. Third-party Modules: Packages created outside the Node.js core, commonly installed through npm (e.g., express, mongoose, lodash).
मराठी सारांश:
  • Node.js मध्ये ३ प्रकारचे modules असतात.
  • 1) Core / Built-in: Node.js मध्ये आधीपासून असतात, install करावे लागत नाहीत. उदा. fs, path, os, http. वापर: require('fs').
  • 2) User-defined: आपण स्वतः बनवलेले. उदा. math.js, auth.js. वापर: require('./math') (./ path आवश्यक).
  • 3) Third-party: इतरांनी बनवलेले, npm वरून download करतो. उदा. express, mongoose. वापर: npm install express नंतर require('express').
  • उदाहरण: घरातले भांडे (core), स्वतः बनवलेले पदार्थ (user-defined), बाजारातून आणलेले (third-party).
Code Example: All 3 Types
const fs = require('fs');              // 1. core (no install, no ./)
const math = require('./math');        // 2. user-defined (needs ./ path)
const express = require('express');    // 3. third-party (npm install express)
Figure 6: Types of Modules Visualization

Q. What are the two Module Systems in Node.js?

  • Node.js supports two major JavaScript module systems: CommonJS and ES Modules (ESM).
  • CommonJS: The traditional module system in Node.js. Uses module.exports to export and require() to import.
  • ES Modules (ESM): The standard JavaScript module system (modern). Uses export to export and import to import.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Node.js मध्ये दोन प्रकारच्या module systems आहेत.
  • CommonJS (जुनी, Node ची default): require() ने import, module.exports ने export. Files .js / .cjs.
  • ES Modules (नवीन, JavaScript standard): import / export. package.json मध्ये "type": "module" किंवा .mjs extension लागते.
  • फरक: CommonJS synchronous load होतो. ESM asynchronous आणि static असतो (tree-shaking शक्य).
  • आता काय वापरायचे? नवीन projects साठी ESM. जुन्या projects मध्ये CommonJS अजूनही खूप वापरले जाते.
Figure 7: CommonJS vs ES Modules

Q. How can we export something in CommonJS?

  • There are two common approaches to exporting logic in CommonJS.
  • 1. Assign an object to module.exports: You can wrap multiple functions inside an object and export the entire object at once.
  • 2. Assign properties individually: You can attach functions one by one using module.exports.propertyName = functionName.
IMPORTANT: module.exports is a VALUE.
  • It represents the final value exported. If you re-assign module.exports = multiply;, the previous exports are completely replaced.
Avoid Confusing: module.exports vs module.exports.property
// ─── Example 1: Assigning a Property ───
module.exports.add = add;
// The exports object now has a new property called `add`.
// Shape: { add: [Function] }

// ─── Example 2: Re-assigning the whole object ───
module.exports = add;
// The exports value IS the function directly.
// Shape: [Function]
What happens: Be very careful not to accidentally overwrite the entire module.exports object when you meant to just add a property.
मराठी सारांश:
  • CommonJS मध्ये काही export करायचे असेल तर आपण module.exports = ... वापरतो.
  • पद्धत 1: object export: module.exports = { add, sub }.
  • पद्धत 2: एक-एक property: module.exports.add = add.
  • पण लक्षात ठेवा, module.exports हे एक value आहे.
  • जर तुम्ही त्याला नवीन function assign केले तर आधीचे सगळे export पुसले जातील.
  • उदाहरण: module.exports.add = add; नंतर module.exports = multiply; केले, तर आता फक्त multiply export होते. add गेला.

Q. What is the difference between a Module and a Function?

  • Module: A larger organizational unit (a container). It is usually a single file that contains multiple related functions, variables, classes, and logic.
  • Function: A reusable piece of executable logic designed to perform a particular task. A function exists inside a module.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Module हे एक मोठे Container (डबा) असते ज्यामध्ये अनेक Functions, Variables, आणि Classes असू शकतात.
  • तर Function हे एक छोटे काम करणारे logic असते जे Module च्या आत असते.
  • Module: सहसा एक file. Export / import ने share होतो. Load झाल्यावर cache होतो.
  • Function: code चा एक block. Call केल्यावर चालतो, आणि कितीही वेळा call करता येतो.
  • उदाहरण: Module = स्वयंपाकघर (सगळी साधने). Function = मिक्सर (एकच काम: वाटणे).
PointModuleFunction
SizeLarge (a whole file)Small (a block of code)
ContainsFunctions, variables, classesStatements / logic
Shared usingmodule.exports / exportCalled by name: add(2, 3)
RunsOnce (on first load, then cached)Every time it is called

Q. Why is the `module` object NOT Global in Node.js?

  • module is NOT a global object. Node.js provides it locally inside each CommonJS module wrapper.
  • Conceptually, Node.js wraps every CommonJS file inside an invisible function before executing it:
    (function (exports, require, module, __filename, __dirname) { /* your code */ });
  • Because module is provided as a local parameter of this wrapper function, it belongs ONLY to that module's scope.
  • This ensures scope isolation, preventing different files from polluting each other's namespaces.
मराठी सारांश:
  • module हा global object नाही.
  • Node.js प्रत्येक फाईलला एका अदृश्य function च्या आत wrap करते, आणि module त्या function चा parameter असतो.
  • Wrapper: (function (exports, require, module, __filename, __dirname) { तुमचा code })
  • म्हणून हे 5 जण (exports, require, module, __filename, __dirname) प्रत्येक file मध्ये local असतात, global नाहीत.
  • यामुळे प्रत्येक फाईलचे variables सुरक्षित राहतात आणि files एकमेकांत गोंधळ घालत नाहीत (scope isolation).
  • उदाहरण: प्रत्येक file ला स्वतःची खोली (function scope) मिळते. खोलीतले सामान (variables) दुसऱ्या खोलीत दिसत नाही.

Q. How do ESM modules handle the `module` object?

  • ES Modules don't have the CommonJS module object or __dirname / __filename variables.
  • ESM is a completely different module mechanism.
  • If you need metadata about the current module in ESM (like the file path), you must use the import.meta object instead.
✔ Corrected: ESM is itself a module system. It uses a different mechanism (`import`/`export` and `import.meta`).
✔ मराठी: ESM ही स्वतः एक वेगळी module system आहे, त्यामुळे त्यात CommonJS प्रमाणे `module` object नसतो; त्याऐवजी `import.meta` वापरले जाते.
Code Example: Getting __dirname in ESM
// app.mjs (ES Module)
import { fileURLToPath } from 'url';
import path from 'path';

const __filename = fileURLToPath(import.meta.url);  // file:///D:/app.mjs → D:\app.mjs
const __dirname = path.dirname(__filename);

// Node 20.11+ shortcut:
console.log(import.meta.dirname, import.meta.filename);
मराठी सारांश (ESM मध्ये module object):
  • ESM मध्ये CommonJS चे module, exports, require, __dirname, __filename नसतात.
  • कारण ESM ही वेगळी यंत्रणा आहे. ती wrapper function वापरत नाही.
  • सध्याच्या file ची माहिती हवी असेल तर import.meta वापरा. उदा. import.meta.url (file चा URL).
  • __dirname हवा असेल तर fileURLToPath + path.dirname वापरा, किंवा Node 20.11+ मध्ये import.meta.dirname.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What is the difference between `module.exports` and `exports`?
  • module.exports is the actual object that gets returned by a require() call.
  • exports is initially just a shorthand reference (pointer) to module.exports.
  • If you directly assign a new value to exports (like exports = add;), you break the reference, and it will NOT export anything. Always use module.exports = ... or exports.property = ....
Q2. Are variables in a module automatically available in the global scope?
  • No. Variables declared inside a Node.js module are private to that module due to the CommonJS wrapper function.
  • To make them accessible outside, they must be explicitly added to module.exports or attached to globalThis.
Q3. What happens if I `require()` the same module in two different files? Does it execute twice?
  • No. Node.js caches modules after the first time they are loaded.
  • When the second file calls require(), Node.js returns the cached module.exports object instead of re-executing the entire file. This improves performance.
Q4. How do you find the directory name of the current file in CommonJS vs ESM?
  • In CommonJS: You use the injected variable __dirname.
  • In ES Modules (ESM): __dirname is not defined. You have to derive it using import.meta.url and the path/url core modules.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Q1 (module.exports vs exports): require() नेहमी module.exports परत देतो. exports हा फक्त त्याचा shortcut (pointer) आहे. exports = fn केले तर link तुटते आणि काहीच export होत नाही. exports.add = fn चालते.
  • Q2 (Variables global होतात का?): नाही. Wrapper function मुळे प्रत्येक file चे variables private असतात.
  • Q3 (एकच module दोनदा require केला तर?): तो दोनदा चालत नाही. पहिल्यांदा चालतो, cache होतो, आणि दुसऱ्यांदा cache मधून मिळतो.
  • Q4 (__dirname): CommonJS मध्ये थेट उपलब्ध. ESM मध्ये import.meta.url वरून काढावा लागतो.

Quick Revision Summary

Term Description
Module A self-contained file containing encapsulated, reusable logic.
CommonJS Default Node.js module system. Uses require() and module.exports.
ES Modules (ESM) Standard JavaScript module system. Uses import and export.
Module Wrapper An invisible function Node wraps around CommonJS files to keep variables private.
import.meta The ESM replacement for getting module-specific metadata (like file paths).

Chapter 5: require() vs import

Q. What is require()?

Concept:
  • require() is a synchronous, runtime-oriented CommonJS (CJS) module-loading mechanism.
  • It is used to load built-in Node.js modules, third-party packages, or local custom modules.
  • It returns the value exported by module.exports.
  • It is NOT a standard JavaScript language function; it belongs exclusively to the CommonJS environment in Node.js.
  • CommonJS require() is synchronous. The execution waits for require() to finish loading the module before continuing to the next line.
  • Node.js supports two major module systems:
    • CommonJS: require() + module.exports
    • ES Modules (ESM): import + export
मराठी सारांश:
  • require() हे CommonJS चे function आहे जे दुसऱ्या फाईलचा (module) code आपल्या फाईलमध्ये आणण्यासाठी वापरले जाते.
  • काय load करता येते? Core modules (require('fs')), npm packages (require('express')), स्वतःच्या files (require('./math')), JSON (require('./data.json')).
  • Return काय करते? त्या file चे module.exports.
  • हे synchronous आहे, म्हणजे जोपर्यंत फाईल load होत नाही तोपर्यंत पुढचा code रन होत नाही.
  • हे JavaScript language चे standard function नाही. ते फक्त Node.js च्या CommonJS मध्ये आहे (browser मध्ये नाही).
  • उदाहरण: library मधून पुस्तक मागवणे. पुस्तक हातात येईपर्यंत आपण counter वर थांबतो (synchronous).
  • Syntax: const value = require(id)
  • id (string): A module name ('fs', 'express') or a path ('./math', '../utils/db').
