  • Returns: Whatever that module assigned to module.exports (an object, function, class or value).
  • Throws: MODULE_NOT_FOUND if the module can't be located.

Q. How does require() resolve and load a module? (Module Cache)

  • When Node sees require("./math"), it conceptually does the following:
  • 1. Resolve Path: Finds where the file is actually located.
  • 2. Check Cache: Node.js checks if this module was already loaded before.
    • If YES (already cached) -> Returns the cached module.exports immediately.
    • If NO (first time) -> Loads the module -> Executes the module -> Caches the exported value -> Returns it.
  • Because of this caching, if you require("./math") multiple times in different files, the math.js file is evaluated only ONCE. Subsequent calls return the cached module.
Figure 8: require() Module Resolution & Caching
मराठी सारांश:
  • Step 1 (Resolve): file नेमकी कुठे आहे ते शोधतो (core module? ./ path? node_modules?).
  • Step 2 (Cache check): आधी load झाली आहे का ते बघतो.
  • हो → cache मधले module.exports लगेच देतो.
  • नाही → file load करतो → चालवतो → result cache मध्ये ठेवतो → परत देतो.
  • Node.js एकदा load केलेली फाईल cache (लक्षात) ठेवते.
  • म्हणजे जर तुम्ही math.js फाईल २ वेळा require केली, तरी ती फक्त पहिल्यांदाच रन होईल, दुसऱ्या वेळी cache मधील result दिला जाईल.
  • उदाहरण: एकदा एखादा phone नंबर शोधला की तो contacts मध्ये save करतो. पुढच्या वेळी पुन्हा शोधावा लागत नाही.
Code Example: Proof of Caching
// counter.js
console.log('counter.js is running!');   // prints only ONCE
let count = 0;
module.exports = { inc: () => ++count };

// app.js
const a = require('./counter');   // prints "counter.js is running!"
const b = require('./counter');   // nothing printed (from cache)
a.inc(); a.inc();
console.log(b.inc());             // 3 → a and b are the SAME object
console.log(a === b);             // true

Q. require() Local File Extension

  • In CommonJS, it commonly allows you to drop the extension: require("./math") instead of require("./math.js"). Node automatically infers the .js extension.
  • IMPORTANT: Do NOT generalize this behavior to ESM. Relative/absolute imports in ESM generally require explicit file extensions (like import math from "./math.js").
मराठी सारांश:
  • CommonJS मध्ये .js extension लिहिणे गरजेचे नसते. उदा. require('./math') चालते.
  • Node कोणत्या क्रमाने शोधतो? ./math → ./math.js → ./math.json → ./math.node → ./math/index.js.
  • पण ESM मध्ये import करताना पूर्ण नाव लिहिणे बंधनकारक आहे: import x from './math.js'.
  • ESM मध्ये extension न लिहिल्यास ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND error येते.

Q. What is import? (ES Modules)

  • import belongs to ES Modules (ESM).
  • ESM is the standardized JavaScript module system.
  • ESM uses import to load modules and export (or export default) to expose them.
Example 1: ESM Exports & Imports
// math.js (Exporting)
export function add(a, b) { return a + b; }                // Named Export
export default function subtract(a, b) { return a - b; }   // Default Export

// app.js (Importing)
import subtract from "./math.js";           // Importing Default (no curly braces)
import { add } from "./math.js";            // Importing Named (needs curly braces)
Memory Trick: Named Export -> Needs { } braces when importing.
Default Export -> Does NOT need { } braces when importing.
मराठी सारांश:
  • ESM (ES Modules) ही JavaScript ची official, standard module पद्धत आहे. Browser आणि Node दोन्हीत चालते.
  • Named export: एका file मधून अनेक. Import करताना { } लागतात आणि नाव तेच वापरावे लागते: import { add } from './math.js'.
  • Default export: एका file मधून फक्त एक. { } लागत नाहीत आणि कोणतेही नाव देता येते: import sub from './math.js'.
  • Rename: import { add as sum } from './math.js'. सगळे एकत्र: import * as math from './math.js'.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: Named = curly braces, Default = curly braces नाही.

Q. How to enable ESM in Node.js?

  • Method 1: Add "type": "module" in your package.json file. All .js files will now be treated as ESM.
  • Method 2: Use the .mjs file extension. (Node treats .mjs files as ESM regardless of package.json).
  • If you need explicitly CommonJS, you can use the .cjs file extension.
Code Example: package.json for ESM
{
  "name": "my-app",
  "type": "module",        // ← every .js file is now ESM
  "main": "app.js"
}

// file extensions always win:
// app.mjs → always ESM      config.cjs → always CommonJS
Setting.js files are.mjs.cjs
No "type" (default)CommonJSESMCommonJS
"type": "commonjs"CommonJSESMCommonJS
"type": "module"ESMESMCommonJS
मराठी सारांश (ESM कसे चालू करायचे):
  • पद्धत 1: package.json मध्ये "type": "module" लिहा. आता सगळ्या .js files ESM म्हणून चालतील.
  • पद्धत 2: file चे नाव app.mjs ठेवा. .mjs नेहमी ESM असते.
  • CommonJS हवे असेल तर: .cjs extension वापरा. ती नेहमी CommonJS असते.
  • Default: "type" लिहिले नसेल तर .js = CommonJS.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: extension (.mjs / .cjs) नेहमी package.json पेक्षा जास्त महत्वाचे.

Q. require() vs static import vs dynamic import() (Detailed Differences)

  • 1. require():
    • Belongs to CommonJS (CJS).
    • Synchronous: It blocks execution until the module is fully loaded.
    • Runtime-oriented: It evaluates dependencies at runtime (you can conditionally require modules inside an if block, though it's still synchronous).
    • Harder for bundlers to analyze statically (poor tree-shaking support).
  • 2. Static import:
    • Belongs to ES Modules (ESM).
    • Statically Analyzed: Dependencies are resolved and linked before the code actually runs.
    • Must be placed at the top level of the file (you cannot put a static import inside an if statement).
    • Very strong static analysis, making it highly Tree-Shaking friendly (dead code elimination).
  • 3. Dynamic import():
    • Belongs to ES Modules (ESM), but operates like a function.
    • Asynchronous: It returns a Promise.
    • Evaluated at runtime. Perfect for Lazy Loading, conditional loading, and code splitting (loading heavy modules only when actually needed).
Code Example: The 3 Approaches
// 1. require() (CommonJS - Synchronous)
const fs = require('fs');
const data = fs.readFileSync('./file.txt');

// 2. Static import (ESM - Must be at top-level)
import { readFile } from 'fs/promises';
const data = await readFile('./file.txt');

// 3. Dynamic import() (ESM - Asynchronous & Conditional)
if (userClicksDownloadPDF) {
    // pdf-generator is a heavy package, so we load it ONLY if needed
    const pdfLib = await import('pdf-generator');
    pdfLib.generate();
}
What happens: The dynamic import() statement prevents the heavy pdf-generator package from loading into memory when the server starts. It only loads when a specific condition is met, saving RAM and startup time.

Comparison Table: require() vs import vs import()

Feature require() static import dynamic import()
System CommonJS ES Modules (ESM) ES Modules (ESM)
Execution Synchronous Asynchronous (Pre-parsed) Asynchronous (Returns Promise)
Placement Anywhere (Runtime) Top-level ONLY Anywhere (Runtime)
Tree Shaking Very Hard Excellent (Static) Hard (Dynamic)
Best Use Case Legacy Node.js apps Modern standard apps Lazy Loading heavy modules
मराठी सारांश (Detailed):
  • 1. require():
    • ही CommonJS ची जुनी पद्धत आहे.
    • ही Synchronous असते, म्हणजे जोपर्यंत फाईल पूर्ण लोड होत नाही, तोपर्यंत पुढचा कोड थांबून राहतो (रन होत नाही).
  • 2. static import:
    • ही ES Modules ची नवीन पद्धत आहे.
    • ही नेहमी फाईलच्या अगदी वर (top-level) लिहावी लागते.
    • यामुळे bundlers (उदा. Webpack) ला न वापरलेला फालतू कोड (dead code) सहज ओळखून काढायला मदत होते. यालाच Tree Shaking म्हणतात.
  • 3. dynamic import():
    • ही Asynchronous असते आणि Promise return करते.
    • जेव्हा आपल्याला एखादी मोठी फाईल नेहमी नको असते, तर फक्त विशिष्ट वेळी (उदा. एखाद्या button वर click केल्यावर) लोड करायची असते, तेव्हा याचा वापर होतो.
    • यामुळे सुरुवातीला मेमरी वाचते. या पद्धतीला Lazy Loading म्हणतात.

Q. Tree Shaking vs Minification

  • Tree Shaking: A build-time optimization technique that uses static analysis to safely remove unused code from the final JavaScript bundle. (Question: "Do we need this code?")
  • Minification: A process that makes the required code smaller by shrinking/removing unnecessary characters like spaces, comments, and long variable names. (Question: "Can we make the required code smaller?")
  • Memory Trick: Tree Shaking -> REMOVES unnecessary code. Minification -> SHRINKS remaining code.
  • Important: Tree shaking is NOT performed by Node.js automatically at runtime. It is performed by compatible build tools/bundlers (like Webpack or Vite) during the build process.
  • ESM (import/export) is tree-shaking friendly because its static structure allows tools to confidently know which exports are used and which are not. CommonJS (require) makes this very hard because imports are dynamic at runtime.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Tree Shaking म्हणजे न वापरलेला फालतू code काढून टाकणे (ESM मुळे हे शक्य होते).
  • उदाहरण: झाड हलवल्यावर वाळलेली पाने (unused code) खाली पडतात. म्हणून नाव "tree shaking".
  • आणि Minification म्हणजे आवश्यक असलेला code ची size कमी करणे (spaces, comments काढून, लांब नावे छोटी करून).
  • फरक लक्षात ठेवा: Tree shaking = code काढतो. Minification = उरलेला code छोटा करतो.
  • Node.js स्वतः Tree shaking करत नाही, ते Bundlers (Webpack, Vite, Rollup) build वेळी करतात.
  • ESM static असल्याने bundler ला कळते की कोणते export वापरले आहेत. CommonJS (require) runtime ला ठरतो, म्हणून त्यात हे अवघड आहे.
Figure 9: Final Memory Trick (CommonJS vs ESM)

Q. The Module Wrapper Function (IIFE)

Concept:
  • Before Node.js executes the code of a CommonJS module, it internally wraps that module's code inside a hidden function.
  • This invisible function is known as the Module Wrapper Function.
  • It behaves exactly like an IIFE (Immediately Invoked Function Expression) in standard JavaScript.
  • Why is it needed?
    • Private Module Scope (Encapsulation): Variables declared in one file don't automatically leak into the global scope and crash variables in other files.
    • Dependency Injection: It provides 5 important variables (exports, require, module, __filename, __dirname) into the module's scope.
  • These 5 variables look like global variables, but they are actually local to the specific module because they are passed as parameters to this hidden function.
  • Note: This wrapper mechanism is strictly for CommonJS. ES Modules (ESM) do not use this wrapper function.
Code Example: Conceptual View of the Wrapper
// 1. When you write this code in your app.js:
const username = "Vishal";
console.log(__filename);
module.exports = username;

// 2. Node.js conceptually wraps it like this before executing:
(function (exports, require, module, __filename, __dirname) {
    
    // --- YOUR CODE STARTS HERE ---
    const username = "Vishal";
    console.log(__filename);
    module.exports = username;
    // --- YOUR CODE ENDS HERE ---

}); // <-- and then immediately invokes it, passing the 5 arguments!
What happens: Because your code is inside a function, const username becomes a local variable of that function. It does not pollute the global Node.js environment!
Figure 10: How Node.js keeps module scope private
मराठी सारांश (Detailed):
  • Node.js कोणताही CommonJS फाईल रन करण्यापूर्वी त्याला एका function मध्ये (Wrapper Function) गुंडाळते.
  • यामुळे त्या फाईलचे variables सुरक्षित (private) राहतात आणि इतर फाईल्समध्ये मिसळत नाहीत.
  • या wrapper मुळेच आपल्याला require आणि __dirname सारख्या गोष्टी वापरता येतात ज्या फक्त त्या फाईल पुरत्या मर्यादित असतात.
  • महत्त्वाची नोंद: ही Wrapper Function पद्धत फक्त CommonJS मध्येच असते. ES Modules (ESM) मध्ये आपण याचा वापर करू शकत नाही.

Q. The 5 Parameters of the Wrapper

  • 1. exports: A shortcut reference to module.exports. (You can do exports.add = add, but you cannot do exports = add to completely overwrite it).
  • 2. require: A function used to import other CommonJS modules.
  • 3. module: An object representing the current module. The most important property is module.exports, which determines what gets exported.
  • 4. __filename: The absolute path to the current module file.
  • 5. __dirname: The absolute path to the directory containing the current module.
मराठी सारांश (Detailed):
  • Wrapper function मुळे फाईलला ५ गोष्टी मिळतात: exports, require, module, __filename, __dirname.
  • हे दिसायला global वाटतात, पण ते फक्त त्याच फाईलसाठी (local) उपलब्ध असतात.
  • तसेच या ५ गोष्टी आपण ES Modules (ESM) मध्ये वापरू शकत नाही (उदा. ESM मध्ये require किंवा __dirname चालत नाही).
  • exports: module.exports चा shortcut. exports.add = add चालते, पण exports = add ने link तुटते.
  • require: दुसरे CommonJS modules आणण्याचे function. module: सध्याच्या file चा object, यातले module.exports बाहेर जाते.
  • __filename: सध्याच्या file चा पूर्ण path. __dirname: ती file ज्या folder मध्ये आहे त्याचा path.
  • ESM मधले पर्याय: import / export, आणि import.meta.url / import.meta.dirname.

Q. Practical Equivalents: CommonJS vs ES Modules (ESM)

Concept:
  • Because ESM does not use the Module Wrapper Function, it does not have access to require, module.exports, __dirname, or __filename.
  • If you are converting a CommonJS project to an ESM project, you must manually recreate these missing variables using ESM's import.meta.url.
  • You also need to change how you export and import functions, and how you load JSON files.
  • 1. Importing & Exporting:
    • In CommonJS, you use require() for importing and module.exports for exporting.
    • In ESM, you must replace require() with import.
    • For exporting in ESM, you must replace module.exports with export default or named export.
  • 2. Getting __dirname & __filename:
    • In CommonJS, these are automatically available via the Module Wrapper Function.
    • In ESM, the wrapper function does not exist, so these variables do not exist.
    • You must manually calculate them using fileURLToPath from the url core module and dirname from the path core module.
  • 3. Importing JSON files:
    • In CommonJS, importing a JSON file is as simple as require('./data.json'). Node automatically parses it into an object.
    • In ESM, using a normal import for JSON will throw an error.
    • You must use a special Import Assertion to tell Node.js it is a JSON file: import data from './data.json' with { type: "json" };
Figure 11: Side-by-Side Comparison of CJS vs ESM Syntax
Code Example: How to recreate __dirname in ESM
// ESM DOES NOT have __dirname. You must create it manually like this:

import path from 'path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'url';

// 1. Get the current file's absolute path using import.meta.url
const __filename = fileURLToPath(import.meta.url);

// 2. Extract the directory name from the file path
const __dirname = path.dirname(__filename);

console.log("Directory path is:", __dirname);
मराठी सारांश (Practical Migration Guide):
  • 1. __dirname आणि __filename: ESM मध्ये हे नसतात. तुम्हाला import.meta.url आणि fileURLToPath वापरून ते मॅन्युअली काढावे लागतात (वरील कोड प्रमाणे).
  • 2. JSON फाईल्स: JSON फाईल्स import करताना ESM मध्ये with { type: "json" } असा खास उल्लेख करावा लागतो.
  • 3. फाईल Extension: CommonJS मध्ये फाईल import करताना require('./app') चालत असे. पण ESM मध्ये फाईलचे पूर्ण नाव extension सहित (उदा. import './app.js') लिहिणे बंधनकारक आहे.
  • 4. Exports: module.exports च्या जागी आता export किंवा export default वापरावे लागते.
  • 5. Top-level Import: require() आपण कोणत्याही if block मध्ये वापरू शकत होतो, पण import हे नेहमी फाईलच्या सर्वात वर (Top-level) लिहावे लागते.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What is require()?
  • require() is Node.js's CommonJS module-loading mechanism used to load a module and receive its exported value synchronously at runtime.
Q2. What is the difference between require() and import?
  • require() belongs to CommonJS, is runtime-oriented, and is synchronous.
  • Static import belongs to ES Modules, and is part of ESM's static module structure, allowing better static analysis.
Q3. What is import() (with parenthesis)?
  • import() is the dynamic ESM loading mechanism. It is asynchronous, returns a Promise, and allows lazy loading or conditional loading of modules.
Q4. Why is ESM better for tree shaking than CommonJS?
  • ESM uses statically analyzable import/export syntax, allowing bundlers to determine exactly which exports are used and safely remove unused code at build time.
  • CommonJS is highly dynamic at runtime, making it difficult for tools to confidently know what can be removed.
Q5. Does import automatically perform tree shaking?
  • No. ESM is tree-shaking-friendly, but a bundler or build tool (like Webpack or Rollup) actually performs the removal. Node.js runtime itself does not tree shake.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Q1 (require काय आहे?): CommonJS चा module loader. Runtime ला, synchronous पद्धतीने module.exports परत देतो.
  • Q2 (require vs import): require = CommonJS, runtime, synchronous, कुठेही लिहिता येतो. import = ESM, static, file च्या top ला, tree-shaking friendly.
  • Q3 (import() कंसासह): dynamic import. Asynchronous, Promise देतो. Lazy loading / गरज असेल तेव्हाच load करण्यासाठी.
  • Q4 (ESM tree shaking ला चांगले का?): static structure मुळे bundler ला नक्की कळते कोणता code वापरला नाही.
  • Q5 (import आपोआप tree shaking करतो का?): नाही. ESM फक्त ते शक्य करते. प्रत्यक्ष काम bundler करतो, Node.js नाही.

Quick Revision Summary

Concept Description
require() Synchronous CommonJS runtime module loader.
import Static ESM syntax for importing dependencies.
import() Dynamic, asynchronous ESM loader that returns a Promise.
Tree Shaking Removing unused code during the build process (done by bundlers).
Minification Shrinking the remaining code (removing spaces/comments).

Chapter 6: The process Object

Q. What is process in Node.js?

Concept:
  • process is a global object provided built-in by Node.js.
  • It gives you vital information about the currently running Node.js process (such as memory usage, process ID, and environment variables).
  • It also gives you control over the process (such as the ability to exit the process manually).
  • Since process is a global object, we do not need to import it (i.e. no require('process') is needed).
  • The process object provides the following key capabilities:
    • Process Information: Get details about the running OS and Node instance (e.g., process.pid, process.platform, process.version).
    • Environment Information: Access system environment variables or configuration data using process.env.
    • Command-line Arguments: Read inputs passed from the terminal when starting the script using process.argv.
    • Memory Usage: Check how much RAM the Node.js process is consuming using process.memoryUsage().
    • Process Control: Manually terminate the application using process.exit() or get the current working directory using process.cwd().
    • Events & Signals: Listen for system events like unhandled errors (uncaughtException) or termination signals (SIGINT).
  • Note on Environment Variables (dotenv):
    • To load custom configuration variables (like API Keys or Database Passwords), we use a package called dotenv.
    • The dotenv package reads a .env file in your project and automatically loads its contents into the process.env object.
मराठी सारांश:
  • process हा Node.js चा एक built-in global object आहे.
  • हा object सध्या चालू असलेल्या Node.js program बद्दल महत्त्वाची माहिती देतो (उदा. Process ID, OS ची माहिती, आणि RAM किती वापरली जात आहे).
  • याचा वापर करून आपण terminal मधून दिलेले inputs (command-line arguments) वाचू शकतो.
  • तसेच, dotenv package च्या मदतीने आपण .env फाईलमधील passwords आणि keys या process.env मध्ये लोड करू शकतो.
  • या object मुळे आपण process ला मध्येच थांबवू (exit) किंवा पूर्णपणे control करू शकतो.

Q. Important Process Properties (pid, platform, arch, versions)

  • process.pid: Returns the Process ID (PID) of the currently running Node.js process. 
    console.log(process.pid); // 10952
  • process.ppid: Returns the Process ID of the parent process that started the current Node.js process. 
    console.log(process.ppid); // 8421
  • process.platform: Returns the operating system platform. Useful for OS-specific code. 
    console.log(process.platform); // 'win32' (Windows), 'linux', or 'darwin' (macOS)
  • process.arch: Returns the CPU architecture. 
    console.log(process.arch); // 'x64', 'arm64', or 'ia32'
  • process.version: Returns the version of the running Node.js. 
    console.log(process.version); // 'v20.9.0'
  • process.versions: Returns an object containing version info for Node.js and its internal dependencies (like v8, openssl, zlib). 
    console.log(process.versions.v8); // '11.3.244.8-node.16'
मराठी सारांश:
  • process.pid आणि process.ppid ने अनुक्रमे सध्याच्या आणि parent process चा ID मिळतो.
  • process.platform आणि process.arch ने आपला OS (उदा. Windows/Linux) आणि CPU ची माहिती मिळते, ज्याचा उपयोग OS नुसार कोड चालवण्यासाठी होतो.
  • process.version मुळे Node.js चे सध्याचे version कळते.
  • process.versions मुळे Node.js सोबत येणाऱ्या इतर dependencies (जसे की v8 engine) चे व्हर्जन्स मिळतात.

Q. Command-line Arguments: argv vs execArgv vs argv0

  • process.argv: An array containing the command-line arguments passed when launching the Node.js process.
    • argv[0] = Node.js executable absolute path.
    • argv[1] = Path of the JavaScript file being executed.
    • argv[2], argv[3]... = User-provided arguments.
    • Example: Running node app.js port=3000 
      console.log(process.argv[2]); // 'port=3000'
  • process.argv0: Contains the original value of argv[0] passed when the Node.js process started. 
    console.log(process.argv0); // 'node'
  • process.execArgv: Contains the command-line arguments passed directly to the Node.js executable (like --inspect), NOT the normal application arguments. 
    // Command: node --inspect app.js
console.log(process.execArgv); // ['--inspect']
Figure 10: Command-Line Argument Flow
मराठी सारांश:
  • टर्मिनलमध्ये आपण Node रन करताना जे शब्द देतो, ते सगळे process.argv च्या array मध्ये सेव्ह होतात.
  • त्यातले पहिले दोन Node चा path आणि file चा path असतात.
  • खरे arguments [2] पासून चालू होतात. म्हणून process.argv.slice(2) वापरतात.
  • process.execArgv: file च्या नावाआधी Node ला दिलेले flags. उदा. --inspect, --watch.
  • process.argv0: Node सुरू करताना दिलेले मूळ नाव (बहुतेक 'node').
  • लक्षात ठेवा: सगळे arguments string असतात. "100" number हवा असेल तर Number() करा.
  • उदाहरण: node app.js hello 100 → argv = [node path, app.js path, 'hello', '100'].

Q. Environment Variables (process.env)

  • process.env provides access to environment variables available to the Node.js process. 
    console.log(process.env.NODE_ENV);
  • Important Use Cases: Configuration, Database URLs, API keys, Environment selection (e.g. development vs production), Feature flags.
  • Security: Never hard-code sensitive configuration directly into source code. Always use environment variables.
मराठी सारांश:
  • process.env चा वापर Database URL किंवा API keys सारख्या private गोष्टी सुरक्षित ठेवण्यासाठी होतो.
  • तसेच आपला प्रोजेक्ट development किंवा production environment मध्ये चालवायचा आहे हे ठरवण्यासाठी याचा उपयोग होतो.
  • कोडमध्ये कधीही sensitive डेटा थेट लिहू नये, त्यासाठी नेहमी environment variables वापरावे.
  • वाचणे: process.env.PORT, process.env.DB_URL. सगळ्या values string असतात, number साठी Number().
  • सेट कसे करायचे? .env file + dotenv, किंवा Node 20.6+ मध्ये node --env-file=.env app.js, किंवा server / hosting platform वर.
  • NODE_ENV: development / production नुसार logs, errors, caching बदलतात.
  • उदाहरण: घराची चावी खिशात (env) ठेवा, दारावर (code / GitHub) टांगू नका.

Q. process.cwd() vs __dirname

  • process.cwd(): Returns the Current Working Directory. It tells us: "From which directory was the Node.js process launched?" 
    console.log(process.cwd()); // e.g. 'D:\MyProject'
  • __dirname (CommonJS): Returns the absolute directory path containing the current JavaScript file being executed. 
    console.log(__dirname); // e.g. 'D:\MyProject\src'
  • The Difference: They can be different! If you run node src/app.js from the root folder:
    • process.cwd() points to the root folder (where the terminal is).
    • __dirname inside app.js points to the src folder (where the file is).
मराठी सारांश:
  • process.cwd() म्हणजे तुम्ही ज्या folder मधून terminal वर node command टाकली आहे तो folder.
  • __dirname म्हणजे तुमची JavaScript फाईल ज्या फोल्डरमध्ये सेव्ह केलेली आहे तो folder.
  • जर तुम्ही एका फोल्डरमधून दुसऱ्या फोल्डरमधील फाईल रन केली, तर हे दोन्ही paths वेगवेगळे असू शकतात.
  • उदाहरण: D:\project मध्ये terminal उघडून node src/app.js चालवले, तर cwd() = D:\project आणि __dirname = D:\project\src.
  • Rule: file शेजारच्या files वाचताना नेहमी path.join(__dirname, 'data.json') वापरा. cwd() command कुठून चालवली त्यानुसार बदलतो.
  • process.cwd() dynamic आहे (बदलतो). __dirname fixed आहे (file जिथे आहे तिथेच). ESM मध्ये __dirname नसतो.

Q. Memory & Uptime

  • process.uptime(): Returns the number of seconds the process has been running (e.g. 12.45 seconds). 
    console.log(process.uptime());
  • process.memoryUsage(): Returns an object detailing memory use. 
    console.log(process.memoryUsage());
    • rss (Resident Set Size): Total memory occupied in RAM.
    • heapTotal: Total memory allocated for V8 heap.
    • heapUsed: Amount of V8 heap memory actually being used.
    • external: Memory used by resources outside V8 (like Buffers).
मराठी सारांश:
  • process.uptime() मुळे program किती सेकंद चालू आहे हे समजते. उदा. health-check API मध्ये "server किती वेळ चालू आहे" दाखवायला.
  • process.memoryUsage() मुळे program ने किती RAM (Memory) वापरली आहे याची सविस्तर माहिती मिळते (bytes मध्ये).
  • rss: process ने RAM मध्ये घेतलेली एकूण जागा.
  • heapTotal: V8 ला दिलेली heap memory. heapUsed: त्यातली प्रत्यक्ष वापरलेली.
  • external: V8 बाहेरची memory (उदा. Buffers).
  • उपयोग: heapUsed सतत वाढत असेल तर memory leak असू शकतो.
  • MB मध्ये: (process.memoryUsage().heapUsed / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(2) + ' MB'

Q. Performance Measurement Tools

  • Date.now(): Returns Unix timestamp in milliseconds. (Wall-clock time, can be affected by system clock adjustments). 
    const start = Date.now(); // e.g. 1698765432100
  • performance.now(): High-resolution elapsed-time measurement in milliseconds. (Monotonic time source, highly accurate for performance testing). Requires const { performance } = require('node:perf_hooks'); 
    const t0 = performance.now(); // e.g. 1234.5678
  • process.hrtime(): Provides high-resolution real-time measurement returning an array [seconds, nanoseconds]. 
    const time = process.hrtime(); // e.g. [ 1800216, 25 ]
  • process.hrtime.bigint(): High-resolution measurement in nanoseconds returning a BigInt. Most precise for elapsed-time measurement. 
    const startBig = process.hrtime.bigint(); // e.g. 3584856425988n
  • console.time("label"): Simple way to measure execution time. (Start with console.time and end with console.timeEnd). 
    console.time("loop");
// ...some code...
console.timeEnd("loop"); // prints -> loop: 10.123ms
मराठी सारांश:
  • Date.now() वेळ मोजण्यासाठी ठीक आहे, पण ते 100% अचूक (accurate) नसते.
  • Code रन व्हायला किती वेळ लागला हे अचूक मोजण्यासाठी performance.now() किंवा process.hrtime() वापरणे जास्त योग्य असते, कारण ते High-resolution वेळ देतात.
  • console.time() हा कोडला लागलेला वेळ मोजण्याचा सर्वात सोपा आणि सरळ मार्ग आहे.
  • Date.now(): milliseconds मध्ये सध्याची वेळ. System clock बदलल्यास गडबड होऊ शकते.
  • performance.now(): high-resolution, monotonic (मागे जात नाही). Benchmark साठी उत्तम.
  • process.hrtime.bigint(): nanoseconds मध्ये, सर्वात अचूक.
  • Code: const t = performance.now(); work(); console.log(performance.now() - t, "ms").
  • उदाहरण: भिंतीवरचे घड्याळ (Date.now) vs शर्यतीचे stopwatch (performance.now).

Q. Process Control (exit, exitCode, kill)

  • process.exit(code): Immediately terminates the Node.js process. Code 0 means success, non-zero (like 1) indicates failure/error. Code after process.exit() will never run. 
    process.exit(1); // Exits with an error
  • process.exitCode: Specifies the exit code that Node will use when the process exits naturally. Best practice is to set process.exitCode = 1; instead of forcing an abrupt process.exit(1) if you want to let pending operations finish first.
  • process.kill(pid, signal): Sends a signal to a process. It does NOT necessarily mean "force kill". It just sends a signal (like SIGTERM) to the given process ID. 
    process.kill(process.pid, 'SIGTERM');
  • process.abort(): Immediately terminates the process and generates a core dump (useful for low-level debugging).
मराठी सारांश:
  • process.exit() ने program तात्काळ बंद होतो आणि त्यापुढील कोणताही कोड चालत नाही.
  • जर तुम्हाला program ची उरलेली कामे संपवून मगच error code देऊन बंद करायचा असेल, तर process.exitCode = 1 वापरणे जास्त सुरक्षित असते.
  • process.kill() ने एखाद्या process ला signal पाठवता येतो (उदा. SIGTERM), तर process.abort() मुळे process लगेच crash होते.
  • Exit codes: 0 = यश, 1 (किंवा non-zero) = error. CI, Docker, PM2 हे code वाचतात.
  • process.kill(pid, signal): नावात "kill" असले तरी फक्त signal पाठवतो. Default SIGTERM.
  • process.abort(): लगेच crash करतो आणि core dump बनवतो (low-level debugging साठी).
  • उदाहरण: exit() = वीज लगेच बंद. exitCode = सगळी कामे संपवून मग दिवा बंद.

Q. process.exit() in Node.js (In Detail)

Concept:
  • process.exit() immediately terminates the running Node.js process.
  • It stops the event loop, even if timers, I/O or server listeners are still pending.
  • Simple example: pulling the power plug. Everything stops instantly, and unsaved work is lost.
  • Syntax: process.exit([code])
  • code (optional): The exit code (number).
    • 0 → success (the default).
    • 1 (or any non-zero value) → failure. Tools like CI and Docker read this code.
  • The 'exit' event fires just before termination (only sync code can run in it).
  • ⚠️ Pending async work (DB writes, file writes, log flushes) may be lost.
  • ✅ Prefer process.exitCode = 1 and let the event loop finish naturally, or close resources first and then exit.
Code Example: Graceful Shutdown Before Exit
const http = require('http');
const server = http.createServer((req, res) => res.end('OK')).listen(3000);

process.on('SIGINT', () => {          // Ctrl + C pressed
    console.log('Shutting down...');
    server.close(() => {              // stop accepting new requests, finish current ones
        console.log('Server closed');
        process.exit(0);              // now exit safely
    });
});

process.on('exit', (code) => console.log(`Exited with code ${code}`));
मराठी सारांश (process.exit):
  • process.exit() मुळे Node.js process लगेच बंद होतो. Event loop मधली pending कामेही थांबतात.
  • उदाहरण: थेट वीज बंद करणे. Save न केलेले काम जाते.
  • Code: 0 = यशस्वी, 1 = error.
  • चांगली पद्धत: आधी server / DB connection बंद करा (graceful shutdown), मग exit करा. किंवा process.exitCode वापरा.

Q. Process Events & Signals

  • exit: Emitted when the Node.js process is about to exit. Only synchronous code can be run here. Async code won't keep the process alive. 
    process.on('exit', (code) => { console.log(`Exiting with code: ${code}`); });
  • beforeExit: Emitted when Node.js has no more work scheduled on the event loop. You CAN schedule more asynchronous work here to keep the process alive. 
    process.on('beforeExit', () => { console.log('Event loop is empty.'); });
  • uncaughtException: Emitted when an exception is not caught and reaches the event loop. In production, log the error and do a graceful restart. Do not try to blindly keep the corrupted app running! 
    process.on('uncaughtException', (err) => { console.error('Uncaught Error:', err); process.exit(1); });
  • unhandledRejection: Emitted when a Promise is rejected and there is no .catch() handler. 
    process.on('unhandledRejection', (reason) => { console.error('Unhandled Promise Rejection:', reason); });
  • SIGINT: Signal generated when the user presses Ctrl + C in the terminal. 
    process.on('SIGINT', () => { console.log('Ctrl+C pressed!'); process.exit(0); });
  • SIGTERM: Termination signal sent by OS or Docker to request a graceful shutdown. 
    process.on('SIGTERM', () => { console.log('SIGTERM received. Shutting down...'); });
मराठी सारांश:
  • exit आणि beforeExit मुळे प्रोग्राम पूर्णपणे बंद होण्याआधी काही शेवटची कामे (उदा. logs सेव्ह करणे) करता येतात.
  • uncaughtException आणि unhandledRejection मुळे अनपेक्षित errors पकडता येतात जेणेकरून application लगेच क्रॅश होऊ नये.
  • SIGINT (Ctrl+C) आणि SIGTERM (OS termination) या signals चा वापर सर्व्हर Gracefully बंद करण्यासाठी होतो.
  • beforeExit: event loop रिकामा झाल्यावर. इथे async काम करता येते. exit: आता बंद होणारच, फक्त sync code.
  • uncaughtException: न पकडलेला error. Log करून process बंद करा (state खराब असू शकते), PM2 restart करेल.
  • unhandledRejection: .catch() नसलेले rejected Promise. Node 15+ मध्ये default ने crash होतो.
  • Docker / Kubernetes container थांबवताना SIGTERM पाठवतात. तो ऐकून server, DB बंद करा.
  • उदाहरण: दुकान बंद करण्याआधीची घंटा (signal). ती ऐकून हिशोब पूर्ण करून मग शटर.

Q. What is Graceful Shutdown?

Definition:
  • Graceful shutdown means stopping the app politely, not suddenly.
  • When the process receives a termination signal (SIGTERM from Docker, Kubernetes or PM2, or SIGINT from Ctrl + C), it first closes its resources properly (active HTTP requests, DB connections, open files) and only then exits.
  • Why? To avoid half-finished requests, corrupted data and leaked connections.
  • Simple example: a shop closing at night. It stops letting new customers in, serves the ones already inside, locks the cash box, and only then shuts the shutter.
Step-by-Step Flow:
  1. Receive the termination signal (SIGTERM).
  2. Stop accepting new incoming HTTP requests (e.g., server.close()).
  3. Wait for currently active requests to finish processing.
  4. Close the Database connections cleanly.
  5. Close any open file resources or streams.
  6. Finally, call process.exit(0) to safely terminate the process.
Example Implementation: 
process.on('SIGTERM', () => {
  console.log('SIGTERM received. Shutting down gracefully...');
  
  // 1. Stop accepting new requests
  server.close(() => {
    console.log('HTTP server closed.');
    
    // 2. Close DB connections
    mongoose.connection.close(false, () => {
      console.log('MongoDB connection closed.');
      
      // 3. Exit safely
      process.exit(0);
    });
  });
});
मराठी सारांश:
  • Graceful Shutdown म्हणजे अचानक program बंद न करता, सर्व database connections आणि सुरु असलेल्या requests नीट बंद करून मगच process थांबवणे.
  • यासाठी SIGTERM (Docker / PM2 कडून) आणि SIGINT (Ctrl + C) signals ऐकतात.
  • Steps: 1) signal मिळाला → 2) नवीन requests घेणे बंद (server.close()) → 3) चालू requests पूर्ण होऊ द्या → 4) DB connection बंद → 5) files / streams बंद → 6) process.exit(0).
  • का? अर्धवट requests, खराब data आणि उघडी राहिलेली connections टाळण्यासाठी.
  • उदाहरण: रात्री दुकान बंद करताना आधी नवीन ग्राहक थांबवतो, आतल्यांना serve करतो, गल्ला बंद करतो, मग शटर ओढतो.

Q. Process Scheduling & IPC

  • process.nextTick(): Schedules a callback to run immediately after the current operation completes, but before the event loop continues to the next phase (e.g. before timers or I/O callbacks). Overusing it recursively can block the event loop. 
    process.nextTick(() => console.log('Runs before setTimeout'));
  • process.send(): Used for Inter-Process Communication (IPC). Allows a Node.js child process to send messages to its parent process. 
    process.send({ hello: 'parent' });
  • process.connected: Indicates whether the current child process still has an active IPC connection to its parent.
मराठी सारांश:
  • process.nextTick() callback ला सर्वात जास्त Priority असते, ते इतर सर्व asynchronous कामांच्या (उदा. setTimeout) आधी रन होते. याचा जास्त वापर केल्यास Event Loop ब्लॉक होऊ शकतो.
  • process.send() चा वापर Parent आणि Child process मध्ये messages पाठवण्यासाठी (Inter-Process Communication) होतो.
  • IPC म्हणजे काय? Inter-Process Communication. दोन वेगळ्या processes एकमेकांशी बोलतात. उदा. child_process.fork() ने बनवलेला child.
  • process.send() फक्त child process मध्ये असते (IPC channel असेल तरच). Parent ला child.on('message') ने message मिळतो.
  • process.connected: child चे parent शी connection अजून चालू आहे का (true / false).
  • उदाहरण: manager (parent) आणि कर्मचारी (child) walkie-talkie वर बोलतात.
Code Example: Parent ⇄ Child IPC
// parent.js
const { fork } = require('child_process');
const child = fork('child.js');                  // creates IPC channel
child.on('message', (msg) => console.log('Parent got:', msg));
child.send({ task: 'sum', nums: [1, 2, 3] });

// child.js
process.on('message', ({ nums }) => {
    const total = nums.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
    process.send({ result: total });              // → Parent got: { result: 6 }
    process.disconnect();
});
Figure 11: The process Object - Complete Interview Map

Q. How to Take Input from the Terminal? (process.stdin & readline)

Concept:
  • Node.js has no prompt() like the browser. Terminal input arrives through process.stdin, a Readable stream of what the user types.
  • Output goes to process.stdout (a Writable stream), which is what console.log uses.
  • The built-in readline module sits on top of stdin and gives an easy way to ask questions and read input line by line.
  • Used for: CLI tools, interactive scripts (setup wizards, quizzes), and reading huge files line by line without loading them fully into memory.
  • Simple example: a bank kiosk asking "Enter your name:" and waiting for you to type and press Enter.
Code Example 1: Ask Questions (readline/promises, Modern)
// ask.js  →  run: node ask.js
const readline = require('readline/promises');
const { stdin: input, stdout: output } = require('process');

async function main() {
    const rl = readline.createInterface({ input, output });

    const name = await rl.question('What is your name? ');
    const age  = Number(await rl.question('Your age? '));       // input is always a string

    console.log(`Hello ${name}, next year you will be ${age + 1}.`);
    rl.close();                                                  // ⚠️ must close, or the program won't exit
}
main();
Code Example 2: Callback Style and Line-by-Line Events
const readline = require('readline');
const rl = readline.createInterface({ input: process.stdin, output: process.stdout });

rl.question('Enter a number: ', (answer) => {
    console.log('Square =', Number(answer) ** 2);
    rl.close();
});

// Mini calculator loop: read every line until the user types "exit"
// rl.setPrompt('> '); rl.prompt();
// rl.on('line', (line) => { if (line === 'exit') return rl.close(); console.log(eval(line)); rl.prompt(); });
// rl.on('close', () => console.log('Bye!'));
Code Example 3: Read a Huge File Line by Line (Memory-Safe)
const fs = require('fs');
const readline = require('readline');

async function countErrors(file) {
    const rl = readline.createInterface({
        input: fs.createReadStream(file),   // stream, not the whole file
        crlfDelay: Infinity                 // treat \r\n (Windows) as one line break
    });
    let errors = 0;
    for await (const line of rl) {          // one line at a time
        if (line.includes('ERROR')) errors++;
    }
    console.log('Error lines:', errors);
}
countErrors('server-10GB.log');
  • Syntax: readline.createInterface({ input, output, prompt, crlfDelay })
  • input: A Readable stream (process.stdin or a file stream).
  • output: A Writable stream for prompts (process.stdout); omit when reading files.
  • rl.question(query[, callback]): Shows query and waits for one line. The promise version returns the answer.
  • 'line' event: Fires for every line entered/read. 'close' event: Fires when input ends or rl.close() is called.
  • for await...of rl: Iterates lines asynchronously (best for files).
  • rl.close(): Ends the interface. Without it, stdin stays open and the process doesn't exit.
  • Raw stdin: process.stdin.on('data', chunk => ...) also works but gives raw chunks (Buffers), not neat lines.
मराठी सारांश (Terminal Input: stdin आणि readline):
  • Node.js मध्ये browser सारखे prompt() नाही. Terminal मधून टाइप केलेला data process.stdin (Readable stream) मधून येतो. Output process.stdout ला जातो.
  • readline module: प्रश्न विचारणे आणि line-by-line input वाचणे सोपे करतो.
  • Modern: require('readline/promises') → await rl.question('नाव? '). शेवटी rl.close() नक्की करा, नाहीतर program बंद होत नाही.
  • Input नेहमी string असतो. Number हवा असेल तर Number().
  • Events: 'line' (प्रत्येक line ला), 'close' (संपल्यावर).
  • मोठी file: fs.createReadStream + readline + for await ने 10GB file पण एक-एक line वाचता येते, memory न भरता.
  • उपयोग: CLI tools, interactive scripts, log files analyse करणे.
  • उदाहरण: बँकेचा kiosk. "तुमचे नाव टाका:" विचारतो आणि Enter दाबेपर्यंत थांबतो.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What is the difference between `process.cwd()` and `__dirname`?
  • process.cwd() returns the directory from which the Node.js command was executed in the terminal.
  • __dirname returns the absolute path of the directory containing the current executing JavaScript file.
Q2. What is the difference between `process.argv` and `process.execArgv`?
  • process.argv contains the application arguments (including the Node and script paths).
  • process.execArgv contains the Node.js runtime arguments (flags like --inspect or --experimental-modules) passed directly to the engine.
Q3. What is the difference between `process.exit()` and `process.exitCode`?
  • process.exit() forces the program to terminate immediately, bypassing pending async operations.
  • process.exitCode = 1 tells Node.js to exit with an error code naturally only after it has finished all pending work in the event loop.
Q4. What is the difference between `exit` and `beforeExit` events?
  • beforeExit is fired when the event loop is empty. You can perform async operations here, which will keep the process alive longer.
  • exit is fired when the process is definitely shutting down. Only synchronous code can be executed here.
Q5. Process Object vs Module Object?
  • process is global and provides info/control over the entire running Node.js instance.
  • module is local to each CommonJS file and handles metadata and exports for that specific file.
मराठी सारांश:
  • process object वरील interview मध्ये cwd() vs __dirname, आणि Graceful shutdown (SIGTERM) चे प्रश्न सर्वात महत्त्वाचे असतात.
  • Q1 (cwd vs __dirname): cwd() = terminal मध्ये command जिथून चालवली तो folder. __dirname = file जिथे आहे तो folder.
  • Q2 (argv vs execArgv): argv = app चे arguments. execArgv = Node चे flags (--inspect).
  • Q3 (exit() vs exitCode): exit() = लगेच बंद. exitCode = 1 = काम संपल्यावर नैसर्गिकरीत्या बंद, फक्त error code सह.
  • Q4 (exit vs beforeExit): beforeExit = event loop रिकामा झाल्यावर, async काम करता येते. exit = आता बंद होणारच, फक्त sync code.
  • Q5 (process vs module): process = global, संपूर्ण Node instance साठी. module = प्रत्येक file साठी local.
  • तसेच uncaughtException आल्यास process लगेच restart का करावी हेही विचारले जाते. कारण app ची state खराब (corrupt) झालेली असू शकते.

Quick Revision Summary

Property / Method Description
process.cwd() Current Working Directory (where the node command was executed).
process.env Object containing environment variables.
process.argv Array of command-line arguments.
process.exit(0) Immediately forces the process to terminate.
process.nextTick() Schedules a callback to fire before the next event loop phase.
performance.now() High-resolution time measurement for precise performance checks.

SECTION 2: CORE MODULES

Built-in modules provided by Node.js (e.g., path, os, fs, buffer, events).

Chapter 7: The Path Module

Q. What are Core Modules (Built-in Modules) in Node.js?

Definition:
  • Core modules are modules built directly into the Node.js runtime.
  • They provide the basic features needed to work with the system: files, paths, OS, network, events.
  • No third-party installation (npm install) is needed. They ship with Node.js.
  • Best practice: import them with the node: prefix (require('node:fs')) so it's clear they are built-in.
  • Node.js provides several modules as part of the runtime itself.
  • These modules are already included in Node.js, so there is no need to install them via npm.
  • Examples of core modules: path, os, fs, http, events, crypto, stream.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Core modules म्हणजे असे modules जे Node.js मध्ये आधीपासूनच दिलेले असतात.
  • यासाठी npm install करण्याची गरज नसते.
  • उदाहरणे: path (paths), fs (files), os (system माहिती), http (server), events, crypto (security), stream.
  • Import: require('node:path'). node: prefix मुळे npm वरील same नावाच्या package शी गोंधळ होत नाही.
  • उदाहरण: नवीन मोबाईल मध्ये आधीपासून असलेले apps (Camera, Calculator). ते Play Store वरून घ्यावे लागत नाहीत.

Q. What is the path Module?

  • The path module provides utilities for working with file and directory paths.
  • It helps us to create, analyze, transform, and manipulate file system paths.
  • It provides utilities such as join(), resolve(), parse(), basename(), dirname(), extname(), normalize(), and relative().
  • It is a built-in module, so we just import it like this:
    const path = require('node:path'); (CommonJS)
    import path from 'node:path'; (ESM)
मराठी सारांश:
  • path module चा वापर फाईलचे आणि फोल्डरचे paths (पत्ता) योग्य प्रकारे हाताळण्यासाठी होतो.
  • यामुळे आपण path जोडणे (join), तोडणे (parse), आणि त्यातील file चे नाव (basename), folder (dirname), extension (extname) शोधणे अशी कामे करू शकतो.
  • हा core module आहे: const path = require('node:path').
  • महत्वाचे: path module फक्त string वर काम करतो. File खरंच आहे का ते तपासत नाही (त्यासाठी fs).
  • उदाहरण: पोस्टाचा पत्ता नीट लिहिणारा clerk. घर आहे की नाही हे तो बघत नाही, फक्त पत्ता बरोबर लिहितो.

Q. Why do you need the path module?

  • Different Operating Systems use different path separators.
  • Windows: Uses backslashes \ (e.g., C:\Users\Vishal\project\app.js).
  • Linux/macOS: Uses forward slashes / (e.g., /Users/Vishal/project/app.js).
  • If we create paths manually using simple string concatenation, our code becomes OS-dependent and might break on another machine.
  • The path module automatically handles platform-specific path differences, making path-related code perfectly portable across Windows, Linux, and macOS.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Windows मध्ये path साठी \ वापरतात तर Linux/Mac मध्ये / वापरतात.
  • आपण जर स्वतः string जोडून path बनवला (dir + '/' + file) तर तो दुसऱ्या OS वर चालणार नाही.
  • path module ही समस्या सोडवते. तो OS ओळखून आपोआप योग्य separator वापरतो.
  • तसेच //, .., . सारख्या चुका साफ करतो.
  • फायदा: एकच code Windows, Linux, macOS सगळीकडे चालतो (portable).
Code Example: Manual String vs path.join
const path = require('node:path');

// ❌ Manual: breaks or looks wrong on other OS
const bad = __dirname + '/data/' + 'users.json';

// ✅ path.join: correct separator on every OS
const good = path.join(__dirname, 'data', 'users.json');
// Windows → D:\app\data\users.json     Linux → /app/data/users.json

Q. CommonJS Module Variables: __dirname vs __filename

  • These are CommonJS module-specific variables, injected by the Node.js wrapper function.
  • __filename: Represents the absolute path of the current CommonJS module file. It contains the drive, directory name, file name, and file extension.
  • __dirname: Represents the absolute path of the directory containing the current CommonJS module. It contains the drive and directory name (without the file name).
  • Note: __dirname and __filename are available directly in CommonJS, but they are NOT provided in ES Modules (ESM). In ESM, you must derive them using import.meta.url.
मराठी सारांश:
  • __filename म्हणजे सध्याच्या फाईलचा पूर्ण पत्ता (फाईलच्या नावासकट). उदा. C:\project\app.js.
  • __dirname म्हणजे ती फाईल ज्या फोल्डरमध्ये आहे त्या फोल्डरचा पत्ता. उदा. C:\project.
  • हे Node.js च्या wrapper function मधून मिळतात (global नाहीत).
  • हे फक्त CommonJS मध्ये चालतात. ESM मध्ये import.meta.url वरून काढावे लागतात.
  • उपयोग: file कुठूनही चालवली तरी बरोबर path मिळावा म्हणून: path.join(__dirname, 'data.json').
  • उदाहरण: __filename = पूर्ण पत्ता (घर नंबरसह). __dirname = फक्त गल्लीचा पत्ता.
Figure 12: Visualizing __dirname vs __filename

Q. Important Methods of the path Module

  • 1. path.parse(): Breaks a path into an object with properties: root, dir, base, ext, name.
  • 2. path.format(): Does the exact reverse of path.parse(). Takes an object and constructs a path string.
  • 3. path.join(): Joins multiple segments into a single path. It handles separators correctly for the current platform (unlike simple string concatenation).
  • 4. path.resolve(): Resolves a sequence of path segments into an absolute path (always returns a full absolute path from root).
  • 5. path.extname(): Returns the extension of the file path (e.g., .txt, .js). Returns empty string if no extension exists.
  • 6. path.basename(): Returns the last portion of a path (usually the file name). You can pass a second argument to remove the extension (e.g., path.basename('abc/ex.pdf', '.pdf') returns ex).
  • 7. path.dirname(): Returns the directory portion of the path (similar to __dirname).
  • 8. path.sep: Returns the platform-specific path separator (\ on Windows, / on POSIX).
  • 9. path.delimiter: Returns the platform-specific delimiter used to separate multiple paths in a list (like in the PATH environment variable). ; on Windows, : on POSIX.
  • 10. path.isAbsolute(): Checks whether a path is absolute or relative. Returns true or false.
  • 11. path.relative(from, to): Returns the relative path from one location to another.
  • 12. path.normalize(): Resolves unnecessary . (current dir), .. (parent dir), and repeated separators where appropriate.
  • 13. path.toNamespacedPath(): Converts a path to a Windows-specific namespace path when applicable. Mainly useful for Windows path handling compatibility.
Code Example: Using path methods
const path = require('node:path');

console.log(path.extname('vishal.txt')); 
// → '.txt'

console.log(path.basename('/users/hi/abc.txt', '.txt')); 
// → 'abc'

console.log(path.join('users', 'hi', 'abc.txt')); 
// → 'users\hi\abc.txt' (on Windows)

console.log(path.resolve('abc', 'vishal.js')); 
// → 'D:\IMP\FULL-NODE-JS\1-Basic\inbultModels\abc\vishal.js' (Absolute Path)

console.log(path.normalize('users//hi/../data')); 
// → 'users\data' (Removes extra slashes and resolves '..')
✔ `path.sep` separates parts of ONE path. `path.delimiter` separates MULTIPLE paths in a list.
✔ मराठी: `path.sep` एका path मधल्या फोल्डर्सना वेगळे करतो (उदा. `\`). तर `path.delimiter` एकापेक्षा जास्त पूर्ण paths ना एकमेकांपासून वेगळे करतो (उदा. `;`).
Figure 13: Core Path Module Operations Map
मराठी सारांश:
  • parse(): path ला object मध्ये तोडतो (root, dir, base, ext, name). format(): उलट, object वरून path बनवतो.
  • join(): तुकडे जोडतो, OS प्रमाणे separator वापरतो.
  • resolve(): नेहमी पूर्ण (absolute) path देतो. गरज पडल्यास cwd पुढे जोडतो.
  • basename(): file चे नाव (दुसरा argument दिल्यास extension शिवाय). dirname(): folder. extname(): extension (.txt).
  • normalize(): //, .., . साफ करतो.
  • isAbsolute(): path पूर्ण आहे का (true / false). relative(from, to): एकापासून दुसऱ्यापर्यंतचा relative path.
  • sep: एका path मधला separator (\ किंवा /). delimiter: अनेक paths च्या यादीतला separator (; किंवा :).

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What is the difference between `path.join()` and `path.resolve()`?
  • path.join() simply concatenates multiple path segments together, taking the OS separator into account, and normalizes the resulting path.
  • path.resolve() resolves a sequence of paths or path segments into an absolute path, working from right to left until an absolute path is constructed. If no absolute path is formed, it prepends the current working directory (cwd).
Q2. Why should we use `path.join()` instead of string concatenation (e.g. `dir + '/' + file`)?
  • String concatenation hardcodes the separator (like /). This breaks on Windows, which uses \.
  • path.join() automatically uses the correct separator for the operating system and normalizes extra slashes (e.g., // becomes /).
Q3. What does `path.normalize()` do?
  • It resolves unnecessary . (current directory) and .. (parent directory) segments, and removes duplicate slashes, resulting in a clean, standard path.
Q4. Are core modules installed via npm?
  • No. Core modules like path, fs, and http are bundled with the Node.js runtime environment. You don't need to run npm install for them.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Q1 (join vs resolve): join फक्त तुकडे जोडतो (relative राहू शकतो). resolve उजवीकडून डावीकडे काम करतो आणि नेहमी absolute path देतो. उदा. join('a','b') → a\b, resolve('a','b') → D:\project\a\b.
  • Q2 (string concatenation का नको?): separator hardcode होतो, Windows वर तुटतो. join OS प्रमाणे बदलतो आणि // साफ करतो.
  • Q3 (normalize): ., .. आणि जास्तीचे slashes काढून स्वच्छ path देतो.
  • Q4 (core modules npm ने install करतात का?): नाही. ते Node.js सोबतच येतात.

Quick Revision Summary

Method / Property Description
path.join() Safely joins multiple paths using OS-specific separators.
path.resolve() Creates a full absolute path from the given segments.
path.basename() Extracts the file name from a path.
path.extname() Extracts the file extension (e.g., .txt).
__dirname Absolute path to the current module's directory (CommonJS only).
path.parse() Converts a string path into an object with root, dir, base, ext, name.

Chapter 8: The OS Module

Q. What is the OS module in Node.js?

Definition:
  • The os module is a built-in (core) Node.js module that provides a set of operating system-related utility methods and properties.
  • It allows a Node.js application to directly interact with and query the underlying OS on which it is running — such as Windows, Linux, or macOS.
  • It is primarily used for system monitoring, diagnostics, and writing OS-aware application logic (e.g., using os.platform() to change behavior on Windows vs Linux).
  • Recommended Import: const os = require("node:os"); — using the node: prefix explicitly tells Node.js to load a built-in module, preventing accidental collision with a third-party npm package of the same name.
  • It allows us to get information about: CPU, Memory (RAM), Hostname, Network Interfaces, User Information, System Uptime, Temp Directory, and OS Version.
  • Since it is a core module, no npm installation is required. It ships with every Node.js installation.
मराठी सारांश:
  • os (Operating System) module चा वापर करून आपण ज्या कम्प्युटरवर Node.js app चालू आहे, त्या कम्प्युटरची संपूर्ण माहिती काढू शकतो (RAM, CPU, OS Version इ.).
  • हे Node.js मध्ये आधीपासूनच बिल्ट-इन असते, त्यासाठी npm install ची आवश्यकता नसते.
  • हे खूपसे system monitoring tools, DevOps scripts, आणि cross-platform apps मध्ये वापरले जाते.
  • Import: const os = require('node:os'). node: prefix मुळे built-in module असल्याचे स्पष्ट होते.
  • माहिती मिळते: CPU (os.cpus()), RAM (totalmem, freemem), OS (platform, type, release), hostname, user, network, uptime, temp folder.
  • उपयोग: health-check API, cluster साठी cores मोजणे, OS प्रमाणे वेगळा code.
  • उदाहरण: गाडीचा dashboard. Speed, fuel, temperature सगळे एकाच ठिकाणी दिसते.

Q. Important OS Module Methods (Memory & CPU)

  • os.totalmem():
    • Returns the total amount of system memory (RAM) in bytes.
    • To convert to GB: (os.totalmem() / 1024 / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(2)
  • os.freemem():
    • Returns the currently available (free/unused) system memory in bytes.
    • Useful for detecting memory pressure in a running application.
  • os.cpus():
    • Returns an array of objects. Each object represents one logical CPU core and contains: model, speed (MHz), and times (user/sys/idle ticks).
    • To get the count of CPU cores: os.cpus().length
    • This is critical for the cluster module where you spawn one worker per CPU core.
  • os.arch(): Returns the CPU architecture string (e.g., 'x64', 'arm64', 'ia32').
  • os.endianness(): Returns CPU byte order — 'LE' (Little Endian, most modern CPUs) or 'BE' (Big Endian).
  • Code Example: 
    const os = require('node:os');

console.log('Total RAM:', (os.totalmem() / 1024 / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(2) + ' GB');
console.log('Free RAM: ', (os.freemem() / 1024 / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(2) + ' GB');
console.log('CPU Cores:', os.cpus().length);
console.log('CPU Model:', os.cpus()[0].model);
console.log('CPU Arch: ', os.arch());
मराठी सारांश (Memory & CPU):
  • os.totalmem() आणि os.freemem() कम्प्युटरची एकूण आणि शिल्लक RAM bytes मध्ये देतात. GB मध्ये बघायला 1024 ने तीनदा भागावे लागते.
  • os.cpus() एक array देतो जिथे प्रत्येक CPU core ची माहिती (model, speed) असते. .length वापरून total cores मिळतात.
  • Cluster module मध्ये प्रत्येक CPU core साठी एक worker process तयार करण्यासाठी os.cpus().length वापरतात.
  • GB मध्ये: (os.totalmem() / 1024 / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(2). bytes → KB → MB → GB.
  • os.cpus()[0].model: CPU चे नाव. speed: MHz मध्ये.
  • os.arch(): x64, arm64. os.endianness(): LE / BE.
  • freemem कमी होत असेल तर memory pressure. Monitoring मध्ये alert लावता येतो.

Q. OS Identity & User Info

  • os.platform(): Returns a short OS platform identifier string. Values: 'win32' (Windows), 'linux' (Linux), 'darwin' (macOS).
  • os.type(): Returns the full OS name as reported by the kernel. Values: 'Windows_NT', 'Linux', 'Darwin'.
  • os.release(): Returns the OS kernel release version string (e.g., '10.0.19045' on Windows 10).
  • os.version(): Returns a more human-readable, detailed OS version string (e.g., 'Windows 10 Enterprise').
  • os.hostname(): Returns the hostname (computer name) of the machine as it appears on the local network.
  • os.userInfo():
    • Returns an object with details about the currently logged-in user.
    • The object contains: username, uid (user ID), gid (group ID), shell (default shell path), homedir (home directory path).
    • Optional argument: os.userInfo({ encoding: 'utf8' }) — specifies the character encoding for output strings.
  • Code Example: 
    const os = require('node:os');

console.log('Platform:  ', os.platform());  // 'win32'
console.log('OS Type:   ', os.type());      // 'Windows_NT'
console.log('OS Release:', os.release());   // '10.0.19045'
console.log('OS Version:', os.version());   // 'Windows 10 Enterprise'
console.log('Hostname:  ', os.hostname());  // 'DESKTOP-ABC123'
console.log('User Info: ', os.userInfo());  
// { username: 'Vishal', homedir: 'C:\\Users\\Vishal', shell: null, ... }
मराठी सारांश (OS Identity):
  • os.platform() short code देतो जसे 'win32' किंवा 'linux'. हा cross-platform code लिहिताना if/else मध्ये वापरतात.
  • os.type() पूर्ण OS नाव देतो जसे 'Windows_NT' किंवा 'Linux'.
  • os.userInfo() सध्या लॉगिन असलेल्या User चे username, home directory, आणि shell इ. देतो.
  • os.hostname() नेटवर्कवर कम्प्युटरचे नाव सांगतो.

Q. Directories, Network & Process Priority

  • os.homedir(): Returns the path of the home directory of the current user. (e.g., C:\Users\Vishal on Windows, /home/vishal on Linux). Useful for creating config files in the user's home folder.
  • os.tmpdir(): Returns the OS's default temporary files directory. (e.g., C:\Users\Vishal\AppData\Local\Temp on Windows, /tmp on Linux). Files here may be cleared on reboot.
  • os.uptime(): Returns the number of seconds the system has been running since the last boot. To convert to hours: (os.uptime() / 3600).toFixed(2).
  • os.networkInterfaces():
    • Returns an object describing all network interfaces on the machine.
    • Each interface entry contains: address (IP), netmask, family (IPv4 or IPv6), mac (MAC address), internal (true if loopback like 127.0.0.1).
    • Commonly used to find the machine's local IP address programmatically.
  • os.loadavg(): Returns an array of [1-minute, 5-minute, 15-minute] load averages. Always returns [0, 0, 0] on Windows as load average is a Unix/Linux-only concept.
  • os.setPriority(pid, priority) / os.getPriority(pid):
    • Sets or gets the OS scheduling priority of a process.
    • pid: The Process ID. If 0 is passed, it applies to the current process.
    • priority: A number from -20 (highest priority) to 19 (lowest priority).
  • Code Example: Full System Monitor 
    const os = require('node:os');

const systemInfo = {
  platform:      os.platform(),
  architecture:  os.arch(),
  cpuCores:      os.cpus().length,
  totalMemoryGB: (os.totalmem() / 1024 / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(2) + ' GB',
  freeMemoryGB:  (os.freemem()  / 1024 / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(2) + ' GB',
  hostname:      os.hostname(),
  homeDir:       os.homedir(),
  tempDir:       os.tmpdir(),
  uptimeHours:   (os.uptime() / 3600).toFixed(2) + ' hours',
};

console.log(systemInfo);

// Get machine's local IPv4 address
const interfaces = os.networkInterfaces();
for (const name of Object.keys(interfaces)) {
  for (const iface of interfaces[name]) {
    if (iface.family === 'IPv4' && !iface.internal) {
      console.log('Local IP:', iface.address); // e.g. 192.168.1.5
    }
  }
}
मराठी सारांश (Directories, Network & Priority):
  • os.homedir() युझरच्या home folder चा path देतो (उदा. C:\Users\Vishal), जे config files save करण्यासाठी उपयोगी आहे.
  • os.tmpdir() temporary files साठी असलेल्या OS folder चा path देतो.
  • os.uptime() कम्प्युटर शेवटच्या boot पासून किती seconds चालू आहे ते सांगते. Hours मध्ये बघायला 3600 ने भागावे.
  • os.networkInterfaces() मुळे कम्प्युटरचा local IP Address, MAC address आणि network माहिती मिळते.
  • os.loadavg() Linux वर system load दाखवतो, पण Windows वर नेहमी [0,0,0] देतो.
Figure 14: OS Module Function Categories

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What is the difference between `os.platform()` and `os.type()`?
  • os.platform() returns the platform identifier (e.g., win32, linux).
  • os.type() returns the operating system's exact name (e.g., Windows_NT, Linux).
Q2. How do you find the total amount of RAM in GB?
  • os.totalmem() returns the RAM in bytes. To get GB, divide it by 1024 three times: os.totalmem() / 1024 / 1024 / 1024.
Q3. How do you get the number of CPU cores in Node.js?
  • By using the length of the array returned by os.cpus(). Example: os.cpus().length.
Q4. What does `os.loadavg()` return on Windows?
  • It returns an array of zeros: [0, 0, 0] because load average is a Unix-specific concept and is not supported natively in Windows.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Q1 (platform vs type): platform() = छोटा code ('win32', 'linux', 'darwin'). type() = पूर्ण OS नाव ('Windows_NT', 'Linux', 'Darwin').
  • Q2 (RAM GB मध्ये): os.totalmem() bytes देतो. 1024 ने तीनदा भागा: bytes → KB → MB → GB.
  • Q3 (CPU cores): os.cpus().length. Cluster मध्ये प्रत्येक core साठी एक worker बनवायला वापरतात.
  • Q4 (Windows वर loadavg): नेहमी [0, 0, 0], कारण load average ही Unix / Linux ची संकल्पना आहे.

Quick Revision Summary

Method Description
os.platform() Returns OS identifier (win32, linux).
os.cpus() Array containing info about each CPU core.
os.totalmem() Total RAM in bytes.
os.freemem() Free RAM in bytes.
os.homedir() Path to the user's home directory.
os.uptime() Computer uptime in seconds.

Chapter 9: The FS Module (File System)

Q. What is the fs module in Node.js?

Definition:
  • fs stands for File System. It is a built-in core Node.js module that provides a comprehensive set of APIs to interact with the underlying computer's file system.
  • It allows Node.js applications to read, write, update, delete, rename, copy, and manage files and directories on disk.
  • We can also work with File metadata (size, creation time, permissions) and Streams for handling large files efficiently.
  • Since it is a core module, no npm installation is required. Import: const fs = require("node:fs");
  • The fs module provides three main API styles:
    • 1. Synchronous APIs (e.g., readFileSync): Blocks the entire JavaScript thread until the operation finishes. Simple but should NOT be used in server applications for every request.
    • 2. Asynchronous Callback APIs (e.g., readFile): Does NOT block the main thread. Accepts a callback (err, data) => {} that is called when the operation is done.
    • 3. Promise-based APIs (e.g., require('node:fs/promises')): The modern recommended approach. Allows use of async/await for cleaner, readable code.
  • Code Example: 3 Ways to Read a File 
    // 1. Synchronous (Blocks thread)
const fs = require('node:fs');
const dataSync = fs.readFileSync('hello.txt', 'utf-8');
console.log(dataSync);

// 2. Asynchronous Callback (Non-blocking)
fs.readFile('hello.txt', 'utf-8', (err, dataAsync) => {
    if (err) throw err;
    console.log(dataAsync);
});

// 3. Promise-based (Modern async/await)
const fsp = require('node:fs/promises');
async function readFilePro() {
    const data = await fsp.readFile('hello.txt', 'utf-8');
    console.log(data);
}
readFilePro();
मराठी सारांश (fs Module Overview):
  • fs (File System) module वापरून आपण Node.js मधून थेट कम्प्युटरच्या फाईल सिस्टमशी interact करू शकतो.
  • यात तीन प्रकारच्या APIs आहेत: Synchronous (थांबून), Asynchronous Callback (background मध्ये + callback), आणि Promise-based (async/await सह modern approach).
  • Server applications मध्ये नेहमी Asynchronous किंवा Promise-based APIs वापरावेत, Synchronous कधीही नको.
  • Import: require('node:fs') (sync + callback) आणि require('node:fs/promises') (promise).
  • मुख्य कामे: file वाचणे, लिहणे, जोडणे (append), नाव बदलणे, delete, folders बनवणे / वाचणे, metadata (size, date).
  • Sync कुठे चालतो? server सुरू होताना एकदाच (config वाचणे) किंवा छोट्या CLI scripts मध्ये.
  • मोठ्या files साठी streams (createReadStream) वापरा, पूर्ण file memory मध्ये घेऊ नका.
  • उदाहरण: fs म्हणजे computer च्या कपाटाची चावी. आत काय आहे पाहणे, ठेवणे, काढणे.

Q. Synchronous vs Asynchronous (The "Parallel" Misconception)

  • Important Interview Point: Many people incorrectly say "Asynchronous fs tasks run in parallel".
  • The Reality: Asynchronous operations don't strictly run "in parallel" in JavaScript's single thread; rather, they are Non-Blocking. Node.js offloads the file operation to the OS, allowing the JavaScript thread to continue executing other code while the I/O operation happens in the background.
  • In server applications, Synchronous methods should be avoided for frequent requests because they block the event loop, delaying other users' requests.
मराठी सारांश (Sync vs Async):
  • Asynchronous म्हणजे सगळी कामे एकाच वेळी (parallel) होतात असे नाही. याचा खरा अर्थ म्हणजे Node.js फाईलचे काम OS ला सोपवतो आणि स्वतः पुढच्या code वर काम करायला मोकळा होतो (Non-blocking).
  • Synchronous methods server मध्ये वापरल्यास संपूर्ण event loop block होतो, त्यामुळे इतर users ला वाट पाहावी लागते.
  • Server applications मध्ये नेहमी Asynchronous पद्धत वापरावी.
  • Non-blocking चा खरा अर्थ: एका वेळी अनेक कामे "होत" नाहीत. Node थांबत नाही, आणि OS / libuv background मध्ये काम करतात.
  • उदाहरण: 100 users नी file मागितली. Sync मध्ये प्रत्येकाला आधीच्याची वाट. Async मध्ये सगळ्यांच्या requests पुढे पाठवल्या जातात.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: async callbacks कोणत्या क्रमाने संपतील याची खात्री नसते.
Figure 15: FS Module — All 3 API Styles with Methods

Q. All 3 API Styles — Complete Examples for Every Method

  • Style 1 — Synchronous (Blocking): All Major Methods 
    const fs = require('node:fs');

// READ
const data = fs.readFileSync('hello.txt', 'utf-8');
console.log('Read:', data);

// WRITE (overwrites if exists)
fs.writeFileSync('hello.txt', 'Hello World!');

// APPEND (adds to end)
fs.appendFileSync('hello.txt', '\nNew Line Added');

// RENAME / MOVE
fs.renameSync('hello.txt', 'renamed.txt');

// COPY
fs.copyFileSync('renamed.txt', 'backup.txt');

// DELETE FILE
fs.unlinkSync('backup.txt');

// CREATE FOLDER
fs.mkdirSync('myLogs/sub', { recursive: true });

// READ FOLDER CONTENTS
const files = fs.readdirSync('myLogs');
console.log('Folder contents:', files);

// FILE METADATA
const stats = fs.statSync('renamed.txt');
console.log('Is File?', stats.isFile(), '| Size:', stats.size, 'bytes');

// CHECK EXISTS
if (fs.existsSync('renamed.txt')) console.log('File exists!');

// DELETE FOLDER
fs.rmSync('myLogs', { recursive: true, force: true });
  • Style 2 — Asynchronous Callback (Non-Blocking): All Major Methods 
    const fs = require('node:fs');

// WRITE
fs.writeFile('hello.txt', 'Hello Async!', (err) => {
    if (err) return console.error(err);
    console.log('Written!');

    // READ (nested inside write callback)
    fs.readFile('hello.txt', 'utf-8', (err, data) => {
        if (err) return console.error(err);
        console.log('Read:', data);

        // APPEND
        fs.appendFile('hello.txt', '\nAppended!', (err) => {
            if (err) return console.error(err);
            console.log('Appended!');

            // RENAME
            fs.rename('hello.txt', 'renamed.txt', (err) => {
                if (err) return console.error(err);
                console.log('Renamed!');

                // STAT (Metadata)
                fs.stat('renamed.txt', (err, stats) => {
                    if (err) return console.error(err);
                    console.log('Size:', stats.size, 'bytes');

                    // DELETE
                    fs.unlink('renamed.txt', (err) => {
                        if (err) return console.error(err);
                        console.log('Deleted!');
                    });
                });
            });
        });
    });
});

// MKDIR
fs.mkdir('asyncFolder', { recursive: true }, (err) => {
    if (err) return console.error(err);
    console.log('Folder created!');

    // READDIR
    fs.readdir('.', (err, files) => {
        if (err) return console.error(err);
        console.log('Files:', files);

        // DELETE FOLDER
        fs.rm('asyncFolder', { recursive: true }, (err) => {
            if (err) return console.error(err);
            console.log('Folder deleted!');
        });
    });
});
  • Style 3 — Promise-based / async-await (Modern Recommended): All Major Methods 
    const fsp = require('node:fs/promises');

async function runAllFsOps() {
    // WRITE
    await fsp.writeFile('hello.txt', 'Hello Promise!');
    console.log('Written!');

    // APPEND
    await fsp.appendFile('hello.txt', '\nAppended Line!');
    console.log('Appended!');

    // READ
    const data = await fsp.readFile('hello.txt', 'utf-8');
    console.log('Read:', data);

    // COPY
    await fsp.copyFile('hello.txt', 'backup.txt');
    console.log('Copied!');

    // RENAME
    await fsp.rename('hello.txt', 'renamed.txt');
    console.log('Renamed!');

    // STAT (Metadata)
    const stats = await fsp.stat('renamed.txt');
    console.log('Is File?', stats.isFile(), '| Size:', stats.size, 'bytes');

    // DELETE FILE
    await fsp.unlink('renamed.txt');
    await fsp.unlink('backup.txt');
    console.log('Files deleted!');

    // CREATE FOLDER
    await fsp.mkdir('promiseFolder/sub', { recursive: true });
    console.log('Folder created!');

    // READ FOLDER
    const files = await fsp.readdir('promiseFolder');
    console.log('Folder contents:', files);

    // DELETE FOLDER
    await fsp.rm('promiseFolder', { recursive: true, force: true });
    console.log('Folder deleted!');
}

runAllFsOps().catch(console.error);
मराठी सारांश (3 API Styles):
  • Synchronous (Red): प्रत्येक method च्या नावाच्या शेवटी Sync असतो. हे simple आहे पण server मध्ये वापरल्यास संपूर्ण program थांबतो.
  • Async Callback (Orange): Sync शिवाय method नाव, शेवटी callback function (err, data) => {} असतो. Non-blocking असल्याने server साठी चांगले, पण nested callbacks मुळे "Callback Hell" होऊ शकते.
  • Promise / async-await (Green): require('node:fs/promises') वापरतो. await वापरल्याने code एकदम clean आणि readable दिसतो. हे सर्वात modern आणि recommended approach आहे.
  • नियम: Sync method नावाला Sync शेवटी (readFileSync), callback method साधे नाव (readFile), promise method fs/promises मधून (await readFile).
  • Errors: Sync → try/catch. Callback → if (err). Promise → try/catch किंवा .catch().
  • कोणते वापरायचे? नवीन code मध्ये Promise / async-await. Sync फक्त startup ला.
  • उदाहरण: Sync = रांगेत उभे राहणे. Callback = "झाले की फोन करा". Promise = token घेऊन बसणे.

Q. Why does readFileSync return a Buffer instead of a string?

  • Files internally contain raw bytes.
  • When no encoding is specified (e.g., fs.readFileSync('hello.txt')), Node.js gives us those raw bytes as a Buffer.
  • To get a readable string, you must either specify the encoding: fs.readFileSync('hello.txt', 'utf-8'), OR call .toString() on the buffer.
मराठी सारांश (Buffer vs String):
  • आपण जेव्हा फाईल वाचतो, तेव्हा Node.js आपल्याला directly string न देता Buffer (raw bytes) देतो कारण फाईल internally bytes मध्ये साठवलेली असते.
  • त्याला readable string बनवण्यासाठी 'utf-8' encoding द्यावी लागते: fs.readFileSync('hello.txt', 'utf-8')
  • किंवा Buffer वर .toString() call करता येते: buffer.toString('utf-8')
  • का Buffer? प्रत्येक file text नसते (image, PDF, zip). Node ला माहीत नसते की file कोणत्या encoding ची आहे, म्हणून सुरक्षितपणे raw bytes देतो.
  • Output फरक: encoding शिवाय <Buffer 48 65 6c 6c 6f>. 'utf-8' सह "Hello".
  • Images / binary files साठी Buffer च ठेवा. toString() केल्यास data खराब होतो.
  • उदाहरण: बंद पाकीट (Buffer). Encoding म्हणजे आतले पत्र कोणत्या भाषेत वाचायचे ते सांगणे.

Q. File CRUD Operations (Synchronous & Async)

  • 1. fs.writeFileSync(file, data, [options]) / fs.writeFile(file, data, [options], callback):
    • Creates a new file and writes data to it. If the file already exists, it completely overwrites the old content.
    • file: Path to the file (string). data: Content to write (string or Buffer). options: e.g., { encoding: 'utf-8', flag: 'w' }.
    fs.writeFileSync('user.txt', 'Hello Vishal');          // Sync
fs.writeFile('user.txt', 'Hello!', (err) => {          // Async
    if (err) throw err;
    console.log('File written!');
});
  • 2. fs.appendFileSync(file, data) / fs.appendFile(file, data, callback):
    • Adds data to the end of an existing file without deleting the old content. Creates the file if it doesn't exist.
    • file: Path to the file. data: Content to append.
    fs.appendFileSync('user.txt', '\nNew line added!');     // Sync
fs.appendFile('user.txt', ' - Welcome!', (err) => {    // Async
    if (err) throw err;
});
  • 3. fs.readFileSync(file, encoding) / fs.readFile(file, encoding, callback):
    • Reads the complete content of a file. Without encoding, returns a raw Buffer.
    • file: Path to the file. encoding: e.g., 'utf-8' to get a readable string.
    const data = fs.readFileSync('user.txt', 'utf-8');      // Sync
fs.readFile('user.txt', 'utf-8', (err, data) => {       // Async
    if (err) throw err;
    console.log(data);
});
  • 4. fs.renameSync(oldPath, newPath) / fs.rename(oldPath, newPath, callback):
    • Renames a file. It can also be used to move a file to a different directory by changing the path.
    • oldPath: Current file path. newPath: New file path/name.
    fs.renameSync('user.txt', 'developer.txt');             // Rename
fs.renameSync('user.txt', 'archive/user.txt');          // Move
  • 5. fs.unlinkSync(file) / fs.unlink(file, callback):
    • Permanently deletes a file. Used only for files, not directories.
    • file: Path to the file to delete.
    fs.unlinkSync('developer.txt');                         // Sync delete
fs.unlink('developer.txt', (err) => {                   // Async delete
    if (err) throw err;
    console.log('File deleted!');
});
  • 6. fs.copyFileSync(src, dest) / fs.copyFile(src, dest, callback):
    • Copies a file from the src (source) path to the dest (destination) path.
    • src: Source file path. dest: Destination file path.
    fs.copyFileSync('original.txt', 'backup.txt');          // Creates a copy
मराठी सारांश (File CRUD):
  • writeFile: फाईल बनवतो आणि त्यात data लिहितो. जुनी फाईल असल्यास संपूर्ण content overwrite होतो.
  • appendFile: फाईलच्या शेवटी नवीन data जोडतो, जुना data टिकतो.
  • readFile: फाईल वाचतो. 'utf-8' न दिल्यास Buffer (bytes) मिळतात, string नाही.
  • rename: फाईलचे नाव बदलतो किंवा दुसऱ्या folder मध्ये हलवतो.
  • unlink: फाईल कायमची delete करतो (फक्त files साठी, folders साठी नाही).
  • copyFile: फाईलची copy तयार करतो वेगळ्या path वर.

Q. Directory Operations

  • 1. fs.mkdirSync(path, [options]) / fs.mkdir(path, [options], callback):
    • Creates a new directory at the specified path.
    • path: Directory path to create. options.recursive: true: Automatically creates parent folders if they don't exist (prevents errors if folder already exists).
    fs.mkdirSync('myFolder/subFolder', { recursive: true }); // Creates both folders
fs.mkdir('logs', { recursive: true }, (err) => {          // Async version
    if (err) throw err;
    console.log('Folder created!');
});
  • 2. fs.readdirSync(path) / fs.readdir(path, callback):
    • Reads the contents of a directory and returns an array of strings (file and folder names) inside it.
    • path: Path of the directory to read.
    const files = fs.readdirSync('myFolder');
console.log(files); // e.g. ['index.js', 'package.json', 'subFolder']
  • 3. fs.rmSync(path, [options]) / fs.rm(path, [options], callback): (Recommended for modern Node.js)
    • Removes a file or directory. options.recursive: true deletes a folder and all its contents.
    • options.force: true suppresses errors if the file/folder doesn't exist.
    fs.rmSync('myFolder', { recursive: true, force: true }); // Delete folder + all contents
fs.rm('logs', { recursive: true }, (err) => {            // Async version
    if (err) throw err;
    console.log('Folder deleted!');
});
  • 4. fs.rmdirSync(path): (Legacy — use rmSync instead)
    • Removes an empty directory only. Throws an error if the directory has files inside it.
मराठी सारांश (Directory Operations):
  • mkdir / mkdirSync: नवीन folder बनवतो. recursive: true दिल्यास parent folders आपोआप तयार होतात.
  • readdir / readdirSync: folder मधील सर्व files आणि sub-folders ची array देतो.
  • rmSync (Modern): recursive: true सह folder आणि त्यातील सर्व content delete करतो. rmdirSync फक्त रिकाम्या folders साठी आहे.
  • mkdir: fs.mkdirSync('a/b/c', { recursive: true }). Folder आधीच असेल तरी error येत नाही.
  • readdir: { withFileTypes: true } दिल्यास प्रत्येक entry file आहे की folder ते isFile() / isDirectory() ने कळते.
  • rm: fs.rmSync('folder', { recursive: true, force: true }). force मुळे folder नसला तरी error नाही.
  • सावधान: rm recursive ने सगळा content कायमचा जातो (Recycle Bin नाही). Path नीट तपासा.
  • उदाहरण: कपाट बनवणे (mkdir), आत काय आहे पाहणे (readdir), संपूर्ण कपाट रिकामे करून फेकणे (rm recursive).

Q. File Information & Metadata

  • fs.existsSync(path):
    • Returns true if a file or folder exists at the given path, otherwise returns false.
    • path: The file or directory path to check.
    • Useful to check before reading or writing to avoid ENOENT (file not found) errors.
    if (fs.existsSync('user.txt')) {
    console.log('File exists! Safe to read.');
} else {
    console.log('File does not exist.');
}
  • fs.statSync(path) / fs.stat(path, callback):
    • Returns a Stats object containing metadata about the file or directory.
    • path: Path to the file or directory.
    • The Stats object contains: stats.size (bytes), stats.birthtime (creation date), stats.mtime (last modified date), stats.isFile(), stats.isDirectory().
    const stats = fs.statSync('hello.txt');
console.log(stats.isFile());        // true
console.log(stats.isDirectory());   // false
console.log(stats.size);            // e.g. 1024 (bytes)
console.log(stats.birthtime);       // 2024-01-01T10:00:00.000Z
console.log(stats.mtime);           // Last modified time
  • fs.accessSync(path, mode) / fs.access(path, mode, callback):
    • Checks if the application has the specified permission to access a file. Throws an error if access is denied.
    • path: File path to check. mode: Permission constant from fs.constants.
