    • Common modes: fs.constants.F_OK (file exists), fs.constants.R_OK (readable), fs.constants.W_OK (writable).
    try {
    fs.accessSync('secret.txt', fs.constants.R_OK | fs.constants.W_OK);
    console.log('File is readable and writable!');
} catch (err) {
    console.log('Access denied:', err.message);
}
मराठी सारांश (Metadata):
  • existsSync(path): फाईल किंवा folder अस्तित्वात आहे का ते check करतो. true किंवा false देतो.
  • statSync(path): फाईलची metadata (size, creation date, modified date) असलेला Stats object देतो. isFile() आणि isDirectory() methods check करता येतात.
  • accessSync(path, mode): आपल्या application ला त्या फाईलवर Read किंवा Write permission आहे का ते check करतो.
  • Stats properties: size (bytes), birthtime (बनवली), mtime (शेवटचा बदल), isFile(), isDirectory().
  • Modes: fs.constants.R_OK (read), W_OK (write), F_OK (आहे का). Permission नसल्यास error throw होतो.
  • Tip: "आहे का तपासा, मग उघडा" ऐवजी थेट उघडून error (ENOENT) handle करणे जास्त सुरक्षित (race condition टळते).
  • उदाहरण: पुस्तकाच्या मागचे लेबल. पाने किती, कधी छापले, कोण वाचू शकतो.
Figure 16: File System (FS) Operation Categories

Important Attributes (Options) in FS Module

Concept:
  • Many fs methods accept an options object to modify their default strict behavior.
  • These attributes (like recursive, force, encoding) are essential for handling real-world file system scenarios safely and efficiently.
  • 1. recursive: true (Used in mkdir and rm):
    • When creating a folder (fs.mkdirSync): Automatically creates all missing parent folders instead of throwing an error.
    • When deleting a folder (fs.rmSync): Automatically deletes all files and nested folders inside the target folder before deleting the folder itself (like Shift+Delete).
  • 2. force: true (Used in rm):
    • When deleting a file or folder (fs.rmSync): Ignores exceptions if the file or folder does not exist (prevents the app from crashing with an ENOENT error).
  • 3. withFileTypes: true (Used in readdir):
    • By default, readdirSync returns an array of string names.
    • With this option, it returns an array of fs.Dirent objects, allowing you to easily check if an item is a file (item.isFile()) or a folder (item.isDirectory()).
  • 4. encoding: 'utf-8' (Used in readFile and writeFile):
    • By default, reading a file returns raw binary data (Buffer).
    • Passing 'utf-8' automatically decodes the Buffer into a human-readable string.
Code Example: Using Attributes in FS
const fs = require('node:fs');

// 1. Recursive Folder Creation (creates parents if missing)
fs.mkdirSync('logs/2026/october', { recursive: true }); 

// 2. Recursive & Forceful Deletion (deletes contents, ignores if not exists)
fs.rmSync('logs', { recursive: true, force: true });

// 3. Reading Directory with File Types
const items = fs.readdirSync('.', { withFileTypes: true });
items.forEach(item => {
    if (item.isDirectory()) console.log(`Folder: ${item.name}`);
});

// 4. Reading file directly into a String (instead of a Buffer)
const text = fs.readFileSync('data.txt', { encoding: 'utf-8' });
मराठी सारांश (FS Attributes):
  • recursive: mkdir मध्ये गायब असलेले parent folders बनवण्यासाठी आणि rm मध्ये फाईल्सने भरलेले फोल्डर कचऱ्यासकट डिलीट करण्यासाठी.
  • force: फाईल किंवा फोल्डर अस्तित्वात नसली तरी error येऊ नये म्हणून.
  • withFileTypes: फोल्डर वाचताना नक्की कोणती फाईल आहे आणि कोणते फोल्डर हे ओळखण्यासाठी.
  • encoding: कॉम्प्युटरची भाषा (Buffer) माणसाला समजणाऱ्या भाषेत (String) वाचण्यासाठी.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What is the difference between `fs.writeFileSync()` and `fs.appendFileSync()`?
  • fs.writeFileSync() overwrites the entire file. If data was present, it gets deleted and replaced.
  • fs.appendFileSync() adds new data to the very end of the file, preserving all the old data.
Q2. Why is asynchronous file execution preferred in Node.js servers?
  • Node.js runs on a single JavaScript thread. If you use synchronous file operations (like readFileSync), the entire server freezes while reading the file. Asynchronous operations prevent blocking, allowing the server to handle other users' requests simultaneously.
Q3. What is the difference between `fs.unlinkSync()` and `fs.rmdirSync()`?
  • fs.unlinkSync() is strictly used to delete files.
  • fs.rmdirSync() is strictly used to delete empty directories/folders.
Q4. What is "Callback Hell" and how do you solve it in fs module?
  • Callback Hell (also called "Pyramid of Doom") happens when multiple asynchronous callback operations are deeply nested inside each other, making code very hard to read and maintain.
  • This is a common problem with the fs callback API when you need to perform multiple sequential file operations.
  • Solution: Use fs/promises with async/await which flattens the code and makes it linear and readable.
// ❌ CALLBACK HELL (Pyramid of Doom)
fs.readFile('a.txt', 'utf-8', (err, data1) => {
    fs.readFile('b.txt', 'utf-8', (err, data2) => {
        fs.writeFile('output.txt', data1 + data2, (err) => {
            fs.rename('output.txt', 'final.txt', (err) => {
                console.log('Done!'); // deeply nested!
            });
        });
    });
});

// ✅ SOLUTION: async/await (Flat & Clean)
const fsp = require('node:fs/promises');
async function mergeFiles() {
    const data1 = await fsp.readFile('a.txt', 'utf-8');
    const data2 = await fsp.readFile('b.txt', 'utf-8');
    await fsp.writeFile('output.txt', data1 + data2);
    await fsp.rename('output.txt', 'final.txt');
    console.log('Done!'); // linear and clean!
}
mergeFiles().catch(console.error);
Q5. What is the difference between `fs.readFileSync` and `fs.createReadStream`? Which is better for large files?
  • fs.readFileSync() / fs.readFile(): Loads the entire file into memory at once (as a Buffer). If the file is 1 GB, it reserves 1 GB of RAM. This is fine for small files but crashes or slows down for large files.
  • fs.createReadStream(): Reads the file in small chunks (default 64KB). Only one chunk is in memory at a time. This is memory-efficient and ideal for large files (videos, logs, CSVs).
  • Verdict: Always use createReadStream for files larger than a few MB.
// ❌ Bad for large files — loads entire file into RAM
const data = fs.readFileSync('large-video.mp4'); // may crash!

// ✅ Good — reads in 64KB chunks, memory efficient
const stream = fs.createReadStream('large-video.mp4');
stream.on('data', (chunk) => {
    console.log('Chunk received:', chunk.length, 'bytes');
});
stream.on('end', () => {
    console.log('File reading complete!');
});
Q6. What is `ENOENT` error and how do you handle file errors properly?
  • ENOENT stands for "Error NO ENTry" — it means the file or directory you are trying to access does NOT exist.
  • It is the most common error when working with the fs module.
  • Best Practice: Always wrap sync calls in try/catch, and always check the err argument in callbacks.
// Sync: use try/catch
try {
    const data = fs.readFileSync('missing.txt', 'utf-8');
} catch (err) {
    if (err.code === 'ENOENT') {
        console.log('File not found!');
    } else {
        throw err; // rethrow unexpected errors
    }
}

// Async callback: check err argument
fs.readFile('missing.txt', 'utf-8', (err, data) => {
    if (err) {
        if (err.code === 'ENOENT') console.log('File not found!');
        else console.error('Unexpected error:', err);
        return;
    }
    console.log(data);
});

// Promise: use try/catch with async/await
async function safeRead() {
    try {
        const data = await fsp.readFile('missing.txt', 'utf-8');
        console.log(data);
    } catch (err) {
        if (err.code === 'ENOENT') console.log('File not found!');
    }
}
Q7. What are `fs.writeFile` flag options and what does each do?
  • The flag option in writeFile / writeFileSync controls how the file is opened before writing.
  • flag: 'w' (default): Opens file for writing. Creates the file if it doesn't exist. Overwrites existing content.
  • flag: 'a': Opens file for appending. Creates the file if it doesn't exist. Does NOT overwrite.
  • flag: 'wx': Like 'w' but fails if the file already exists. Used to prevent accidental overwrites.
  • flag: 'r': Opens file for reading only. Fails if the file does not exist.
  • flag: 'r+': Opens file for both reading and writing.
// Default: overwrite existing content
fs.writeFileSync('log.txt', 'Entry 1', { flag: 'w' });

// Append to existing content (same as appendFileSync)
fs.writeFileSync('log.txt', '\nEntry 2', { flag: 'a' });

// Fail if file already exists (safe write)
try {
    fs.writeFileSync('config.json', '{}', { flag: 'wx' });
} catch (err) {
    console.log('Config already exists, not overwriting!');
}
Q8. How do you watch a file for changes in Node.js?
  • fs.watch(path, callback): Watches a file or directory for changes. Fires the callback when the file is modified, renamed, or deleted. It is event-driven and very efficient.
  • fs.watchFile(path, callback): Also watches a file, but uses polling (checks the file every interval). Less efficient than fs.watch. Use only when fs.watch is unreliable (e.g., on network file systems).
  • Common use cases: Dev servers (like Nodemon), log monitoring, config hot-reload.
// fs.watch — Event-driven (Efficient)
const watcher = fs.watch('config.json', (eventType, filename) => {
    console.log(`Event: ${eventType}`);     // 'change' or 'rename'
    console.log(`File: ${filename}`);       // 'config.json'
    console.log('File was modified! Reloading...');
});

// Stop watching after 10 seconds
setTimeout(() => watcher.close(), 10000);

// fs.watchFile — Polling-based (Less efficient, but more reliable)
fs.watchFile('config.json', { interval: 1000 }, (curr, prev) => {
    if (curr.mtime !== prev.mtime) {
        console.log('File changed! New size:', curr.size, 'bytes');
    }
});
Q9. Promise-based `fs/promises` vs Callback-based `fs` — Which is better and why?
  • Callback-based `fs`: Old API, still works. Can lead to deeply nested Callback Hell. Error handling requires manual if (err) check in every function.
  • Promise-based `fs/promises`: Modern API (available since Node.js 10+). Works with async/await. Error handling is centralized in a single try/catch block. Code is flat, readable, and maintainable.
  • Verdict: Always prefer fs/promises with async/await in all modern Node.js applications. Use callback-based fs only when working with legacy codebases.
// ❌ Callback (error-prone, nested)
fs.readFile('a.txt', 'utf-8', (err, data) => {
    if (err) return console.error(err);
    fs.writeFile('b.txt', data, (err) => {
        if (err) return console.error(err);
        console.log('Done!');
    });
});

// ✅ Promise (clean, flat, single try/catch)
async function copyFile() {
    try {
        const data = await fsp.readFile('a.txt', 'utf-8');
        await fsp.writeFile('b.txt', data);
        console.log('Done!');
    } catch (err) {
        console.error('Something went wrong:', err.message);
    }
}
copyFile();

Quick Revision Summary

Method Description
fs.readFileSync() Reads a file. Returns a Buffer unless 'utf-8' is specified.
fs.writeFileSync() Creates or completely overwrites a file.
fs.appendFileSync() Adds data to the end of a file without overwriting.
fs.unlinkSync() Deletes a file permanently.
fs.mkdirSync() Creates a new folder.
fs.existsSync() Returns boolean if file/folder exists or not.

Chapter 9.5: The Timers Module

Q. What is the Timers Module in Node.js?

Concept:
  • The timers module in Node.js provides an essential API for scheduling the execution of functions after a specified period of time.
  • It allows developers to handle asynchronous, time-based operations like delays, repeating intervals, and immediate background execution.
  • Unlike many other core modules, the functions in the timers module are globally available. This means you do not need to explicitly use require('timers') to access basic functions like setTimeout or setInterval.
  • Node.js timers are deeply integrated with the Node.js Event Loop and execute in specific phases (like the Timers phase and the Check phase).
  • Timers in Node.js work very similarly to the timers API in web browsers.
  • They are tightly integrated with the Node.js Event Loop.
  • Important Global Timer Methods:
    • setTimeout(): Runs code once after a delay.
    • setInterval(): Runs code repeatedly after a delay.
    • setImmediate(): Runs code immediately after the current I/O event callbacks finish.
मराठी सारांश (Timers Module):
  • timers module वापरून आपण आपला code ठराविक वेळेनंतर रन करू शकतो.
  • हे Node.js मध्ये globally available असते, त्यामुळे याला require() करायची गरज नसते.
  • यामध्ये मुख्यत्वे setTimeout, setInterval आणि setImmediate यांचा वापर होतो.
  • setTimeout: एकदाच, किमान delay नंतर. setInterval: दर delay नी पुन्हा पुन्हा. setImmediate: I/O नंतर लगेच (Check phase).
  • थांबवणे: clearTimeout, clearInterval, clearImmediate. त्यासाठी return झालेला id साठवा.
  • Promise version: require('timers/promises') → await setTimeout(1000).
  • Timers event loop शी जोडलेले आहेत. Delay ही किमान वेळ, अचूक नाही.
  • उदाहरण: alarm (setTimeout), दर तासाची घंटा (setInterval), "हे संपले की लगेच" (setImmediate).

Q. 1. setTimeout()

Concept:
  • setTimeout is a global timer function used to schedule the execution of a specific callback function after a minimum specified delay.
  • It is designed to execute the provided block of code only once.
  • It returns a Timeout object which can be passed to the clearTimeout() function if you need to cancel the execution before the timer expires.
Syntax & Arguments: setTimeout(callback, delay, [args...])
  • callback: The function containing the code you want to execute after the timer finishes.
  • delay: The time to wait in milliseconds (e.g., 1000 = 1 second) before executing the callback.
  • args...: Optional arguments that will be directly passed into the callback function when it executes.

Q. 2. setInterval()

Concept:
  • setInterval is a global timer function used to repeatedly execute a specific callback function at a regular, continuous time interval.
  • Once started, it will continue to execute indefinitely in the background until it is explicitly stopped by the developer.
  • It returns a Timeout object which must be passed to the clearInterval() function to stop the repeating execution cycle.
Syntax & Arguments: setInterval(callback, delay, [args...])
  • callback: The function containing the code to execute repeatedly.
  • delay: The time interval in milliseconds between each execution of the callback.
  • args...: Optional arguments that will be directly passed into the callback function on every tick.

Q. 3. setImmediate()

Concept:
  • setImmediate is a global timer function designed specifically for Node.js to execute a callback function asynchronously, but as soon as possible.
  • It schedules the callback to run immediately after the current I/O event callbacks finish (specifically in the "Check" phase of the Event Loop).
  • It returns an Immediate object which can be passed to the clearImmediate() function to cancel the pending execution before it fires.
Syntax & Arguments: setImmediate(callback, [args...])
  • callback: The function to execute at the end of the current event loop phase.
  • args...: Optional arguments passed directly into the callback function.
  • Note: It does NOT take a delay argument because it is inherently meant to execute immediately after the current phase finishes.
Code Example: Using and Clearing Timers
// 1. setTimeout (Runs once)
const timeoutId = setTimeout((name) => {
    console.log(`Hello, ${name}! This runs after 2 seconds.`);
}, 2000, 'Vishal');

// To cancel it before it runs:
// clearTimeout(timeoutId);

// 2. setInterval (Runs repeatedly)
let count = 0;
const intervalId = setInterval(() => {
    count++;
    console.log(`Interval tick: ${count}`);
    
    // Stop the interval after 3 ticks
    if (count === 3) {
        clearInterval(intervalId);
        console.log("Interval stopped!");
    }
}, 1000);

// 3. setImmediate (Runs immediately after current I/O phase)
const immediateId = setImmediate(() => {
    console.log("This runs immediately after the current event loop phase!");
});
console.log("This prints FIRST because it is normal synchronous code!");
मराठी सारांश (Syntax & Clearing):
  • setTimeout एखादा कोड एकदाच रन करतो, तर setInterval तो कोड वारंवार रन करतो.
  • दोन्ही methods delay (milliseconds मध्ये) घेतात.
  • कोणताही timer थांबवण्यासाठी आपण तो timer एका variable मध्ये सेव्ह करतो आणि मग clearTimeout किंवा clearInterval ला देतो.
  • setImmediate ला वेळ द्यावा लागत नाही, ते चालू असलेले काम संपल्यावर लगेच रन होते.
  • setTimeout(cb, delay, ...args): cb = चालवायचे function, delay = किमान किती ms थांबायचे, args = cb ला द्यायचे values. उदाहरण: OTP 30 सेकंदांनी expire करणे.
  • setInterval(cb, delay, ...args): दर delay ms नी cb चालवतो, clearInterval करेपर्यंत. उदाहरण: घड्याळ, दर 5 सेकंदांनी data refresh.
  • setImmediate(cb, ...args): delay नाही. Event loop च्या Check phase मध्ये, I/O नंतर लगेच.
  • Return values: setTimeout / setInterval → Timeout object. setImmediate → Immediate object. हेच clear functions ला देतात.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: delay ही किमान वेळ आहे, अचूक नाही. Event loop busy असेल तर उशीर होतो.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What is the difference between `setTimeout(..., 0)` and `setImmediate()`?
  • Both schedule a callback to run asynchronously as soon as possible, but they run in different phases of the Event Loop.
  • setTimeout(..., 0) runs in the Timers phase of the event loop.
  • setImmediate() runs in the Check phase of the event loop.
  • If called within an I/O cycle (like reading a file), setImmediate() will always execute before setTimeout(..., 0).
Q2. Is Node.js `setTimeout` guaranteed to run exactly on time?
  • No. Timers in Node.js do not guarantee exact timing. The specified delay is the minimum amount of time before execution.
  • If the Event Loop is blocked by a heavy synchronous operation (like a massive for loop), the timer callback will be delayed until the synchronous operation finishes.
मराठी सारांश (Timers Interview):
  • Q1 (setTimeout 0 vs setImmediate): setTimeout → Timers phase, setImmediate → Check phase. I/O callback च्या आत setImmediate नेहमी आधी. Main file मध्ये क्रम बदलू शकतो.
  • Q2 (setTimeout अचूक वेळेवर चालतो का?): नाही. Delay ही किमान वेळ आहे. मोठा synchronous loop चालू असेल तर timer उशिरा चालतो.
  • उदाहरण: "10 मिनिटांनी चहा देतो" म्हणजे किमान 10 मिनिटे. आचारी busy असेल तर 15 पण होऊ शकतात.
  • Tip: इतर कामे संपल्यानंतर लगेच काहीतरी चालवायचे असेल तर setImmediate वापरा. Delay हवा असेल तर setTimeout.
  • setInterval ची काळजी: callback ला delay पेक्षा जास्त वेळ लागला तर calls एकमेकांवर येतात. अशा वेळी recursive setTimeout वापरा.

Chapter 10: The Buffer Module

Q. What is a Buffer in Node.js?

Concept:
  • A Buffer is a built-in Node.js class used to handle raw binary data directly in memory.
    • (Raw Binary Data means data that is stored at the lowest machine level as a sequence of bytes (0s and 1s), rather than as human-readable text).
  • It represents data as a sequence of bytes and is commonly used with file systems, network streams, images, and encryption.
  • JavaScript originally only handled strings, objects, arrays, and numbers. It didn't have a way to handle binary data (like images, videos, TCP packets). Buffer solves this in Node.js.
  • A Buffer is a sequence of 8-bit unsigned values (from 0 to 255).
  • Buffer is globally available in Node.js, so you don't necessarily need to import it, though modern practice recommends const { Buffer } = require('node:buffer');.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Buffer म्हणजे बायनरी डेटा (0 आणि 1) मेमरीमध्ये साठवण्यासाठी वापरलेली जागा.
  • Raw binary data: माणसाला वाचता येणारा text नाही, तर machine level वरील bytes.
  • का लागतो? साध्या JavaScript मध्ये string, number, object होते, पण images / video / network packets सारखा binary data हाताळायची सोय नव्हती. Node.js ने Buffer दिला.
  • प्रत्येक byte ची value 0 ते 255 (8-bit) असते.
  • कुठे वापरतात? files वाचणे (fs), network streams, images, encryption (crypto).
  • Buffer global आहे, require करावा लागत नाही.
  • उदाहरण: Buffer म्हणजे सामान ठेवायचा डबा, ज्यात प्रत्येक कप्प्यात एक byte असतो.

Q. Key Characteristics of a Buffer

  • Fixed-Size (Size is Immutable): Once a Buffer is created, its size (length) CANNOT be changed. If you need more space, you must create a new, larger Buffer.
  • Mutable Content: Even though the size is fixed, the data inside the Buffer can be changed/modified at any time (e.g., buf[0] = 88;).
  • External Memory: Buffers allocate memory outside of the V8 JavaScript engine's normal heap garbage collection mechanism. It interacts directly with system RAM for efficiency.
  • Uint8Array: Under the hood, Buffer is built on top of JavaScript's Uint8Array.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Buffer चा आकार एकदा ठरवला की तो बदलता येत नाही (Fixed size).
  • पण आतला डेटा आपण बदलू शकतो (Mutable).
  • Buffer ची मेमरी V8 इंजिनच्या बाहेर, थेट सिस्टिमच्या RAM मध्ये साठवली जाते.
  • उदाहरण (size fixed): Buffer.alloc(5) ला 5 bytes. 6 वा byte लिहिला तरी जात नाही. मोठी जागा हवी तर नवीन Buffer बनवा.
  • उदाहरण (content mutable): buf[0] = 77 ने पहिला byte बदलतो. "NodeJS" → "ModeJS".
  • External memory का? मोठा binary data V8 heap बाहेर ठेवल्याने GC वरचा भार कमी आणि OS / I/O सोबत जलद काम.
  • Uint8Array: Buffer हा JavaScript च्या Uint8Array वर आधारित आहे (प्रत्येक byte 0–255).
  • उदाहरण: ठराविक कप्प्यांचा डबा. कप्पे वाढवता येत नाहीत, पण आतले सामान बदलता येते.
Figure 17: V8 Heap vs External Buffer Memory
Figure 18: Buffer Methods Classification

Q. Methods to Create a Buffer

Concept:
  • Buffers must be created by allocating memory or converting existing data (like strings or arrays) into binary format.
  • We cannot use the new Buffer() constructor anymore (it is deprecated for security reasons). We use static methods like .from(), .alloc(), or .allocUnsafe().
1. Syntax: Buffer.from(data, [encoding])
  • data: The string, array, or another buffer to convert into a Buffer.
  • encoding: (Optional) The character encoding of the string (e.g., 'utf-8').
  • Usage: Used to directly convert an existing string into binary data.

2. Syntax: Buffer.alloc(size)
  • size: The number of bytes to allocate (Integer).
  • Usage: Creates a clean, safe Buffer of the specified size where all bytes are initialized to 0. (Safe but slightly slower).

3. Syntax: Buffer.allocUnsafe(size)
  • size: The number of bytes to allocate (Integer).
  • Usage: Creates a Buffer without initializing the memory. It is extremely fast but may contain old, sensitive garbage data. It MUST be fully overwritten before reading.
मराठी सारांश (Buffer Creation):
  • Buffer.from(data, encoding): आधीच्या string / array पासून Buffer बनवतो. उदा. Buffer.from('Hi').
  • Buffer.alloc(size) वापरून आपण सुरक्षित (0 ने भरलेला) Buffer बनवतो. थोडा slow पण safe.
  • Buffer.allocUnsafe(size) हा फास्ट असतो पण त्यात जुना कचरा डेटा (garbage values) असू शकतो, त्यामुळे तो काळजीपूर्वक वापरावा. वापरण्यापूर्वी पूर्ण overwrite करा.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: new Buffer() आता deprecated आहे (security मुळे). वापरू नका.
  • उदाहरण: alloc = धुतलेला स्वच्छ डबा. allocUnsafe = न धुतलेला डबा (आधीचे अन्न असू शकते).

Q. Encoding and Conversion

Concept:
  • Since Buffers are just 0s and 1s, we frequently need to convert them back to human-readable strings or specific formats like Base64 or Hex.
Syntax: buf.toString([encoding])
  • encoding: The format to convert the binary data into.
    • 'utf-8' (Default): Standard text conversion.
    • 'hex': Converts bytes into a hexadecimal string.
    • 'base64': Converts the Buffer into a Base64 string (widely used for transmitting images/files over text protocols like HTTP). Base64 does NOT compress data, it just changes format.
  • Length Trap: "😀".length in JS is 2 (characters), but Buffer.from("😀").length is 4 (because UTF-8 uses 4 bytes to represent this emoji).
मराठी सारांश (Encoding आणि Conversion):
  • Buffer मध्ये फक्त 0 आणि 1 असतात. वाचण्यायोग्य बनवायला buf.toString(encoding) वापरतात.
  • 'utf-8' (default): साधा text. 'hex': hexadecimal string (उदा. 4e6f). 'base64': images / files text स्वरूपात पाठवण्यासाठी.
  • Base64 data compress करत नाही, फक्त format बदलतो (उलट size ~33% वाढते).
  • Length trap: "😀".length = 2, पण Buffer.from("😀").length = 4 bytes. String length आणि byte length वेगळे असू शकतात.

Q. Other Important Buffer Operations

1. Syntax: buf.write(string)
  • string: The text you want to inject/overwrite into an existing buffer.
  • Usage: Modifies the content of an existing buffer in-place.

2. Syntax: Buffer.compare(buf1, buf2)
  • buf1, buf2: The two buffers to compare byte-by-byte.
  • Returns: -1 if buf1 is less, 1 if buf1 is greater, and 0 if they are exactly identical.

3. Syntax: Buffer.concat(list)
  • list: An array of multiple Buffer objects.
  • Returns: A single, new combined Buffer.
Code Example: Basic Buffer Operations
// 1. Create a buffer from string
const buf1 = Buffer.from("NodeJS");
console.log(buf1); // <Buffer 4e 6f 64 65 4a 53>
console.log(buf1.toString()); // "NodeJS"

// 2. Modify Buffer Content
buf1[0] = 77; // ASCII for 'M'
console.log(buf1.toString()); // "ModeJS" (Mutable content!)

// 3. Convert to Base64
console.log(buf1.toString("base64")); // "TW9kZUpT"

// 4. Concatenate Buffers
const bufA = Buffer.from("Hello ");
const bufB = Buffer.from("World");
const combined = Buffer.concat([bufA, bufB]);
console.log(combined.toString()); // "Hello World"

// 5. Writing to a Buffer
const bufAlloc = Buffer.alloc(10);
bufAlloc.write("vishal");
console.log(bufAlloc.toString()); // "vishal"

// 6. Comparing Buffers
const b1 = Buffer.from("ABC");
const b2 = Buffer.from("BCD");
console.log(Buffer.compare(b1, b2)); // -1 (b1 is less than b2)
मराठी सारांश:
  • Buffer मधील डेटा माणसाला वाचता यावा यासाठी toString() चा वापर होतो.
  • buf.write(string): आधीच्या Buffer मध्ये नवीन text लिहितो (त्याच जागी overwrite).
  • Buffer.compare(b1, b2): byte-by-byte तुलना. -1 (b1 लहान), 0 (सारखे), 1 (b1 मोठा). Sorting साठी उपयोगी.
  • Buffer.concat([b1, b2]): अनेक Buffers जोडून एक नवीन Buffer. Stream मधून आलेले chunks जोडायला वापरतात.
  • buf[0] = 77: index ने थेट byte बदलता येतो (content mutable आहे).

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What is the difference between `Buffer.alloc()` and `Buffer.allocUnsafe()`?
  • Buffer.alloc(size) creates a zero-filled, initialized Buffer. It is slower but very safe.
  • Buffer.allocUnsafe(size) creates an uninitialized Buffer. It is faster but might contain old, sensitive memory data. It should always be completely overwritten before reading.
Q2. Are Buffers mutable or immutable?
  • The size of a Buffer is immutable (fixed).
  • The content inside a Buffer is mutable (can be changed).
Q3. Is a Buffer an Array?
  • No. A Buffer behaves like an array of bytes and uses indexing (buf[0]), but it is actually a subclass of Uint8Array. It lives in external memory, not the standard JS array heap.
Q4. What is the relationship between Buffers and Streams? (Missing Important Concept)
  • This is a critical concept: Streams process data in chunks, and those chunks are Buffers.
  • When you read a large file using fs.createReadStream(), or receive a large video over HTTP, Node.js doesn't load the entire file into RAM.
  • Instead, it breaks the data into smaller pieces (chunks). By default, these chunks are returned as Buffer objects.
  • Buffers are the fundamental data structures that make Node.js Streams work efficiently.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Interview मध्ये alloc() vs allocUnsafe() हमखास विचारतात. allocUnsafe() फास्ट असतो पण सुरक्षित नसतो.
  • "Buffer Mutable असतो का?" यावर सांगा की त्याचा आकार फिक्स (immutable) असतो, पण आतला डेटा बदलता (mutable) येतो.
  • Buffers आणि Streams: Streams जो डेटा तुकड्या-तुकड्यात (chunks) आणतात, ते तुकडे म्हणजेच Buffers असतात.
  • Buffer Array आहे का? नाही. Array सारखा index (buf[0]) वापरता येतो, पण तो Uint8Array चा subclass आहे आणि V8 heap बाहेर राहतो.
  • alloc vs allocUnsafe: alloc = 0 ने भरलेला, सुरक्षित. allocUnsafe = जुना data असू शकतो, वापरण्यापूर्वी पूर्ण overwrite करा.
  • new Buffer() deprecated आहे. Buffer.from / alloc वापरा.

Quick Revision Summary

Method / Concept Description
Buffer.from() Creates a Buffer from string/array data.
Buffer.alloc(size) Creates a zero-filled safe Buffer of fixed size.
Buffer.allocUnsafe(size) Creates a fast but uninitialized Buffer.
buf.toString('base64') Converts the Buffer to a base64 encoded string.
Buffer.concat([b1, b2]) Joins multiple buffers together.
Memory Location External memory, outside the normal V8 heap.

Chapter 10.5: The URL Module

Q. What is the URL Module?

Concept:
  • The url module provides utilities for URL resolution and parsing.
  • It helps us break down a long web address into individual components (protocol, hostname, port, query parameters).
  • Modern Node.js applications use the WHATWG URL API (a web standard), which means the URL class is globally available and does not require importing.
मराठी सारांश:
  • URL Module चा वापर एखाद्या मोठ्या web address मधून वेगवेगळी माहिती (domain, path, query string) वेगळी करण्यासाठी होतो.
  • URL चे भाग: https: (protocol), www.example.com (hostname), 8080 (port), /products/item (pathname), ?id=123 (search / query), #details (hash).
  • आधुनिक Node.js मध्ये आपण Global URL Class चा वापर करतो (WHATWG standard, browser सारखेच), त्यामुळे require('url') लिहिण्याची गरज पडत नाही.
  • उदाहरण: पत्ता "पुणे, कोथरूड, गल्ली 5, घर 12" तुकड्यांत वेगळा करणे. शहर, भाग, गल्ली, घर.
  • उपयोग: server मध्ये req.url मधून path आणि query values काढणे.
Figure 19: URL Components Anatomy

Q. The Global URL Class

Syntax: new URL(input, [base])
  • input: The absolute or relative URL string you want to parse.
  • base: (Optional) The base URL if the input is relative.
Example: Using Global URL Class
const myUrl = new URL('https://www.example.com:8080/products/item?id=123&category=tech#details');
console.log(myUrl); // Returns the parsed URL object

Q. Important Properties of URL Object

  • href: The full constructed URL string.
  • hostname: Only the domain name (e.g., 'www.example.com').
  • port: Only the port number (e.g., '8080').
  • host: Hostname + Port (e.g., 'www.example.com:8080').
  • pathname: The path part of the URL (e.g., '/products/item').
  • search: The full query string including `?` (e.g., '?id=123').
  • searchParams: Returns a powerful URLSearchParams object to manage queries.
Example: Accessing Properties
const urlObj = new URL('https://vishal:pass123@www.domain.com:3000/api/users?status=active#top');

console.log(urlObj.hostname); // "www.domain.com"
console.log(urlObj.port);     // "3000"
console.log(urlObj.host);     // "www.domain.com:3000"
console.log(urlObj.pathname); // "/api/users"
console.log(urlObj.search);   // "?status=active"
console.log(urlObj.hash);     // "#top"
मराठी सारांश (URL Class आणि Properties):
  • Syntax: new URL(input, base). input = parse करायचा URL. base = input relative असेल तर त्याचा मूळ URL.
  • उदाहरण (base): new URL('/about', 'https://site.com') → https://site.com/about.
  • चुकीचा URL दिल्यास TypeError: Invalid URL येतो, म्हणून try/catch वापरा.
  • href: पूर्ण URL. hostname: फक्त domain. port: फक्त port. host: domain + port.
  • pathname: /api/users. search: ?status=active (raw string). hash: #top.
  • searchParams: query values सहज वाचायला / बदलायला मदत करणारा object.

Q. Working with Query Strings (URLSearchParams)

Concept:
  • The searchParams property returns a URLSearchParams object which acts like a Map for query parameters.
  • It makes reading, adding, or deleting ?key=value pairs extremely easy without manual string manipulation.
Important Methods of URLSearchParams:
  • 1. get(name): Returns the value of a specific query parameter.
    urlObj.searchParams.get('status') // "active"
  • 2. getAll(name): Returns an Array of all values for a parameter (useful when URLs have duplicate keys like ?tag=js&tag=node).
    urlObj.searchParams.getAll('tag') // ["js", "node"]
  • 3. has(name): Returns true if the parameter exists.
    urlObj.searchParams.has('status') // true
  • 4. set(name, val): Sets the value of a parameter (if it already exists, it overwrites it).
    urlObj.searchParams.set('status', 'inactive')
  • 5. append(name, val): Adds a new parameter. If it exists, it creates a duplicate.
    urlObj.searchParams.append('limit', '10')
  • 6. delete(name): Completely removes a parameter from the URL.
    urlObj.searchParams.delete('status')
Example: Modifying URLs Dynamically
const productUrl = new URL('https://amazon.com/search?category=shoes');

// 1. Reading values
console.log(productUrl.searchParams.get('category')); // "shoes"

// 2. Modifying the URL safely (no string concatenation!)
productUrl.searchParams.append('brand', 'nike');
productUrl.searchParams.set('category', 'sneakers'); // Overwrites "shoes"
productUrl.pathname = '/sale'; // Modifying the path directly

console.log(productUrl.href); 
// Output: 'https://amazon.com/sale?category=sneakers&brand=nike'
मराठी सारांश (URLSearchParams):
  • url.searchParams हा Map सारखा object आहे, जो ?key=value सहज हाताळतो. String तोडण्याची गरज नाही.
  • get(name): एक value. getAll(name): एकाच key च्या सगळ्या values (array).
  • has(name): key आहे का (true / false).
  • set(name, val): value बदला (असेल तर overwrite). append(name, val): नवीन जोडा (duplicate पण चालते).
  • delete(name): key काढून टाका.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: सगळ्या values string असतात. '10' number हवा असेल तर Number() करा.
  • उदाहरण: Amazon वर filter लावणे: category=shoes, brand=nike. हे सगळे query parameters आहेत.

Q. Legacy url Module vs Modern API

  • Previously, developers used const url = require('url'); and methods like url.parse().
  • url.parse() is now deprecated. Always use the global new URL() class instead.
  • However, the module is still useful for specific system path conversions:
    • url.pathToFileURL(path): Converts system path (C:\Users\file.txt) to a file:// URL.
    • url.fileURLToPath(url): Converts a file:// URL back to a system path.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. Do we need to require the URL module to parse URLs?
  • No. In modern Node.js, the WHATWG URL and URLSearchParams classes are globally available. You only need to require the `node:url` module for specific utility functions like fileURLToPath.
Q2. What is the difference between host and hostname?
  • hostname refers to just the domain (e.g., www.google.com).
  • host includes both the domain AND the port number (e.g., www.google.com:8080).
Q3. What is the difference between "Query Parameters", url.search, and url.searchParams?
  • Query Parameters: This is the universal, theoretical name for the ?key=value part of a URL.
  • url.search: A property that returns the exact raw string representation of the query parameters (e.g., "?id=123&category=tech"). You would have to manually split this string to extract the data.
  • url.searchParams: A property that returns a smart JavaScript Object (URLSearchParams). It parses the raw string automatically and provides helper methods (like .get('id')) to easily extract data without manual string manipulation.
मराठी सारांश (Legacy url आणि Interview):
  • जुनी पद्धत: require('url').parse(). आता deprecated आहे, वापरू नका.
  • नवीन: global new URL().
  • url module फक्त fileURLToPath() (file:// → system path) आणि pathToFileURL() (उलट) साठी लागतो. उदा. ESM मध्ये __dirname काढायला.
  • Q1: URL parse करायला require लागत नाही, कारण URL global आहे.
  • Q2 (host vs hostname): hostname = फक्त domain. host = domain + port.
  • Q3 (search vs searchParams): search = raw string ("?id=123"). searchParams = methods असलेला smart object (.get('id')).

Chapter 10.6: The HTTP Module

Q. What is the HTTP Module?

Concept:
  • The http module is a core built-in module in Node.js that allows Node.js to transfer data over the Hyper Text Transfer Protocol (HTTP).
  • It enables Node.js to act as an HTTP Server (listening for requests from clients like browsers) and as an HTTP Client (making requests to other servers).
  • It is the foundational building block for all web frameworks in Node.js, such as Express.js.
मराठी सारांश:
  • http module चा वापर करून आपण Node.js मध्ये स्वतःचा वेब सर्वर बनवू शकतो जो इंटरनेटवरून येणाऱ्या requests ला उत्तर देऊ शकतो.
  • HTTP = HyperText Transfer Protocol. Browser आणि server यांच्यात data पाठवण्याचे नियम.
  • दोन भूमिका: (1) HTTP Server: browser कडून requests घेणे (http.createServer). (2) HTTP Client: दुसऱ्या server ला request पाठवणे (http.get, http.request).
  • हा core module आहे, install करावा लागत नाही. Secure (SSL) साठी https module.
  • Express.js सारखे मोठे frameworks आतून याच http module चा वापर करतात.
  • उदाहरण: http module म्हणजे दुकानाचा counter. ग्राहक (browser) मागणी (request) करतो, दुकानदार (server) माल (response) देतो.
Figure 20: How HTTP Module handles Req and Res

Q. Essential Methods & Properties Breakdown

Figure 21: HTTP Methods and Properties Hierarchy
  • 1. Global http Module Methods
    • http.createServer((req, res) => {...}): Creates a new HTTP server. The callback runs every time a user makes a request to the server. Returns an http.Server instance.
    • server.listen(port, [host]): Starts the server so it actively listens for incoming connections on the specified port.
  • 2. The Request Object (req) - (Inherits from Readable Stream)
    • req.url: The route path and query string requested by the client (e.g., '/api/users?id=1').
    • req.method: The HTTP verb used (e.g., 'GET', 'POST', 'PUT', 'DELETE'). Used for routing logic.
    • req.headers: A JavaScript object containing all HTTP headers sent by the browser (like user-agent, authorization tokens, cookies).
    • req.on('data', chunk => {}): Since `req` is a readable stream, you must listen to the 'data' event to read the body of a POST request chunk by chunk.
  • 3. The Response Object (res) - (Inherits from Writable Stream)
    • res.statusCode: A property to manually set the status (e.g., res.statusCode = 404;).
    • res.setHeader(name, value): Adds a single HTTP header to the response (e.g., res.setHeader('Content-Type', 'text/html');).
    • res.writeHead(statusCode, [headers]): A powerful method that sets the status code AND multiple headers at the same time. (Better than using statusCode and setHeader separately).
    • res.write(chunk): Sends a chunk of the response body to the client. Can be called multiple times.
    • res.end([data]): The most critical method. It signals to the server that all headers and body chunks have been sent, and the response is complete. If you forget to call this, the browser will keep loading infinitely!
More Request / Response Details:
  • req.on('end', () => {}): Fires when the whole request body has been received. Parse the collected chunks here.
  • https module: Same API, but launches an SSL/TLS (secure) server. It needs a certificate and a key.
  • Real-world uses of the http module:
    • Building REST APIs without Express.
    • Creating custom HTTP clients (http.request, http.get).
    • Testing backend logic before adopting a framework.
    • Streaming data between services.
Code Example: Reading a POST Body (req.on('data') + req.on('end'))
const http = require('http');

http.createServer((req, res) => {
    if (req.method === 'POST' && req.url === '/users') {
        let body = '';
        req.on('data', (chunk) => body += chunk);   // collect pieces
        req.on('end', () => {                        // all data received
            const user = JSON.parse(body);
            res.writeHead(201, { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' });
            res.end(JSON.stringify({ message: 'User created', user }));
        });
    } else {
        res.writeHead(404);
        res.end('Not found');
    }
}).listen(3000);

Q. What is the writeHead() Method in Node.js?

Concept:
  • res.writeHead() sets the HTTP status code and response headers in one call, before the body is sent.
  • Headers are extra information (metadata) about the response: what type it is, whether to cache it, who can access it, and so on.
  • Simple example: the label on a parcel (headers) is written before putting things inside (body). The label tells the receiver what's inside and how to handle it.
  • Syntax: res.writeHead(statusCode[, statusMessage][, headers])
  • statusCode: A number, for example 200 (OK), 201 (Created), 404 (Not Found) or 500 (Server Error).
  • statusMessage (optional): Custom text, for example 'OK'.
  • headers (optional): An object such as { 'Content-Type': 'text/html' }.
  • Rules:
    • It must be called before res.write() / res.end().
    • It can be called only once per response. A second call throws an "headers already sent" error.
    • It merges with headers set earlier through res.setHeader() (values in writeHead win).
Diagram: HTTP Response Structure
Code Example: writeHead with Multiple Headers
res.writeHead(200, {
    'Content-Type': 'application/json',
    'Cache-Control': 'no-cache',
    'Access-Control-Allow-Origin': '*',
    'X-Content-Type-Options': 'nosniff'
});
res.end(JSON.stringify({ name: 'Vishal' }));

Q. Common HTTP Response Headers (List)

CategoryHeaderMeaning
Content-Type
(what format)		'Content-Type': 'text/html'HTML response
'Content-Type': 'text/plain'Plain text
'Content-Type': 'application/json'JSON
'Content-Type': 'application/xml'XML
'Content-Type': 'image/png'PNG image
'Content-Type': 'image/jpeg'JPEG image
Caching
(store copy?)		'Cache-Control': 'no-cache'Must re-check with the server before using a cached copy
'Cache-Control': 'public, max-age=3600'Cache for 1 hour
'Cache-Control': 'private, max-age=0, no-store'Never store (private data)
CORS
(who can call)		'Access-Control-Allow-Origin': '*'Allow all domains
'Access-Control-Allow-Origin': 'https://example.com'Allow one specific domain
'Access-Control-Allow-Methods': 'GET, POST, PUT, DELETE'Allowed HTTP methods
'Access-Control-Allow-Headers': 'Content-Type, Authorization'Allowed request headers
Security'Strict-Transport-Security': 'max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains'Force HTTPS
'X-Content-Type-Options': 'nosniff'Prevent MIME-type sniffing
'X-Frame-Options': 'DENY'Prevent clickjacking (no iframes)
'Content-Security-Policy': "default-src 'self'"Load content only from your own site
Authentication'WWW-Authenticate': 'Basic realm="Secure Area"'Response header: asks the client to log in (with 401)
'Authorization': 'Bearer <token>'Request header: the client sends its token (read via req.headers.authorization)
मराठी सारांश (writeHead आणि Headers):
  • res.writeHead(status, headers) एकाच वेळी status code आणि headers सेट करते. Body पाठवण्यापूर्वीच वापरायचे.
  • उदाहरण: Parcel वरचे label (headers) आधी लिहितो, मग आत सामान (body) टाकतो.
  • एका response मध्ये फक्त एकदाच वापरता येते.
  • Content-Type: data कोणत्या format मध्ये आहे (html, json, image).
  • Cache-Control: browser ने copy साठवावी का आणि किती वेळ.
  • CORS headers: कोणत्या websites ना आपल्या API ला call करू द्यायचे.
  • Security headers: HTTPS सक्ती, clickjacking आणि MIME sniffing पासून संरक्षण.
  • Authorization: हा request header आहे. Client token पाठवतो.
  • req.on('end'): POST body पूर्ण आल्यावर चालते.
Code Example: Building a Basic HTTP Server
const http = require('http');

// 1. Create the server
const server = http.createServer((req, res) => {
    
    // 2. Routing based on req.url
    if (req.url === '/') {
        res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'text/html' });
        res.write('<h1>Welcome to Home Page!</h1>');
        res.end(); // Must call .end() to finish the response
    } 
    else if (req.url === '/api/user') {
        res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' });
        res.end(JSON.stringify({ name: "Vishal", role: "Developer" }));
    } 
    else {
        // Handle 404 Not Found
        res.writeHead(404, { 'Content-Type': 'text/plain' });
        res.end('404: Page Not Found');
    }
});

// 3. Start listening on Port 3000
server.listen(3000, () => {
    console.log("Server is up and running on http://localhost:3000");
});
मराठी सारांश (Methods & Flow):
  • http.createServer() वापरून सर्वर बनतो. हा सर्वर प्रत्येक वेळी नवीन request आल्यावर आतले function चालवतो.
  • req.url मध्ये युजरने कोणता रस्ता (path) मागितला आहे ते समजते (उदा. '/about').
  • res.writeHead() ने आपण status code पाठवतो (जसे 200 म्हणजे OK, 404 म्हणजे Not Found) आणि res.end() कॉल करणे अनिवार्य असते, नाहीतर ब्राउझर लोड होतच राहतो (hang होतो).
  • शेवटी server.listen(port) वापरून सर्वर सुरु (start) केला जातो.

Q. What is Routing in Node.js?

Definition:
  • Routing is the process of determining what the server should do when a client makes a request to a specific URL (Endpoint) using a specific HTTP Method (GET, POST, etc.).
  • In Simple Words: Routing is like a traffic policeman for your server. When a user asks for a specific page (like /home or /contact), routing catches that request and directs it to the correct block of code to send the right data back.
Core Concepts of Routing:
  • 1. The Endpoint (URL Path):
    • This is the specific path requested by the client after the domain name.
    • Example: In http://localhost:3000/api/users, the endpoint is /api/users.
    • In Node.js, we extract this using the req.url property.
  • 2. The HTTP Method (Action):
    • This defines the intention or action the client wants to perform on that endpoint.
    • GET: Used to retrieve or fetch data (e.g., loading a webpage or reading JSON).
    • POST: Used to send or submit new data to the server (e.g., submitting a login form).
    • PUT / PATCH: Used to modify or update existing data on the server.
    • DELETE: Used to remove data from the server.
    • In Node.js, we extract this using the req.method property.
  • 3. Manual Routing in Core Node.js:
    • Unlike frameworks like Express.js (which have built-in routing functions), pure Node.js requires manual routing.
    • You must manually write `if-else` or `switch` conditional statements inside the http.createServer callback to check if `req.url` matches a specific string.
  • 4. The 404 Fallback Route:
    • Always include a default `else` block at the end of your routing logic.
    • This acts as a "catch-all" to send a 404 Not Found error if the client requests an endpoint that you haven't defined.
मराठी सारांश (Routing):
  • Routing म्हणजे काय? युजरने कोणती URL (path) मागितली आहे आणि कोणता Method (GET/POST) वापरला आहे हे तपासून त्याला योग्य तोच page किंवा data परत देणे याला Routing म्हणतात.
  • Core Node.js मध्ये Routing: Node.js मध्ये आपल्याला req.url (उदा. '/home') आणि req.method (उदा. 'GET') तपासून if-else conditions द्वारे मॅन्युअली Routing करावे लागते.
  • 404 Route: जर युजरने चुकीची URL टाकली (जी आपल्या code मध्ये नाही), तर त्याला "404 Page Not Found" एरर देणे खूप महत्वाचे असते.
  • एका page साठी अनेक paths: if (req.url === '/' || req.url === '/home'). दोन्ही URLs वर तेच page.
  • Query string ची अडचण: req.url मध्ये ?id=5 पण येतो ('/users?id=5'), म्हणून === '/users' जुळत नाही. new URL(req.url, 'http://localhost').pathname वापरून फक्त path काढा.
  • उदाहरण: Routing म्हणजे traffic police. येणाऱ्या गाडीला (request) तिच्या पत्त्यानुसार (URL + method) योग्य रस्त्याला (code block) पाठवतो.
  • Tip — multiple paths, one page: if (req.url === '/' || req.url === '/home') { ... }
  • Tip — ignore the query string when routing: req.url includes it (e.g. '/users?id=5'). Use const { pathname, searchParams } = new URL(req.url, 'http://localhost'); and route on pathname.
  • In Express, all of this is handled for you by app.get(path, handler) (see Chapter 15).
Code Example: Advanced Routing (Handling Methods and Paths)
const http = require('http');

const server = http.createServer((req, res) => {
    // Extract url and method
    const path = req.url;
    const method = req.method;

    // Route 1: Home Page (GET /)
    if (path === '/' && method === 'GET') {
        res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'text/html' });
        res.end('<h1>Welcome to the Home Page</h1>');
    } 
    // Route 2: About Page (GET /about)
    else if (path === '/about' && method === 'GET') {
        res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'text/html' });
        res.end('<h1>About Us</h1>');
    } 
    // Route 3: API Endpoint (GET /api/users)
    else if (path === '/api/users' && method === 'GET') {
        res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' });
        res.end(JSON.stringify([{ id: 1, name: 'Vishal' }, { id: 2, name: 'Admin' }]));
    }
    // Route 4: API Endpoint (POST /api/users) -> Receiving Data
    else if (path === '/api/users' && method === 'POST') {
        // Logic to handle incoming data would go here
        res.writeHead(201, { 'Content-Type': 'text/plain' });
        res.end('New user created successfully!');
    }
    // Route 5: 404 Fallback
    else {
        res.writeHead(404, { 'Content-Type': 'text/plain' });
        res.end('404 Error: The requested route does not exist.');
    }
});

server.listen(3000, () => {
    console.log("Server listening on port 3000...");
});

Q. How to Read Request Body (POST Data) in HTTP?

Concept:
  • When a client sends data to the server (e.g., submitting a form via POST method), the data is not available immediately on the req object.
  • Because Node.js is non-blocking, it receives this data in small parts called Chunks (Streams).
  • To read this data, we must listen to the 'data' and 'end' events on the req object (which is a Readable Stream).
Step-by-Step Process & All Available Events (Syntax & Arguments):
  • 1. The 'data' event (Receiving Chunks): req.on('data', (chunk) => { ... })
    • When is it fired? Automatically fired by Node.js every time a new chunk (piece) of the request body arrives over the network.
    • Callback Argument (chunk): This is a Buffer object representing the raw binary data of that specific piece. It is NOT a readable string yet.
    • What we do: Since data comes in pieces, we cannot process it immediately. We create an empty array (e.g., const bodyChunks = []) outside the event and continuously use bodyChunks.push(chunk) to collect all the pieces.
  • 2. The 'end' event (Finished Receiving): req.on('end', () => { ... })
    • When is it fired? Fired exactly once when the entire request body has been successfully received and there are no more chunks left.
    • Callback Argument: None (it does not receive any data).
    • What we do: Now that we have all the pieces in our array, we must join them. We use Buffer.concat(bodyChunks) to merge all small buffers into one large Buffer. Then, we use .toString() to convert that final Buffer into a readable text/string.
  • 3. The 'error' event (Handling Failures): req.on('error', (err) => { ... })
    • When is it fired? Fired immediately if something goes wrong during the data transmission (e.g., the user's internet disconnects halfway through the upload).
    • Callback Argument (err): An Error object containing the details of what went wrong.
    • What we do: We log the error and send a 500 Internal Server Error response back so the application doesn't crash or hang.
  • 4. The 'close' event (Unexpected Termination): req.on('close', () => { ... })
    • When is it fired? Fired when the underlying connection was closed or aborted unexpectedly before the entire request was finished.
    • What we do: Generally used for cleanup tasks (like stopping a heavy database query if the user impatiently closed their browser window).
मराठी सारांश (Reading Request Body):
  • जेव्हा युजर फॉर्म भरून (POST request) डेटा पाठवतो, तेव्हा तो डेटा सर्वरला एकाच वेळी पूर्ण मिळत नाही.
  • तो डेटा छोट्या-छोट्या तुकड्यांमध्ये (Chunks) येतो कारण Node.js Streams वापरतो.
  • हा डेटा गोळा करण्यासाठी आपल्याला req.on('data', ...) वापरून येणारे तुकडे (chunks) एका Array मध्ये साठवावे लागतात.
  • आणि जेव्हा सगळा डेटा येऊन संपतो, तेव्हा req.on('end', ...) वापरून त्या सगळ्या तुकड्यांना जोडून (Buffer.concat) मूळ माहिती (String) तयार केली जाते.
Code Example: Reading POST Data
const http = require('http');

const server = http.createServer((req, res) => {
    // Only handle POST request for '/submit'
    if (req.url === '/submit' && req.method === 'POST') {
        const bodyChunks = []; // Array to store incoming chunks

        // 1. Listen for data chunks
        req.on('data', (chunk) => {
            console.log("Received a chunk of data...");
            bodyChunks.push(chunk); // Store raw buffer chunk
        });

        // 2. Listen for the end of the request
        req.on('end', () => {
            // Combine all chunks into one Buffer, then convert to readable String
            const parsedBody = Buffer.concat(bodyChunks).toString();
            
            console.log("Complete Data Received: ", parsedBody);
            
            // Send response back
            res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'text/plain' });
            res.end('Data received successfully!');
        });
    } else {
        res.writeHead(404, { 'Content-Type': 'text/plain' });
        res.end('Not Found');
    }
});

server.listen(3000, () => console.log("Server running..."));

Q. How to Send a Response back to the Client?

Concept:
  • After processing a request (like saving data to a database), the server MUST send a response back to the client.
  • If the server does not send a response, the user's browser will keep spinning and eventually show a "Connection Timeout" error.
  • The res object (which is a Writable Stream) is used to send this response in three distinct steps: Headers, Body, and Finish.
Step-by-Step Process (Methods to Send Response):
  • 1. Set Headers & Status: res.writeHead(statusCode, headers)
    • Always send the status code (e.g., 200 for Success, 404 for Not Found) and the Content-Type (like 'text/html' or 'application/json') first.
    • Example: res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' });
  • 2. Send the Body (Optional): res.write(data)
    • Used to send the actual content (HTML string, JSON string, or Buffer chunks).
    • Because it is a stream, you can call res.write() multiple times to send data in pieces.
  • 3. End the Response (Mandatory): res.end([data])
    • This is the most critical step. It tells Node.js that the response is complete and the connection can be closed.
    • You can also pass the final data directly inside .end(data) instead of using .write(), which is the most common approach.
    • Example: res.end(JSON.stringify({ status: "success" }));
मराठी सारांश (Sending Response):
  • कोणतीही request आल्यावर सर्वरला उत्तर (Response) देणे बंधनकारक असते, नाहीतर ब्राउझर hang होतो.
  • सर्वात आधी res.writeHead() वापरून status (उदा. 200 OK) आणि माहितीचा प्रकार (JSON किंवा HTML) सांगितला जातो.
  • त्यानंतर res.write() वापरून माहिती पाठवली जाते.
  • आणि शेवटी res.end() कॉल करणे अनिवार्य आहे, ज्याने सर्वरला समजते की उत्तर पूर्ण झाले आहे. आपण थेट res.end("Hello") असेही वापरू शकतो.

Q. How to Parse a JSON Request Body & Send a JSON Response?

1. Parsing Incoming JSON Data (Request)
  • When the client (like Postman or a React frontend) sends a JSON object in a POST request, the data arrives as Buffer chunks.
  • After combining the chunks and converting them to a string using .toString(), you simply get a plain text string that looks like JSON (e.g., '{"name":"Vishal"}').
  • To actually use this data as a JavaScript Object, you MUST parse it using the built-in JSON.parse() method.
  • Syntax: const jsObject = JSON.parse(parsedBodyString);
  • Warning: If the client sends invalid JSON, JSON.parse() will throw a fatal error and crash the server. Always wrap it inside a try...catch block!
2. Sending a JSON Response
  • HTTP can only transmit text or binary data. It cannot transmit native JavaScript objects directly over the network.
  • To send an object or an array back to the client, you MUST convert it into a string using the built-in JSON.stringify() method.
  • You must also set the 'Content-Type' header to 'application/json' so the receiving client knows how to handle the string correctly.
  • Syntax: res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }); res.end(JSON.stringify(myObject));
मराठी सारांश (JSON Handling):
  • जेव्हा क्लायंट JSON मध्ये डेटा पाठवतो, तेव्हा तो सर्वरला String च्या रूपात मिळतो. त्याला खऱ्या JavaScript Object मध्ये बदलण्यासाठी आपण JSON.parse() वापरतो.
  • तसेच, जेव्हा सर्वरला उत्तर (Response) म्हणून एखादा Object किंवा Array पाठवायचा असतो, तेव्हा आपण तो थेट पाठवू शकत नाही. आपल्याला JSON.stringify() वापरून त्याला आधी String मध्ये बदलावे लागते.
  • नेहमी लक्षात ठेवा की JSON response पाठवताना Content-Type: application/json हा हेडर पाठवणे अनिवार्य आहे.
  • Body वाचणे: req.on('data') मध्ये chunks जोडा, req.on('end') मध्ये JSON.parse(body).
  • Error handling: चुकीचा JSON आल्यास JSON.parse error देतो. try/catch वापरून 400 Bad Request पाठवा.
  • Status code: नवीन data बनला तर 201, नाहीतर 200.
  • Express मध्ये हे सगळे express.json() आणि res.json() आपोआप करतात.
  • उदाहरण: पत्र (string) उघडून वाचणे (parse), उत्तर लिहून पाकिटात घालणे (stringify).
Code Example: Parsing and Sending JSON API
const http = require('http');

const server = http.createServer((req, res) => {
    if (req.url === '/api/login' && req.method === 'POST') {
        const bodyChunks = [];
        
        req.on('data', chunk => bodyChunks.push(chunk));
        
        req.on('end', () => {
            const rawString = Buffer.concat(bodyChunks).toString(); // Output: '{"username":"admin"}'
            
            try {
                // 1. Parsing the Request Body (String to Object)
                const userData = JSON.parse(rawString); 
                console.log(userData.username); // Now we can access properties
                
                // 2. Sending a JSON Response (Object to String)
                const responseData = { success: true, message: `Welcome ${userData.username}!` };
                
                res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' });
                res.end(JSON.stringify(responseData)); // Must stringify the object!
                
            } catch (error) {
                // Handle invalid JSON to prevent server crash
                res.writeHead(400, { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' });
                res.end(JSON.stringify({ success: false, message: "Invalid JSON format" }));
            }
        });
    }
});

server.listen(3000, () => console.log("JSON API Server running..."));

Chapter 11: The Events Module

Q. What is the Events Module in Node.js?

Concept:
  • The events module is a built-in Node.js module that facilitates the event-driven architecture.
  • It provides the EventEmitter class, which allows you to create, fire (emit), and listen to custom events.
  • Node.js is heavily event-driven. Many of Node's built-in modules (like fs, http, stream) are built directly on top of the EventEmitter architecture.
  • To use it, you import the class and create an instance: const { EventEmitter } = require('node:events');
मराठी सारांश:
  • Node.js मधील सगळी कामे इव्हेंट्स (Events) च्या आधारावर चालतात.
  • events module आपल्याला स्वतःचे इव्हेंट्स बनवायला (emit) आणि त्यांना ऐकायला (listen) मदत करते.
  • या module मधला मुख्य भाग म्हणजे EventEmitter class.
  • fs, http, stream सारखे core modules आतून EventEmitter वरच बनलेले आहेत. म्हणून server.on('request') सारखे लिहिता येते.
  • Import: const { EventEmitter } = require('node:events'), मग const e = new EventEmitter().
  • उदाहरण: शाळेची घंटा (emit). घंटा वाजली की विद्यार्थी (listeners) वर्गात जातात.

Q. What are Events in Node.js?

Concept:
  • An event is a named signal that something has happened in your application.
  • Examples: a request was received, a file finished reading, or a database sent a response.
  • In Node.js, an event has two parts:
    • Event name: a string, for example 'data' or 'orderPlaced'.
    • Event payload: the data sent with it, for example an order ID.
  • Events are the core of Node.js’s event-driven architecture and are used to handle async operations efficiently.
  • Simple example: a doorbell. The bell ringing is the event, and you opening the door is the reaction (the listener).
Types of Occurrences Events Represent:
  • Completion of an async operation (file reading completed).
  • An HTTP request received.
  • Data available from a stream.
  • A custom user-defined event triggered ('userLogin').
Diagram: Emitter → Event → Listeners

Q. How Do Events Work in Node.js? (Components)

  • Node.js follows an event-driven architecture: emitter objects trigger named events, and registered listeners (callbacks) handle them.
  • The built-in events module provides the EventEmitter class, which creates events, emits events and listens to events.
  • Components:
    • Event Source / Emitter: The object that emits events (an instance of EventEmitter).
    • Event Listener (Handler): The function that runs when the event occurs.
    • emit(): Triggers an event.
    • on(): Registers a listener that runs every time.
    • once(): Registers a listener that runs only once, then removes itself.
    • off() / removeListener(): Removes a specific listener.
    • removeAllListeners(): Removes all listeners for an event.
    • setMaxListeners(n): Sets the maximum number of listeners (default 10).
    • listenerCount(event): Returns how many listeners an event has.

Q. Common Events in Node.js

ModuleEventsWhen Triggered
fs (fs.watch)'change', 'rename'A file is modified or renamed.
http'request', 'connection', 'close'An HTTP request is received, or a connection opens or closes.
stream'data', 'end', 'error'Reading or writing data chunks.
net / socket.io'connect', 'message', 'disconnect'Real-time communication.
Custom'userLogin', 'orderPlaced'User-defined events through EventEmitter.
Code Example: Built-in Events (Stream + HTTP)
const fs = require('fs');
const http = require('http');

// Stream events
const stream = fs.createReadStream('big.txt', 'utf8');
stream.on('data', (chunk) => console.log('Got chunk of', chunk.length));
stream.on('end', () => console.log('Finished reading'));
stream.on('error', (err) => console.error('Error:', err.message));

// HTTP 'request' event
const server = http.createServer();
server.on('request', (req, res) => res.end('Hello!'));
server.listen(3000);

Q. When to Use EventEmitter?

  • User actions: Handle custom actions such as login, logout or signup.
  • System logging: Emit logs for errors and warnings.
  • Inter-module communication: Pass messages between modules in large apps without tight coupling.
  • Real-time updates: Chat apps, notifications, streaming systems.
Code Example: Inter-Module Communication (User Signup)
const EventEmitter = require('events');
const appEvents = new EventEmitter();

// Email module listens
appEvents.on('userRegistered', (user) => console.log(`📧 Welcome email sent to ${user.email}`));
// Logger module listens
appEvents.on('userRegistered', (user) => console.log(`📝 Log: new user ${user.name}`));

// Signup module just emits — it doesn't know who is listening
appEvents.emit('userRegistered', { name: 'Vishal', email: 'vishal@test.com' });

Q. What is an Event Handler Function?

Concept:
  • An event handler is a callback function that runs automatically when a specific event occurs.
  • It is the "response" part: it tells the program what to do when the event happens.
  • It is also called an event listener.
How It Works:
  1. A handler is registered with .on() or .once().
  2. The event is emitted (triggered) with .emit().
  3. The emitter finds all handlers for that event name.
  4. Each handler runs, in the order it was registered, and receives the emitted data.
Code Example: Event Handler
const EventEmitter = require('events');
const emitter = new EventEmitter();

// This named function is the EVENT HANDLER
function greetHandler(name) {
    console.log(`Hello, ${name}!`);
}

emitter.on('greet', greetHandler);   // register handler
emitter.emit('greet', 'Vishal');     // trigger → Output: Hello, Vishal!
In Short:
  • Events are signals that something happened, such as a finished I/O operation or a user request.
  • An EventEmitter emits named events, and listeners (event handlers) respond to them.
  • The events module enables this, making Node.js non-blocking and highly scalable.
मराठी सारांश (Events, Common Events, Event Handler):
  • Event म्हणजे काय? काहीतरी घडले याचे नाव असलेले signal. उदा. request आली, file वाचून झाली.
  • उदाहरण: Doorbell वाजणे = event. दार उघडणे = listener (handler).
  • Event ला नाव ('data') आणि data (payload) असतो.
  • Components: Emitter (event fire करणारा), Listener (ऐकणारा), emit(), on(), once(), off(), removeAllListeners(), setMaxListeners() (default 10).
  • Common events: fs → change / rename, http → request / close, stream → data / end / error, socket → connect / message / disconnect.
  • EventEmitter कधी वापरायचा? Login / logout सारख्या user actions, logging, modules मध्ये संवाद, real-time chat / notifications.
  • Event Handler: Event घडल्यावर आपोआप चालणारे callback function. .on() ने register करतो, .emit() ने trigger करतो.

Q. What is an EventEmitter, Uses & Advantages?

  • What is it? An EventEmitter is simply an object that sends out a signal (event) when something happens. Other parts of your code can "listen" for that signal and execute a callback function in response.
  • Uses:
    • Handling HTTP requests (Server emits a 'request' event).
    • Reading/Writing files (Streams emit 'data' and 'end' events).
    • Building real-time chat applications or notification systems.
  • Advantages:
    • Decoupling: The sender (emitter) doesn't need to know anything about the receiver (listener). This makes the code highly modular.
    • Asynchronous Flow: Perfect for non-blocking I/O tasks. You don't wait for a task to finish; you just listen for an event to fire when it finishes.
    • Multiple Listeners: A single event can trigger multiple independent actions across different parts of the application.
मराठी सारांश (EventEmitter, Uses आणि Advantages):
  • EventEmitter म्हणजे काय? काहीतरी घडले की signal (event) पाठवणारा object. Code चे इतर भाग ते signal ऐकून त्यांचे function चालवतात.
  • Uses: HTTP requests ('request'), files / streams ('data', 'end'), chat apps, notifications.
  • Decoupling: event पाठवणाऱ्याला कोण ऐकत आहे हे माहीत नसते. Code modular राहतो.
  • Async flow: काम संपण्याची वाट पाहत नाही, event आल्यावर react करतो.
  • Multiple listeners: एका event वर अनेक कामे. उदा. order आली → email + stock update + SMS.

Q. EventEmitter and the Publish–Subscribe (Pub/Sub) Pattern

Concept:
  • The EventEmitter class is Node.js's implementation of the Observer / Publish–Subscribe (Pub/Sub) design pattern.
  • Publisher (Emitter): The object that generates and fires an event using .emit().
  • Subscriber (Listener): The callback registered for an event with .on() / .once(), which reacts when the event fires.
  • The events module is not just for custom events. It is the backbone of Node.js itself: HTTP, FS, Streams and Net all extend (inherit from) EventEmitter.
Diagram: EventEmitter — Publish/Subscribe Architecture
  • Point 1 — Loose coupling: The publisher and subscribers don't know about each other. The publisher fires an event by name, and any number of subscribers registered for that name react. This keeps code modular, scalable and maintainable.
  • Point 2 — Why core modules inherit EventEmitter:
    • HTTP Server: emits 'request' for every incoming request.
    • Readable Streams: emit 'data' as chunks arrive and 'end' when done.
    • fs: emits events when file operations (e.g. fs.watch, streams) progress or complete.
    • Because they inherit EventEmitter, you can call .on() directly on them.
  • Point 3 — emit() is synchronous: .emit() calls all listeners immediately and in order. The "async" part comes from when the event is emitted (for example, after I/O finishes).
In Short (EventEmitter):
  • EventEmitter is a core Node.js class from the events module, used to create and handle custom events.
  • It is the foundation of Node's event-driven architecture: we emit events and attach listeners that respond to them.
  • With .on(), .once() and .emit(), apps react to events instead of waiting for them, which makes Node.js scalable, efficient and non-blocking.
  • Most core modules (HTTP, Streams, FS) use EventEmitter internally.
⚡ Short Interview Answer — Q. What is EventEmitter in Node.js?
  • EventEmitter is a class from Node.js's built-in events module that implements the Publish–Subscribe pattern.
  • Objects emit named events with .emit(), and listener functions registered with .on() react to them.
  • Core modules like HTTP, FS and Streams inherit from EventEmitter, which makes them event-driven by nature.
  • Key methods: .on() (permanent listener), .once() (one-time listener), .emit() (fire event), .off() (remove listener).
मराठी सारांश (Pub/Sub Pattern):
  • EventEmitter हा Publish–Subscribe (Pub/Sub) design pattern वर आधारित आहे.
  • Publisher (Emitter): event fire करतो (.emit()). उदा. "order आला!"
  • Subscriber (Listener): event ऐकून react करतो (.on()). उदा. "Kitchen, order तयार कर!"
  • Loose coupling: दोघांना एकमेकांबद्दल माहिती नसते. फक्त event चे नाव common.
  • HTTP Server, File System, Streams हे सगळे EventEmitter extend करतात. म्हणून server.on('request', ...) लिहिता येते.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: emit() listeners ना लगेच (synchronously) आणि क्रमाने call करतो.
  • उदाहरण: YouTube channel (publisher) video टाकतो, subscribers ना notification जाते. Channel ला प्रत्येक subscriber माहीत असण्याची गरज नसते.

Q. Key Concepts: Listeners and Emitters

  • 1. Listening (.on): You can attach event handlers using the .on('eventName', callback) method. The callback executes whenever that specific event is fired.
  • 2. Emitting (.emit): You use .emit('eventName') to trigger the event. You can also pass data (arguments) to the event handlers.
  • 3. Multiple Callbacks: You can register multiple .on() listeners for the same single event. When emitted, all of them will run in the order they were registered.
मराठी सारांश:
  • .on() चा वापर करून आपण इव्हेंट ऐकतो (Listen) आणि .emit() चा वापर करून आपण इव्हेंट फायर (Trigger) करतो.
  • .emit('name', data) ने listener ला data (arguments) पण पाठवता येतो.
  • आपण एकाच इव्हेंटसाठी अनेक कामे सेट करू शकतो. सगळे listeners register केलेल्या क्रमाने चालतात.
  • महत्वाचे: listener आधी (.on) लावावा, मग .emit. नाहीतर event वाया जातो.
  • उदाहरण: radio station (emit) प्रसारण करते, ज्यांनी तो channel लावला (on) तेच ऐकतात.
Figure 18: How EventEmitter works

Q. Essential EventEmitter Methods

1. Syntax: emitter.on(eventName, listener)
  • eventName: The name of the event (string).
  • listener: The callback function to execute.
  • Usage: Adds a listener. The callback runs every time the event is emitted.

2. Syntax: emitter.emit(eventName, [...args])
  • eventName: The name of the event to fire.
  • ...args: (Optional) Data to pass to the listener functions.
  • Usage: Synchronously calls all registered listeners for the event.

3. Syntax: emitter.once(eventName, listener)
  • Usage: Adds a one-time listener. It is called only the first time the event is emitted, and then automatically unregisters itself.

4. Syntax: emitter.off(eventName, listener) or removeListener
  • Usage: Removes a specific listener from the event array.

5. Syntax: emitter.removeAllListeners([eventName])
  • Usage: Removes all listeners for a specific event, or all listeners for all events if no name is provided.
Code Example: Basic Emitter Methods
const { EventEmitter } = require('events');
const myEmitter = new EventEmitter();

// 1. Registering multiple listeners (.on)
myEmitter.on('userJoined', (username, id) => {
    console.log(`Welcome ${username}! Your ID is ${id}`);
});
myEmitter.on('userJoined', () => {
    console.log(`Updating database for new user...`);
});

// 2. Registering a one-time listener (.once)
myEmitter.once('firstLogin', () => {
    console.log('Sending welcome email (Will only happen once!)...');
});

// 3. Emitting the events (.emit)
myEmitter.emit('firstLogin'); // Output: Sending welcome email...
myEmitter.emit('firstLogin'); // Ignored! (.once already ran)

myEmitter.emit('userJoined', 'Vishal', 101); // Output: Welcome Vishal! + Updating database...
मराठी सारांश (EventEmitter Methods):
  • on(eventName, listener): listener जोडतो. Event प्रत्येक वेळी आला की चालतो.
  • emit(eventName, ...args): event fire करतो. सगळे listeners synchronously, क्रमाने चालतात. Listener असल्यास true, नसल्यास false return करतो.
  • once(eventName, listener): फक्त पहिल्यांदाच चालतो, नंतर आपोआप निघून जातो. उदा. welcome email.
  • off / removeListener(eventName, listener): एक ठराविक listener काढतो. तोच function reference द्यावा लागतो.
  • removeAllListeners([eventName]): त्या event चे सगळे listeners काढतो. नाव न दिल्यास सगळ्या events चे.
  • listenerCount(eventName): त्या event साठी किती listeners लावले आहेत ते सांगतो.
6. Syntax: emitter.listenerCount(eventName)
  • Usage: Returns the number of listeners currently registered for that event.
Important details:
  • emit() returns true if the event had listeners, false if none were registered.
  • To remove a listener with off(), you must pass the exact same function reference used with on(). A new arrow function will not match.
Code Example: .off() — Removing a Listener
const { EventEmitter } = require('events');
const myEmitter = new EventEmitter();

// ⚠️ Use a NAMED function so you can remove it later
function onData(data) {
    console.log('Data received:', data);
}

myEmitter.on('data', onData);
console.log(myEmitter.listenerCount('data'));   // 1

myEmitter.emit('data', 'chunk 1');              // → "Data received: chunk 1"

myEmitter.off('data', onData);                  // remove the listener
console.log(myEmitter.emit('data', 'chunk 2')); // false → no listeners, nothing printed

Q. PRO TIP 1: The special 'error' Event (Crash Risk!)

  • In Node.js, the 'error' event is treated specially.
  • If an EventEmitter emits an 'error' event, and no listener is registered to handle it, the Node.js process will print the stack trace and CRASH (exit).
  • Best Practice: ALWAYS register an .on('error', (err) => {...}) listener on your emitters to prevent your server from crashing.
मराठी सारांश (Important Error Handling):
  • Node.js मध्ये 'error' नावाचा इव्हेंट खूप खास (Special) असतो.
  • जर आपण 'error' नावाचा इव्हेंट फायर (emit) केला, आणि त्याला पकडण्यासाठी (listen) कोणीच नसेल (म्हणजेच .on('error', ...) लिहिलं नसेल), तर Node.js ला वाटतं की काहीतरी गंभीर चूक झाली आहे.
  • यामुळे पूर्ण Node.js सर्वर क्रॅश होतो आणि प्रोग्राम तिथेच थांबतो (Crash & Exit).
  • Best Practice: सर्व्हर क्रॅश होण्यापासून वाचवण्यासाठी नेहमी प्रत्येक Emitter वर .on('error', (err) => {...}) लिहून एरर हँडल करावी.

Q. PRO TIP 2: Extending EventEmitter (Real-World Use)

  • In large applications, we rarely create empty new EventEmitter() instances.
  • Instead, custom classes extend the EventEmitter class. This gives the custom class the ability to emit its own internal events.
  • This is exactly how Node.js core modules work (e.g., the http.Server class extends EventEmitter, which is why we can do server.on('request', ...)).
Code Example: Extending EventEmitter
const { EventEmitter } = require('events');

// Our custom class gets all features of EventEmitter!
class MyServer extends EventEmitter {
    start() {
        console.log("Server is starting...");
        // Emitting an event from inside the class
        this.emit('started', { port: 8080 });
    }
}

const server = new MyServer();

// Listening to the custom event
server.on('started', (data) => {
    console.log(`Successfully running on port ${data.port}`);
});

server.start();
मराठी सारांश (EventEmitter Extend करणे):
  • मोठ्या apps मध्ये रिकामा new EventEmitter() कमी वापरतात. त्याऐवजी आपला class extends EventEmitter करतो.
  • त्यामुळे आपल्या class ला .on(), .emit() सारख्या सगळ्या सुविधा मिळतात, आणि class आतून this.emit() करू शकतो.
  • Node.js core असेच काम करते. http.Server हा EventEmitter extend करतो, म्हणून server.on('request') चालते.
  • उदाहरण: MyServer सुरू झाला की 'started' event देतो. बाहेरचा code तो ऐकून "running on port 8080" छापतो.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What happens if you trigger `.emit()` before setting `.on()`?
  • Nothing happens. The event is fired, but because there are no listeners registered yet, the event is completely ignored. Listeners must be set before the event is emitted.
Q2. What is the difference between `.on()` and `.once()`?
  • .on() will execute its callback every time the event is fired, no matter how many times.
  • .once() will execute its callback only the first time the event is fired, and then it automatically unregisters itself.
Q3. What happens if an EventEmitter throws an 'error' event without a listener?
  • It throws an unhandled exception and crashes the entire Node.js process. Always attach an .on('error') listener to prevent crashes.
Q4. What happens if multiple listeners are attached to the same event? Is there a limit?
  • When the event is emitted, all attached listeners will execute synchronously in the exact order they were registered.
  • Limit Warning: By default, Node.js allows a maximum of 10 listeners per event to prevent memory leaks.
  • If you add more than 10, Node.js will print a MaxListenersExceededWarning in the console.
  • Fix: You can increase this limit using emitter.setMaxListeners(n).
मराठी सारांश (Interview Questions Breakdown):
  • Q. .on() च्या आधी .emit() कॉल केला तर काय होईल?
    उत्तर: काहीच होणार नाही (No Error, No Output). इव्हेंट फायर होतो पण ऐकण्यासाठी (listener) कोणीच नसल्यामुळे तो इव्हेंट इग्नोर (वाया) जातो. म्हणून नेहमी आधी .on() लिहा आणि नंतरच .emit() करा.
  • Q. .on() आणि .once() मधला फरक काय?
    उत्तर: .on() वापरला तर इव्हेंट जितक्या वेळा फायर होईल, तितक्या वेळा कोड चालेल. पण .once() वापरला तर कोड फक्त पहिल्यांदाच चालेल आणि नंतर तो आपोआप बंद (unregister) होईल.
  • Q. 'error' इव्हेंट हँडल नाही केला तर काय होईल?
    उत्तर: जर आपण error हँडल केला नाही (listener नसेल), तर Node.js सर्व्हर क्रॅश होईल आणि संपूर्ण ॲप बंद पडेल.
  • Q. एकाच इव्हेंटला एकापेक्षा जास्त Listeners लावले तर काय होईल?
    उत्तर: सर्व Listeners क्रमाने (synchronously) चालतील, ज्या क्रमाने ते लिहिले आहेत. Node.js मध्ये एका इव्हेंटसाठी जास्तीत जास्त 10 Listeners ची limit असते. जर 10 पेक्षा जास्त लावले, तर "Memory Leak Warning" येते. ही limit वाढवण्यासाठी setMaxListeners(n) वापरतात.

Quick Revision Summary

Method Description
.on(event, cb) Listens continuously for an event.
.emit(event, args) Fires/triggers the event and passes data.
.once(event, cb) Listens for an event exactly one time.
.off(event, cb) Removes a specific listener.
extends EventEmitter Object-oriented way to make custom classes event-driven.
'error' Event Crashes Node.js if not listened to.

Chapter 12: The Stream Module

Q. What is a Stream in Node.js?

Concept:
  • A Stream is an abstract interface and a fundamental concept in Node.js that lets you read data from a source or write data to a destination in a continuous, chunk-by-chunk fashion.
  • Ongoing Process: Think of a stream as an ongoing process where we have to read (or write) data chunk by chunk as it comes in, rather than waiting for the entire dataset to load into memory.
  • This means you process data piece by piece as soon as it becomes available.
  • Important: All streams in Node.js are instances of the EventEmitter class. This means they broadcast events (like 'data', 'end', and 'error') which we can listen to.
  • Analogy: Streaming is exactly like watching a video on YouTube. You watch it in real-time as small parts (buffer chunks) arrive, instead of waiting to download the entire 2GB video file before you can press play.
  • Why is it used? Streams are heavily used in Node.js for reading/writing huge files, handling HTTP requests/responses, and processing network sockets without crashing the server due to RAM limits.
मराठी सारांश (Detailed):
