  • Stream म्हणजे काय? Stream म्हणजे असा मार्ग ज्यातून डेटा पूर्णपणे एकाच वेळी न येता, छोट्या-छोट्या तुकड्यांमध्ये (Chunks) सतत वाहत असतो.
  • फायदा (Advantage): जर तुम्हाला 2GB ची फाईल वाचायची असेल, तर Node.js ती पूर्ण फाईल मेमरी (RAM) मध्ये लोड करत नाही. त्याऐवजी, तो थोडा-थोडा डेटा वाचतो आणि प्रोसेस करतो. यामुळे कितीही मोठी फाईल असली तरी मेमरी (RAM) वाचते आणि सर्वर क्रॅश होत नाही (Highly Memory Efficient).
  • उदाहरण (Example): यूट्यूब (YouTube) किंवा नेटफ्लिक्स (Netflix). आपण तिथे पूर्ण चित्रपट आधी डाउनलोड करत नाही, तर थोडा-थोडा डेटा येत राहतो आणि आपण तो बघत राहतो. हेच काम Node.js मध्ये Streams करतात.
  • सर्वात महत्वाची गोष्ट म्हणजे सर्व Streams हे EventEmitter असतात, म्हणजेच ते 'data' आणि 'end' सारखे इव्हेंट्स फायर करतात.
Figure 19: Why Streams are Memory Efficient

Q. 4 Types of Streams (In Detail)

  • 1. Readable Stream: A stream from which you can read data sequentially. It acts as a source.
    • Examples: fs.createReadStream() (Reading a file), process.stdin (Reading user input from terminal), req in HTTP (Reading incoming request body).
  • 2. Writable Stream: A stream to which you can write data sequentially. It acts as a destination.
    • Examples: fs.createWriteStream() (Writing to a file), process.stdout (Printing to the console), res in HTTP (Sending a response back to the client).
  • 3. Duplex Stream: A stream that is BOTH Readable and Writable. The read and write channels operate independently (like a two-way road).
    • Example: A TCP network socket (net.Socket). You can read data coming from a client and write data back to the client simultaneously.
  • 4. Transform Stream: A very special type of Duplex stream. Here, the data you WRITE is mathematically modified or transformed before it is READ.
    • Examples: zlib.createGzip() (Data goes in as text, gets compressed, and comes out as a zip binary), Encryption streams (Data goes in raw, comes out encrypted).
Figure 20: 4 Types of Node.js Streams
मराठी सारांश:
  • Streams चे ४ प्रकार असतात.
  • 1. Readable: ज्यातून data वाचतो (source). उदा. fs.createReadStream(), process.stdin, HTTP req. उदाहरण: नळ (पाणी बाहेर येते).
  • 2. Writable: ज्यात data लिहितो (destination). उदा. fs.createWriteStream(), process.stdout, HTTP res. उदाहरण: बादली (पाणी आत जाते).
  • 3. Duplex: वाचणे आणि लिहिणे दोन्ही, पण दोन्ही बाजू स्वतंत्र. उदा. TCP socket. उदाहरण: phone call (ऐकणे आणि बोलणे एकाच वेळी).
  • 4. Transform: Duplex चा खास प्रकार. आत आलेला data बदलून बाहेर देतो. उदा. zlib.createGzip() (compress), encryption. उदाहरण: water filter (गढूळ पाणी आत, स्वच्छ बाहेर).

Q. Common Stream Events (Detailed Explanation)

  • 'data' (Readable): This event is fired continuously whenever a new chunk of data becomes available to read. When you listen to this event, the stream automatically starts flowing.
  • 'end' (Readable): This event is fired when the stream has reached the end of the file/source and there is no more data left to read. Useful for closing connections or sending a final response.
  • 'error' (Readable/Writable): Fired immediately if there is any error during the reading or writing process (e.g., file not found, permission denied). If you don't handle this, your server will crash!
  • 'finish' (Writable): This event is fired on a Writable stream when you have called .end() and all chunks have been successfully flushed/written to the destination.
Code Example: Handling Streams Manually with Events
const fs = require('fs');
const http = require('http');

const server = http.createServer((req, res) => {
    // 1. Create a readable stream
    const readStream = fs.createReadStream("large-data.txt", "utf-8");
    
    // 2. Listen to the 'data' event
    readStream.on("data", (chunk) => {
        res.write(chunk); // Send chunk to the client/browser
    });

    // 3. Listen to the 'end' event
    readStream.on("end", () => {
        res.end(); // Close the response when file ends
    });

    // 4. Handle errors!
    readStream.on("error", (err) => {
        console.error(err);
        res.statusCode = 500;
        res.end("File not found");
    });
});
मराठी सारांश (Stream Events):
  • 'data' (Readable): नवीन chunk (तुकडा) आला की प्रत्येक वेळी fire होतो. हा listener लावला की stream वाहू लागतो (flowing mode).
  • 'end' (Readable): source मधला सगळा data संपला की एकदा fire होतो. इथे response बंद करतात.
  • 'error' (Readable / Writable): file सापडली नाही, permission नाही अशा चुकांवर fire होतो. Handle न केल्यास server crash होतो.
  • 'finish' (Writable): .end() केल्यावर सगळा data destination मध्ये लिहून पूर्ण झाला की fire होतो.
  • Flow: data → data → data → end (किंवा मध्येच error).

Q. The stream.pipe() Method (The Easy Way)

Concept:
  • stream.pipe() is a utility method that connects a Readable stream directly to a Writable stream.
  • It automatically handles the reading, writing, and flow control (backpressure) so you don't have to manually write .on('data') and .on('end') events.
  • Benefits: Memory efficiency, heavily simplified code, and you can "chain" multiple pipes (e.g., Read → Compress → Write).
Syntax: readableStream.pipe(destinationStream, [options])
  • readableStream: The source stream you are reading from (e.g., fs.createReadStream).
  • destinationStream: The writable stream you are sending data to (e.g., HTTP res, or fs.createWriteStream).
  • Returns: The destination stream, which allows for chaining like a.pipe(b).pipe(c).
Code Example: Using .pipe()
const fs = require('fs');
const http = require('http');

const server = http.createServer((req, res) => {
    const readStream = fs.createReadStream("large-data.txt", "utf-8");
    
    // Connect the read stream directly to the HTTP response (which is a Writable stream!)
    // This ONE line replaces all the .on('data') and .on('end') logic!
    readStream.pipe(res);
    
    readStream.on("error", (err) => {
        res.statusCode = 500;
        res.end("Error occurred");
    });
});
मराठी सारांश:
  • .pipe() चा वापर करून आपण एका Readable stream मधून डेटा थेट Writable stream मध्ये पाठवू शकतो.
  • Syntax: readable.pipe(destination). Destination stream परत देतो, म्हणून chaining शक्य: a.pipe(b).pipe(c).
  • यामुळे कोड खूप सोपा होतो (फक्त एका लाईनमध्ये काम होते). .on('data') आणि .on('end') स्वतः लिहावे लागत नाहीत.
  • Backpressure: लिहिणारा (writable) slow असेल तर pipe वाचणे आपोआप थांबवतो, म्हणजे memory भरत नाही. Node.js हे स्वतः हाताळते.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: pipe errors पुढे पाठवत नाही. प्रत्येक stream वर 'error' handle करा, किंवा stream.pipeline() वापरा.
  • उदाहरण: टाकीतून (readable) पाइपने थेट बागेत (writable) पाणी. बादलीने फेऱ्या मारायची गरज नाही.

Q. What is Backpressure in Streams?

Concept:
  • Backpressure happens when data arrives faster than the destination can handle (write) it.
  • Example: reading a file from a fast SSD (very fast) and sending it to a user on a slow mobile network (slow).
  • If nothing controls the speed, the extra data piles up in memory (RAM), and the server can slow down or crash with an out-of-memory error.
  • Backpressure handling is the mechanism that tells the fast side: "Stop! Wait until I am ready."
  • Simple example: pouring water from a big bucket into a bottle with a funnel. If you pour too fast, the funnel overflows. You pause, let it drain, then pour again.
How Node.js Handles Backpressure (Step by Step):
  1. The readable stream reads a chunk and calls writable.write(chunk).
  2. write() returns true → the writable's internal buffer still has space, so keep sending.
  3. write() returns false → the buffer is full. The reader must pause (readable.pause()).
  4. When the writable empties its buffer, it emits the 'drain' event.
  5. On 'drain', the reader resumes (readable.resume()), and the cycle repeats.
  • ✅ .pipe() and pipeline() do all of this automatically.
  • ⚠️ If you use .on('data') + write() manually and ignore the return value, you have no backpressure.
Diagram: Backpressure Flow (write() → false → pause → 'drain' → resume)
Code Example 1: ❌ No Backpressure (Memory Can Explode)
const fs = require('fs');
const reader = fs.createReadStream('huge-10GB.log');
const writer = fs.createWriteStream('copy.log');

reader.on('data', (chunk) => {
    writer.write(chunk);    // ❌ return value ignored → chunks pile up in RAM
});
Code Example 2: ✅ Manual Backpressure Handling
reader.on('data', (chunk) => {
    const canContinue = writer.write(chunk);   // true = space left, false = buffer full
    if (!canContinue) {
        reader.pause();                          // stop reading
        writer.once('drain', () => reader.resume()); // continue when writer is ready
    }
});
reader.on('end', () => writer.end());
  • Syntax: writable.write(chunk[, encoding][, callback])
  • chunk: The data to write (string or Buffer).
  • encoding (optional): For string chunks, e.g. 'utf8'.
  • callback (optional): Called when this chunk has been flushed.
  • Returns: true if more data can be written now; false if you should wait for 'drain'.
  • 'drain' event: Emitted by the writable when its buffer is emptied and it can accept more data.
  • readable.pause() / readable.resume(): Stop / restart the flow of 'data' events.
मराठी सारांश (Backpressure):
  • Backpressure म्हणजे काय? data येण्याचा वेग (readable) लिहिण्याच्या वेगापेक्षा (writable) जास्त असणे.
  • उदाहरण: SSD वरून file खूप वेगाने वाचली जाते, पण user चे mobile network slow आहे. जास्तीचा data RAM मध्ये साचतो.
  • नियंत्रण नसेल तर memory भरते आणि server slow होतो किंवा crash होतो (out of memory).
  • Node.js ची पद्धत: write() ने false दिले → buffer भरला → reader pause() → writer रिकामा झाला की 'drain' event → reader resume().
  • .pipe() आणि pipeline() हे सगळे आपोआप करतात.
  • हाताने .on('data') + write() वापरून return value दुर्लक्षित केली तर backpressure handle होत नाही.
  • उदाहरण: मोठ्या बादलीतून नरसाळ्याने (funnel) बाटलीत पाणी ओतणे. नरसाळे भरले की थांबा, रिकामे झाले की पुन्हा ओता.

Q. What is highWaterMark?

Concept:
  • highWaterMark is the size limit of a stream's internal buffer.
  • It is the threshold at which a stream says "I'm full", which is what triggers backpressure.
  • It is a limit, not a hard cap: you can still write past it, but write() starts returning false to ask you to stop.
  • Simple example: the "full" line marked on a water tank. Water can technically go a little higher, but at that line the pump should stop.
Default Values:
  • Normal streams: 16 KB (16384 bytes). Since Node.js 22 the default is 64 KB.
  • fs.createReadStream: 64 KB per chunk.
  • Object mode streams (objectMode: true): measured in number of objects, default 16.
How it works:
  • Readable: the stream reads ahead from the source until its buffer reaches highWaterMark, then stops reading until data is consumed. It also sets the usual chunk size.
  • Writable: once buffered (not yet written) data reaches highWaterMark, write() returns false.
Choosing a value:
  • Bigger → fewer, larger chunks → faster for big files, but more RAM per stream.
  • Smaller → less RAM per stream (good with many users at once), but more overhead.
  • Usually the defaults are fine. Tune only after measuring.
Code Example: Setting highWaterMark
const fs = require('fs');

// Read in 1 MB chunks instead of the default 64 KB
const reader = fs.createReadStream('video.mp4', { highWaterMark: 1024 * 1024 });

// Writable buffer of 32 KB
const writer = fs.createWriteStream('copy.mp4', { highWaterMark: 32 * 1024 });

reader.on('data', (chunk) => console.log('Chunk size:', chunk.length)); // ≈ 1048576

console.log(reader.readableHighWaterMark);  // 1048576
console.log(writer.writableHighWaterMark);  // 32768
  • Syntax: fs.createReadStream(path, { highWaterMark, encoding, start, end })
  • highWaterMark: Buffer size in bytes (or object count in object mode).
  • encoding: e.g. 'utf8' to get strings instead of Buffers.
  • start / end: Read only a byte range of the file (useful for video seeking).
  • readable.readableHighWaterMark / writable.writableHighWaterMark: Read-only properties showing the current limit.
मराठी सारांश (highWaterMark):
  • highWaterMark म्हणजे stream च्या internal buffer ची मर्यादा (size limit).
  • ही मर्यादा गाठली की stream "मी भरलो" असे सांगतो, म्हणजे backpressure सुरू होतो.
  • ही hard limit नाही. पुढे लिहिता येते, पण write() false देऊन थांबायला सांगतो.
  • Default: साधे streams 16 KB (Node 22+ मध्ये 64 KB), fs.createReadStream 64 KB, object mode मध्ये 16 objects.
  • Readable: एवढा data आधीच वाचून ठेवतो, आणि साधारण chunk size पण हीच. Writable: एवढा data साचला की write() = false.
  • मोठी value: मोठ्या files साठी fast, पण जास्त RAM. छोटी value: कमी RAM, जास्त users साठी चांगली.
  • उदाहरण: पाण्याच्या टाकीवरची "full" ची रेघ. तिथे पोहोचलो की पंप बंद करायचा.

Q. What is stream.pipeline()? (Better than .pipe())

Concept:
  • stream.pipeline() connects several streams in a chain (source → transforms → destination), like .pipe().
  • Like .pipe(), it handles backpressure automatically.
  • Unlike .pipe(), it also forwards errors from any stream to one place, and destroys all streams (closes files and sockets) if any one fails.
  • That's why pipeline() is the recommended way to connect streams in production.
  • Simple example: a factory assembly line with one supervisor. If any machine breaks, the supervisor stops the whole line and reports the problem, instead of other machines running uselessly.
The Problem with .pipe():
  • a.pipe(b).pipe(c) does not forward errors. An error in a is not seen by c.
  • You must add .on('error') to every stream, or the process can crash.
  • If one stream fails, the others are not closed → open file handles and memory leaks.
Point.pipe()pipeline()
Backpressure✅ Automatic✅ Automatic
Error forwarding❌ No, add 'error' on every stream✅ One callback / one catch
Cleanup on failure❌ Other streams stay open✅ Destroys all streams
async/await❌ No✅ stream/promises version
RecommendedSimple scripts✅ Production code
Code Example 1: pipeline() with Callback (Compress a File)
const fs = require('fs');
const zlib = require('zlib');
const { pipeline } = require('stream');

pipeline(
    fs.createReadStream('input.txt'),     // 1. source (Readable)
    zlib.createGzip(),                     // 2. transform (compress)
    fs.createWriteStream('input.txt.gz'),  // 3. destination (Writable)
    (err) => {                             // 4. ONE callback for success or any error
        if (err) console.error('Pipeline failed:', err.message);
        else console.log('Compression done!');
    }
);
Code Example 2: pipeline() with async/await (Modern, Recommended)
const fs = require('fs');
const zlib = require('zlib');
const { pipeline } = require('stream/promises');

async function compress() {
    try {
        await pipeline(
            fs.createReadStream('input.txt'),
            zlib.createGzip(),
            fs.createWriteStream('input.txt.gz')
        );
        console.log('Compression done!');
    } catch (err) {
        console.error('Pipeline failed:', err.message);  // error from ANY stream lands here
    }
}
compress();
Code Example 3: Streaming a File to an HTTP Response Safely
const http = require('http');
const fs = require('fs');
const { pipeline } = require('stream');

http.createServer((req, res) => {
    res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'video/mp4' });
    pipeline(fs.createReadStream('movie.mp4'), res, (err) => {
        if (err) console.error('Stream error:', err.message);  // e.g. user closed the tab
    });
}).listen(3000);
// slow user → backpressure handled; user disconnects → file stream closed automatically
  • Syntax (callback): stream.pipeline(source, ...transforms, destination, callback)
  • Syntax (promise): await require('stream/promises').pipeline(source, ...transforms, destination)
  • source: A Readable stream (or an async iterable).
  • ...transforms: Zero or more Transform / Duplex streams (e.g. zlib.createGzip(), encryption).
  • destination: A Writable stream (file, HTTP res).
  • callback(err): Called once at the end. err is undefined on success, or the first error from any stream.
  • Returns: The callback version returns the last stream; the promise version returns a Promise that resolves when done.
मराठी सारांश (stream.pipeline):
  • pipeline() अनेक streams साखळीसारखे जोडतो: source → transform → destination.
  • .pipe() सारखाच backpressure आपोआप सांभाळतो.
  • .pipe() पेक्षा चांगला का? (1) कोणत्याही stream मधला error एकाच callback / catch मध्ये येतो. (2) एक stream fail झाला तर बाकी सगळे आपोआप बंद होतात, म्हणजे files उघड्या राहत नाहीत आणि memory leak नाही.
  • .pipe() मध्ये प्रत्येक stream वर 'error' handle करावा लागतो, नाहीतर crash.
  • Modern पद्धत: require('stream/promises').pipeline + async/await + try/catch.
  • उपयोग: file compress करणे (gzip), मोठी file / video HTTP response मध्ये पाठवणे, file copy करणे.
  • उदाहरण: कारखान्याची assembly line आणि एक supervisor. एक मशीन बिघडले की supervisor संपूर्ण line थांबवतो आणि तक्रार सांगतो.
  • Rule: production मध्ये .pipe() ऐवजी नेहमी pipeline() वापरा.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. Why are Streams preferred over `fs.readFile` for large files?
  • fs.readFile() loads the entire file into RAM before sending it. If the file is 2GB and the server only has 1GB of RAM, the application will crash.
  • A Stream reads and sends the file in tiny chunks (e.g., 64KB at a time), keeping RAM usage extremely low regardless of the file size.
Q2. Can we pipe a Writable stream into a Readable stream?
  • No. Data always flows FROM a Readable stream TO a Writable stream. readStream.pipe(writeStream) is correct. writeStream.pipe(...) will throw an error.
Q3. What is a Duplex stream vs a Transform stream?
  • Both can read and write. But in a Duplex stream (like a TCP socket), the read and write channels are independent. In a Transform stream (like zlib compression), the output data is mathematically computed from the input data.
Q4. What is backpressure and how does Node.js handle it?
  • Backpressure is when the writable side can't keep up with the readable side, so data piles up in memory.
  • write() returns false when the buffer reaches highWaterMark; the reader pauses until the writable emits 'drain', then resumes.
  • .pipe() and pipeline() do this automatically.
Q5. What is highWaterMark?
  • The size limit of a stream's internal buffer (bytes, or object count in object mode). Reaching it triggers backpressure. Defaults: 16 KB for generic streams (64 KB from Node 22), 64 KB for fs.createReadStream.
Q6. Why use pipeline() instead of .pipe()?
  • .pipe() doesn't forward errors or close the other streams when one fails, which can leak file handles or crash the app.
  • pipeline() forwards any error to one callback / catch and destroys all streams on failure. It also supports async/await via stream/promises.
मराठी सारांश (Backpressure, highWaterMark, pipeline Interview):
  • Q4: writer मागे पडला की data RAM मध्ये साचतो, याला backpressure म्हणतात. write() = false → pause → 'drain' → resume.
  • Q5: highWaterMark = buffer ची मर्यादा. Default 16 KB (Node 22+ मध्ये 64 KB), fs read stream साठी 64 KB.
  • Q6: pipeline() सगळे errors एका ठिकाणी देतो आणि fail झाल्यास सगळे streams बंद करतो. .pipe() हे करत नाही.
  • उदाहरण (backpressure): SSD वरून वेगाने वाचलेली file slow internet वरच्या user ला पाठवताना data साचू नये म्हणून pause / resume.
  • Rule: production मध्ये pipeline() (किंवा stream/promises सह await pipeline) वापरा.
मराठी सारांश (Interview Questions Breakdown):
  • Q. मोठी फाईल वाचण्यासाठी fs.readFile का वापरू नये?
    उत्तर: कारण fs.readFile संपूर्ण फाईल एकाच वेळी मेमरी (RAM) मध्ये लोड करते. जर फाईल 2GB ची असेल आणि RAM 1GB असेल, तर सर्वर क्रॅश (Out of memory) होईल. त्याऐवजी Stream वापरावे जे डेटा छोट्या chunks मध्ये वाचते.
  • Q. Writable stream ला Readable stream मध्ये pipe करू शकतो का?
    उत्तर: नाही. डेटा नेहमी Readable मधून Writable कडे जातो. (readStream.pipe(writeStream) बरोबर आहे, पण उलट केले तर एरर येईल).
  • Q. Duplex आणि Transform stream मध्ये काय फरक आहे?
    उत्तर: दोन्ही read/write करतात. पण Duplex मध्ये read आणि write चॅनेल वेगळे असतात (उदा. TCP socket). तर Transform मध्ये आत आलेला डेटा बदलून (compute करून) बाहेर पाठवला जातो (उदा. फाईल compress करणे).
  • Stream चे 4 प्रकार: Readable (वाचणे), Writable (लिहिणे), Duplex (दोन्ही, स्वतंत्र), Transform (बदलून पुढे).
  • Chunk size: fs.createReadStream default ने 64 KB चे तुकडे देते, म्हणून 10 GB file पण कमी RAM मध्ये.
  • Events: data, end, error, finish. Error handle न केल्यास server crash.

Quick Revision Summary

Concept / Method Description
Readable Stream Used for reading data (fs.createReadStream).
Writable Stream Used for writing data (fs.createWriteStream).
.pipe() Connects a readable stream to a writable stream automatically.
'data' Event Fired when a new chunk of data arrives.
'end' Event Fired when there is no more data to read.

Chapter 13: The Crypto Module

Q. What is the Crypto module in Node.js?

Concept:
  • The crypto module provides cryptographic functionality that includes a set of wrappers for OpenSSL's hash, HMAC, cipher, decipher, sign, and verify functions.
  • It is used to secure data, hash passwords, generate random tokens, and encrypt/decrypt sensitive information.
  • Since it is a core module, it does not require installation and can be imported directly using const crypto = require('node:crypto');.
मराठी सारांश:
  • crypto हा Node.js चा एक in-built (core) module आहे, जो डेटा आणि माहिती अत्यंत सुरक्षित (secure) ठेवण्यासाठी वापरला जातो.
  • याचा मुख्य उपयोग passwords सुरक्षितपणे hash करण्यासाठी (जेणेकरून ते database मध्ये कुणालाही वाचता येऊ नयेत) होतो.
  • तसेच Sessions, OTPs आणि API keys साठी Unique Random Tokens (जसे की UUID) बनवण्यासाठी याचा वापर होतो.
  • याव्यतिरिक्त, private माहिती लपवण्यासाठी (Encrypt/Decrypt करण्यासाठी) आणि डेटाची authenticity तपासण्यासाठी हा module खूप महत्त्वाचा आहे.
  • हा module Node.js मध्ये आधीपासूनच असल्यामुळे याला install करण्याची गरज नसते, फक्त require('node:crypto') लिहून तो वापरता येतो.

Q. Important Cryptographic Concepts

Figure: Summary of Core Cryptographic Concepts
  • 1. Hashing (e.g., SHA-256):
    • Definition: Hashing is a mathematical one-way function that maps data of any arbitrary size to a fixed-size string of characters, typically representing a digital fingerprint of the data.
    • You cannot reverse a hash back to the original data (it is strictly a one-way process).
    • Mainly used for storing passwords securely so that even if the database is hacked, actual passwords remain hidden.
    Example: 
    const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update('password123').digest('hex');
  • 2. Encryption & Decryption (e.g., AES-256):
    • Definition: Encryption is a two-way cryptographic process that scrambles readable data (plaintext) into an unreadable format (ciphertext) using an algorithm and a secret key.
    • The data can be decrypted back to its original form only if you have the exact corresponding secret key.
    • Used for protecting sensitive user data (like credit card details or chat messages) while transmitting or storing.
    Example: 
    // Encryption requires a Cipher, Decryption requires a Decipher with the same key
const cipher = crypto.createCipheriv('aes-256-cbc', key, iv);
  • 3. HMAC (Hash-based Message Authentication Code):
    • Definition: HMAC is a specific type of message authentication code (MAC) involving a cryptographic hash function (like SHA-256) combined with a secret cryptographic key.
    • Used to verify both data integrity (ensuring data hasn't changed) and authenticity (ensuring it came from the right sender).
    • Widely used in JWT (JSON Web Tokens) and securing API webhooks.
    Example: 
    const hmac = crypto.createHmac('sha256', 'my_secret_key').update('Message').digest('hex');
  • 4. Salting:
    • Definition: A salt is unique, randomly generated data that is added as an additional input to a one-way hash function before hashing a password.
    • It guarantees a unique hash even if two different users happen to have the exact same password.
    • Protects against common security vulnerabilities like dictionary attacks and rainbow table attacks.
    Example: 
    const salt = crypto.randomBytes(16).toString('hex');
const hashedPassword = crypto.scryptSync('password123', salt, 64).toString('hex');
मराठी सारांश (Cryptography च्या मूलभूत संकल्पना):
  • Hashing: data ला ठराविक लांबीच्या "fingerprint" मध्ये बदलणे. One-way, परत मूळ data मिळत नाही. उदा. file बदलली का हे तपासणे.
  • Encryption: data कुलूपबंद करणे. Key असेल तर परत उघडता येतो (two-way). उदा. chat messages.
  • HMAC: Hash + secret key. Data बदलला नाही (integrity) आणि योग्य व्यक्तीकडूनच आला (authenticity) हे तपासतो. JWT, webhooks मध्ये वापरतात.
  • Salt: password ला hash करण्यापूर्वी जोडलेला random data. दोघांचा password same असला तरी hash वेगळा येतो. Rainbow table attacks थांबतात.
  • उदाहरण: Hash = बोटांचे ठसे (ठशावरून माणूस बनवता येत नाही). Encryption = तिजोरी (चावीने उघडते).

Q. Types of Cryptographic Algorithms & Which is Better?

  • 1. General Hashing Algorithms (For Data & Files):
    • MD5 & SHA-1: Extremely fast but OUTDATED and INSECURE. Easily broken by modern computers (collision attacks). Do not use them.
    • SHA-256 / SHA-512: Secure, standard algorithms used for hashing data (like verifying file integrity, digital signatures).
    • Verdict: Use SHA-256 or SHA-512 for general data hashing. 
      const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update('DocumentData').digest('hex');
  • 2. Password Hashing Algorithms (Key Derivation):
    • Standard hashing (like SHA-256) is too fast. Hackers can compute billions of SHA-256 hashes per second (brute-force). Password algorithms are intentionally made slow and memory-heavy to prevent this.
    • PBKDF2: Older standard. Decent, but mostly replaced by newer algorithms.
    • bcrypt: Very popular, highly secure, slow by design. Requires a third-party npm package (npm i bcrypt).
    • scrypt: Highly secure, memory-hard (stops hackers from using GPUs to crack passwords). Best part: It is built-in to the Node.js crypto module!
    • Argon2: The most secure and modern algorithm (won the Password Hashing Competition). Requires a third-party package.
    • Verdict: Use scrypt if you want zero external dependencies (built-in). Use bcrypt or Argon2 for modern industry-standard security. 
      // Example using bcrypt (npm i bcrypt)
const bcrypt = require('bcrypt');
const hashedPwd = await bcrypt.hash('mypassword123', 10); // 10 is the salt rounds
  • 3. Encryption Algorithms:
    • Definition of Symmetric Encryption: A method where the same single secret key is used to both lock (encrypt) and unlock (decrypt) the data. It is like a house key that both locks and unlocks the door.
      Best Algorithm: AES-256 (Advanced Encryption Standard). It is extremely fast and used for encrypting database fields, files, and chat messages.
    • Definition of Asymmetric Encryption: A method that uses a pair of different keys: a Public Key (shared with everyone to lock data) and a Private Key (kept strictly secret to unlock it). It is like a mailbox where anyone can drop a letter (public key), but only you have the key to open it (private key).
      Best Algorithm: RSA. It is slower than symmetric encryption but highly secure, mainly used for SSL/TLS (HTTPS) certificates and SSH keys.
    • Verdict: Use AES-256-CBC or AES-256-GCM for standard encryption of your application data. 
      // AES-256 Encryption Example
const cipher = crypto.createCipheriv('aes-256-cbc', Buffer.from(key), iv);
let encrypted = cipher.update('Sensitive Data', 'utf8', 'hex');
encrypted += cipher.final('hex');
मराठी सारांश (अल्गोरिदमची निवड):
  • 1. Data Hashing (डेटासाठी):
    • नेहमी SHA-256 किंवा SHA-512 वापरा (उदा. फाईलचे checksums किंवा डिजिटल सिग्नेचरसाठी).
    • MD5 किंवा SHA-1 कधीही वापरू नका कारण ते आता जुने आणि असुरक्षित (hackable) आहेत.
  • 2. Password Hashing (पासवर्डसाठी):
    • Passwords साठी SHA-256 चालत नाही कारण ते खूप फास्ट आहे, ज्यामुळे हॅकर्स एका सेकंदात करोडो passwords तपासून पाहू शकतात.
    • Passwords सुरक्षित ठेवण्यासाठी नेहमी bcrypt, Argon2 (थर्ड पार्टी पॅकेजेस) किंवा Node.js मधील इन-बिल्ट scrypt वापरा.
    • हे अल्गोरिदम्स मुद्दाम slow आणि memory-heavy डिझाईन केलेले असतात ज्यामुळे हॅकिंग करणे अत्यंत कठीण जाते.
  • 3. Encryption (डेटा लपवण्यासाठी):
    • Symmetric (एकाच चावीने लॉक/अनलॉक): AES-256 हा सर्वोत्तम, सर्वात फास्ट आणि जास्त वापरला जाणारा अल्गोरिदम आहे (उदा. database मधील माहिती किंवा चॅट्स लपवण्यासाठी).
    • Asymmetric (पब्लिक आणि प्रायव्हेट चावी): RSA वापरला जातो. हा slow असतो पण HTTPS/SSL certificates आणि सुरक्षित कनेक्शनसाठी (Secure Key Exchange) वापरला जातो.

Q. Generating Random Values (UUIDs / Tokens)

  • randomBytes(): Generates cryptographically strong pseudorandom data. Commonly used to generate session IDs, API tokens, or salts.
  • randomUUID(): Generates a standard Version 4 UUID (Universally Unique Identifier).
  • Example: 
    const crypto = require('node:crypto');

// Generate a random 16-byte token (converted to hex string)
const token = crypto.randomBytes(16).toString('hex');
console.log(token); // e.g., 'a1b2c3d4e5f67890123456789abcdef0'

// Generate a UUID
const uuid = crypto.randomUUID();
console.log(uuid); // e.g., '123e4567-e89b-12d3-a456-426614174000'

Q. Hashing Data (e.g., SHA-256)

  • Hashing is commonly used to verify file integrity or store passwords securely (although scrypt or bcrypt are better for passwords).
  • Syntax & Arguments:
    • crypto.createHash(algorithm)
    • algorithm: The hashing algorithm to use (e.g., 'sha256', 'md5').
    • .update(data): The actual data or string you want to hash.
    • .digest(encoding): Outputs the final hash. encoding is usually 'hex' (readable string) or 'base64'.
  • Example: 
    const crypto = require('node:crypto');

const secretMessage = "Hello World";
const hash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(secretMessage).digest('hex');

console.log(hash); 
// 'a591a6d40bf420404a011733cfb7b190d62c65bf0bcda32b57b277d9ad9f146e'
मराठी सारांश (Hashing आणि Tokens):
  • Hashing: म्हणजे कोणत्याही आकाराच्या text ला किंवा डेटाला एका विशिष्ट आणि समान लांबीच्या कोडमध्ये (Hash) बदलणे.
  • हे पूर्णपणे one-way असते, म्हणजे तयार झालेल्या Hash वरून मूळ text परत कधीही मिळवता येत नाही (जसे की createHash('sha256') वापरून).
  • randomBytes(): याचा उपयोग अतिशय सुरक्षित आणि unique random data बनवण्यासाठी होतो. हे Session IDs किंवा Passwords साठी Salt बनवताना खूप उपयोगी ठरते.
  • randomUUID(): याचा उपयोग standard आणि युनिव्हर्सल युनिक आयडी (उदा. 123e4567-...) तयार करण्यासाठी होतो.
  • या दोन्ही Random पद्धतींचा वापर मुख्यत्वे 'Forgot Password' लिंकसाठी किंवा API Authentication साठी सुरक्षित tokens बनवण्यासाठी मोठ्या प्रमाणावर केला जातो.

Q. Hashing Passwords with scrypt

  • scrypt is a password-based key derivation function designed to be computationally intensive, making brute-force attacks much harder.
  • It is the recommended built-in method for hashing passwords in Node.js.
  • Syntax & Arguments:
    • crypto.scrypt(password, salt, keylen, callback)
    • password: The plain text password entered by the user.
    • salt: A random string added to the password before hashing to ensure uniqueness.
    • keylen: The desired length of the generated hash (e.g., 64 bytes).
    • callback(err, derivedKey): The function executed after hashing is complete. derivedKey is the final hashed password (returned as a Buffer).
  • Example: Password Hashing Flow 
    const crypto = require('node:crypto');

const password = "mySuperSecretPassword";
const salt = crypto.randomBytes(16).toString('hex'); // Create a random salt

// Async scrypt hashing (password, salt, key length)
crypto.scrypt(password, salt, 64, (err, derivedKey) => {
  if (err) throw err;
  console.log('Salt:', salt);
  console.log('Hashed Password:', derivedKey.toString('hex'));
});
Figure: Secure Password Hashing Flow using Salt and Scrypt
मराठी सारांश:
  • कधीही युझरचे passwords database मध्ये थेट (plain text) जसेच्या तसे सेव्ह करू नयेत, त्यांना Hash करणे गरजेचे असते.
  • फक्त Hashing पुरेसे नसते, म्हणून password च्या आधी एक Salt (random string) जोडला जातो जेणेकरून दोन युझर्सचे passwords सारखे असले तरी त्यांचा Hash वेगळा येईल.
  • Node.js मध्ये passwords सुरक्षित ठेवण्यासाठी scrypt हे इन-बिल्ट फंक्शन वापरले जाते.
  • हे फंक्शन मुद्दाम heavy आणि slow डिझाईन केलेले असते, जेणेकरून हॅकर्स ला Brute-force (एक एक करून passwords तपासून पाहणे) किंवा Dictionary attacks करणे कठीण जाते.

Q. Full Usage Example: HMAC (Signing & Verifying)

  • This shows how a message (like a Webhook payload or a JWT) is signed, and then verified to ensure it wasn't tampered with.
  • Syntax & Arguments:
    • crypto.createHmac(algorithm, key)
    • algorithm: The hashing algorithm to use (e.g., 'sha256').
    • key: The secret cryptographic key used to sign the data. Only those with the key can verify or create the signature.
    • .update(data): The actual message or payload to sign.
    • .digest(encoding): Outputs the final HMAC signature in the specified encoding (like 'hex').
  • Code Example: 
    const crypto = require('node:crypto');

const secretKey = 'my_super_secret_key';
const message = JSON.stringify({ userId: 101, amount: 500 });

// 1. Sender: Creates an HMAC signature for the message
const signature = crypto.createHmac('sha256', secretKey).update(message).digest('hex');
console.log('Signature:', signature);

// 2. Receiver: Verifies the received message with their own generated signature
const receivedMessage = message; 
const receivedSignature = signature;

const verifySignature = crypto.createHmac('sha256', secretKey).update(receivedMessage).digest('hex');
if (verifySignature === receivedSignature) {
    console.log('Success: Data is authentic and has not been changed!');
} else {
    console.log('Error: Data was tampered with!');
}
  • Tip: In production, compare signatures with crypto.timingSafeEqual(Buffer.from(a), Buffer.from(b)) instead of === to prevent timing attacks.
मराठी सारांश (HMAC Sign आणि Verify):
  • Syntax: crypto.createHmac(algorithm, key).update(data).digest('hex').
  • algorithm: उदा. 'sha256'. key: गुप्त चावी (दोघांकडेच असते). update: sign करायचा message. digest: final signature (hex).
  • Sender: message + secret key → signature बनवतो आणि दोन्ही पाठवतो.
  • Receiver: आलेल्या message वरून स्वतः signature बनवतो. दोन्ही same असतील तर data खरा आणि न बदललेला.
  • उदाहरण: सही केलेला चेक. सही (signature) जुळली तर चेक खरा. कोणी रक्कम बदलली तर सही जुळत नाही.
  • Tip: तुलना करताना timingSafeEqual वापरा (timing attack पासून संरक्षण).

Q. Full Usage Example: Symmetric Encryption (AES-256)

  • This shows how you encrypt and then later decrypt sensitive data (like a chat message or bank account number).
  • Syntax & Arguments (Encryption/Decryption):
    • crypto.createCipheriv(algorithm, key, iv) / crypto.createDecipheriv(algorithm, key, iv)
    • algorithm: The encryption algorithm to use (e.g., 'aes-256-cbc').
    • key: The secret key used to lock/unlock. For AES-256, it MUST be exactly 32 bytes long.
    • iv: Initialization Vector. A random string (16 bytes for CBC) used to ensure that encrypting the same text twice yields different ciphertexts. Must be passed along with the encrypted data to decrypt it.
    • .update(data, inputEncoding, outputEncoding): Processes the data.
    • .final(outputEncoding): Returns any remaining encrypted/decrypted data.
  • Code Example: 
    const crypto = require('node:crypto');

// Setup: Key and IV (Initialization Vector)
const algorithm = 'aes-256-cbc';
const key = crypto.randomBytes(32); // 32 bytes required for AES-256
const iv = crypto.randomBytes(16);  // 16 bytes required for CBC mode

const sensitiveData = "This is a highly confidential message!";

// 1. ENCRYPT (Lock)
const cipher = crypto.createCipheriv(algorithm, key, iv);
let encrypted = cipher.update(sensitiveData, 'utf8', 'hex');
encrypted += cipher.final('hex');
console.log('Encrypted Data:', encrypted);

// 2. DECRYPT (Unlock)
const decipher = crypto.createDecipheriv(algorithm, key, iv);
let decrypted = decipher.update(encrypted, 'hex', 'utf8');
decrypted += decipher.final('utf8');
console.log('Decrypted Data:', decrypted); // Should match original!
मराठी सारांश (AES-256 Encryption / Decryption):
  • Symmetric: एकाच key ने lock (encrypt) आणि unlock (decrypt).
  • algorithm: 'aes-256-cbc'. key: नक्की 32 bytes. iv (Initialization Vector): 16 bytes random. एकच text दोनदा encrypt केला तरी output वेगळा येतो.
  • Encrypt: createCipheriv → update(data, 'utf8', 'hex') → final('hex').
  • Decrypt: createDecipheriv (तीच key आणि तोच iv) → update(encrypted, 'hex', 'utf8') → final('utf8').
  • लक्षात ठेवा: iv गुप्त नसतो, तो encrypted data सोबत साठवतात. Key मात्र .env मध्ये सुरक्षित ठेवा.
  • उदाहरण: बँक account नंबर database मध्ये encrypt करून ठेवणे.
SECTION 3: PACKAGE MANAGEMENT & WEB SERVERS

Chapter 14: Web Server, Routing & APIs (HTTP Module)

Q. What is a Web Server?

Definition:
  • A web server is an application that listens for incoming HTTP requests from clients (like browsers).
  • It sends back appropriate responses: HTML pages, JSON data, images, and so on.
  • Node.js provides a built-in http module to create a highly efficient, event-driven, non-blocking web server.
  • http.createServer((req, res) => {}) takes a callback that fires every time a new request hits the server.
  • req (Request): Contains info about the incoming request (e.g., req.url, req.method, headers).
  • res (Response): Used to send data back to the client (e.g., res.write(), res.end()).
Code Example: Creating a Basic Web Server
const http = require("node:http");

const server = http.createServer((req, res) => {
    res.end("Hello, Welcome to Node.js server!");
});

// Start the server on port 4000
server.listen(4000, "127.0.0.1", () => {
    console.log("Server started on port 4000");
});
मराठी सारांश:
  • Web Server म्हणजे असा program जो ब्राउझर कडून आलेल्या HTTP request ला हाताळतो आणि योग्य ते response (उत्तर) पाठवतो.
  • Node.js मध्ये आपण http module चा वापर करून आपला स्वतःचा server बनवू शकतो.
  • http.createServer((req, res) => {}): प्रत्येक नवीन request आल्यावर हे callback चालते.
  • req: आलेल्या request ची माहिती (req.url, req.method, headers). res: उत्तर पाठवण्यासाठी (res.write(), res.end()).
  • server.listen(4000) ने server port 4000 वर सुरू होतो. Browser मध्ये http://localhost:4000.

Q. Key Functions of a Web Server (In Detail)

Concept:
  • A web server is software (and sometimes hardware) that receives HTTP requests from clients (browsers, mobile apps), processes them and sends back HTTP responses (HTML, JSON, images).
  • Its main purpose is to serve web content (HTML, CSS, JS, data) over the internet or a private network using HTTP/HTTPS.
  • Examples: Apache, Nginx, IIS, and any Node.js server you build with the http module.
  • Simple example: a restaurant waiter. They take your order (request), go to the kitchen (processing) and bring back your food (response).
  • 1. Receive Requests:
    • Listens for connections on a port: 80 for HTTP, 443 for HTTPS (or 3000/8000 in development).
    • Waits for a client (browser or API call) to send a request.
  • 2. Handle HTTP Methods:
    • GET → retrieve data. POST → send/create data. PUT / PATCH → update data. DELETE → remove data.
    • Routes requests based on the URL path (/home, /about).
  • 3. Process the Request:
    • Works out which resource the client wants.
    • Finds static files or runs code to build dynamic content (DB queries).
  • 4. Serve Content:
    • Static content: Files stored on the server (HTML, CSS, JS, images). Every user gets the same file.
    • Dynamic content: Generated on the fly by code (Node.js, PHP, Python), for example "Welcome, Vishal".
  • 5. Security and SSL:
    • Secure communication over HTTPS, managing SSL/TLS certificates that encrypt data.
    • Protects against unauthorized access and data interception.
  • 6. Customizable and Extensible: Custom routes, middleware (logging, authentication), database and API integration.
  • 7. Event-Driven and Non-Blocking (Node.js): Node.js servers handle many requests at once without blocking the main thread, which is the key to scalability.
Diagram: Request → Web Server → Response
मराठी सारांश (Web Server ची कामे):
  • Web server म्हणजे client कडून HTTP request घेऊन, त्यावर काम करून, response परत पाठवणारा software.
  • उदाहरण: हॉटेलचा waiter. Order घेतो (request), kitchen ला देतो (processing), जेवण आणतो (response).
  • Port: HTTP = 80, HTTPS = 443.
  • Methods: GET (आणणे), POST (पाठवणे), PUT / PATCH (बदलणे), DELETE (काढणे).
  • Static content: सगळ्यांना same file. Dynamic content: code ने प्रत्येकासाठी वेगळे तयार होते.
  • Security: HTTPS / SSL ने data encrypt होतो.
  • Node.js server non-blocking असल्याने एकाच वेळी अनेक requests handle करतो.

Q. How to Create a Node.js Server? (Step by Step)

  1. Import the http module: const http = require('http');. It returns an object with many fields and methods, such as createServer.
  2. Create the server instance: Call http.createServer(requestListener) and pass a function (the request listener).
  3. Write the response logic inside the listener using req and res.
  4. Start listening: server.listen(port, callback).
Code Example: Creating a Server (Commented)
const http = require('http');

// createServer takes a requestListener function with 2 parameters:
//   req → info about the incoming request (url, method, headers)
//   res → used to send the response back
const server = http.createServer((req, res) => {
    res.end('Welcome to the Node.js application!');
});

// listen(port, callback) → start accepting requests
server.listen(8000, () => {
    console.log('Server started on http://localhost:8000');
});
  • Syntax: server.listen(port[, host][, backlog][, callback])
  • port: The port number to listen on (for example, 8000).
  • host (optional): The address, for example '127.0.0.1' (default: all interfaces).
  • backlog (optional): The maximum length of the queue of pending connections.
  • callback (optional): Runs once the server starts listening.

Q. What is the requestListener in createServer()?

Concept:
  • The request listener is the callback function passed to http.createServer().
  • It runs automatically for every incoming HTTP request. Internally, it is attached to the server’s 'request' event.
  • Syntax: http.createServer(requestListener), where requestListener = (req, res) => { ... }
  • Simple example: a receptionist who greets every visitor who walks in through the door.
It Receives Two Arguments:
  • 1. req: Request object (http.IncomingMessage)
    • Represents the incoming HTTP request.
    • Holds request info: req.url, req.method, req.headers.
    • It is a readable stream, so the body is read with req.on('data') / req.on('end').
  • 2. res: Response object (http.ServerResponse)
    • Represents the HTTP response that you send back.
    • Used to set the status and headers and to send the body: res.writeHead(), res.setHeader(), res.write(), res.end().
    • It is a writable stream.
Code Example: Same Server Using the 'request' Event
const http = require('http');

const server = http.createServer();        // no listener passed

// identical to passing the function to createServer()
server.on('request', (req, res) => {
    console.log(req.method, req.url);       // e.g. GET /about
    res.end('Handled by request listener');
});

server.listen(3000);

Q. What Does createServer() Return?

  • http.createServer() creates and returns an http.Server instance (the server object).
  • This object is an EventEmitter, so it can:
    • Listen for requests: server.listen(port).
    • Emit events: 'request', 'connection', 'listening', 'close', 'error'.
    • Stop: server.close() stops accepting new connections.
  • In short, createServer() builds your web server and returns it, so that you can start it with listen().
Code Example: Using the Returned Server Object
const http = require('http');
const server = http.createServer((req, res) => res.end('Hello Node.js!'));

console.log(server instanceof http.Server);   // true

server.on('listening', () => console.log('Server running on port 3000'));
server.on('error', (err) => console.error('Server error:', err.message)); // e.g. EADDRINUSE
server.on('close', () => console.log('Server stopped'));

server.listen(3000);
मराठी सारांश (Server तयार करणे, requestListener, createServer Return):
  • Steps: require('http') → createServer(listener) → listen(port).
  • requestListener: प्रत्येक request आल्यावर आपोआप चालणारे function. उदा. प्रत्येक पाहुण्याचे स्वागत करणारी receptionist.
  • req (IncomingMessage): आलेल्या request ची माहिती (url, method, headers, body). Readable stream.
  • res (ServerResponse): उत्तर पाठवण्यासाठी (status, headers, body). Writable stream.
  • createServer() काय return करते? http.Server object. तो EventEmitter आहे, म्हणून त्यावर listen(), close(), आणि 'request' / 'error' / 'close' events वापरता येतात.

Q. Node.js Program Lifecycle

Concept:
  • When you run node app.js, Node.js parses the whole file, registers variables and functions, and runs the top-level code.
  • A normal script then exits. But a server program never exits. Why? Because of the event loop.
  • The event loop keeps running as long as there is work to do, meaning as long as event listeners or pending tasks are registered.
  • createServer() + listen() registers a request listener that is never unregistered, so the process stays alive and waits for requests.
  • The process ends only when there is no work left, when you call server.close() / process.exit(), or when you press Ctrl + C.
  • Node.js uses this event-driven approach for everything, not only servers.
Diagram: Node.js Program Lifecycle
Code Example: Script That Exits vs Server That Stays Alive
// script.js → runs and EXITS (no pending work)
console.log('Hello');

// server.js → NEVER exits (request listener keeps the event loop busy)
const http = require('http');
http.createServer((req, res) => res.end('Hi')).listen(3000);
console.log('Waiting for requests...');
मराठी सारांश (Program Lifecycle):
  • node app.js → Node संपूर्ण file parse करतो → variables / functions register करतो → top-level code चालवतो.
  • साधी script काम संपले की बंद होते.
  • पण server बंद होत नाही, कारण Event Loop मध्ये request listener कायम registered असतो.
  • Event loop जोपर्यंत काम (listeners, timers, I/O) आहे तोपर्यंत फिरत राहतो.
  • बंद कधी? काम संपल्यावर, server.close() किंवा process.exit() केल्यावर, किंवा Ctrl + C दाबल्यावर.

Q. Working with JSON

  • JSON (JavaScript Object Notation) is a lightweight format for transferring data between server and client.
  • JSON.stringify(object): Converts a normal JS Object into a JSON String (to send over network).
  • JSON.parse(jsonString): Converts a JSON String back into a usable JS Object.
  • Rules of JSON: keys must be in double quotes; no functions, undefined or comments; values can be string, number, boolean, null, array or object.
Code Example: stringify and parse
const user = { name: 'Vishal', age: 25 };

const text = JSON.stringify(user);        // '{"name":"Vishal","age":25}'  (string)
const back = JSON.parse(text);            // { name: 'Vishal', age: 25 }   (object)

JSON.stringify(user, null, 2);            // pretty-printed with 2-space indent
// JSON.parse('{bad json}')               // ❌ throws SyntaxError → wrap in try/catch
मराठी सारांश (JSON):
  • JSON = JavaScript Object Notation. Server आणि client मध्ये data पाठवण्याचा हलका, text format.
  • JSON.stringify(obj): JS object → JSON string (network वर पाठवण्यापूर्वी).
  • JSON.parse(str): JSON string → JS object (आल्यानंतर वापरण्यासाठी).
  • नियम: keys नेहमी double quotes मध्ये. Functions, undefined, comments चालत नाहीत.
  • चुकीचा JSON parse केल्यास error येतो, म्हणून try/catch वापरा.
  • उदाहरण: सामान (object) पार्सलमध्ये पॅक करणे (stringify), आणि पोहोचल्यावर उघडणे (parse).

Q. Creating a Simple JSON API

  • An API (Application Programming Interface) route returns raw data (usually JSON) instead of HTML.
  • To build an API, we set the header Content-Type: application/json and send a stringified JSON object.
  • Pro Tip: If your API returns data from a large .json file, read it synchronously once at the top of the file using fs.readFileSync(). Do not read it inside createServer, or it will unnecessarily read the file from the hard drive for every single user request!
Code Example: A Simple User API
const http = require("node:http");
const fs = require("node:fs");

// READ FILE ONLY ONCE when server starts
const apiData = fs.readFileSync("userApi.json", "utf-8");

const server = http.createServer((req, res) => {
    if (req.url === "/api/users") {
        res.writeHead(200, { "Content-Type": "application/json" });
        // Send the pre-loaded JSON data directly!
        res.end(apiData);
    } else {
        res.writeHead(404);
        res.end("API route not found");
    }
});
मराठी सारांश:
  • API बनवताना आपण HTML ऐवजी JSON डेटा पाठवतो.
  • नेहमी लक्षात ठेवा, जर डेटा फाईलमधून वाचायचा असेल तर तो सर्वर सुरु होताना एकदाच readFileSync ने वाचावा.
  • Request आल्यावर वारंवार वाचू नये, यामुळे सर्वर slow होतो.
  • API route: /api/users वर res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }) आणि res.end(data).
  • का एकदाच वाचायचे? 1000 requests = 1000 वेळा disk वाचणे टळते. Data memory मध्ये तयार राहतो.
  • मर्यादा: file बदलली तर server restart करावा लागतो. वारंवार बदलणारा data database मध्ये ठेवा.
  • चुकीच्या route ला 404 आणि JSON error पाठवा.
  • उदाहरण: सकाळी एकदाच menu card छापून ठेवणे. प्रत्येक ग्राहकासाठी नवीन छापत नाही.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What is the difference between res.write() and res.end()?
  • res.write() can be called multiple times to send chunks of the response body. It keeps the connection open.
  • res.end() signals that all headers and body have been sent. The server considers the response complete and closes the connection. You MUST call res.end(), or the browser will keep spinning infinitely.
Q2. Why do we need res.writeHead()?
  • It sends HTTP response headers to the client. We need it to tell the browser the Status Code (e.g., 200 for Success, 404 for Not Found) and the Content-Type (e.g., text/html or application/json), so the browser knows how to parse the data.
Q3. Why is it bad practice to read a JSON file asynchronously inside the server callback?
  • If you use fs.readFile() inside createServer, the server will read the file from the hard drive on every single request. For 1000 users, it reads the disk 1000 times, which is extremely slow and resource-heavy. Reading it synchronously once outside the server caches it in memory.
Q4. What is the difference between setHeader() and writeHead()?
  • setHeader(): Useful for adding or overriding individual headers incrementally before sending the response.
  • writeHead(): Ideal for setting the status code and multiple headers at once. It forces the headers to be sent immediately.
Q5. What is the res.write() method?
  • The res.write() method is used to send a chunk of the response body to the client. It can be called multiple times to stream data to the client before ending the response with res.end().
Q6. What is a Stream in Node.js?
  • A stream is an ongoing process of reading or writing data chunk by chunk, without waiting for the entire file to be loaded in memory. We use Buffers to hold multiple chunks and work with them.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Q1 (write vs end): res.write() अनेक वेळा, तुकड्यांमध्ये data पाठवतो (connection उघडे). res.end() response पूर्ण करतो. तो न केल्यास browser loading करत राहतो.
  • Q2 (writeHead का?): browser ला status code (200, 404) आणि Content-Type (html / json) सांगण्यासाठी, म्हणजे browser data नीट समजू शकतो.
  • Q3 (file request च्या आत का वाचू नये?): प्रत्येक request ला disk वाचली जाते. 1000 users = 1000 वेळा. म्हणून server सुरू होताना एकदाच वाचा.
  • Q4 (setHeader vs writeHead): setHeader = एक-एक header, नंतर बदलता येतो. writeHead = status + अनेक headers एकदम, आणि लगेच पाठवतो.
  • Q5 / Q6: res.write() streaming साठी. Stream = data तुकड्या-तुकड्यात वाचणे / लिहिणे, पूर्ण file memory मध्ये न घेता.

Quick Revision Summary

Concept / Method Description
http.createServer() Creates a new web server instance.
req.url & req.method Used to manually handle Routing (e.g., /home).
res.writeHead() Sets the HTTP status code and response headers.
JSON.stringify() Converts JS Object -> JSON String (before sending).
JSON.parse() Converts JSON String -> JS Object (after receiving).
SECTION 4: EXPRESS.JS FRAMEWORK

Chapter 15: Express.js Basics & Routing

Q. What is Express.js?

Concept:
  • Express.js is a fast, unopinionated, minimalist web framework for Node.js.
  • It provides a robust set of features to develop web and mobile applications seamlessly.
  • Instead of writing complex, native HTTP server code with lots of if/else statements, Express provides a very simple and clean API to build websites and REST APIs.
  • It abstracts away the low-level HTTP module details and makes your code more efficient, readable, and highly scalable.
  • To create an application, you simply import Express and call its top-level function: const app = express();.
  • Install: npm install express.
  • Unopinionated: Express does not force a fixed folder structure. You decide how to organize the code.
  • Minimalist: The core is small. Extra features come from middleware (cors, helmet, multer, morgan).
  • Main features: routing (app.get, app.post), middleware (app.use), easy responses (res.json, res.send), static files, template engines.
मराठी सारांश (Express.js):
  • Express.js हा Node.js साठीचा fast, minimal web framework आहे.
  • Native http module मध्ये खूप if/else लिहावे लागतात. Express मध्ये तेच काम सोप्या, स्वच्छ code ने होते.
  • Unopinionated: folder structure तुम्ही ठरवा, Express जबरदस्ती करत नाही.
  • Minimalist: core छोटा. गरजेप्रमाणे middleware (cors, multer) जोडायचे.
  • Setup: npm install express → const app = express() → routes → app.listen(3000).
  • उदाहरण: Node.js = कच्चे साहित्य, Express = तयार किचन सेटअप. स्वयंपाक लवकर होतो.

Q. What is the role of a Framework?

Concept:
  • A framework provides structure, reusable libraries and built-in tools on top of a runtime environment.
  • It simplifies development and enforces best practices.
  • It helps build scalable applications quickly.
  • Simplified Development: A framework provides pre-built libraries, classes, and functions that save developers from writing repetitive code. (e.g., Instead of handling raw HTTP requests, Express.js simplifies routing and middleware handling).
  • Provides Structure: Frameworks enforce a consistent architecture (like MVC), making large applications easier to build, scale, and maintain.
  • Abstraction Over Runtime Environment: A framework sits on top of the runtime environment (like Node.js) and provides higher-level features such as routing, ORM, authentication, and security.
  • Improves Productivity and Reusability: Developers can focus on business logic rather than low-level details because the framework already provides solutions to common problems.
मराठी सारांश (Framework):
  • Framework म्हणजे तयार असलेल्या टूल्स आणि लायब्ररींचा संच.
  • यामुळे डेव्हलपर्सला सारखा-सारखा तोच कोड लिहावा लागत नाही, आणि ॲप्लिकेशन झटपट आणि सुरक्षितपणे तयार होते.
  • Simplified Development: तयार functions मिळतात. उदा. raw HTTP ऐवजी Express ने सोपे routing.
  • Structure: MVC सारखी ठराविक रचना, त्यामुळे मोठे projects सांभाळणे सोपे.
  • Abstraction: runtime (Node.js) च्या वर बसून routing, authentication, security सारख्या high-level सुविधा देतो.
  • Productivity: developer ला low-level गोष्टींऐवजी business logic वर लक्ष देता येते.
  • उदाहरण: घर बांधताना तयार आराखडा (framework). प्रत्येक वीट कुठे ठेवायची हे शून्यातून ठरवावे लागत नाही.

Q. What are the differences between a Framework and a Runtime Environment?

  • Runtime Environment: Provides the infrastructure required to execute code. It manages things like memory allocation, I/O handling, execution context, and APIs. (Example: Node.js is a runtime environment for executing JavaScript outside the browser).
  • Framework: A set of tools, libraries, and conventions built on top of a runtime environment. Its goal is to simplify application development by providing reusable solutions like routing and middleware. (Example: Express.js is a framework that runs on Node.js to make building APIs easier).
मराठी सारांश (Framework vs Runtime):
  • Runtime: code चालवण्यासाठी लागणारी पायाभूत व्यवस्था (memory, I/O, APIs). उदा. Node.js.
  • Framework: runtime च्या वर बनलेली tools आणि नियमांची रचना, app लवकर बनवण्यासाठी. उदा. Express.js.
  • नाते: Framework ला runtime लागतो (Express ला Node.js), पण runtime ला framework लागत नाही.
  • उदाहरण: Runtime = रस्ता आणि वीज. Framework = त्यावर चालणारी तयार बस सेवा.

Q. Differences between Node.js and Express.js

Node.js (Runtime Environment) Express.js (Framework)
A server-side JavaScript runtime environment built on Google’s V8 engine. A web application framework built on top of Node.js.
Allows JavaScript to run outside the browser, providing core modules (http, fs, os) for backend development. Provides features like routing, middleware, request/response handling to simplify server and API development.
It is low-level, meaning you need to manually handle tasks like setting up servers and parsing URLs. Makes Node.js development much faster, more structured, and highly abstracted.
In Short:
  • Node.js is a runtime environment that lets JavaScript run on the server.
  • Express.js is a framework built on Node.js.
  • Express makes building web apps and APIs easier with features such as routing and middleware.
Code Example: Basic Express Server
const express = require('express');
const app = express(); // Create an express application

app.get("/", (req, res) => {
    res.send("Hello World from Express!");
});

app.listen(8000, () => {
    console.log("Server started on port 8000...");
});
मराठी सारांश:
  • Express.js हा Node.js चा एक Framework आहे.
  • Node.js: V8 वर बनलेला server-side runtime. http, fs देतो, पण low-level आहे. Server, routing, URL parsing सगळे हाताने.
  • Express.js: Node.js च्या वर बनलेला framework. Routing, middleware, req / res सोपे करतो.
  • native http module मध्ये खूप गुंतागुंतीचा कोड लिहावा लागतो, पण Express मध्ये तोच कोड एकदम सोपा आणि वाचायला सोईस्कर होतो.
  • Code: app.get('/', (req, res) => res.send('Hello')) आणि app.listen(8000). एवढ्यात server तयार.

Q. What is an Express Route (Routing)?

Definition & Concept:
  • Routing refers to how an application's endpoints (URIs) respond to client requests.
  • An Express Route is a specific path on the server (like /about or /api/users) combined with an HTTP method (GET, POST, PUT, DELETE).
  • When a client makes a request that matches a defined route, Express executes the associated callback function (the route handler) to send a response.
Syntax & Implementation:
  • Syntax: app.METHOD(PATH, HANDLER)
  • app is an instance of express.
  • METHOD is an HTTP request method, in lowercase (e.g., get, post).
  • PATH is the path on the server (the endpoint URL).
  • HANDLER is the callback function executed when the route is matched. It receives (req, res) objects.
  • Wildcard Route (*): To handle 404 Pages (Page Not Found), you can create a route for * at the very bottom of your routing file.
Code Example: Handling Multiple Routes
const express = require("express");
const app = express();

// 1. Home route (sending HTML)
app.get("/", (req, res) => {
  res.send("<h1>Welcome to Home Page!</h1>");
});

// 2. About route (sending an Object)
app.get("/about", (req, res) => {
  res.send({ name: "Vishal", age: 23 });
});

// 3. Contact route (sending an Array of Objects)
app.get("/contact", (req, res) => {
  res.send([{ name: "Vishal" }, { name: "Rahul" }]);
});

// 4. 404 Handler (MUST be placed at the end)
app.get("*", (req, res) => {
  res.status(404).send("<h1>404: Page Not Found!</h1>");
});

app.listen(3000, () => console.log("Running..."));
मराठी सारांश:
  • Express मध्ये Routing खूप सोपी असते.
  • Syntax: app.METHOD(PATH, HANDLER). METHOD = get / post / put / delete. PATH = URL. HANDLER = (req, res) => {}.
  • app.get('/path', callback) वापरून आपण वेगवेगळे पेजेस बनवू शकतो.
  • res.send() ने HTML, object, array काहीही पाठवता येते.
  • जर युजरने चुकीचा URL टाकला तर त्याला 404 एरर दाखवण्यासाठी शेवटी * (Wildcard) route वापरतात. तो नेहमी शेवटी ठेवा, कारण Express वरून खाली route जुळवतो.
  • टीप (Express 5): '*' ऐवजी '/*splat' किंवा शेवटी app.use((req, res) => res.status(404).send(...)) वापरा.

Q. res.send() vs res.json()

  • res.send(): A general-purpose method. It automatically detects the content type (HTML, String, Array, Object) and sets the header accordingly.
  • res.json(): Specifically used to send JSON data. It guarantees the header is set to Content-Type: application/json and correctly handles null/undefined values by converting them to an empty JSON object.
मराठी सारांश:
  • res.send() आपण HTML किंवा कोणताही डेटा पाठवण्यासाठी वापरू शकतो. तो data पाहून Content-Type आपोआप ठरवतो (string → html, object → json).
  • पण जर तुम्हाला नक्कीच JSON डेटा पाठवायचा असेल, तर res.json() वापरणे जास्त सुरक्षित आणि योग्य आहे.
  • res.json() नेहमी Content-Type: application/json सेट करतो. null / undefined पण valid JSON म्हणून पाठवतो.
  • Rule: APIs साठी नेहमी res.json(), web pages साठी res.send() किंवा res.render().
Code Example: send vs json
app.get('/page', (req, res) => res.send('<h1>Hi</h1>'));        // text/html
app.get('/api/user', (req, res) => res.json({ id: 1 }));        // application/json
app.get('/api/err', (req, res) => res.status(404).json({ error: 'Not found' }));

Q. Serving Static Files (CSS, Images, JS)

  • By default, Express does not allow the browser to access files in your project directory for security reasons.
  • To serve static assets (like CSS files, images, or client-side JavaScript), you must use the built-in middleware express.static().
  • To apply middleware in Express, you use the app.use() keyword.
Code Example: Serving a "public" folder
const express = require('express');
const path = require('path');
const app = express();

// 1. Get the absolute path to the "public" folder
const staticPath = path.join(__dirname, 'public');

// 2. Tell Express to use this folder for static files
app.use(express.static(staticPath));

app.get('/', (req, res) => {
    res.send('Hello World'); // HTML will now be able to load CSS/Images from /public!
});

app.listen(8000, () => console.log('Server started'));
मराठी सारांश:
  • Security साठी Express default ने project मधल्या files browser ला देत नाही.
  • HTML मध्ये CSS किंवा Images जोडण्यासाठी ते फाईल्स Public करावे लागतात.
  • त्यासाठी express.static('फोल्डरचे नाव') हा middleware वापरतात.
  • Middleware लागू करण्यासाठी app.use() वापरले जाते.
  • महत्वाचे: URL मध्ये folder चे नाव येत नाही. public/css/style.css हे browser मध्ये /css/style.css असे मिळते.
  • path.join(__dirname, 'public') वापरा, म्हणजे server कुठूनही चालवला तरी path बरोबर राहतो.

Q. Parsing Request Bodies (express.json & express.urlencoded)

Concept & Syntax:
  • In native HTTP, reading a request body requires manually listening to data and end events. Express provides built-in middlewares to do this automatically.
  • express.json(): Parses incoming requests with JSON payloads. It automatically converts the JSON string into a JS object accessible via req.body.
  • express.urlencoded({ extended: true }): Parses incoming requests with URL-encoded payloads (like HTML form submissions). The extended: true option allows parsing nested objects using the qs library.
  • You must apply these middlewares globally using app.use() before defining your routes.
Code Example: Reading req.body
const express = require('express');
const app = express();

app.use(express.json());                          // for JSON bodies (APIs, fetch, Postman)
app.use(express.urlencoded({ extended: true }));  // for HTML form submissions

app.post('/users', (req, res) => {
    const { name, email } = req.body;             // ✅ already a JS object
    res.status(201).json({ message: 'User created', name, email });
});
// Without the middleware above → req.body is undefined
मराठी सारांश (Request Body Parsing):
  • Native HTTP मध्ये body वाचायला req.on('data') आणि req.on('end') स्वतः लिहावे लागतात. Express हे middleware ने आपोआप करतो.
  • express.json(): JSON body (Postman, fetch, APIs) → req.body मध्ये JS object.
  • express.urlencoded({ extended: true }): HTML form चा data → req.body. extended: true मुळे nested objects पण parse होतात.
  • Rule: हे app.use() ने routes च्या आधी लावा. नाहीतर req.body = undefined.
  • उदाहरण: बंद पाकीट (raw body) उघडून आतले पत्र (object) वाचायला देणारा मदतनीस.

Q. What is Express Router (Modular Routing)?

Definition & Concept:
  • Express Router is a built-in class in Express.js used to create modular, mountable route handlers.
  • A Router instance is often referred to as a "mini-application" because it acts as a complete routing and middleware system, capable of handling its own isolated HTTP requests.
  • The Problem: In a real-world production application, you might have hundreds of endpoints (e.g., 30 routes for Users, 40 for Products, 30 for Orders). If you write all these app.get() and app.post() methods in a single app.js file, the file becomes massive, chaotic, and impossible to maintain.
  • The Solution: Express Router solves this by allowing you to split your routes into separate, dedicated files based on their specific domain or feature (e.g., keeping all user-related routes strictly in routes/userRoutes.js).
  • Once these modular router files are created and exported, they are simply imported back into the main server file and "mounted" to a specific base URL path using the app.use() method.
Step-by-Step Implementation:
  • Step 1 (Create the Router): First, import Express and call express.Router() to create a new router object. This object will act as your modular mini-app. 
    // In routes/users.js
const express = require('express');
const router = express.Router();
  • Step 2 (Define Routes): Instead of using app.get(), attach your endpoints directly to the router object using router.get(), router.post(), etc. Note: The paths you define here are relative to where the router will be mounted later. 
    router.get('/profile', (req, res) => {
    res.send('User Profile Data');
});
  • Step 3 (Export the Router): To make this router available to your main application, you must export it at the bottom of the file. 
    module.exports = router;
  • Step 4 (Import & Register in Main App): Finally, in your central app.js file, import the router and use app.use() to mount it to a specific base URL prefix (e.g., /users). Because of this prefix, the /profile route defined in Step 2 is now automatically accessible at /users/profile. 
    // In main app.js
const express = require('express');
const app = express();

const userRoutes = require('./routes/users'); // Import
app.use('/users', userRoutes); // Register with base-path

app.listen(3000, () => console.log('Server running...'));
मराठी सारांश (Express Router):
  • जर आपण सर्व Routes एकाच app.js फाईलमध्ये लिहिले तर कोड खूप मोठा आणि गोंधळाचा होतो.
  • यासाठी आपण express.Router() वापरून Routes चे तुकडे करतो (जसे Users चे routes वेगळ्या फाईलमध्ये, Products चे वेगळ्या).
  • आणि मग त्यांना मेन फाईलमध्ये app.use('/basepath', router) ने जोडतो, ज्यामुळे MVC architecture बनवणे सोपे जाते.
  • Steps: (1) const router = express.Router() (2) router.get('/profile', ...) (3) module.exports = router (4) app.use('/users', router) → URL /users/profile.
  • Router मधले paths relative असतात. app.use मधला prefix पुढे जोडला जातो.
  • Router-level middleware: router.use(auth) ने फक्त त्या router चे routes संरक्षित होतात.
  • Router ला "mini-app" म्हणतात, कारण त्याचे स्वतःचे routes आणि middleware असतात.
  • उदाहरण: मोठ्या mall मध्ये प्रत्येक मजल्याचा वेगळा विभाग (router). Main gate (app) फक्त कोणत्या मजल्यावर जायचे ते सांगतो.

Q. What is Middleware in Express? (In Detail)

Concept:
  • Middleware is a function that runs between the request arriving and the response being sent. It sits in the "middle" of the request–response cycle.
  • It receives three arguments: req (request), res (response) and next (a function that passes control to the next middleware).
  • A middleware can:
    • Run any code (logging, timing).
    • Modify req / res (e.g. add req.user, parse req.body).
    • End the cycle by sending a response (e.g. 401 Unauthorized).
    • Call next() to pass control onward.
  • An Express app is basically a chain (pipeline) of middleware functions. Even route handlers are middleware.
  • Simple example: airport security checks. Ticket check → ID check → baggage scan → boarding. Each checkpoint either lets you go to the next one (next()) or stops you (sends a response).
Diagram: Request Flowing Through the Middleware Chain
Syntax & Arguments: function middleware(req, res, next) { ... }
  • req: The request object. You can read it or add properties (e.g. req.user, req.startTime).
  • res: The response object. Use it to end the cycle (res.status(401).json(...)).
  • next: Call next() to go to the next middleware. Call next(err) to jump straight to the error-handling middleware.
  • Rule: every middleware must either send a response or call next(). If it does neither, the request hangs forever.
  • Registering: app.use(fn) (all routes and methods), app.use('/api', fn) (paths starting with /api), or per-route: app.get('/admin', fn1, fn2, handler).
5 Types of Middleware:
  • 1. Application-level: Bound to the app with app.use() or app.METHOD(). Runs for the whole app (or a path). Example: a request logger.
  • 2. Router-level: Same as application-level, but bound to an express.Router() with router.use(). Runs only for that router's routes. Example: auth check for all /admin routes.
  • 3. Built-in: Shipped with Express: express.json(), express.urlencoded(), express.static().
  • 4. Third-party: Installed from npm: cors, helmet, morgan, cookie-parser, multer.
  • 5. Error-handling: Has 4 arguments (err, req, res, next). Runs only when next(err) is called or an error is thrown. Must be registered last.
Code Example: All 5 Types Together
const express = require('express');
const cors = require('cors');
const app = express();

// 4. Third-party
app.use(cors());

// 3. Built-in
app.use(express.json());

// 1. Application-level (custom logger, runs for EVERY request)
app.use((req, res, next) => {
    req.startTime = Date.now();
    console.log(`${req.method} ${req.url}`);
    next();                                   // ✅ pass control on
});

// Custom auth middleware (reusable)
function requireAuth(req, res, next) {
    if (req.headers.authorization !== 'Bearer secret123') {
        return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Unauthorized' });  // ❌ chain stops here
    }
    req.user = { id: 1, role: 'admin' };       // add data for later handlers
    next();
}

// 2. Router-level
const adminRouter = express.Router();
adminRouter.use(requireAuth);                  // protects ALL admin routes
adminRouter.get('/dashboard', (req, res) => res.json({ hello: req.user.role }));
app.use('/admin', adminRouter);

// Per-route middleware (only this route)
app.get('/profile', requireAuth, (req, res) => res.json(req.user));

// Route that throws → goes to error handler
app.get('/crash', (req, res, next) => next(new Error('Something broke')));

// 5. Error-handling (4 args, LAST)
app.use((err, req, res, next) => {
    res.status(500).json({ error: err.message });
});

app.listen(3000);
Order of Execution (Very Important):
  • Middleware runs in the exact order it is registered, from top to bottom.
  • express.json() must come before routes that read req.body.
  • Auth middleware must come before the routes it protects.
  • The 404 handler and the error handler must come last.
  • Once a response is sent, later middleware for that request doesn't run (unless next() was also called, which causes "headers already sent" errors).
मराठी सारांश (Middleware):
  • Middleware म्हणजे काय? request आल्यापासून response जाईपर्यंत "मध्ये" चालणारे function. (req, res, next) मिळतात.
  • काय करू शकतो? code चालवणे (logging), req / res बदलणे (req.user), response पाठवून थांबवणे (401), किंवा next() ने पुढे पाठवणे.
  • नियम: एकतर response पाठवा किंवा next() call करा. दोन्ही नाही केले तर request कायम अडकून राहतो (hang).
  • next(err): थेट error-handling middleware कडे जातो.
  • 5 प्रकार: (1) Application-level (app.use), (2) Router-level (router.use), (3) Built-in (express.json, express.static), (4) Third-party (cors, helmet, morgan), (5) Error-handling (4 arguments, शेवटी).
  • क्रम महत्वाचा: वरून खाली क्रमाने चालतात. express.json() routes च्या आधी, auth संरक्षित routes च्या आधी, 404 आणि error handler सर्वात शेवटी.
  • उदाहरण: विमानतळावरील तपासण्या. Ticket → ID → bag scan → boarding. प्रत्येक checkpoint पुढे जाऊ देतो (next) किंवा थांबवतो (response).

Q. req.params vs req.query vs req.body

Concept:
  • Data from the client can reach your Express route in three main places.
  • req.params → values from route parameters in the URL path: /users/:id + /users/42 → { id: '42' }.
  • req.query → values from the query string after ?: /products?category=shoes&page=2 → { category: 'shoes', page: '2' }.
  • req.body → data sent in the request body (POST/PUT/PATCH), usually JSON or form data. Needs express.json() / express.urlencoded().
  • Simple example: ordering food. params = which restaurant (/restaurants/12); query = filters (?veg=true&sort=price); body = the actual order form (items, address).
Pointreq.paramsreq.queryreq.body
Comes fromURL path segmentURL after ?Request body
Example URL/users/42/users?role=adminPOST /users + JSON
Route definition/users/:id/users (no change)/users (no change)
Used forIdentify one specific resourceFilter, search, sort, paginateCreate / update data
HTTP methodsAnyMostly GETPOST, PUT, PATCH
Required?Yes (part of the route)OptionalDepends on the route
Needs middlewareNoNo✅ express.json()
Visible in URL / logsYesYesNo (better for sensitive data)
Code Example: All Three in One Route
app.use(express.json());

// Request:  PATCH /shops/7/products/15?notify=true
// Body:     { "price": 499, "stock": 20 }
app.patch('/shops/:shopId/products/:productId', (req, res) => {
    console.log(req.params);   // { shopId: '7', productId: '15' }   ← strings!
    console.log(req.query);    // { notify: 'true' }                 ← string, not boolean
    console.log(req.body);     // { price: 499, stock: 20 }          ← real JSON types

    const productId = Number(req.params.productId);   // convert type
    const notify = req.query.notify === 'true';
    res.json({ productId, notify, updated: req.body });
});

// Optional query with default values (pagination)
app.get('/products', (req, res) => {
    const page  = Number(req.query.page)  || 1;
    const limit = Number(req.query.limit) || 10;
    res.json({ page, limit });
});
  • Type trap: req.params and req.query values are always strings. Convert with Number(), or compare with === 'true'.
  • Missing values: a missing query key is undefined, so give defaults (|| 1).
  • Multiple params: /shops/:shopId/products/:productId → both appear in req.params.
  • Optional param (Express 5): /users{/:id}. (Express 4 used /users/:id?.)
  • Security: never trust any of the three. Always validate them (see Security Basics).
  • req.body is undefined? You forgot app.use(express.json()), or the client didn't send Content-Type: application/json.
मराठी सारांश (req.params vs req.query vs req.body):
  • req.params: URL च्या path मधील values. Route: /users/:id, request: /users/42 → req.params.id = '42'. एक ठराविक resource ओळखण्यासाठी.
  • req.query: URL मध्ये ? नंतरचे values. /products?category=shoes&page=2. Filter, search, sort, pagination साठी. Optional.
  • req.body: request च्या body मधला data (POST / PUT / PATCH). नवीन data बनवणे / बदलणे. express.json() लागतो. URL मध्ये दिसत नाही.
  • महत्वाचे: params आणि query नेहमी string असतात. Number() ने बदला. Query नसेल तर undefined, म्हणून default द्या.
  • req.body = undefined? express.json() विसरलात, किंवा client ने JSON header पाठवला नाही.
  • तिघांवरही विश्वास ठेवू नका. नेहमी validate करा.
  • उदाहरण: जेवण order करणे. params = कोणते hotel (/hotels/12), query = filters (?veg=true), body = order form (पदार्थ, पत्ता).

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What is Middleware in Express? (Basic Definition)
  • Middleware functions are functions that have access to the request (req) and response (res) objects. They sit "in the middle" of the request-response cycle and can modify data, run code, or end the request. (e.g., express.static is a middleware).
Q2. Why is the 404 route app.get('*') placed at the very end of the file?
  • Express checks routes from top to bottom. The * (wildcard) means "match absolutely everything". If you put it at the top, it will immediately catch every request (even valid ones like /home) and return 404. It must be a fallback at the bottom.
Q3. What is the difference between res.send() in Express and res.end() in native Node.js?
  • In native Node.js, you have to manually set the Content-Type with res.writeHead() and then call res.end(). Express's res.send() automatically determines the Content-Type, sets the headers, and implicitly ends the response for you.
Q4. How do you parse incoming request bodies in Express.js?
  • The incoming data is sent as a stream. To parse the request body easily, we use middleware like express.urlencoded({extended: true}) or the body-parser third-party package.
Q5. Is it possible to use all HTTP requests (GET, POST, PUT, PATCH) with middleware?
  • Yes, it is possible. You can chain middleware or apply it to specific HTTP verbs and routes.
Q6. How do we handle modular routing in Express?
  • Express gives a simple way to outsource routing using express.Router(). This allows splitting routes into separate files (e.g. admin routes, shop routes) for better MVC architecture.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Q1 (Middleware म्हणजे काय?): req आणि res मिळणारे, request-response च्या "मध्ये" बसणारे function. Data बदलू शकते, code चालवू शकते, किंवा request संपवू शकते. उदा. express.static.
  • Q2 (404 route शेवटी का?): Express वरून-खाली (top to bottom) routes चेक करते. जर * (सगळे routes) सर्वात वर ठेवले, तर ते सर्व पानांना 404 एरर दाखवेल!
  • Q3 (res.send vs res.end): Native मध्ये writeHead + end हाताने. Express चा send Content-Type ठरवतो, headers लावतो आणि response संपवतो.
  • Q4 (Body parse कसे?): express.json() / express.urlencoded() (पूर्वी body-parser package).
  • Q5: हो, middleware कोणत्याही HTTP method आणि ठराविक routes वर लावता येते.
  • Q6 (Modular routing): express.Router() ने routes वेगळ्या files मध्ये (admin, shop) ठेवतो.

Quick Revision Summary

Concept / Method Description
express() Creates the Express application instance.
app.get(path, callback) Defines a route to handle GET requests.
res.send() / res.json() Sends data back to the client (auto-sets headers).
app.get('*', ...) Wildcard route, used as a fallback for 404 Not Found.
app.use(express.static(path)) Middleware to serve static files (CSS, Images, etc.).

Chapter 16: Template Engines (HBS) & Partials

Q. What is a Template Engine?

Definition:
  • A template engine lets you write HTML-like template files with placeholders (for example, {{name}}).
  • At runtime, it replaces those placeholders with real data (dynamic content).
  • It then produces a final HTML page, which is sent to the client. This is called server-side rendering.
  • Simple example: a wedding invitation card with a blank for the name. The same card is printed with a different name for each guest.
  • Why? It separates HTML structure from backend logic. Instead of writing messy res.send("<h1>" + name + "</h1>"), you just pass data to a cleaner HTML-like file.
  • Popular engines in Express: Pug, EJS, and Handlebars (HBS).
  • In Express, we use res.render('filename', { data }) instead of res.send() to display a template.
Code Example: Setting up Handlebars (HBS)
