const express = require("express");
const path = require("path");
const app = express();

// 1. Tell Express where the views folder is (mandatory directory)
const viewsPath = path.join(__dirname, "views");
app.set("views", viewsPath);

// 2. Set the Template Engine
app.set("view engine", "hbs");

app.get("/", (req, res) => {
    // 3. Render the "index.hbs" file and pass dynamic data
    res.render("index", {
        name: "Vishal Shinde"
    });
});

app.listen(8000, () => console.log("Server started..."));
मराठी सारांश:
  • Template Engine चा वापर करून आपण HTML मध्ये dynamic data (उदा. युजरचे नाव) टाकू शकतो.
  • का? res.send("<h1>" + name + "</h1>") सारखा गोंधळ टाळण्यासाठी. HTML आणि backend logic वेगळे राहतात.
  • लोकप्रिय engines: Pug, EJS, Handlebars (HBS).
  • Setup: app.set('views', path) (templates कुठे आहेत) आणि app.set('view engine', 'hbs') (कोणते engine).
  • आणि res.send() ऐवजी res.render('index', { name: 'Vishal' }) वापरतो.
  • index.hbs मध्ये: <h1>Hello {{name}}</h1> → browser ला मिळते <h1>Hello Vishal</h1>.
  • उदाहरण: लग्नपत्रिका. नावाची जागा रिकामी. प्रत्येक पाहुण्यासाठी वेगळे नाव छापले जाते.

Q. What are Partials?

Definition:
  • Partials are small, reusable template pieces that represent part of a web page.
  • Typical examples: a common header, navbar or footer.
  • In Handlebars, a partial is included with {{> header}}.
  • Instead of writing the navbar and footer code on 50 different pages, you write it once in a partial file (like header.hbs).
  • Then, you just inject that partial into your main pages.
  • Benefits: Reusability, easy maintenance (change the navbar once, it updates everywhere), and consistent design.
Code Example: Registering Partials with HBS
const express = require("express");
const path = require("path");
const hbs = require("hbs"); // Require HBS to register partials
const app = express();

const viewPath = path.join(__dirname, "views");
const partialsPath = path.join(__dirname, "partials");

app.set("views", viewPath);
app.set("view engine", "hbs");

// Tell HBS where to find the partials folder
hbs.registerPartials(partialsPath);

app.get("/", (req, res) => {
  res.render("index", { name: "Vishal" });
});

app.listen(8000, () => console.log("Running..."));
मराठी सारांश:
  • Partials म्हणजे website चे असे छोटे भाग जे अनेक पानांवर सारखे असतात (उदा. Navbar किंवा Footer).
  • हे भाग एकदाच लिहून आपण त्यांना अनेक पेजेस मध्ये वापरू शकतो.
  • HBS मध्ये यासाठी hbs.registerPartials(partialsPath) वापरावे लागते (कोणत्या folder मध्ये partials आहेत ते सांगणे).
  • वापर: page मध्ये {{> header}} लिहिले की partials/header.hbs चा code तिथे येतो.
  • फायदे: reusability, navbar एकदाच बदलला की 50 पानांवर बदलतो, design सगळीकडे सारखे.
  • उदाहरण: वहीच्या प्रत्येक पानावर शाळेचा शिक्का (stamp). तो एकदाच बनवला, सगळीकडे वापरला.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What is the default directory for template files in Express?
  • By default, Express looks for template files in a folder named views in the root directory. However, you can change this default location using app.set('views', customPath).
Q2. What is the difference between res.send() and res.render()?
  • res.send() sends raw data (strings, objects, arrays) directly to the browser. res.render() takes a template file, injects variables/data into it on the server side, generates the final HTML, and then sends that HTML to the browser.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Q1 (default folder): Express default ने views नावाचा folder शोधतो.
  • Interview मध्ये विचारतात की views फोल्डरचे नाव बदलता येते का? उत्तर: हो, app.set('views', customPath) वापरून आपण दुसरे नाव (उदा. templates) ठेवू शकतो.
  • Q2 (send vs render): res.send() = raw data थेट पाठवतो. res.render() = template मध्ये data भरून, server वर HTML तयार करून मग पाठवतो.
  • Template engine set करणे: app.set('view engine', 'hbs'). मग res.render('index', { name }). Extension लिहावे लागत नाही.
  • Partials: hbs.registerPartials(path) आणि page मध्ये {{> header}}.
  • Escaping: {{name}} HTML escape करतो (XSS पासून सुरक्षित). {{{name}}} raw HTML, user data साठी टाळा.

Quick Revision Summary

Method Description
app.set("view engine", "hbs") Tells Express which template engine to use.
app.set("views", path) Changes or sets the directory where template files are stored.
res.render("file", { data }) Renders a template file and injects dynamic data into it.
hbs.registerPartials(path) Registers a folder containing reusable components (header, footer).
SECTION 5: RESTful APIs

Chapter 17: RESTful APIs & Express Router

Q. What is an API?

Definition:
  • API = Application Programming Interface.
  • It is a set of rules and protocols that lets one software application communicate with another.
  • One side asks (request), the other side answers (response), in an agreed format (usually JSON).
  • Every time you use an app like Facebook, check the weather, or book a flight, you are using APIs to fetch data from remote servers.
  • Example: A weather app calls GET https://api.weather.com/today?city=Pune and receives { "temp": 31 }.
  • The client does not need to know how the server works internally. It only needs the API's endpoints and format.
मराठी सारांश:
  • API म्हणजे दोन सॉफ्टवेअरला एकमेकांशी बोलण्यासाठी बनवलेला मार्ग (नियमांचा संच).
  • जसे आपण हॉटेलमध्ये वेटरला ऑर्डर देतो आणि तो किचनमधून जेवण आणतो, तसेच API आपल्या App कडून Request घेते आणि Server कडून Data आणते.
  • आपल्याला किचन कसे चालते हे माहीत असण्याची गरज नसते. फक्त menu (API endpoints) माहीत हवा.
  • उदाहरण: Weather app → GET /today?city=Pune → उत्तर { "temp": 31 }.
  • Facebook login, Google Maps, UPI payment हे सगळे APIs वापरतात.

Q. What is a RESTful API?

Definition:
  • REST stands for REpresentational State Transfer.
  • A RESTful API follows the REST architectural style.
  • It uses standard HTTP methods (GET, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE) on URLs to read and change data (resources).
  • 1. Stateless Communication: The server does NOT store any client session or history. Every single HTTP request must contain all the necessary information to complete it.
  • 2. Resource-Based: Data is treated as "Resources" (like users, posts, photos). Each resource is accessed via a unique URI (e.g., https://api.example.com/users).
  • 3. JSON Format: Data is typically exchanged in JSON format because it is lightweight and easy for JS to parse.

Q. Standard HTTP Methods in REST

  • GET: To read or retrieve data.
  • POST: To create new data or a new resource.
  • PUT: To update an existing resource (Full replacement).
  • PATCH: To update an existing resource (Partial update).
  • DELETE: To remove a resource.
Action (CRUD)MethodEndpointSuccess Status
Get all usersGET/api/users200 OK
Get one userGET/api/users/5200 OK
Create userPOST/api/users201 Created
Replace userPUT/api/users/5200 OK
Update some fieldsPATCH/api/users/5200 OK
Delete userDELETE/api/users/5204 No Content
मराठी सारांश (REST API आणि HTTP Methods):
  • REST = REpresentational State Transfer. APIs बनवण्याची एक पद्धत (architecture style).
  • Stateless: server client ची मागची माहिती लक्षात ठेवत नाही. प्रत्येक request मध्ये सगळी माहिती (token, id) हवी.
  • Resource-based: data = resource (users, posts). प्रत्येकाचा वेगळा URL: /api/users.
  • JSON: data सहसा JSON मध्ये.
  • GET = वाचणे, POST = नवीन बनवणे, PUT = पूर्ण बदलणे, PATCH = थोडे बदलणे, DELETE = काढणे.
  • नियम: URL मध्ये नाम (noun) ठेवा, क्रियापद (verb) नको. /api/users बरोबर, /getUsers चुकीचे. Action method सांगते.

Q. Complete CRUD REST API Example (All 5 Routes in Express)

Concept:
  • CRUD = Create, Read, Update, Delete: the four basic operations on any data.
  • In REST, they map to HTTP methods: POST (create), GET (read), PUT/PATCH (update), DELETE (delete).
  • Below is a complete, working users API. An in-memory array stands in for a database, so you can run it without one.
  • Simple example: a contacts app on your phone: add a contact (POST), view the list (GET), edit (PUT/PATCH), delete (DELETE).
Code Example: Users CRUD API (app.js)
const express = require('express');
const app = express();
app.use(express.json());                                   // read JSON bodies into req.body

let users = [                                              // fake "database"
    { id: 1, name: 'Vishal', email: 'vishal@test.com' },
    { id: 2, name: 'Amit',   email: 'amit@test.com' }
];
let nextId = 3;

// 1. READ ALL  →  GET /api/users   (optional ?name=vi filter)
app.get('/api/users', (req, res) => {
    const { name } = req.query;
    const result = name
        ? users.filter(u => u.name.toLowerCase().includes(name.toLowerCase()))
        : users;
    res.status(200).json(result);
});

// 2. READ ONE  →  GET /api/users/:id
app.get('/api/users/:id', (req, res) => {
    const user = users.find(u => u.id === Number(req.params.id));
    if (!user) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'User not found' });
    res.status(200).json(user);
});

// 3. CREATE  →  POST /api/users   body: { "name": "...", "email": "..." }
app.post('/api/users', (req, res) => {
    const { name, email } = req.body;
    if (!name || !email) {
        return res.status(400).json({ error: 'name and email are required' });
    }
    const newUser = { id: nextId++, name, email };
    users.push(newUser);
    res.status(201).json(newUser);                          // 201 Created
});

// 4a. FULL UPDATE  →  PUT /api/users/:id   (replace the whole user)
app.put('/api/users/:id', (req, res) => {
    const index = users.findIndex(u => u.id === Number(req.params.id));
    if (index === -1) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'User not found' });
    const { name, email } = req.body;
    if (!name || !email) {
        return res.status(400).json({ error: 'PUT needs the full object: name and email' });
    }
    users[index] = { id: users[index].id, name, email };
    res.status(200).json(users[index]);
});

// 4b. PARTIAL UPDATE  →  PATCH /api/users/:id   (change only sent fields)
app.patch('/api/users/:id', (req, res) => {
    const user = users.find(u => u.id === Number(req.params.id));
    if (!user) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'User not found' });
    const { name, email } = req.body;
    if (name !== undefined) user.name = name;
    if (email !== undefined) user.email = email;
    res.status(200).json(user);
});

// 5. DELETE  →  DELETE /api/users/:id
app.delete('/api/users/:id', (req, res) => {
    const exists = users.some(u => u.id === Number(req.params.id));
    if (!exists) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'User not found' });
    users = users.filter(u => u.id !== Number(req.params.id));
    res.status(204).send();                                  // 204 No Content
});

// 404 for unknown routes (keep LAST)
app.use((req, res) => res.status(404).json({ error: 'Route not found' }));

app.listen(3000, () => console.log('API running on http://localhost:3000'));
Testing the API (curl / Postman)
GET    http://localhost:3000/api/users                → [ {id:1...}, {id:2...} ]
GET    http://localhost:3000/api/users?name=vi        → [ {id:1, name:'Vishal'...} ]
GET    http://localhost:3000/api/users/1              → {id:1, name:'Vishal'...}
POST   http://localhost:3000/api/users   {"name":"Rahul","email":"r@test.com"} → 201
PUT    http://localhost:3000/api/users/1 {"name":"Vishal S","email":"v@new.com"} → 200
PATCH  http://localhost:3000/api/users/1 {"email":"only@email.com"}              → 200
DELETE http://localhost:3000/api/users/2              → 204 (empty body)
GET    http://localhost:3000/api/users/99             → 404 {"error":"User not found"}
Key Points Used in This Example:
  • req.params: Values from the URL path. /api/users/:id + request /api/users/5 → req.params.id === '5' (always a string, so convert with Number()).
  • req.query: Values after ? in the URL. ?name=vi → req.query.name === 'vi'. Used for filtering, search, pagination.
  • req.body: Data sent in the request body (POST/PUT/PATCH). Needs app.use(express.json()).
  • res.status(code).json(data): Sets the HTTP status and sends JSON. Always send the right status code.
  • Status codes: 200 OK (read/update), 201 Created (POST), 204 No Content (DELETE), 400 Bad Request (invalid input), 404 Not Found (missing user/route).
  • return before res.status(...) in error checks, so the rest of the handler doesn't run (prevents "headers already sent" errors).
  • PUT vs PATCH: PUT needs the whole object (replace); PATCH changes only the fields sent.
  • In a real app, replace the array with a database (MongoDB/MySQL) and move routes into express.Router() + controllers (MVC).
मराठी सारांश (Complete CRUD API):
  • CRUD = Create (POST), Read (GET), Update (PUT / PATCH), Delete (DELETE). कोणत्याही data वरची 4 मूलभूत कामे.
  • GET /api/users → सगळे users (200). ?name=vi ने filter.
  • GET /api/users/:id → एक user (200), नसेल तर 404.
  • POST /api/users → नवीन user (201 Created). name / email नसेल तर 400.
  • PUT /api/users/:id → पूर्ण user बदला (सगळे fields हवेत). PATCH → फक्त पाठवलेले fields बदला.
  • DELETE /api/users/:id → user काढा (204 No Content).
  • req.params = URL मधील :id (string असतो, Number() करा). req.query = ? नंतरचे values. req.body = पाठवलेला JSON data (express.json() लागतो).
  • Error check मध्ये return res.status(...) लिहा, नाहीतर पुढचा code पण चालतो ("headers already sent" error).
  • खऱ्या project मध्ये array ऐवजी database, आणि routes express.Router() + controllers (MVC) मध्ये.
  • उदाहरण: मोबाईल मधील contacts. नवीन contact (POST), यादी पाहणे (GET), edit (PUT / PATCH), delete (DELETE).

Q. HTTP Status Codes (Complete Interview Table)

Concept:
  • An HTTP status code is a 3-digit number the server sends with every response to say what happened to the request.
  • The first digit tells the category, so a client can understand the result even for a code it has never seen.
  • Correct codes let the frontend, mobile apps, browsers, caches and monitoring tools react properly (show an error, retry, redirect).
  • Simple example: a courier tracking status: "Delivered" (2xx), "Moved to a new address" (3xx), "Wrong address given by you" (4xx), "Courier van broke down" (5xx).
The 5 Categories:
  • 1xx Informational: Request received, continue (rare in everyday APIs). Example: 101 Switching Protocols (WebSocket upgrade).
  • 2xx Success: The request worked.
  • 3xx Redirection: The client must go somewhere else (or use its cache).
  • 4xx Client Error: The client made a mistake (bad data, not logged in, wrong URL).
  • 5xx Server Error: The server failed while handling a valid request.
CodeNameMeaning / When to Use
200OKSuccess. GET, PUT or PATCH returned data.
201CreatedA new resource was created (POST). Often returns the new item.
204No ContentSuccess with nothing to return (often DELETE).
301Moved PermanentlyThe URL changed forever (SEO-friendly redirect).
302Found (Temporary Redirect)Redirect temporarily, e.g. to /login.
304Not ModifiedThe cached copy is still valid; no body is sent.
400Bad RequestInvalid input: missing fields, wrong format, bad JSON.
401UnauthorizedNot logged in: no token, or an invalid/expired token. "Who are you?"
403ForbiddenLogged in, but not allowed: e.g. a normal user calling an admin route. "I know you, but no."
404Not FoundThe route or resource doesn't exist.
405Method Not AllowedThe route exists, but not for this method (e.g. DELETE on a read-only route).
409ConflictClashes with current state, e.g. the email is already registered.
413Payload Too LargeUpload or body is bigger than allowed.
415Unsupported Media TypeWrong Content-Type (e.g. XML sent where JSON is expected).
422Unprocessable EntityFormat is OK but values fail validation (e.g. age = -5). Some APIs use 400 for this.
429Too Many RequestsRate limit exceeded; slow down.
500Internal Server ErrorUnexpected bug or crash on the server.
502Bad GatewayA proxy (e.g. Nginx) got a bad response from the Node app behind it.
503Service UnavailableServer is down for maintenance or overloaded; try later.
504Gateway TimeoutThe proxy waited too long for the app.
⭐ Interview Favourite: 401 vs 403
  • 401 Unauthorized = an authentication problem. The server doesn't know who you are (no or invalid token). Fix: log in.
  • 403 Forbidden = an authorization problem. The server knows who you are, but you lack permission. Logging in again won't help.
  • Example: no token → 401. A valid "user" token calling DELETE /admin/users/5 → 403.
Other common confusions:
  • 400 vs 422: 400 = the request is malformed; 422 = it's well-formed, but the values are invalid.
  • 404 vs 403: some APIs return 404 instead of 403 to hide that a private resource even exists.
  • 500 vs 503: 500 = a bug; 503 = temporarily unavailable (expected to come back).
  • Never return 200 with { error: ... } in the body. Use the correct status code.
Code Example: Using the Right Status Codes in Express
app.post('/api/users', async (req, res) => {
    const { email, password } = req.body;
    if (!email || !password) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'email and password required' });

    const exists = await User.findOne({ email });
    if (exists) return res.status(409).json({ error: 'Email already registered' });

    const user = await User.create({ email, password });
    res.status(201).json(user);                                  // created
});

app.delete('/api/users/:id', requireAuth, async (req, res) => {
    if (req.user.role !== 'admin') return res.status(403).json({ error: 'Admins only' });
    const deleted = await User.findByIdAndDelete(req.params.id);
    if (!deleted) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'User not found' });
    res.status(204).send();                                       // success, no body
});
// requireAuth sends 401 when the token is missing/invalid
// the global error handler sends 500 for unexpected errors
  • Syntax: res.status(code) sets the code and returns res, so you can chain .json() / .send().
  • Shortcut: res.sendStatus(404) sets the code and sends its name ("Not Found") as the body.
  • Native Node: res.writeHead(201, headers) or res.statusCode = 201.
  • Redirect: res.redirect(301, '/new-url') (default is 302).
मराठी सारांश (HTTP Status Codes):
  • Status code = server प्रत्येक response सोबत पाठवतो तो 3-अंकी नंबर. Request चे काय झाले ते सांगतो.
  • पहिला अंक = प्रकार: 1xx माहिती, 2xx यश, 3xx redirect, 4xx client ची चूक, 5xx server ची चूक.
  • 2xx: 200 OK, 201 Created (POST ने नवीन बनले), 204 No Content (DELETE, body नाही).
  • 3xx: 301 कायमचा redirect, 302 तात्पुरता redirect, 304 cache मधलेच वापरा.
  • 4xx: 400 चुकीचा input, 401 login नाही, 403 परवानगी नाही, 404 सापडले नाही, 409 conflict (email आधीच आहे), 422 validation fail, 429 खूप requests.
  • 5xx: 500 server bug, 502 / 504 proxy (Nginx) ची अडचण, 503 maintenance / overload.
  • 401 vs 403 (नक्की विचारतात): 401 = "तू कोण?" (login / token नाही). 403 = "ओळखतो, पण तुला परवानगी नाही" (user ने admin route वापरला).
  • नियम: error असताना कधीही 200 पाठवू नका. योग्य code + res.status(code).json(...).
  • उदाहरण: courier tracking. "पोहोचले" (2xx), "नवीन पत्त्यावर पाठवले" (3xx), "तुम्ही चुकीचा पत्ता दिला" (4xx), "courier ची गाडी बिघडली" (5xx).

Q. REST API Best Practices (Naming, Pagination, Filtering, Versioning, Idempotency)

Concept:
  • REST best practices are conventions that make APIs predictable, easy to use and easy to change without breaking existing clients (web, mobile apps).
  • A good API lets a developer guess the endpoint without reading the docs.
  • Simple example: a well-organized supermarket. Every aisle is labelled the same way, so you can find anything without asking.
1. Resource Naming
  • Use nouns, not verbs: /users ✅, /getUsers ❌. The HTTP method is the verb.
  • Use plural collection names: /users, /users/42.
  • Use lowercase with hyphens: /order-items (not /orderItems or /order_items).
  • Nested resources for clear relationships (max 2 levels): /users/42/orders, /users/42/orders/7.
  • Non-CRUD actions: use a sub-resource noun, e.g. POST /orders/7/cancellation or (pragmatically) POST /orders/7/cancel.
2. Pagination
  • Never return 100,000 rows at once. Return a page plus metadata.
  • Offset / page-based: GET /products?page=2&limit=20. Simple, supports "jump to page 10", but slow on huge tables and can skip/duplicate items when data changes.
  • Cursor-based: GET /posts?limit=20&cursor=abc123 (cursor = last item's id/time). Fast and stable for feeds and infinite scroll, but no "jump to page N".
  • Always cap limit (e.g. max 100) so clients can't request everything.
3. Filtering, Sorting, Searching, Field Selection
  • Filter: /products?category=shoes&minPrice=500&inStock=true
  • Sort: /products?sort=-price,name (- = descending).
  • Search: /products?q=running
  • Fields: /users?fields=name,email (send only what's needed).
  • Whitelist allowed filter/sort fields; never pass the query object straight into the DB (injection risk).
4. Versioning
  • Breaking changes (renaming fields, changing response shape) must not break old clients, especially mobile apps users haven't updated.
  • URL versioning (most common): /api/v1/users, then /api/v2/users. Easy to see and route.
  • Header versioning: Accept: application/vnd.myapp.v2+json. Cleaner URLs, harder to test.
  • Keep the old version running for a while, and announce its deprecation.
5. Idempotency
  • An operation is idempotent if doing it once or many times has the same effect on the server.
  • Idempotent: GET, PUT, DELETE (deleting user 5 twice still leaves user 5 deleted), HEAD, OPTIONS.
  • Not idempotent: POST (sending "create order" twice creates two orders). PATCH can be either, depending on the change (set price=500 yes; increment stock by 1 no).
  • Why it matters: networks fail and clients retry. Retrying an idempotent request is safe; retrying a POST can double-charge a customer.
  • Fix for POST: an Idempotency-Key header (a unique id per action). The server stores the key with the result; a repeated key returns the saved result instead of creating a duplicate. Payment APIs like Stripe use this.
6. Consistent Responses
  • Same JSON shape for success and errors, e.g. { "data": ..., "meta": ... } and { "error": { "code": "USER_NOT_FOUND", "message": "..." } }.
  • Correct status codes (see above), consistent camelCase field names, ISO-8601 dates (2026-10-03T10:00:00Z).
  • Use HTTPS, authentication, rate limiting, and document the API (OpenAPI / Swagger).
Code Example: Versioned, Paginated, Filtered, Sorted List Endpoint
const v1 = express.Router();
app.use('/api/v1', v1);                                   // versioning

// GET /api/v1/products?page=2&limit=20&category=shoes&sort=-price
v1.get('/products', async (req, res) => {
    const page  = Math.max(1, Number(req.query.page) || 1);
    const limit = Math.min(100, Number(req.query.limit) || 20);   // cap the limit

    const filter = {};                                    // whitelist filters only
    if (req.query.category) filter.category = String(req.query.category);
    if (req.query.inStock === 'true') filter.stock = { $gt: 0 };

    const allowedSort = ['price', 'name', 'createdAt'];
    const sortField = String(req.query.sort || 'createdAt').replace('-', '');
    const sort = allowedSort.includes(sortField)
        ? { [sortField]: req.query.sort?.startsWith('-') ? -1 : 1 }
        : { createdAt: -1 };

    const [items, total] = await Promise.all([
        Product.find(filter).sort(sort).skip((page - 1) * limit).limit(limit),
        Product.countDocuments(filter)
    ]);

    res.json({
        data: items,
        meta: { page, limit, total, totalPages: Math.ceil(total / limit) }
    });
});

// Idempotency key for POST (prevents duplicate orders on retry)
v1.post('/orders', async (req, res) => {
    const key = req.get('Idempotency-Key');
    if (key) {
        const saved = await IdempotencyRecord.findOne({ key });
        if (saved) return res.status(saved.status).json(saved.body);   // same result again
    }
    const order = await Order.create(req.body);
    if (key) await IdempotencyRecord.create({ key, status: 201, body: order });
    res.status(201).json(order);
});
मराठी सारांश (REST Best Practices):
  • Naming: नाम (noun) वापरा, क्रियापद नको: /users ✅, /getUsers ❌. अनेकवचन, lowercase + hyphen (/order-items). Nested: /users/42/orders.
  • Pagination: सगळा data एकदम देऊ नका. ?page=2&limit=20 (offset) किंवा ?cursor=abc (cursor, infinite scroll साठी चांगले). limit वर मर्यादा (उदा. 100). Response मध्ये meta (total, totalPages).
  • Filter / Sort / Search: ?category=shoes&sort=-price&q=running. फक्त परवानगी असलेले fields स्वीकारा (whitelist).
  • Versioning: /api/v1/.... मोठे बदल करताना v2 बनवा, म्हणजे जुने mobile apps तुटत नाहीत.
  • Idempotency: एकदा किंवा अनेकदा केले तरी परिणाम same. GET, PUT, DELETE idempotent. POST नाही (दोनदा केल्यास दोन orders). उपाय: Idempotency-Key header. Payments मध्ये खूप महत्वाचे.
  • Consistency: response चा आकार नेहमी same, योग्य status codes, camelCase, ISO dates, Swagger documentation.
  • उदाहरण: नीटनेटका supermarket. प्रत्येक रांगेला एकाच पद्धतीने label, विचारल्याशिवाय वस्तू सापडते.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. What is the exact difference between PUT and PATCH?
  • PUT (Full Update): It replaces the entire resource. If a User object has 5 fields and you only want to change the "name", sending a PUT request with only the "name" will erase the other 4 fields (unless you send all 5 fields again). It is idempotent.
  • PATCH (Partial Update): It only modifies the specific fields you send. If you send only the "name", it updates the name and leaves the other 4 fields completely untouched.
Q2. What does "Stateless" mean in REST architecture?
  • Stateless means that the server does not remember anything about the client between requests. The client must include authentication tokens, IDs, and all required data in every single request.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Interview चा सर्वात आवडता प्रश्न: PUT आणि PATCH मध्ये काय फरक आहे?
  • उत्तर: PUT संपूर्ण Data बदलून टाकतो (म्हणून जुना डेटा डिलीट होऊ शकतो जर आपण पूर्ण डेटा पाठवला नाही).
  • पण PATCH फक्त तेवढाच भाग बदलतो जो आपण पाठवला आहे, बाकीचा डेटा सुरक्षित राहतो.
  • उदाहरण: user { name, email, age }. PUT मध्ये फक्त { name } पाठवले तर email आणि age जाऊ शकतात. PATCH मध्ये फक्त name बदलते.
  • Idempotent: PUT idempotent आहे (कितीही वेळा पाठवा, परिणाम same). PATCH नेहमी नसतो (उदा. "stock 1 ने वाढवा").
  • Stateless (Q2): server client ची मागची माहिती लक्षात ठेवत नाही. प्रत्येक request मध्ये token / id सगळे हवे.
  • Status codes: यशस्वी update ला 200, resource नसेल तर 404.

Quick Revision Summary

Concept / Method Description
REST Representational State Transfer (an architectural style for APIs).
GET / POST / DELETE Methods to Read, Create, and Delete data.
PUT Method to fully replace/update a resource.
PATCH Method to partially update specific fields of a resource.
express.Router() Used to modularize and split API routes into separate files.
SECTION 6: NODE.JS INTERNAL ARCHITECTURE

Chapter 18: Architecture (V8 Engine, Event Loop & Concurrency)

Q. Why does Node.js use the V8 Engine?

Definition:
  • V8 is an open-source, high-performance JavaScript Engine developed by Google and written in C++. It was originally built to power the Chrome browser.
  • A JavaScript Engine is a program that reads (parses), compiles, and executes JavaScript code. V8 does this by compiling JS directly into native machine code — not interpreting it line-by-line.
  • Node.js embeds V8 to allow JavaScript to run outside the browser, on the server-side. This is the core reason Node.js exists!
  • V8 manages two critical memory areas: the Call Stack (tracks function execution) and the Heap (stores objects and variables).
Diagram: Node.js Architecture — V8 + libuv
  • Point 1 — JIT (Just-In-Time) Compilation:
    • Traditional interpreters read and execute code one line at a time (slow). Traditional compilers compile everything upfront (fast but inflexible).
    • V8 uses JIT Compilation — it compiles JS code at runtime, just before execution. It profiles "hot" (frequently used) code paths and compiles those to highly optimized machine code.
    • Result: JavaScript runs at speeds comparable to compiled languages like C++.
  • Point 2 — Garbage Collection:
    • V8 has an automatic Garbage Collector that continuously scans the Heap and frees memory occupied by objects that are no longer referenced.
    • This prevents memory leaks and means developers don't need to manually manage memory (unlike C/C++).
    • V8 uses a generational garbage collection strategy — short-lived objects are collected quickly, long-lived objects are managed separately.
  • Point 3 — Why Node.js chose V8:
    • V8 is extremely fast, open-source, and actively maintained by Google.
    • By embedding V8, Ryan Dahl (creator of Node.js) was able to run JavaScript on the server-side, sharing code between frontend and backend.
    • Node.js wraps V8 with additional C++ bindings to give JS access to the OS (file system, networking, etc.) — things a browser JS engine cannot do.
  • Point 4 — Performance Optimizations (Inside V8):
    • Inline Caching:
      • V8 remembers (caches) where it found a property the last time.
      • The next time the same code runs, it skips the search and goes directly there.
      • Result: repeated function calls and property lookups become much faster.
    • Hidden Classes:
      • JS objects have no fixed shape, so V8 secretly creates an internal "shape" (hidden class) for objects that share the same properties.
      • Objects with the same shape share the same hidden class, so property access is as fast as in C++.
      • Tip: Always add properties in the same order (for example, in the constructor) so objects share one hidden class.
  • Point 5 — Cross-Platform:
    • V8 is written in C++ and runs on Windows, macOS and Linux.
    • That is why the same Node.js code runs on every OS.
  • Point 6 — Perfect Partner for Non-Blocking I/O:
    • V8 only executes JavaScript (it has no file or network access).
    • libuv handles async I/O (files, network, timers).
    • Together, they give fast execution plus concurrency.
Code Example: Hidden Classes — Good vs Bad Object Creation
// ✅ GOOD: same properties, same order → both share ONE hidden class (fast)
function User(name, age) {
    this.name = name;
    this.age = age;
}
const u1 = new User('Vishal', 25);
const u2 = new User('Amit', 30);

// ❌ BAD: different order → V8 creates DIFFERENT hidden classes (slower)
const a = {}; a.name = 'Vishal'; a.age = 25;
const b = {}; b.age = 30; b.name = 'Amit';
In Short:
  • V8 is Google’s open-source, high-performance JavaScript engine written in C++.
  • It compiles JS into machine code at runtime (JIT), so code runs very fast.
  • It manages memory automatically using Garbage Collection.
  • It uses Inline Caching and Hidden Classes for extra speed.
  • It powers both Chrome and Node.js.
  • Node.js uses V8 for speed, efficiency, scalability and cross-platform support.
मराठी सारांश (V8 Engine):
  • V8 म्हणजे काय? Google ने बनवलेले, C++ मध्ये लिहिलेले open-source JavaScript Engine. Chrome आणि Node.js दोन्ही यावर चालतात.
  • JIT Compilation: JS code line-by-line वाचत नाही, तर runtime ला थेट machine code मध्ये बदलते. त्यामुळे खूप fast.
  • Garbage Collection: वापरात नसलेली memory आपोआप साफ करते. Memory leak टाळते.
  • Inline Caching: मागच्या वेळी property कुठे सापडली ते लक्षात ठेवते. पुढच्या वेळी थेट तिथे जाते.
  • Hidden Classes: एकसारख्या objects ना एकच internal "shape" देते. म्हणून properties नेहमी एकाच क्रमाने लिहा.
  • Cross-Platform: Windows, macOS, Linux सगळीकडे चालते.
  • V8 + libuv: V8 JS चालवते, libuv files/network सारखे async काम करते. दोघे मिळून Node.js fast आणि non-blocking बनवतात.

Q. What is libuv?

Definition:
  • libuv is a multi-platform C library that was originally developed for Node.js. It provides Node.js with its asynchronous, non-blocking I/O capabilities.
  • It is the reason Node.js can perform operations like reading files, making network requests, and interacting with databases — all without blocking the main thread.
  • libuv abstracts OS-level async mechanisms: epoll (Linux), IOCP (Windows), kqueue (macOS) — giving Node.js cross-platform consistency.
  • Point 1 — The Event Loop:
    • libuv implements and manages the Event Loop — the continuous cycle that checks for pending callbacks and executes them when the Call Stack is free.
  • Point 2 — The Thread Pool:
    • For operations that cannot be done asynchronously by the OS (like FS operations, DNS lookups, Crypto), libuv uses a Thread Pool.
    • Default size: 4 threads. Can be increased via: UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE=8 node app.js.
    • These background threads handle the heavy lifting while the main JS thread stays free.
  • Point 3 — What libuv handles:
    • File System (fs) operations, DNS lookups, Cryptography (crypto module), TCP/UDP networking, Child Processes.
मराठी सारांश (libuv):
  • libuv ही C मध्ये लिहिलेली, multi-platform library आहे जी Node.js ला async, non-blocking I/O ची ताकद देते.
  • Event Loop: libuv च event loop चालवते. Stack रिकामा झाला की तयार callbacks चालवते.
  • Thread Pool: default 4 threads. fs, DNS, crypto सारखी कामे, जी OS async करू शकत नाही, ती इथे होतात. UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE=8 ने वाढवता येतात.
  • Cross-platform: Linux (epoll), Windows (IOCP), macOS (kqueue). प्रत्येक OS ची वेगळी पद्धत libuv लपवते, म्हणून Node सगळीकडे सारखा चालतो.
  • उदाहरण: libuv म्हणजे हॉटेलचे kitchen manager. Orders (कामे) cooks (threads) मध्ये वाटतो, आणि तयार झाले की waiter (event loop) ला कळवतो.

Q. What is Asynchronous Programming in Node.js?

Concept:
  • Asynchronous programming is a way of writing code so that slow operations do not block the main execution thread.
  • Node.js starts a slow task (reading a file, a DB query, an API call) and does not wait for it to finish.
  • It immediately moves on to the next line of code.
  • When the task finishes, Node.js handles the result using a callback, a Promise or async/await.
  • In one line: tasks do not block the execution.
  • Simple example: ordering food online. You place the order and keep working. When the food arrives, the doorbell rings (callback). You don't stand at the door waiting.
Key Points:
  • Non-blocking: Operations do not block the execution thread.
  • Does not wait: While a previous task is still in progress, the program moves to the next task.
  • Non-deterministic:
    • The finish order of async tasks is not guaranteed.
    • Example: if you start reading two files, the smaller one may finish first even though it started second.
Why Use Asynchronous Programming?
  • To avoid blocking: One slow task never freezes the whole app.
  • To use the single thread well: The one thread can start other tasks while waiting for a slow operation.
  • Better performance and responsiveness: The server keeps answering other users.
Flow Comparison (Remember This):
  • Synchronous: Start task → wait until it completes → move to the next task (blocking).
  • Asynchronous: Start task → register a callback → move on → an event notifies when it's done → the callback runs (non-blocking).
Code Example: Asynchronous (Non-Blocking) Code
const fs = require('fs');

console.log('1. Start');

// readFile does NOT block: Node.js hands it to libuv and moves on
fs.readFile('bigFile.txt', 'utf-8', (err, data) => {
    if (err) return console.error(err);
    console.log('3. File read done');      // runs LATER
});

console.log('2. End (did not wait for the file!)');

// OUTPUT:
// 1. Start
// 2. End (did not wait for the file!)
// 3. File read done
  • Syntax: fs.readFile(path, options, callback)
  • path: The file to read.
  • options: An encoding such as 'utf-8' (optional). Without it you get a Buffer.
  • callback(err, data): Runs when reading finishes. err is null on success, and data is the file content.
In Short:
  • Asynchronous programming lets Node.js start a slow task, move on without waiting, and handle the result later.
  • The result is handled through callbacks, Promises or async/await.
  • This keeps the single thread free, fast and responsive.
मराठी सारांश (Asynchronous Programming):
  • Asynchronous म्हणजे काय? एखादे slow काम (file वाचणे, DB query, API call) सुरू करायचे आणि ते संपेपर्यंत न थांबता पुढचे काम करायचे.
  • काम पूर्ण झाल्यावर Node.js आपल्याला callback / Promise / async-await द्वारे result देतो.
  • उदाहरण: Online जेवण order केले. दारात उभे न राहता आपण आपले काम करतो. जेवण आल्यावर doorbell वाजते (callback).
  • Non-blocking: एक काम दुसऱ्याला थांबवत नाही.
  • Non-deterministic: कोणते काम आधी संपेल याची खात्री नसते. छोटी file आधी वाचून होऊ शकते.
  • का वापरतात? Blocking टाळण्यासाठी, एकाच thread ला वाट पाहताना दुसरी कामे करू देण्यासाठी, आणि app fast व responsive ठेवण्यासाठी.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: Sync = सुरू करा → थांबा → पुढे. Async = सुरू करा → callback नोंदवा → पुढे जा → काम झाल्यावर callback चालतो.

Q. What is the Difference between Synchronous and Asynchronous?

Point Synchronous Asynchronous
Execution Tasks run one after another, in sequence. Tasks can start, move on and complete independently.
Waiting The program waits for each task to finish. The program does not wait. It moves to the next task immediately.
Nature Blocking: one task blocks the next until it completes. Non-blocking: tasks don't block each other.
Performance Slower for I/O-heavy tasks (causes delays). Faster and more efficient for I/O-heavy tasks.
Responsiveness Can become unresponsive if a task takes long. Stays responsive because tasks are handled concurrently.
Order Deterministic (always the same order). Non-deterministic (finish order may vary).
Use Cases Simple scripts, startup config loading, CLI tools. File handling, API calls, DB queries, timers, servers.
Example fs.readFileSync() fs.readFile(), setTimeout(), fetch()
Diagram: Synchronous vs Asynchronous Timeline
मराठी सारांश (Sync vs Async):
  • Synchronous: कामे एकामागून एक. प्रत्येक काम संपेपर्यंत program थांबतो (blocking). I/O कामांसाठी slow. काम लांब असेल तर app hang होतो. साध्या scripts साठी योग्य.
  • Asynchronous: काम सुरू करून लगेच पुढे जातो (non-blocking). I/O कामांसाठी fast. App नेहमी responsive राहतो. File handling, API calls, timers, servers साठी वापरतात.
  • Order: Sync मध्ये क्रम नेहमी same (deterministic). Async मध्ये क्रम बदलू शकतो (non-deterministic).
  • उदाहरण: readFileSync() = sync, readFile() = async.

Q. Single-Threaded vs Multi-Threaded Programming

  • Single-Threaded:
    • The program has only ONE main thread of execution. All synchronous code executes in a single Call Stack — one operation at a time.
    • If a task blocks (e.g., an infinite loop or fs.readFileSync), the entire program freezes — no other requests can be handled.
    • Node.js is single-threaded by design for JavaScript execution. This avoids the complexity of thread management.
    • ✅ Advantage: Simpler code, no race conditions, no thread synchronization needed.
    • ⚠️ Disadvantage: CPU-heavy tasks block the entire thread.
  • Multi-Threaded:
    • A program creates multiple threads that execute independently and concurrently (e.g., Java, C# servers).
    • Each thread gets its own call stack. Multiple requests can be processed truly in parallel.
    • ⚠️ Problem: Race conditions, Deadlocks, and complex synchronization logic.
  • Deadlock (Important Concept):
    • A Deadlock is a situation in multi-threaded programs where two threads are each waiting for the other to release a resource, causing an infinite freeze.
    • Example: Thread A holds Lock 1, waits for Lock 2. Thread B holds Lock 2, waits for Lock 1. → Both frozen forever.
    • ✅ Node.js completely avoids deadlocks because there is only one JS thread — no resource contention!
मराठी सारांश (Single vs Multi-Threaded):
  • Single-threaded: एकच main thread, एकच call stack. एका वेळी एक काम. Heavy loop आला तर सगळे अडकते. Node.js JS साठी असेच आहे.
  • फायदा: सोपा code, race condition नाही, synchronization ची गरज नाही.
  • Multi-threaded: अनेक threads, प्रत्येकाचा वेगळा call stack, खरे parallel काम (Java, C#).
  • तोटा: race conditions, deadlocks, गुंतागुंतीचे synchronization.
  • Deadlock: Thread A ने Lock 1 धरला आणि Lock 2 ची वाट पाहतो. Thread B ने Lock 2 धरला आणि Lock 1 ची वाट पाहतो. दोघे कायम अडकले.
  • Node.js मध्ये एकच JS thread असल्याने deadlock होत नाही.

Q. What is Single-Threaded Programming? (In Detail)

Concept:
  • Thread: The smallest unit that can execute code (one "worker" inside a program).
  • Single-threaded means the program has only one main thread of execution.
  • In JavaScript (browser and Node.js), this one thread runs all your JS code, one task at a time, line by line.
  • One thread = one Call Stack. All synchronous code runs on this one stack.
  • If a task blocks (an infinite while loop or heavy calculation), the entire program gets stuck.
  • Simple example: a shop with only one cashier. The cashier serves one customer at a time.
Key Points:
  • Single-threaded ≠ Synchronous:
    • Being single-threaded does NOT mean everything runs synchronously.
    • JS handles async tasks with the Event Loop, Callback Queue and Web APIs / libuv.
    • It is still one thread, but the async work is managed by the event loop.
  • Concurrency via the Event Loop:
    • JS cannot run two pieces of JS code in parallel on the main thread.
    • But it achieves concurrency: the event loop decides when async callbacks (timers, promises, I/O) are pushed to the call stack.
  • Advantages:
    • Simplicity: No multi-thread problems (deadlocks, race conditions).
    • Predictability: Execution is easy to follow and debug.
    • Low memory: No memory spent on extra threads.
  • Disadvantages:
    • Blocking operations stop everything (bad for CPU-heavy work).
    • True parallelism needs background workers (Web Workers in browsers, Worker Threads in Node.js).
In Short:
  • JavaScript is single-threaded: it has one main thread of execution.
  • That does not make it synchronous-only. Thanks to the event loop, it can handle timers, promises and I/O asynchronously.
  • The model is simple, but blocking code is dangerous because it freezes the whole program.
मराठी सारांश (Single-Threaded):
  • Thread म्हणजे code चालवणारा सर्वात लहान unit (एक कामगार).
  • Single-threaded म्हणजे program मध्ये फक्त एकच main thread असतो. सर्व JS code तोच चालवतो, एका वेळी एकच काम.
  • उदाहरण: दुकानात एकच cashier. तो एका वेळी एकाच customer ला serve करतो.
  • महत्वाचे: Single-threaded म्हणजे Synchronous नाही! Event Loop मुळे JS async कामेही करू शकते.
  • फायदे: सोपे, deadlock / race condition नाही, कमी memory.
  • तोटे: heavy CPU काम आले तर संपूर्ण program अडकतो. त्यासाठी Worker Threads वापरावे लागतात.

Q. What is the Relation between Synchronous and Asynchronous in a Single Thread?

Concept:
  • "Synchronous" and "single-threaded" are related but NOT the same.
  • Synchronous describes how tasks run: one after another, blocking.
  • Single-threaded describes how many workers there are: only one main thread.
  • Single Thread:
    • Only one main execution thread.
    • Blocking is a risk, but it can be avoided with async operations (callbacks, promises, async/await).
    • Single Thread + Async = efficient I/O handling. This is the Node.js model.
    • Main issue: all async callbacks still depend on that one thread, managed by the event loop.
  • Synchronous:
    • Tasks run one after another, without skipping or overlapping.
    • Blocking happens naturally: the next task waits until the current one finishes.
    • Sync + Single Thread = very inefficient for long tasks (everything freezes).
    • Main issue: blocking.
Code Example 1: Synchronous + Single Thread (Program Freezes)
console.log("Task 1");
while (true) {}          // infinite loop BLOCKS the only thread
console.log("Task 2");   // ❌ never runs
Code Example 2: Single Thread but Asynchronous (No Freeze)
console.log("Start");
setTimeout(() => {
    console.log("Async Task");   // runs after 1 second via the event loop
}, 1000);
console.log("End");

// OUTPUT: Start → End → Async Task
  • Syntax: setTimeout(callback, delay, ...args)
  • callback: The function to run later.
  • delay: The minimum wait time in milliseconds (default 0).
  • ...args: Optional values passed to the callback.
  • Returns: A Timeout object, which you can cancel with clearTimeout().
In Short:
  • Synchronous = tasks run one after another (blocking).
  • Single-threaded = only one main thread.
  • A single-threaded runtime like JS can still run async tasks through the event loop, which prevents blocking and allows concurrency.
  • The real danger: heavy blocking code freezes the single thread, and the whole app becomes unresponsive.
मराठी सारांश (Sync आणि Single Thread चा संबंध):
  • Synchronous = कामे कशी चालतात (एकामागून एक, blocking).
  • Single-threaded = कामगार किती आहेत (फक्त एक).
  • Single thread + Sync = लांब काम आल्यास सगळे अडकते (while(true) उदाहरण).
  • Single thread + Async = Node.js model. Event loop मुळे thread अडकत नाही (setTimeout उदाहरण).
  • धोका: heavy blocking code लिहिल्यास एकमेव thread अडकतो आणि संपूर्ण app बंद पडल्यासारखा होतो.

Q. What is Multithreading Programming? (In Detail)

Concept:
  • Multithreaded programming is a model where a single program creates multiple threads that run independently and concurrently inside the same process.
  • Each thread has its own call stack but usually shares the same memory, so communication between threads is fast.
  • Simple example: a restaurant with many waiters. Each waiter serves a different table at the same time.
  • Purpose:
    • Execute multiple tasks in parallel.
    • Improve performance for CPU-heavy (computation) work.
    • Keep servers and UI apps responsive.
  • How It Works (Step by Step):
    1. Request arrival: The server receives tasks (Task1, Task2, Task3).
    2. Thread creation: A new thread is created for each task (T1, T2, T3).
    3. Parallel execution: Threads run at the same time, so Task2 does not wait for Task1.
    4. Result: Tasks finish faster.
  • Interview Points:
    • Threads share memory, so they are lighter than multiple processes.
    • Ideal for CPU-bound tasks and for handling many operations at once.
  • Advantages:
    • Parallel execution → better performance for CPU-heavy tasks.
    • Better CPU utilization (all cores are used).
    • Can handle multiple users or requests at the same time.
    • Responsive UIs (games, editors).
  • Disadvantages:
    • Complexity: Managing many threads is hard.
    • Deadlock: Two threads wait on each other forever.
    • Race condition: Two threads change the same shared data at the same time, giving wrong or inconsistent results.
    • Overhead: Too many threads use memory and CPU switching time, which can slow things down.
Diagram: Single-Threaded vs Multi-Threaded
Diagram: Deadlock
Code Example: Race Condition (Concept, pseudo-code)
// Shared bank balance = 100
// Thread A: read balance (100) → add 50 → write 150
// Thread B: read balance (100) → subtract 30 → write 70
// Both read 100 at the SAME time → final balance = 70 or 150 ❌
// Correct answer should be 120 → this wrong result is a RACE CONDITION
मराठी सारांश (Multithreading):
  • Multithreading म्हणजे एकाच program मध्ये अनेक threads (कामगार) एकाच वेळी काम करतात आणि memory share करतात.
  • उदाहरण: हॉटेलमध्ये अनेक waiters, प्रत्येक जण वेगळ्या टेबलला एकाच वेळी serve करतो.
  • कसे चालते: Request आली → प्रत्येक task साठी नवीन thread → सगळे parallel चालतात → काम लवकर संपते.
  • फायदे: CPU heavy कामे fast, सर्व CPU cores वापरले जातात, अनेक users एकाच वेळी handle.
  • तोटे: Complex, Deadlock (दोन threads एकमेकांची वाट पाहत कायम अडकतात), Race condition (दोघे एकाच data ला एकाच वेळी बदलतात → चुकीचा result), खूप threads झाल्यास overhead.

Q. Where is Multithreading Used?

  • Desktop Applications: Games, video editors, image rendering tools.
  • Web Servers: Handling many client requests at once (for example, Java/Tomcat creates a thread per request).
  • Scientific Computations: Parallel processing of large data.
  • Database Systems: Running queries concurrently.
  • Operating Systems: Background tasks and I/O handling.
  • ✅ Ideal for: CPU-bound tasks (heavy calculations, data processing).
  • ❌ Not ideal for: I/O-bound tasks (waiting on disk or network). These are better handled with async I/O, as Node.js does.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Games, video editors, web servers, databases, OS मध्ये multithreading वापरतात.
  • Heavy calculation (CPU-bound) कामांसाठी उत्तम.
  • File / network वाट पाहण्याच्या (I/O-bound) कामांसाठी Node.js सारखे async I/O जास्त चांगले.
  • CPU-bound: CPU सतत काम करते (video encoding, image processing, गणिते). I/O-bound: बहुतेक वेळ वाट पाहण्यात (DB, file, network).
  • Web servers (Java Tomcat) प्रत्येक request साठी thread वापरतात. Node.js त्याऐवजी event loop वापरतो.
  • Node.js मध्ये CPU-bound कामासाठी Worker Threads वापरता येतात.
  • उदाहरण: स्वयंपाकघरात अनेक आचारी (threads) एकाच वेळी वेगवेगळे पदार्थ बनवतात. हे CPU-heavy कामासाठी उत्तम.

Q. Why is Node.js / JavaScript Not Multi-Threaded by Default?

  • Single-threaded by design: Node.js runs JS on one main thread (V8) and uses one event loop for all operations.
  • Avoids multi-thread problems: Multiple threads bring complexity, deadlocks, race conditions and high memory usage.
  • Concurrency without threads: Node.js uses async, non-blocking I/O, so many tasks are processed on one thread without waiting.

Q. Why Single-Threaded? (Benefits)

  • Simpler to use: No thread synchronization or deadlocks.
  • Less complexity and memory: No memory is spent on a thread for every request.
  • Built for I/O-heavy work: In web servers and APIs, most time is spent waiting on I/O, not using the CPU. One thread plus async I/O is perfect for that.
मराठी सारांश (Node.js Single-threaded का?):
  • Node.js मुद्दाम single-threaded बनवले आहे. एक main thread आणि एक event loop.
  • Multithreading मुळे deadlock, race condition, जास्त memory असे प्रश्न येतात. ते टाळले.
  • Web servers मध्ये बहुतेक वेळ DB / file / network ची वाट पाहण्यात जातो. त्यासाठी एक thread + async I/O पुरेसे आहे.
  • सोपे: locks, synchronization, thread management ची गरज नाही. Code समजायला आणि debug करायला सोपा.
  • कमी memory: प्रत्येक request साठी नवीन thread (प्रत्येकी ~1 MB stack) नाही. हजारो connections कमी RAM मध्ये.
  • तोटा: heavy CPU काम main thread अडवते. त्यासाठी Worker Threads किंवा Cluster.
  • उदाहरण: एक हुशार waiter जो order घेऊन kitchen ला देतो आणि लगेच पुढच्या टेबलकडे जातो. अनेक waiters ठेवण्यापेक्षा स्वस्त.

Q. How Does Node.js Handle Concurrency if It's Single-Threaded? (How Node.js Still Handles Many Tasks)

  • Instead of multiple threads, Node.js uses: Non-blocking I/O + the Event Loop + libuv (C library) + Callbacks / Promises / async-await.
  • Together, these let Node.js handle many tasks concurrently while JavaScript runs on one thread.
Answer:
  • Node.js achieves concurrency by combining the single main JS thread with the Event Loop, Non-blocking I/O, and the libuv Thread Pool.
  • The main thread never waits. It delegates work to libuv and immediately handles the next request.
  • Step-by-Step: How a file read request is handled:
    1. Step 1 — Request Arrives: A user request triggers fs.readFile('data.txt', callback). Node.js registers the callback and the operation.
    2. Step 2 — Delegation: Node.js immediately delegates the file reading task to libuv. The main thread does NOT wait — it moves on to handle the next incoming request.
    3. Step 3 — Background Processing: libuv assigns the file read to a thread from its Thread Pool. The OS reads the file in the background.
    4. Step 4 — Task Completion: When the OS finishes, it signals libuv (via epoll/IOCP). libuv pushes the callback into the Event Queue.
    5. Step 5 — Execution: The Event Loop checks: "Is the Call Stack empty?" When it's empty, it picks the callback and executes it.
Code Example: Concurrency in Action — 3 Requests Handled Simultaneously
const fs = require('fs');
console.log("=== Server received 3 requests simultaneously ===");

// Request 1: Slow I/O — delegated to libuv Thread Pool
fs.readFile('large-file.txt', 'utf8', (err, data) => {
    console.log("Request 1 done: File read complete");
});

// Request 2: Also delegated to libuv
fs.readFile('another-file.txt', 'utf8', (err, data) => {
    console.log("Request 2 done: Another file read complete");
});

// Request 3: Instant sync — runs on main thread directly
console.log("Request 3 done: Instant response");
console.log("=== Main thread is FREE to handle more! ===");

// OUTPUT ORDER:
// === Server received 3 requests simultaneously ===
// Request 3 done: Instant response
// === Main thread is FREE to handle more! ===
// Request 2 done: Another file read complete   (back from libuv)
// Request 1 done: File read complete           (back from libuv)
In Short:
  • Node.js is single-threaded, but it handles concurrency using non-blocking I/O and the event loop.
  • Async tasks are delegated to libuv / the OS.
  • When they complete, their callbacks are executed by the event loop.
  • So Node.js processes many tasks efficiently without multiple threads, which makes it highly scalable for I/O-heavy apps.
मराठी सारांश (Single Thread वर Concurrency):
  • Node.js मध्ये JS एकाच thread वर चालते, तरी हजारो requests handle होतात.
  • Step 1: Request आली → async काम (file / DB) सुरू.
  • Step 2: Node.js ते काम libuv / OS ला देतो आणि स्वतः पुढच्या request कडे जातो.
  • Step 3: libuv / OS background मध्ये काम करतात.
  • Step 4: काम संपले की OS libuv ला सांगतो, libuv callback queue मध्ये टाकतो.
  • Step 5: Call stack रिकामा झाल्यावर Event Loop तो callback चालवतो.
  • उदाहरण: एकच cashier orders घेतो, kitchen ला देतो, पुढच्या customer कडे जातो. जेवण तयार झाल्यावर kitchen bell वाजवते.

Q. How Does Node.js Achieve Asynchronous Programming?

Concept:
  • Node.js achieves async (non-blocking) programming using the Event Loop, libuv and its Thread Pool, and async patterns: Callbacks, Promises and async/await.
  • Together, these let Node.js handle many operations concurrently without blocking the main thread.
  • 1. Single-Threaded Core:
    • JS runs on a single main thread using the V8 engine.
    • It can execute only one operation at a time, but it appears to do many at once because I/O is handled asynchronously.
  • 2. Event Loop (The Heart of Async):
    • It continuously checks the call stack and the callback queues to decide what to run next.
    • When your code starts an async operation (file read, HTTP request, timer), Node.js does not wait. Instead:
      • It delegates the operation to libuv.
      • The main thread continues with other code.
      • When the operation finishes, its callback is queued.
      • When the call stack is empty, the event loop executes the callback.
    • It has 6 phases: timers, pending callbacks, idle/prepare, poll, check and close callbacks.
  • 3. libuv and the Thread Pool:
    • libuv manages the event loop and a thread pool (4 threads by default).
    • Heavy tasks (file reading, DNS lookup, encryption) are offloaded to the thread pool.
    • The main thread stays free for other requests.
  • 4. Callbacks (Original Way): A function that runs when the task is finished.
  • 5. Promises: Solve callback hell. A Promise represents the future completion (or failure) of an async task, and supports .then() and .catch().
  • 6. Async/Await (Modern Way): Built on Promises, it makes async code look synchronous. await pauses only inside that async function, while the event loop keeps running other tasks.
Code Example 1: Callback Style
const fs = require("fs");

fs.readFile("file.txt", "utf8", (err, data) => {
    if (err) throw err;
    console.log(data);   // runs when the thread pool finishes reading
});
// File reading happens in the background (thread pool).
// Once done, the callback runs through the event loop.
Code Example 2: Promise Style (.then / .catch)
const fsp = require("fs").promises;

fsp.readFile("file.txt", "utf8")
    .then(data => console.log(data))      // success
    .catch(err => console.error(err));    // failure
Code Example 3: Async/Await Style (Recommended)
const fsp = require("fs").promises;

async function readFile() {
    try {
        const data = await fsp.readFile("file.txt", "utf8"); // pauses ONLY this function
        console.log(data);
    } catch (err) {
        console.error(err);
    }
}

readFile();
console.log("I run before the file content!"); // event loop is not blocked
Summary Table: Building Blocks of Async in Node.js
ComponentRole
Event LoopContinuously checks for and executes ready callbacks.
libuvManages the event loop and the background thread pool.
Thread PoolRuns heavy tasks (file I/O, crypto, compression, DNS).
CallbacksThe original way to handle async completion.
Promises / Async-AwaitThe modern, cleaner way to handle async operations.
Non-blocking I/OLets many I/O tasks run concurrently.
In Short:
  • Node.js runs JavaScript on one main thread and offloads heavy I/O work to background threads or the OS through libuv.
  • When the background tasks finish, their results go back to the event loop.
  • The event loop runs the matching callbacks, Promises or async/await code, all without blocking the main thread.
मराठी सारांश (Node.js Async कसे साध्य करते?):
  • Single thread: JS एकाच thread वर V8 मध्ये चालते.
  • Event Loop: Async काम libuv ला देतो, पुढे जातो. काम संपल्यावर callback queue मध्ये येतो आणि stack रिकामा झाल्यावर चालतो.
  • libuv Thread Pool: 4 threads (default). File, DNS, crypto सारखी heavy कामे इथे होतात.
  • Callbacks: जुनी पद्धत. Nesting वाढल्यास "callback hell".
  • Promises: .then() / .catch(). जास्त स्वच्छ code.
  • Async/Await: सर्वात modern. Code synchronous सारखा दिसतो. await फक्त त्या function ला थांबवतो, संपूर्ण program ला नाही.

Q. How Does Node.js Know an Asynchronous Task is Finished?

Concept:
  • Node.js does NOT keep checking whether a task is done.
  • The OS tells libuv when the task finishes.
  • libuv then queues the callback (or Promise resolution), and the Event Loop runs it when the call stack is empty.
  • Simple example: a courier. You don't call the courier every minute. The courier rings your bell when the parcel arrives.
Step-by-Step Flow:
  1. Async Task Starts: You call an async function (for example, fs.readFile). Node.js does not run it on the main thread. It delegates the task to libuv.
  2. libuv Handles the Operation: libuv (a C library) manages the event loop, the thread pool and I/O polling. It asks the OS to do the real disk or network work.
  3. OS Monitors the Task: The OS works in the background and uses platform-specific notification systems:
    • Linux → epoll
    • macOS / BSD → kqueue
    • Windows → IOCP (I/O Completion Ports)
  4. libuv Receives the Notification: When the OS finishes, it sends a signal to libuv. libuv puts the callback (or Promise resolution) in the event loop’s queue.
  5. Event Loop Executes the Callback: The event loop checks: "Is the call stack empty?" If yes, it runs the callback, and your JS finally gets the result (file content, API response).
Diagram: How Node.js Knows a Task is Done
Key Interview Points:
  • Node.js uses libuv to manage async I/O.
  • libuv delegates the work to the OS and listens for completion signals.
  • The OS notifies libuv through epoll, kqueue or IOCP.
  • libuv queues the callback or Promise resolution.
  • The event loop runs it when the call stack is empty.
  • The main thread is never blocked. It keeps running other tasks.
मराठी सारांश (Async काम संपले हे Node.js ला कसे कळते?):
  • Node.js स्वतः वारंवार "काम झाले का?" असे विचारत नाही. OS स्वतः सांगतो.
  • उदाहरण: Courier वाल्याला सारखा फोन करत नाही. Parcel आले की तो bell वाजवतो.
  • Step 1: fs.readFile सुरू → काम libuv ला दिले.
  • Step 2: libuv हे काम OS कडे पाठवतो.
  • Step 3: OS background मध्ये काम करतो (Linux: epoll, Mac: kqueue, Windows: IOCP).
  • Step 4: काम संपले की OS libuv ला signal देतो, libuv callback queue मध्ये टाकतो.
  • Step 5: Call stack रिकामा झाल्यावर Event Loop callback चालवतो आणि आपल्याला result मिळतो.

Q. What are the Key Concepts behind Node.js Concurrency? (Single-Threaded but Concurrent Request Handling)

Concept:
  • Concurrency = the ability to handle multiple tasks during the same period of time.
  • Node.js is single-threaded but concurrent.
  • It achieves this with six building blocks: the Event Loop, Non-blocking I/O, the libuv Thread Pool, Event Loop Phases, Async APIs, and optional Cluster / Worker Threads.
  • 1. Single-Threaded Event Loop:
    • One main thread (call stack) runs all user JS code.
    • The event loop keeps asking: "Is the call stack empty? Are there pending callbacks or promises?"
    • For async tasks, Node.js registers a callback and continues with other work.
  • 2. Non-Blocking I/O:
    • Traditional I/O blocks the thread. Node.js delegates I/O to the OS / libuv instead.
    • When the OS signals completion, the callback is pushed to the queue.
    • Benefit: The main thread is never idle. It keeps serving other requests.
  • 3. libuv Thread Pool:
    • 4 threads by default (can be changed with UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE).
    • Used for file system operations, DNS lookups and crypto (pbkdf2, bcrypt).
    • Keeps the main thread responsive while heavy work runs in parallel.
  • 4. Event Loop Phases: Timers → Pending (I/O) Callbacks → Idle/Prepare → Poll → Check (setImmediate) → Close Callbacks. They give an orderly schedule for async callbacks.
  • 5. Async APIs:
    • Callbacks → traditional, but can cause callback hell.
    • Promises → cleaner chaining.
    • Async/Await → syntactic sugar over Promises, the most readable.
  • 6. Cluster and Worker Threads (Scalability for CPU work):
    • Cluster module: Starts multiple Node.js processes (one per CPU core) that share the same server port.
    • Worker Threads: Run JS code in parallel threads for CPU-intensive tasks.
Diagram: How Node.js Handles Many Requests on One Thread
Code Example 1: Event Loop — Node.js Does Not Wait
console.log("Task 1");
setTimeout(() => {
    console.log("Task 2 (via event loop)");
}, 2000);
console.log("Task 3");

// OUTPUT: Task 1 → Task 3 → (after 2s) Task 2 (via event loop)
Code Example 2: Thread Pool — Crypto Does Not Block
const crypto = require("crypto");

console.log("Hashing started...");
crypto.pbkdf2("password", "salt", 100000, 64, "sha512", (err, key) => {
    console.log("Hashing complete!");         // runs in libuv thread pool
});
console.log("Non-blocking: still responsive to other tasks");

// OUTPUT: Hashing started... → Non-blocking: still responsive... → Hashing complete!
  • Syntax: crypto.pbkdf2(password, salt, iterations, keylen, digest, callback)
  • password: The text to hash.
  • salt: Random extra data so that identical passwords give different hashes.
  • iterations: How many times to repeat hashing (more = safer but slower).
  • keylen: The length of the output key in bytes.
  • digest: The hash algorithm, for example 'sha512'.
  • callback(err, derivedKey): Runs when hashing finishes.
Code Example 3: Cluster Module — Use All CPU Cores
const cluster = require("cluster");
const http = require("http");
const os = require("os");

if (cluster.isPrimary) {
    // Primary process: create one worker per CPU core
    os.cpus().forEach(() => cluster.fork());
} else {
    // Each worker runs its own server on the SAME port
    http.createServer((req, res) => {
        res.end(`Handled by worker ${process.pid}`);
    }).listen(3000);
}
  • cluster.isPrimary: true in the main (parent) process.
  • cluster.fork(): Creates a new worker process (a copy of this file).
  • os.cpus(): Returns an array with one entry per CPU core.
Code Example 4: Worker Threads — Heavy Calculation Without Blocking
const { Worker, isMainThread, parentPort } = require("worker_threads");

if (isMainThread) {
    const worker = new Worker(__filename);         // run this same file in a new thread
    worker.on("message", (sum) => console.log("Sum:", sum));
    console.log("Main thread is free!");
} else {
    let sum = 0;
    for (let i = 0; i < 1e9; i++) sum += i;        // heavy CPU work
    parentPort.postMessage(sum);                    // send result to main thread
}
  • new Worker(filename): Starts a new thread that runs the given file.
  • isMainThread: true in the main thread, false inside a worker.
  • parentPort.postMessage(value): Sends data from the worker back to the main thread.
  • worker.on('message', fn): Receives that data in the main thread.
Summary Table: Node.js Concurrency
ConceptWhat It DoesBenefit
Single-Threaded Event LoopExecutes JS and manages async callbacksCore concurrency mechanism
Non-Blocking I/ODelegates I/O to the OS / libuvKeeps the main thread free
libuv Thread PoolBackground threads for heavy tasksPrevents blocking
Event Loop PhasesStructured callback schedulingEfficient task handling
Async APIsCallbacks, Promises, async/awaitManage async flow
Cluster / Worker ThreadsMulti-core utilizationScalability for CPU tasks
For CPU-Intensive Workloads, Node.js Offloads to:
  • libuv Thread Pool: For built-in async functions (crypto, zlib, fs).
  • Worker Threads: For your own parallel JS code.
  • Cluster Module: Spreads load across processes on all CPU cores.
  • This keeps the main thread free for new requests.
In Short:
  • Single-threaded does NOT mean single-task.
  • Node.js delegates I/O to libuv / the OS and keeps running other code.
  • Finished tasks are queued, and the event loop runs them when the stack is free.
  • CPU-heavy work goes to the thread pool or worker threads.
  • The cluster module uses all CPU cores.
  • “Event Loop + libuv + Thread Pool + Callback Queue” work together to give concurrency.
मराठी सारांश (Concurrency चे Key Concepts):
  • Concurrency म्हणजे एकाच कालावधीत अनेक कामे हाताळणे.
  • 1. Event Loop: एक thread JS चालवतो. Event loop सतत बघतो, stack रिकामा आहे का, callback तयार आहे का.
  • 2. Non-blocking I/O: File / DB / network काम OS ला देतो. Main thread कधीच रिकामा बसत नाही.
  • 3. Thread Pool: 4 threads. File, DNS, crypto (password hashing) सारखी heavy कामे.
  • 4. Event Loop Phases: Timers → Pending → Idle → Poll → Check → Close.
  • 5. Async APIs: Callback → Promise → Async/Await.
  • 6. Cluster: प्रत्येक CPU core साठी वेगळा Node process. Worker Threads: heavy calculation वेगळ्या thread मध्ये.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: Single-threaded म्हणजे single-task नाही!

Quick Interview Answers (Concurrency)

Q. How is Node.js single-threaded but concurrent?
  • It runs JS on one thread.
  • It achieves concurrency using the event loop, non-blocking I/O, async APIs (callback, promise, async/await) and libuv’s background threads.
Q. What enables concurrency in Node.js?
  • The event loop, async I/O, the libuv thread pool, and async APIs such as Promises and async/await.
Q. What if an operation is CPU-bound?
  • Offload it to the libuv thread pool (built-in functions) or to Worker Threads (custom code).
  • Or scale across cores with the Cluster module.
Q. How does Node.js stay scalable?
  • Through its event-driven architecture: thousands of I/O operations are managed without blocking and without creating a thread for each one.

Q. The Event Loop (Deep Dive — 6 Phases)

Definition:
  • The Event Loop is the core mechanism managed by libuv that gives Node.js its non-blocking behavior.
  • It is a continuous loop that constantly checks: "Is the Call Stack empty? Are there pending callbacks?" If yes, it picks the next callback and pushes it to the Call Stack.
  • The Event Loop processes callbacks in a strict order of 6 phases. Each phase has its own FIFO queue.
Diagram: The Node.js Event Loop — 6 Phases
  • Phase 1 — Timers: Executes setTimeout() and setInterval() callbacks when their timers expire. ⚠️ Delay is a minimum threshold, not exact.
  • Phase 2 — Pending Callbacks: Executes I/O callbacks deferred from the previous cycle (e.g., TCP error reports).
  • Phase 3 — Idle / Prepare: Internal use only. Developers don't interact with this phase.
  • Phase 4 — Poll (Most Important):
    • Retrieves new I/O events and executes their callbacks. Node.js spends most time here.
    • If no events are ready, the Event Loop blocks and waits here for new I/O events.
    • Stops waiting if a Timer expires or setImmediate() has callbacks.
  • Phase 5 — Check: Executes setImmediate() callbacks — always runs after Poll phase.
  • Phase 6 — Close Callbacks: Executes cleanup callbacks — e.g., socket.on('close', ...).
मराठी सारांश (Event Loop चे 6 Phases):
  • Event Loop हा libuv चालवतो. सतत फिरत राहतो आणि stack रिकामा असेल तर पुढचा callback चालवतो. प्रत्येक phase ची स्वतःची FIFO रांग असते.
  • 1. Timers: setTimeout / setInterval ची वेळ झाली असेल तर त्यांचे callbacks. Delay ही किमान वेळ आहे.
  • 2. Pending Callbacks: मागच्या फेरीतून राहिलेले I/O callbacks (उदा. TCP errors).
  • 3. Idle / Prepare: Node चे अंतर्गत काम. आपला code इथे नसतो.
  • 4. Poll (सर्वात महत्वाचा): नवीन I/O events घेतो आणि त्यांचे callbacks चालवतो. काम नसेल तर इथेच थांबून वाट पाहतो.
  • 5. Check: setImmediate चे callbacks. नेहमी Poll नंतर.
  • 6. Close: cleanup callbacks, उदा. socket.on('close').
  • लक्षात ठेवा: "T-P-I-P-C-C" (Timers, Pending, Idle, Poll, Check, Close).

Q. Microtasks Queue & process.nextTick()

Concept:
  • Microtasks are NOT part of the 6-phase Event Loop. They are managed separately by Node.js with highest priority.
  • The Microtask Queue is completely emptied between EVERY phase of the Event Loop.
  • There are two Microtask Queues in strict priority order.
  • 1. nextTick Queue (Highest Priority):
    • Holds callbacks from process.nextTick(fn). Executes immediately after current sync operation — before Promises, before the next Event Loop phase.
    • ⚠️ Recursive process.nextTick() can starve the Event Loop — I/O events will never be processed!
  • 2. Promise Queue (Second Priority):
    • Holds callbacks from .then(), .catch(), async/await. Runs after nextTick queue is fully empty.
  • Priority Order (Memorize for Interviews!):
    • 🥇 Synchronous code → 🥈 process.nextTick() → 🥉 Promise.then() → 4️⃣ setImmediate() → 5️⃣ setTimeout()
मराठी सारांश (Microtasks Queue):
  • Microtasks हे event loop च्या 6 phases चा भाग नाहीत. ते Node.js स्वतः सांभाळतो आणि त्यांना सर्वात जास्त priority असते.
  • प्रत्येक phase नंतर microtask queue पूर्ण रिकामी केली जाते, मगच पुढचा phase.
  • 1. nextTick queue: process.nextTick() चे callbacks. सर्वात आधी. Recursive वापरल्यास event loop अडकतो (starvation).
  • 2. Promise queue: .then(), .catch(), await नंतरचा code. nextTick रिकामी झाल्यावर.
  • क्रम: Sync → nextTick → Promise → setImmediate → setTimeout (I/O callback च्या आत. Main file मध्ये शेवटच्या दोघांचा क्रम बदलू शकतो).
  • उदाहरण: Microtasks = VIP रांग. प्रत्येक काउंटर (phase) नंतर आधी VIP लोकांना serve केले जाते.
Code Example: Execution Order Proof (Must Know for Interviews!)
console.log("1. SYNC: Start");

setTimeout(() => console.log("5. MACROTASK: setTimeout"), 0);
setImmediate(() => console.log("4. MACROTASK: setImmediate"));
Promise.resolve().then(() => console.log("3. MICROTASK: Promise.then()"));
process.nextTick(() => console.log("2. MICROTASK: process.nextTick()"));

console.log("1. SYNC: End");

// ACTUAL OUTPUT:
// 1. SYNC: Start
// 1. SYNC: End
// 2. MICROTASK: process.nextTick()
// 3. MICROTASK: Promise.then()
// 4. MACROTASK: setImmediate
// 5. MACROTASK: setTimeout
  • ⚠️ Note: In the main module, the order of setTimeout(fn, 0) and setImmediate() can vary from run to run, depending on how fast the process starts.
  • Inside an I/O callback, setImmediate() always runs first.

Q. Key Characteristics of the Event Loop

Concept:
  • The event loop is the core mechanism that lets Node.js handle async, non-blocking operations even though JS runs on one thread.
  • It coordinates between synchronous code (call stack) and async callbacks (queues), keeping Node.js responsive for I/O-heavy work such as HTTP requests and file reads.
  • Simple example: a security guard walking in a circle around 6 doors (phases). At each door they let waiting people (callbacks) in, one by one. After every door they first serve the VIPs (microtasks).
  • 1. Single-Threaded: Only one operation runs at a time on the main thread, inside the V8 engine.
  • 2. Non-Blocking I/O: I/O is never awaited on the main thread. It is offloaded to the libuv thread pool or OS async mechanisms.
  • 3. Continuous Loop: It keeps running as long as there is work. It stops only when:
    • The program calls process.exit(), OR
    • No pending work remains (no timers, I/O, listeners or microtasks).
  • 4. Tick: One full round through all 6 phases is called one loop iteration (a "tick").
  • 5. Six Queues: In each iteration the loop visits 6 queues. Each holds a different type of callback that must eventually run on the call stack.

Q. What is the Event Queue? (Event Queue & Event Loop)

Concept:
  • The Event Queue (callback queue) is a waiting line where callbacks of finished async tasks are stored until they can run.
  • The Event Loop is the mechanism that keeps checking: "Is the call stack empty?" If yes, it takes callbacks from the queues and runs them.
  • In Node.js there is not just one queue. The loop goes through 6 phase queues plus the microtask queues.
  • Callbacks are taken in FIFO order (First In, First Out), like a ticket queue.
  • Simple example: a doctor’s waiting room. Patients (callbacks) sit in line, and when the doctor (call stack) is free, the receptionist (event loop) sends in the next patient.
How Does the Event Queue Work?
  1. Events are emitted: Async operations (fs.readFile, setTimeout) finish and push their callbacks into their queues.
  2. The event loop monitors the queues: It continuously checks whether the call stack is empty.
  3. Execution: If the stack is empty, it picks the next callback (FIFO) and runs it.
  4. Repeat: If that callback starts another async task, that task's callback later goes back into a queue.
Role of the Event Queue:
  • Manages async tasks: Holds callbacks for later execution.
  • Ensures non-blocking behavior: Node.js never waits for slow operations. It just picks the result from the queue later.
  • Maintains performance: Callbacks run in order, efficiently, even with thousands of events.
Diagram: Call Stack ⇄ Event Loop ⇄ Event Queue
मराठी सारांश (Event Loop Characteristics आणि Event Queue):
  • Event Loop: एकाच thread वर async कामे handle करणारी सतत फिरणारी यंत्रणा. Sync code आणि async callbacks यांच्यात समन्वय साधते.
  • केव्हा थांबतो? process.exit() केल्यावर किंवा कोणतेही काम (timers, I/O, listeners) शिल्लक नसल्यावर.
  • Tick: 6 phases ची एक पूर्ण फेरी म्हणजे एक tick.
  • Event Queue: पूर्ण झालेल्या async कामांचे callbacks वाट पाहत बसतात ती रांग (FIFO, जो आधी आला तो आधी).
  • उदाहरण: डॉक्टरचे waiting room. Patients (callbacks) रांगेत. डॉक्टर (call stack) मोकळे झाले की receptionist (event loop) पुढच्या patient ला आत पाठवते.
  • Role: async कामे सांभाळणे, non-blocking ठेवणे, हजारो events असले तरी performance टिकवणे.

Q. Internal Components of Node.js Execution (V8 + libuv)

Concept:
  • Two major components execute your Node.js code: the V8 Engine (runs JS) and the libuv Library (handles async work and the event loop).
  • 1. V8 Engine: Executes JavaScript code. It has 2 main parts:
    • Heap Memory: Where memory is allocated for variables, objects and functions you declare. (The garbage collector cleans it.)
    • Call Stack:
      • Executes code in LIFO order (Last In, First Out), like a stack of plates.
      • Each function call is pushed onto the stack, and popped off when it returns.
      • Execution always starts with the Global Execution Context (the global scope) at the bottom of the stack.
  • 2. libuv Library: A multi-platform C library for Node.js.
    • Implements the event loop.
    • Provides a thread pool for background tasks (file I/O, DNS, crypto).
    • Provides cross-platform non-blocking I/O for files, networking and timers.
    • It is the bridge between JavaScript (V8) and the OS.
    • Strategy: libuv first uses the OS’s native async mechanism (for example, network sockets). If that is not possible (for example, file reads), it uses its thread pool, so the main thread never blocks.
  • Queue Ownership (Important Note):
    • The Timer, I/O, Check and Close queues are part of the event loop in libuv.
    • The microtask queues (nextTick and Promise) are NOT part of libuv. The Node.js runtime (and V8 for promises) manages them.
Diagram: V8 (Heap + Call Stack) and libuv (Event Loop + Thread Pool)
Code Example: Call Stack in Action (LIFO)
function multiply(a, b) { return a * b; }      // pushed 3rd, popped 1st
function square(n) { return multiply(n, n); }  // pushed 2nd, popped 2nd
function printSquare(n) {                      // pushed 1st, popped 3rd
    console.log(square(n));
}
printSquare(4);   // 16

// Stack (top → bottom): multiply → square → printSquare → Global Context
मराठी सारांश (V8 आणि libuv Components):
  • V8 मध्ये दोन भाग: Heap (variables, objects, functions साठी memory) आणि Call Stack (functions LIFO पद्धतीने चालतात, ताटांच्या थप्पीसारखे).
  • Execution नेहमी Global Execution Context पासून सुरू होतो.
  • libuv: Event loop, thread pool (4 threads), cross-platform async I/O देते. V8 आणि OS यांच्यातील पूल.
  • libuv आधी OS ची native async सुविधा वापरतो. ती नसेल (उदा. file read) तर thread pool वापरतो.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: Timer / I/O / Check / Close queues libuv च्या. nextTick आणि Promise queues Node.js runtime च्या.

Q. Execution Flow of the Event Loop (Step by Step)

  1. Run all synchronous code first (call stack). User sync code always has priority over async code.
  2. The event loop starts only when the call stack is empty.
  3. Run all microtasks: process.nextTick() first → then Promise callbacks.
  4. Timers phase (setTimeout, setInterval) → run microtasks again.
  5. Pending callbacks phase → run microtasks again.
  6. Poll phase (I/O callbacks) → run microtasks again.
  7. Check phase (setImmediate) → run microtasks again.
  8. Close callbacks phase → run microtasks again.
  9. Loop again while work remains. (Since Node 11, microtasks also run between each individual timer or immediate callback.)
Diagram: Event Loop Cycle with Microtasks in the Center
Key Takeaways:
  • Node.js uses an event-driven, non-blocking model.
  • V8 executes code. libuv manages async operations and the event loop.
  • Microtasks (nextTick, Promises) run between phases, before any other callback.
  • Order: Synchronous → Microtasks → Event Loop Phases (Timers → I/O → Check → Close).
  • Node.js stays responsive by offloading slow work to libuv’s thread pool.
मराठी सारांश (Event Loop Execution Flow):
  • 1. आधी सगळा synchronous code चालतो.
  • 2. Call stack रिकामा झाल्यावरच event loop सुरू होतो.
  • 3. आधी microtasks: nextTick → मग Promises.
  • 4. मग Timers → microtasks → Pending → microtasks → Poll → microtasks → Check → microtasks → Close → microtasks.
  • 5. काम शिल्लक असेपर्यंत हे चक्र चालू राहते.
  • लक्षात ठेवा: Microtasks हे VIP आहेत. प्रत्येक phase नंतर आधी त्यांना संधी मिळते.

Q. What is process.nextTick()? (In Detail)

Concept:
  • process.nextTick() is a Node.js-specific async API (it doesn't exist in browsers).
  • It schedules a callback to run immediately after the current synchronous operation finishes, before the event loop moves on.
  • It runs before Promises, timers, I/O and setImmediate.
  • In plain words, it tells Node.js: “Finish what you're doing right now, then run this before anything else.”
  • Simple example: a teacher says, “After this sentence, before the next topic, note down this point.” It is done right away, before moving on.
  • Syntax: process.nextTick(callback[, ...args])
  • callback: The function to run on the next tick.
  • ...args: Optional extra arguments passed to the callback.
  • Returns: undefined.
How Does It Work (Execution Order)?
  1. Calling process.nextTick(cb) adds cb to the nextTick queue.
  2. When the current JS operation (stack) finishes, Node.js checks the nextTick queue.
  3. All callbacks in it run before the Promise queue and before the next event loop phase.
