  4. A nextTick added inside another nextTick goes into the same queue and also runs before continuing. ⚠️ Recursive use can starve the event loop (I/O never runs).
  • Order: Synchronous code → process.nextTick() → Promise microtasks (.then/.catch) → setTimeout/setImmediate (in their phases).
Why Use process.nextTick()?
  • 1. Run code right after the current operation: After the current function finishes, but before any I/O or timers.
  • 2. Let setup finish first: For example, in a constructor, defer an emit so that the user has time to attach listeners.
  • 3. Handle errors asynchronously: Instead of throwing synchronously (which may crash before handlers exist), emit the error on the next tick.
  • 4. Make APIs consistently async: A function should always call its callback asynchronously, even when the data is already available (cached). This avoids confusing mixed behavior.
  • 5. Break up recursion: Breaks deep synchronous recursion (avoids stack overflow), but use it carefully.
Code Example 1: Basic Order
console.log("Start");
setTimeout(() => console.log("setTimeout"), 0);
Promise.resolve().then(() => console.log("Promise"));
process.nextTick(() => console.log("nextTick"));
console.log("End");

// OUTPUT: Start → End → nextTick → Promise → setTimeout
Code Example 2: Emit Event After Listener is Attached
const EventEmitter = require('events');

class MyServer extends EventEmitter {
    constructor() {
        super();
        // ❌ this.emit('ready') here → nobody is listening yet!
        process.nextTick(() => this.emit('ready'));  // ✅ wait until setup is done
    }
}

const server = new MyServer();
server.on('ready', () => console.log('Server is ready!'));  // attached in time
Code Example 3: Consistent Async API (Error Handling)
function getUser(id, callback) {
    if (!id) {
        // ✅ report the error asynchronously, like the success path
        return process.nextTick(callback, new Error('ID is required'));
    }
    setTimeout(() => callback(null, { id, name: 'Vishal' }), 100);
}

getUser(null, (err, user) => {
    if (err) return console.error(err.message);  // "ID is required"
    console.log(user);
});
Pointprocess.nextTick()
PurposeSchedule a callback to run immediately after the current operation
Runs beforePromises, timers, I/O callbacks, setImmediate
Best forQuick follow-up work, finishing setup, async error handling
DangerOveruse or recursion can starve the event loop
मराठी सारांश (process.nextTick):
  • काय आहे? Node.js चे खास function. चालू sync काम संपताच, इतर कोणत्याही गोष्टीपूर्वी (Promise, setTimeout, I/O) callback चालवते.
  • उदाहरण: शिक्षक म्हणतात, "हे वाक्य संपले की पुढचा topic सुरू करण्यापूर्वी हा point लिहा."
  • Syntax: process.nextTick(callback, ...args)
  • क्रम: Sync → nextTick → Promise → setTimeout / setImmediate.
  • का वापरायचे? Setup पूर्ण झाल्यावर event emit करण्यासाठी, errors async पद्धतीने देण्यासाठी, API नेहमी async ठेवण्यासाठी.
  • धोका: nextTick मध्ये पुन्हा nextTick (recursion) केल्यास event loop अडकतो, I/O कधीच चालत नाही.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. Between setTimeout and Promise.resolve().then(), which one executes first?
  • The Promise executes first. Promise callbacks go to the Microtask Queue. The Event Loop always empties the full Microtask Queue before moving to the next phase (including Timers where setTimeout lives).
Q2. Between process.nextTick() and a Promise, which executes first?
  • process.nextTick() always executes first. The nextTick Queue has higher priority than the Promise Queue within Microtasks.
Q3. What is the difference between setTimeout(fn, 0) and setImmediate(fn)?
  • setImmediate() runs in the Check phase — always after the Poll phase (after I/O).
  • Inside an I/O callback: setImmediate() always runs before setTimeout(fn, 0).
मराठी सारांश:
  • V8 Engine म्हणजे काय? — Google ने बनवलेला JavaScript Engine. JIT Compilation मुळे JS code खूप fast execute होतो. Garbage Collector memory manage करतो.
  • libuv म्हणजे काय? — Node.js मधील C library जी async I/O handle करते. Thread Pool (default 4 threads) आणि Event Loop यात असतात.
  • Single-Threaded असून Concurrency कशी?
    • Node.js I/O tasks libuv च्या Thread Pool कडे पाठवतो. Main thread delegate करून पुढे निघून जातो.
    • उदाहरण: कॅशियर (main thread) orders घेतो → kitchen (libuv) ला पाठवतो → पुढच्या customer कडे जातो → kitchen तयार झाल्यावर notify करते.
  • Event Loop चे 6 Phases लक्षात ठेवा:
    • ① Timers (setTimeout) → ② Pending Callbacks → ③ Idle/Prepare → ④ Poll (main I/O) → ⑤ Check (setImmediate) → ⑥ Close Callbacks
  • Execution Order (Interview साठी महत्वाचे):
    • 🥇 Sync code → 🥈 process.nextTick() → 🥉 Promise.then() → 4️⃣ setImmediate() → 5️⃣ setTimeout()

Chapter 18.5: Control Flow & Event-Driven Programming

Q. What is Control Flow in Node.js?

Definition:
  • Control Flow is the order and sequence in which individual statements, instructions, function calls, and asynchronous callbacks are evaluated and executed by the JavaScript engine in a Node.js application.
  • In a traditional (synchronous) language, code runs top-to-bottom, one line at a time. Control flow is linear and predictable.
  • In Node.js, control flow is non-linear because asynchronous operations (like reading a file, making a DB call, or a network request) are delegated to the background (libuv) and their results come back later via callbacks, Promises, or Async/Await.
  • Proper control flow management is critical in Node.js to prevent blocking, avoid callback hell, and ensure operations execute in the correct order.
  • Control flow can be managed using 3 patterns: Callbacks (oldest) → Promises (cleaner) → Async/Await (most modern & readable).
Diagram: Sync vs Async Control Flow
  • Point 1 — Why Control Flow matters in Node.js:
    • Node.js is single-threaded. If one operation blocks the thread (e.g., a heavy synchronous task), no other requests can be processed until it finishes.
    • Control flow patterns let us write code that doesn't block — the thread stays free to handle other requests while waiting for I/O results.
  • Point 2 — Synchronous Control Flow:
    • Code executes line-by-line, top to bottom. Each statement must finish before the next one starts.
    • It BLOCKS the main thread during execution. No other code can run until the current operation finishes.
    • Example: fs.readFileSync() — blocks the entire thread until the file is fully read.
    • ⚠️ Problem: Suitable only for scripts, not for servers handling multiple users.
  • Point 3 — Asynchronous Control Flow:
    • Long-running operations (file I/O, DB queries, HTTP calls) are delegated to libuv/OS in the background.
    • Node.js does NOT wait. It immediately moves to the next line.
    • When the background operation is complete, a Callback / Promise / Async result is pushed back to the Event Loop and executed.
    • ✅ Benefit: The main thread stays free, making Node.js highly scalable.
  • Point 4 — Three Patterns to Manage Async Control Flow:
    • 1. Callbacks (Oldest): A function passed as an argument, called when the async task is done. Simple but can lead to "Callback Hell" (deeply nested functions).
    • 2. Promises (Cleaner): An object representing a value that will be available in the future. Uses .then() and .catch(). Avoids nesting, more readable.
    • 3. Async/Await (Most Modern): Syntactic sugar built on Promises. Makes async code look and behave like synchronous code. Most readable and recommended in modern Node.js.
Code Example 1: Synchronous (Blocking) Control Flow
const fs = require('fs');

console.log("1. Start");

// ❌ BLOCKS the thread - no other code can run until file is fully read
const data = fs.readFileSync('file.txt', 'utf8');
console.log("2. File content:", data);

console.log("3. End");
// Output ORDER: 1 → 2 → 3 (always sequential, blocked on file read)
Code Example 2: Asynchronous (Non-Blocking) Control Flow — Callback
const fs = require('fs');

console.log("1. Start");

// ✅ Non-blocking - Node.js delegates to OS and moves to next line immediately
fs.readFile('file.txt', 'utf8', (err, data) => {
    // This callback runs LATER when file reading is done
    console.log("3. File content:", data); // runs AFTER "2. End"
});

console.log("2. End"); 
// Output ORDER: 1 → 2 → 3 (file read happens in background!)
Code Example 3: Async/Await (Modern & Recommended)
const fs = require('fs').promises;

async function readMyFile() {
    console.log("1. Start");

    // ✅ Looks synchronous but is NON-BLOCKING under the hood
    const data = await fs.readFile('file.txt', 'utf8');
    console.log("2. File content:", data);

    console.log("3. End");
}

readMyFile();
// Output ORDER: 1 → 2 → 3 (clean, readable, non-blocking!)
⚡ Short Interview Answer — Q. What is Control Flow in Node.js?
  • Control Flow is the order in which code statements and async callbacks are executed in a Node.js application.
  • Because Node.js is single-threaded and non-blocking, async tasks are delegated to libuv in the background, and the main thread continues executing.
  • When the async task finishes, the result is brought back via the Event Loop using Callbacks, Promises, or Async/Await.
  • The 3 patterns to manage async control flow: Callbacks → Promises → Async/Await (in order from oldest to modern).
मराठी सारांश:
  • Control Flow म्हणजे काय? — आपल्या program मधील code कोणत्या क्रमाने (order मध्ये) execute होतो, याला Control Flow म्हणतात.
  • Synchronous Control Flow: — code वरून खाली (line-by-line) execute होतो. एक काम पूर्ण झाल्याशिवाय दुसरे सुरू होत नाही. यामुळे Thread Block होतो. (उदा: readFileSync)
  • Asynchronous Control Flow: — फाईल वाचणे, DB query, किंवा HTTP request यांसारखी कामे Node.js libuv ला देतो आणि स्वतः पुढचे काम करत राहतो. काम झाल्यावर Callback/Promise द्वारे result परत येतो.
  • Sync vs Async उदाहरण:
    • Sync = रेल्वे टिकीट काउंटर वर रांगेत उभे राहणे (blocking). पुढचा माणूस टिकीट मिळाल्याशिवाय पुढे जाऊ शकत नाही.
    • Async = Online टिकीट book करणे. आपण order दिला आणि पुढे निघालो. Ticket confirm झाल्यावर SMS येतो (callback).
  • Async Control Flow manage करण्याचे 3 मार्ग:
    • 📌 Callbacks: सगळ्यात जुना मार्ग. Function मध्ये दुसरे Function पाठवायचे. जास्त nesting झाल्यास "Callback Hell" होतो.
    • 📌 Promises: .then() आणि .catch() वापरतात. Callbacks पेक्षा जास्त readable.
    • 📌 Async/Await: सगळ्यात modern आणि recommended. Async code synchronous सारखा दिसतो, वाचायला सोपा असतो.
  • Interview मध्ये लक्षात ठेवा: Node.js non-blocking आहे कारण ते async control flow वापरते — heavy tasks background ला पाठवतो, Event Loop द्वारे result परत आणतो.

Q. Why Does Control Flow Matter in Node.js?

  • Node.js apps often run many async operations (file, DB, API) at once.
  • These may finish in a different order than they were started (non-deterministic).
  • Without proper control flow, you can get bugs, for example using data before it has arrived.
  • Proper control flow ensures that:
    • Tasks execute in the desired order (for example, login → then fetch profile).
    • Errors are handled correctly (nothing is silently lost).
    • The program does not hang or crash because of uncoordinated async calls.
Code Example: Wrong vs Right Control Flow
const fs = require('fs');

// ❌ WRONG: data is used before the file is read
let content;
fs.readFile('file.txt', 'utf8', (err, data) => { content = data; });
console.log(content);   // undefined!

// ✅ RIGHT: use the data only after it arrives
async function main() {
    const data = await fs.promises.readFile('file.txt', 'utf8');
    console.log(data);  // correct content
}
main();

Q. Control Flow Patterns in Node.js (More Explanation)

  • 1. Synchronous Control Flow: Runs line by line. Each statement finishes before the next starts (for example, variable assignment or math).
  • 2. Asynchronous Control Flow: Does not wait for I/O. Handled with callbacks, Promises, async/await and the event loop.
  • 3. Callback-Based (Traditional):
    • A function is passed as an argument and runs when the operation finishes.
    • Problem: Deep nesting leads to callback hell.
  • 4. Promise-Based:
    • Represents the future completion of an async task.
    • Chain with .then() and handle errors with .catch().
  • 5. Async/Await (Best Practice):
    • Built on Promises but looks synchronous.
    • Errors are handled with try/catch. Recommended for modern projects.
  • 6. Event-Driven (EventEmitter): Functions run when specific events occur. Useful for streams, sockets and server requests.
  • 7. Event Loop and Non-Blocking I/O (Underlying Engine): Delegates I/O, timers and network calls to the OS / libuv. Finished callbacks are queued and run by the event loop.
  • 8. libuv Thread Pool: Handles heavy I/O tasks in background threads.
  • Control flow libraries: Libraries like async help run tasks in series (one by one), parallel (all together) or waterfall (each passes its result to the next).
Diagram: Evolution of Async Control Flow Patterns
Code Example 1: Callback Hell (Problem)
getUser(1, (user) => {
    getOrders(user.id, (orders) => {
        getPayment(orders[0].id, (payment) => {
            console.log(payment);   // ❌ "pyramid of doom", hard to read and debug
        });
    });
});
Code Example 2: Promise Chaining (.then / .catch)
fetch("https://jsonplaceholder.typicode.com/todos/1")
    .then(res => res.json())          // step 1: convert response to JSON
    .then(data => console.log(data))  // step 2: use the data
    .catch(err => console.error(err)); // any error in the chain lands here
Code Example 3: Parallel Execution with Promise.all (Async/Await)
async function fetchMultiple() {
    try {
        // both requests start AT THE SAME TIME
        const [res1, res2] = await Promise.all([
            fetch("https://jsonplaceholder.typicode.com/todos/1"),
            fetch("https://jsonplaceholder.typicode.com/todos/2")
        ]);
        const data1 = await res1.json();
        const data2 = await res2.json();
        console.log(data1, data2);
    } catch (err) {
        console.error("One request failed:", err);
    }
}
fetchMultiple();
  • Syntax: Promise.all(iterableOfPromises)
  • Argument: An array of Promises.
  • Returns: One Promise that resolves to an array of all results, in the same order.
  • ⚠️ If any one rejects, the whole Promise.all rejects.
  • Promise.race(array): Settles as soon as the first Promise settles (useful for timeouts).
  • Promise.allSettled(array): Waits for all and gives each result's status. It never rejects early.
Code Example 4: Event-Driven Control Flow
const EventEmitter = require("events");
const emitter = new EventEmitter();

emitter.on("done", () => console.log("Task done!"));  // what to do WHEN it happens
emitter.emit("done");                                   // make it happen
Best Practices for Control Flow:
  • Prefer async/await for readability.
  • Use Promises for chaining operations.
  • Use Promise.all() / Promise.race() for parallel execution.
  • Use EventEmitter for custom event-driven logic.
  • Always handle errors (try/catch or .catch()).
  • Avoid ...Sync methods in servers.
In Short:
  • Control flow is the order in which sync and async code runs.
  • Node.js manages it with the event loop, callbacks, Promises, async/await and EventEmitters.
  • The pattern evolution is Callbacks → Promises → Async/Await (preferred).
  • Under the hood, the event loop, EventEmitter and non-blocking I/O provide concurrency.
मराठी सारांश (Control Flow का महत्वाचे आणि Patterns):
  • का महत्वाचे? अनेक async कामे वेगळ्या क्रमाने संपू शकतात. योग्य control flow नसेल तर data येण्यापूर्वीच वापरला जातो (undefined bug).
  • योग्य control flow मुळे कामे बरोबर क्रमाने होतात, errors handle होतात, आणि program hang / crash होत नाही.
  • Callbacks: जुनी पद्धत. जास्त nesting = callback hell (pyramid).
  • Promises: .then() chain आणि .catch() ने error.
  • Async/Await: सर्वात सोपे. try/catch ने error. Modern projects मध्ये हेच वापरा.
  • Promise.all: अनेक कामे एकाच वेळी (parallel) चालवायला. एक जरी fail झाले तर सगळे fail.
  • EventEmitter: एखादी घटना घडल्यावर function चालवायला.
  • Best practice: Async/Await वापरा, नेहमी errors handle करा, server मध्ये Sync methods टाळा.

Q. What is a Callback? (Error-First Callbacks & Callback Hell)

Concept:
  • A callback is a function passed as an argument to another function, to be called later, when a task finishes.
  • It was the original way Node.js handled async results (fs.readFile(path, callback)).
  • Error-first callback (Node.js convention): the first argument is always the error (null if none), and the result comes after: (err, data) => {}.
  • Callback hell ("pyramid of doom"): when async steps depend on each other, callbacks nest inside callbacks. The code drifts right, becomes hard to read and debug, and needs an error check at every level.
  • Simple example: "Call me when the parcel arrives" (callback). Callback hell is "Call me when the parcel arrives, then I'll call the courier, then he'll call the warehouse..." with each call waiting inside the previous one.
Code Example: Error-First Callback and Callback Hell
const fs = require('fs');

// Error-first callback
fs.readFile('user.json', 'utf8', (err, data) => {
    if (err) return console.error('Failed:', err.message);  // 1st arg = error
    console.log('Data:', data);                              // 2nd arg = result
});

// ❌ Callback hell: each step nested inside the previous one
getUser(1, (err, user) => {
    if (err) return handle(err);
    getOrders(user.id, (err, orders) => {
        if (err) return handle(err);
        getPayment(orders[0].id, (err, payment) => {
            if (err) return handle(err);
            sendReceipt(payment, (err) => {
                if (err) return handle(err);
                console.log('Done!');        // pyramid of doom →→→
            });
        });
    });
});

Q. What is a Promise? (States, then / catch / finally)

Concept:
  • A Promise is an object that represents a value that will be available in the future: the eventual result (or failure) of an async task.
  • Instead of passing a callback in, the function returns a Promise, and you attach handlers with .then() / .catch().
  • Chaining .then() keeps the code flat instead of nested, and one .catch() handles errors from the whole chain.
  • Simple example: a restaurant token. You get the token (the Promise) immediately. Later it is either fulfilled (food ready) or rejected (item out of stock).
3 States of a Promise:
  • Pending: Initial state. The task is still running.
  • Fulfilled (resolved): The task succeeded. resolve(value) was called → .then() runs.
  • Rejected: The task failed. reject(error) was called (or an error was thrown) → .catch() runs.
  • Once fulfilled or rejected, a Promise is settled and its state can never change again.
Diagram: Promise States
Code Example: Creating and Using a Promise
// Creating a Promise
function getUser(id) {
    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
        setTimeout(() => {
            if (id > 0) resolve({ id, name: 'Vishal' });     // → fulfilled
            else reject(new Error('Invalid id'));              // → rejected
        }, 1000);
    });
}

// Using it: flat chain instead of nesting
getUser(1)
    .then(user => getOrders(user.id))     // return next Promise → chain continues
    .then(orders => getPayment(orders[0].id))
    .then(payment => console.log('Paid:', payment))
    .catch(err => console.error('Error anywhere in chain:', err.message))
    .finally(() => console.log('Always runs (cleanup, hide loader)'));
  • Syntax: new Promise((resolve, reject) => { ... })
  • resolve(value): Marks the Promise fulfilled with value.
  • reject(error): Marks it rejected. Pass an Error object, not a string, so you get a stack trace.
  • .then(onFulfilled): Runs on success. Whatever it returns becomes the next Promise in the chain.
  • .catch(onRejected): Runs if any earlier step rejected or threw.
  • .finally(fn): Runs either way; it receives no value.
  • Shortcuts: Promise.resolve(v) / Promise.reject(err) create already-settled Promises.

Q. Promise Combinators: Promise.all, allSettled, race, any

Concept:
  • These static methods run several Promises in parallel and combine their results in different ways.
  • They take an array (iterable) of Promises and return one new Promise.
MethodResolves whenRejects whenResultUse case
Promise.allAll succeedAny one fails (fail-fast)Array of values (same order)Load user + orders + settings together; all are required
Promise.allSettledAll finish (success or fail)NeverArray of {status, value / reason}Send 100 emails; report which failed
Promise.raceThe first to settle succeedsThe first to settle failsFirst settled value / errorTimeout: request vs a 5s timer
Promise.anyThe first to succeedAll fail (AggregateError)First fulfilled valueFastest of several mirror servers
Code Example: The 4 Combinators
const ok   = (v, ms) => new Promise(r => setTimeout(() => r(v), ms));
const fail = (e, ms) => new Promise((_, j) => setTimeout(() => j(new Error(e)), ms));

// all → fails fast if ANY fails
await Promise.all([ok('A', 100), ok('B', 200)]);          // ['A', 'B']
await Promise.all([ok('A', 100), fail('B broke', 50)]);   // ❌ throws 'B broke'

// allSettled → never rejects, gives every outcome
await Promise.allSettled([ok('A', 100), fail('B broke', 50)]);
// [ {status:'fulfilled', value:'A'}, {status:'rejected', reason: Error('B broke')} ]

// race → first SETTLED wins (success or failure)
await Promise.race([ok('slow', 500), fail('timeout', 100)]);  // ❌ 'timeout'

// any → first SUCCESS wins, ignores failures
await Promise.any([fail('x', 50), ok('fast', 100), ok('slow', 300)]);  // 'fast'

Q. What is Async/Await?

Concept:
  • async/await is syntax built on top of Promises that makes async code look and read like normal synchronous code.
  • async before a function → the function always returns a Promise (a returned value becomes a resolved Promise; a thrown error becomes a rejected one).
  • await before a Promise → pauses that async function until the Promise settles, then gives you the value (or throws the error).
  • ⚠️ await pauses only that function, not the whole program. The event loop keeps serving other requests.
  • Errors are handled with normal try / catch / finally.
  • Simple example: reading a recipe step by step: "wait for the water to boil, then add rice". It reads in order, but other people in the kitchen keep working while you wait.
Code Example: Same Flow with Async/Await
async function checkout(userId) {
    try {
        const user    = await getUser(userId);          // wait, then continue
        const orders  = await getOrders(user.id);
        const payment = await getPayment(orders[0].id);
        console.log('Paid:', payment);
        return payment;                                  // → resolved Promise
    } catch (err) {
        console.error('Checkout failed:', err.message);  // any await that rejects lands here
        throw err;                                       // re-throw if caller must know
    } finally {
        console.log('Cleanup');
    }
}

// ⚠️ Sequential vs Parallel
async function loadDashboard(id) {
    // ❌ Slow: one after another (300ms + 300ms + 300ms)
    // const u = await getUser(id); const o = await getOrders(id); const s = await getStats(id);

    // ✅ Fast: independent tasks in parallel (~300ms total)
    const [user, orders, stats] = await Promise.all([getUser(id), getOrders(id), getStats(id)]);
    return { user, orders, stats };
}
  • Syntax: async function name() { const x = await promise; } or const fn = async () => { ... }
  • await works only inside an async function (or at the top level of an ES module).
  • Don't await in a loop for independent tasks. Collect the Promises and use Promise.all.
  • Forgetting await gives you a pending Promise instead of the value, a very common bug.
  • Inside Array.forEach, await doesn't wait. Use for...of (sequential) or Promise.all(arr.map(...)) (parallel).
PointCallbacksPromisesAsync/Await
Readability❌ Nested (callback hell)⚠️ Flat chain✅ Reads like sync code
Error handlingif (err) at every levelOne .catch()try / catch
Parallel tasksHard (manual counters)Promise.allawait Promise.all
ReturnsNothing (result goes to the callback)A PromiseA Promise
IntroducedOriginal Node.jsES2015 (ES6)ES2017
मराठी सारांश (Callbacks → Promises → Async/Await):
  • Callback: दुसऱ्या function ला दिलेले function, जे काम संपल्यावर नंतर चालते. Node ची पद्धत error-first: (err, data). पहिला argument error, नसेल तर null.
  • Callback hell: एकावर एक अवलंबून कामे असल्यास callbacks आत आत जातात (pyramid). वाचायला, debug करायला कठीण, प्रत्येक level ला error check.
  • Promise: भविष्यात मिळणाऱ्या result चे object. 3 states: Pending (चालू), Fulfilled (यश, .then), Rejected (अपयश, .catch). एकदा settle झाले की बदलत नाही.
  • .then() chain ने code सपाट (flat) राहतो. एकाच .catch() मध्ये सगळ्या errors. .finally() नेहमी चालते.
  • Promise.all: सगळे यशस्वी हवे, एक fail = सगळे fail. allSettled: सगळ्यांचा निकाल (कधीच reject नाही). race: जो पहिला संपेल (यश / अपयश). any: जो पहिला यशस्वी, सगळे fail झाले तरच error.
  • async/await: Promises वरचीच सोपी पद्धत. Code synchronous सारखा दिसतो. async function नेहमी Promise देते. await फक्त त्या function ला थांबवतो, संपूर्ण program ला नाही.
  • Errors try / catch ने. स्वतंत्र कामे एकामागून एक await करू नका, await Promise.all([...]) वापरा (जलद).
  • सामान्य चुका: await विसरणे (Promise मिळतो, value नाही). forEach मध्ये await थांबत नाही, for...of वापरा.
  • उदाहरण: हॉटेलचा token (Promise). Token लगेच मिळतो. नंतर जेवण तयार (fulfilled) किंवा "संपले" (rejected).

Q. What is util.promisify()? (And the util Module)

Concept:
  • util.promisify(fn) converts a callback-based function (Node's error-first (err, result) style) into a function that returns a Promise.
  • This lets you use old callback APIs with async/await, without wrapping each one in new Promise(...) by hand.
  • It works only for functions whose last argument is an error-first callback.
  • Many core modules now have ready Promise versions (fs/promises, timers/promises, dns/promises, stream/promises), so you mostly need promisify for older libraries or your own callback functions.
  • Simple example: a plug adapter. Your old two-pin charger (callback API) fits the new three-pin socket (async/await) through the adapter.
Code Example: promisify in Action
const util = require('util');
const fs = require('fs');

// Before: callback style
fs.readFile('a.txt', 'utf8', (err, data) => { /* ... */ });

// After: promise style with promisify
const readFileAsync = util.promisify(fs.readFile);

async function main() {
    try {
        const data = await readFileAsync('a.txt', 'utf8');
        console.log(data);
    } catch (err) {
        console.error(err.message);
    }
}
main();

// Your own callback function
function getUser(id, cb) {
    setTimeout(() => (id > 0 ? cb(null, { id }) : cb(new Error('bad id'))), 100);
}
const getUserAsync = util.promisify(getUser);
getUserAsync(5).then(console.log);            // { id: 5 }

// Promise-based timers (no promisify needed)
const { setTimeout: sleep } = require('timers/promises');
await sleep(1000);                            // wait 1 second (in an async function / ESM)

// Reverse: Promise → callback
const cbStyle = util.callbackify(async (x) => x * 2);
cbStyle(4, (err, result) => console.log(result));   // 8
  • Syntax: const fnAsync = util.promisify(original)
  • original: A function whose last parameter is a callback (err, value) => {}.
  • Returns: A new function with the same arguments (minus the callback) that returns a Promise: it resolves with value or rejects with err.
  • ⚠️ If the function uses this (an object method), bind it first: util.promisify(obj.method.bind(obj)).
Other Useful util Functions:
  • util.callbackify(asyncFn): the reverse, Promise → callback style.
  • util.format('%s is %d', 'age', 25): printf-style string formatting (used by console.log).
  • util.inspect(obj, { depth: null, colors: true }): print deeply nested objects fully.
  • util.types.isPromise(x), util.isDeepStrictEqual(a, b): type checks and deep comparison.
  • util.parseArgs(): parse command-line arguments (Node 18.3+).
मराठी सारांश (util.promisify):
  • util.promisify(fn) हे जुन्या callback ((err, result)) पद्धतीच्या function ला Promise देणाऱ्या function मध्ये बदलते.
  • त्यामुळे जुने APIs async/await सोबत वापरता येतात, प्रत्येकासाठी हाताने new Promise लिहावे लागत नाही.
  • अट: function चा शेवटचा argument error-first callback असावा.
  • आता अनेक core modules चे Promise versions तयार आहेत (fs/promises, timers/promises, stream/promises). Promisify मुख्यतः जुन्या libraries किंवा स्वतःच्या callback functions साठी.
  • this वापरणाऱ्या methods ना आधी .bind(obj) करा.
  • इतर util: callbackify (उलट), format, inspect (खोल objects पूर्ण दाखवणे), isDeepStrictEqual, parseArgs.
  • उदाहरण: plug adapter. जुना charger (callback) नवीन socket (async/await) मध्ये adapter ने बसतो.

Q. What is Event-Driven Programming?

Definition:
  • Event-Driven Programming is a programming paradigm where the flow and execution of the application is controlled and determined by events — such as user actions, messages from other programs, sensor outputs, or the completion of asynchronous I/O operations.
  • Instead of following a rigid, top-to-bottom sequence of instructions, the program waits for events to occur and then reacts to them by executing the appropriate handler function.
  • In Node.js, everything is event-driven. When an HTTP request arrives, when a file finishes reading, or when a database query returns — all of these are events.
  • This model allows Node.js to handle thousands of concurrent connections without creating new threads, making it highly scalable and memory-efficient.
  • The two key components are: Event Emitter (the source that fires events) and Event Listener (the function that reacts to those events).
Diagram: Traditional vs Event-Driven Programming
  • Point 1 — How Traditional Programming Works (Problem):
    • In traditional (non-event-driven) programming, the thread WAITS and BLOCKS at each I/O operation.
    • Example: Read file → wait → Query DB → wait → Send Response. The thread is idle during all the waits.
    • ⚠️ This wastes CPU time and cannot scale — if 1000 users send requests simultaneously, you need 1000 threads!
  • Point 2 — How Event-Driven Programming Solves It:
    • Instead of waiting, the program registers an Event Listener (a callback function) for the operation and immediately moves on to handle other tasks.
    • When the operation finishes (e.g., file reading is complete), an Event is Emitted.
    • The Event Loop picks up this event and executes the registered listener function.
    • ✅ The single thread is always busy doing useful work — never sitting idle, waiting for I/O.
  • Point 3 — Key Terminology:
    • Event: A signal that something has happened (e.g., file read complete, HTTP request received, timer expired).
    • Event Emitter: The object/module that generates and fires the event using .emit().
    • Event Listener: The callback function registered using .on() that executes when the event fires.
    • Event Loop: The mechanism that constantly monitors the event queue and executes pending listeners when the call stack is empty.
    • Event Queue: The waiting line where callbacks of emitted events or finished async tasks sit until the event loop picks them up, one by one (FIFO).
    • Callbacks: The functions registered to respond to specific events.
    • You can attach multiple listeners to the same event.
  • Point 4 — Real-World Analogies:
    • 🍽️ Restaurant Token System: You order food (register a listener), get a token and sit down (move on). When food is ready, waiter calls your token (event emitted) → you collect your food (listener executes). You didn't stand and wait!
    • 📱 Phone Notification: You make a WhatsApp call (async task), don't wait staring at screen, and continue your work. When the other person picks up (event emitted) → notification fires (listener executes).
    • 📦 Online Shopping: Place an order (register event), continue your life (move on). When the delivery arrives (event emitted) → doorbell rings (listener executes).
  • Point 5 — Why Node.js is Built on This Model:
    • Node.js uses a single-threaded Event Loop. With event-driven programming, it can handle millions of I/O operations concurrently without spawning new threads.
    • Most of Node's built-in modules (HTTP, FS, Streams, Net) are built on top of the EventEmitter class.
Code Example: Event-Driven HTTP Server (Real-world)
const http = require('http');

// 'request' is an EVENT that fires when a user visits the server
// The function below is the EVENT LISTENER
const server = http.createServer((req, res) => {
    // This code runs ONLY when the 'request' event is emitted
    console.log("Event fired: request received!");
    res.end("Hello from Node.js Event-Driven Server!");
});

// The server LISTENS for 'request' events (non-blocking)
server.listen(3000, () => {
    console.log("Server is ready — waiting for events...");
});

// Node.js does NOT block here — it goes to the Event Loop
// and waits for the next 'request' event to fire!
Code Example: Custom Event-Driven Flow
const EventEmitter = require('events');
const emitter = new EventEmitter();

// Step 1: Register an Event Listener (.on)
emitter.on('orderPlaced', (orderId) => {
    // This runs when 'orderPlaced' event is emitted
    console.log(`Kitchen notified! Preparing order #${orderId}`);
});

console.log("Customer placed order...");

// Step 2: Emit the Event (.emit) — triggers the listener above
emitter.emit('orderPlaced', 42);

console.log("Cashier is free to handle next customer!");
// Output:
// Customer placed order...
// Kitchen notified! Preparing order #42
// Cashier is free to handle next customer!
⚡ Short Interview Answer — Q. What is Event-Driven Programming?
  • Event-Driven Programming is a paradigm where the flow of the application is controlled by events rather than a fixed sequence of steps.
  • Instead of blocking and waiting, the program registers a listener for an event and immediately moves on.
  • When the event occurs (e.g., file read complete, HTTP request), the Event Loop picks it up and fires the registered listener.
  • Node.js is entirely built on this model — using the EventEmitter class, allowing it to handle thousands of concurrent I/O operations on a single thread.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Event-Driven Programming म्हणजे काय? — ज्या Programming मध्ये program चा flow Events वर अवलंबून असतो (म्हणजे एखाद्या गोष्टीचे Signal आल्यावर code execute होतो), त्याला Event-Driven Programming म्हणतात.
  • Traditional Programming मध्ये काय होतं? — Thread एका कामासाठी थांबायचा (Block व्हायचा). File वाचेपर्यंत थांब, DB query येईपर्यंत थांब — यामुळे server slow व्हायचा.
  • Event-Driven मध्ये काय होतं?
    • आपण Event साठी एक Listener (callback function) register करतो.
    • Node.js पुढचे काम करायला निघून जातो (block होत नाही).
    • जेव्हा काम पूर्ण होतं, तेव्हा Event emit होतो आणि Listener आपोआप execute होतो.
  • रोजच्या जीवनातील उदाहरण (Restaurant):
    • तुम्ही हॉटेलमध्ये जेवण order करता (Listener register केला).
    • Token घेऊन बसता — काउंटरवर उभे राहत नाही (Non-blocking).
    • जेवण तयार झाल्यावर waiter token call करतो (Event emit झाला).
    • तुम्ही उठून जेवण घेता (Listener execute झाला).
  • Key Terms लक्षात ठेवा:
    • 📌 Event: काही तरी घडलंय याचं Signal (उदा: request आला, file वाचली गेली).
    • 📌 Event Emitter: तो जो Event fire करतो — .emit() वापरतो.
    • 📌 Event Listener: तो function जो Event ऐकतो आणि react करतो — .on() वापरतो.
    • 📌 Event Loop: सतत check करतो — "कोणता Event आला का? आला असेल तर Listener run कर!"
  • Interview मध्ये लक्षात ठेवा: Node.js मध्ये HTTP Server, File System, Streams — सगळे EventEmitter वापरतात. म्हणूनच Node.js एकाच Thread वर हजारो requests handle करू शकतो!

Q. How Does Node.js Use the Event-Driven Model in Real Scenarios?

  • HTTP Server: Listens for 'request' events.
  • Streams: Emit 'data', 'end' and 'error' events.
  • File System: Async fs.readFile / fs.writeFile use callbacks and events on completion.
  • Socket.io: Real-time apps listen for 'connection' and 'message' events.

Q. Why Event-Driven? (Key Benefits)

  • Non-blocking I/O: Node.js never waits for I/O operations.
  • Highly scalable: A single thread can handle thousands of clients concurrently.
  • Efficient for I/O tasks: Perfect for APIs, file operations and DB queries.
  • Better performance: Less idle time and better use of resources.
  • Simplified flow: Events and callbacks make async logic easy to organize.
  • Responsive: Reacts quickly to user actions or new data.
  • Loose coupling: The emitter doesn't need to know who is listening, which keeps code modular.
मराठी सारांश (Event-Driven चे फायदे):
  • Real use: HTTP server ('request'), Streams ('data', 'end', 'error'), fs callbacks, Socket.io ('connection', 'message').
  • Non-blocking: I/O ची वाट पाहत थांबत नाही.
  • Scalable: एकाच thread वर हजारो clients.
  • Fast आणि Responsive: CPU रिकामा बसत नाही. User action ला लगेच प्रतिसाद.
  • Modular code: Emitter ला listener कोण आहे हे माहीत असण्याची गरज नाही.

Chapter 19: Networking Basics (DNS, IP, TCP)

Q. What are IP Address and Ports?

  • IP Address: It identifies a device on a network.
    IPv4 (e.g., 192.168.1.10 - 32 bits)
    IPv6 (e.g., 2001:db8::1 - 128 bits).
  • Port: A port identifies a specific service/process running on that IP. (e.g., 192.168.1.10:3000).
    Common Ports: HTTP=80, HTTPS=443, FTP=21, SSH=22.
  • localhost / 127.0.0.1: Your own computer (loopback address).
  • Port range: 0–65535. Ports 0–1023 are reserved for well-known services; dev servers usually use 3000, 5000, 8000 and so on.
  • Error EADDRINUSE: Another program is already using that port.
मराठी सारांश (IP Address आणि Ports):
  • IP Address: network वरील device चा पत्ता. IPv4 (192.168.1.10, 32-bit) आणि IPv6 (2001:db8::1, 128-bit).
  • Port: त्या device वरील ठराविक program / service चा नंबर. उदा. 192.168.1.10:3000.
  • उदाहरण: IP = इमारतीचा पत्ता. Port = फ्लॅट नंबर. पत्र योग्य फ्लॅटपर्यंत पोहोचायला दोन्ही लागतात.
  • Common ports: HTTP 80, HTTPS 443, SSH 22, FTP 21.
  • localhost (127.0.0.1) = आपलाच computer.
  • EADDRINUSE error = तो port आधीच कोणीतरी वापरत आहे.

Q. What is DNS (Domain Name System)?

Definition:
  • DNS translates human-readable domain names (like google.com) into computer-readable IP addresses (like 142.250.x.x).
  • Without DNS, we would have to remember complex IP numbers to visit websites.
  • Results are cached (browser, OS, resolver) so the lookup doesn't happen every time.
  • A Record: Maps domain to IPv4.
  • AAAA Record: Maps domain to IPv6.
  • CNAME Record: Alias from one domain to another.
DNS Resolution Flow (How it finds the IP)
मराठी सारांश:
  • DNS म्हणजे इंटरनेटची Phonebook!
  • आपण नाव टाकतो (google.com) आणि DNS त्याचा नंबर (IP Address) शोधून आणतो.
  • हे काम 4 टप्प्यात होते: Recursive -> Root -> TLD -> Authoritative Server.
  • Recursive Resolver: ISP चा server, आपल्या वतीने शोधाशोध करतो. Root: ".com" कुठे आहे ते सांगतो. TLD: "google.com" चा authoritative server कुठे ते सांगतो. Authoritative: नेमका IP देतो.
  • Records: A (domain → IPv4), AAAA (domain → IPv6), CNAME (एका नावाचे दुसरे नाव / alias).
  • उत्तर cache होते, म्हणून पुढच्या वेळी जलद.
  • Node.js मध्ये: require('dns').lookup('google.com', (err, ip) => ...).

Q. TCP vs UDP

  • TCP (Transmission Control Protocol): Reliable, Connection-oriented. It guarantees data delivery in the correct order. Used for web browsing, emails.
  • UDP (User Datagram Protocol): Fast, Connectionless. It does not guarantee delivery. Used for Video calling, Live streaming, Gaming.
PointTCPUDP
ConnectionConnection-oriented (handshake first)Connectionless
ReliabilityGuaranteed delivery, re-sends lost packetsNo guarantee, lost packets are gone
OrderData arrives in orderOrder not guaranteed
SpeedSlower (more checks)Faster
Used forWeb (HTTP), email, file transferVideo calls, live streaming, gaming, DNS
Node.js modulenetdgram
मराठी सारांश (TCP vs UDP):
  • TCP: विश्वासार्ह. आधी connection बनवतो, data पोहोचला याची खात्री करतो, क्रमाने देतो. थोडा slow. Web, email साठी.
  • UDP: जलद, पण खात्री नाही. Packet हरवला तर हरवला. Video call, gaming, live streaming साठी (थोडा data गेला तरी चालते, पण उशीर नको).
  • उदाहरण: TCP = registered post (पोच पावती मिळते). UDP = पत्रके वाटणे (पोहोचले का ते बघत नाही).
  • TCP वैशिष्ट्ये: 3-way handshake, packets क्रमाने, हरवलेले पुन्हा पाठवतो (retransmission), flow control. HTTP / HTTPS, email, file transfer TCP वर.
  • UDP वैशिष्ट्ये: handshake नाही, header लहान, overhead कमी. DNS, video call, online games, live streaming.
  • Node.js modules: TCP साठी net, UDP साठी dgram.
  • निवड: data पूर्ण आणि अचूक हवा → TCP. वेग महत्वाचा आणि थोडा loss चालतो → UDP.

Q. What is the TCP 3-Way Handshake?

  • Before TCP transfers any data, it establishes a reliable connection.
  • 1. SYN: Client says "Hello, can we connect?"
  • 2. SYN-ACK: Server says "Yes, I am ready!"
  • 3. ACK: Client says "Confirmed."
  • Only after these 3 steps does real data start flowing.
Diagram: TCP 3-Way Handshake
मराठी सारांश (TCP 3-Way Handshake):
  • TCP data पाठवण्यापूर्वी connection पक्के करतो.
  • 1. SYN: client विचारतो, "आपण connect होऊ का?"
  • 2. SYN-ACK: server म्हणतो, "हो, मी तयार आहे."
  • 3. ACK: client म्हणतो, "ठीक आहे, पक्के."
  • उदाहरण: फोन call. "Hello?" → "Hello, बोला" → "ठीक, ऐका..." मगच खरी बोलणी.

Q. Complete Web Flow (Interview Favorite)

Q. What happens when you type https://www.google.com in a browser?
  • This classic question tests whether you understand the full journey of a request, from the address bar to the painted page.
  1. DNS Resolution: Browser finds the IP address of google.com.
  2. TCP Connection: Browser establishes a reliable connection using the 3-Way Handshake.
  3. TLS Handshake: Browser secures the connection (HTTPS encryption).
  4. HTTP Request: Browser sends a GET / request.
  5. HTTP Response: Server sends the HTML, CSS, JS.
  6. Rendering: Browser paints the webpage on the screen.
मराठी सारांश:
  • Interview मध्ये विचारतात: "URL टाकल्यावर काय होते?" उत्तर 4 शब्दात: DNS → TCP → TLS → HTTP.
  • आधी IP शोधतो (DNS), मग Connection बनवतो (TCP), मग Security लावतो (TLS), आणि शेवटी Data मागतो (HTTP).
  • 1. DNS: google.com चा IP शोधणे. 2. TCP: 3-way handshake ने connection. 3. TLS: HTTPS साठी encryption keys ठरवणे.
  • 4. HTTP Request: GET /. 5. Response: server HTML, CSS, JS पाठवतो. 6. Rendering: browser page screen वर काढतो.

Quick Revision Summary

Protocol Role
DNS Translates Domain Name to IP Address.
TCP Reliable, connection-oriented data transfer.
TLS / SSL Secures and encrypts the connection (HTTPS).
HTTP Application layer to send Request/Response data.

Chapter 20: Encoding & Data Representation

Q. What is Encoding in Node.js?

Definition:
  • Encoding is the rule for converting between human-readable text and binary data (raw bytes).
  • The same bytes can mean different text under different encodings, so the encoding must match on both sides.
  • When you read a file using fs.readFileSync("demo.txt") without specifying an encoding, Node.js returns a Buffer (raw bytes).
  • If you specify an encoding like "utf8", Node.js converts those bytes into a human-readable String.
  • Formula: Buffer (Bytes) + Encoding = Text (String)

Q. Common Encodings in Node.js

  • 1. UTF-8: The most commonly used text encoding. It supports almost all writing systems, including English, Marathi, Chinese, and Emojis (😊).
  • 2. ASCII: Represents a limited set of 128 characters (A-Z, 0-9, basic punctuation). It cannot represent Emojis or Marathi/Hindi text.
  • 3. Base64: Represents binary data as text. Useful when you need to send binary data (like images or tokens) over systems that only expect text (like JSON or URLs).
  • 4. Hex: Represents binary data using hexadecimal characters (0-9, a-f). Commonly used for debugging, hashes, and cryptography.
Code Example: Converting to Base64 and Hex
const buffer = Buffer.from("Hello");

// Convert to Base64
console.log(buffer.toString("base64")); // Output: SGVsbG8=

// Convert to Hex
console.log(buffer.toString("hex")); // Output: 48656c6c6f
मराठी सारांश (Encoding):
  • Encoding म्हणजे text आणि bytes (binary) यांच्यात रूपांतर करण्याचा नियम.
  • Encoding न देता readFileSync('a.txt') केले तर Buffer मिळतो. 'utf8' दिले तर string मिळते.
  • सूत्र: Buffer (bytes) + Encoding = Text.
  • UTF-8: सर्वात जास्त वापर. मराठी, हिंदी, emoji सगळे चालते.
  • ASCII: फक्त 128 अक्षरे (A-Z, 0-9). मराठी / emoji नाही.
  • Base64: binary (image, file) ला text स्वरूपात पाठवायला (JSON, URL मध्ये).
  • Hex: 0-9, a-f अक्षरांत bytes दाखवणे. Hashes, debugging साठी.

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. Is Base64 an encryption method?
  • No. Base64 is NOT encryption. It is just a text representation of binary data. Anyone can easily decode a Base64 string back to its original form using Buffer.from(encoded, "base64").toString("utf8").
Q2. What is the difference between Buffer and Encoding?
  • A Buffer stores raw binary data (bytes). An Encoding tells Node.js how to interpret or convert those raw bytes into a string or characters.
मराठी सारांश:
  • लक्षात ठेवा: Base64 किंवा Hex हे Encryption (सुरक्षा) नाहीयेत!
  • ते फक्त Data ला दुसऱ्या रूपात दाखवण्याचे मार्ग आहेत.
  • जसे आपण मराठी शब्द English अक्षरात लिहितो (उदा. "Kasa ahes?"), तसंच Base64 मुळे binary data text स्वरूपात दिसतो.
  • कोणीही तो वाचू शकतो: Buffer.from(str, 'base64').toString().
  • Buffer vs Encoding: Buffer = कच्चे bytes साठवतो. Encoding = त्या bytes चा अर्थ कसा लावायचा ते सांगते.

Quick Revision Summary

Encoding Main Purpose
UTF-8 Standard encoding for Unicode text (Supports all languages & emojis).
ASCII Basic English characters only.
Base64 Converting binary data (images, files) into a text format.
Hex Representing bytes as hexadecimal text (used in hashes).
SECTION 7: ADVANCED WEB PATTERNS

Chapter 21: State Management (Cookies, Sessions, LocalStorage)

Q. Why do we need State Management?

Concept:
  • HTTP is a stateless protocol: every request is completely independent.
  • After you log in, the server forgets who you are on the very next page load.
  • To solve this, we use state management techniques (cookies, sessions, JWT) so the server can "remember" you.
  • Simple example: a shopkeeper with no memory. Every time you walk in, you must show your membership card again.

Q. What is a Cookie?

  • A Cookie is a small piece of data (~4KB) stored on the Client-Side (User's Browser).
  • It is sent automatically with every HTTP request to the server.
  • Use Cases: Storing preferences (Dark Mode), small tracking identifiers, or Session IDs.
  • Security: Less secure because it is stored on the client side and can be modified.

Q. What is a Session?

  • A Session stores user data on the Server-Side (in Memory, Database, or Redis).
  • How it works:
  • When a user logs in, the server creates a Session and generates a unique Session ID.
  • This ID is sent to the browser and saved as a Cookie.
  • On the next request, the browser sends the Session ID back.
  • The server looks up the corresponding data in its database.
  • Security: More secure because sensitive data (like passwords or cart items) stays on the server.
मराठी सारांश:
  • HTTP हा भुलक्कड (Stateless) आहे.
  • तो युजरला लगेच विसरतो.
  • म्हणून आपण Cookie वापरतो.
  • Cookie म्हणजे एक छोटी पावती (Token) जी Browser स्वतःकडे ठेवतो.
  • Session म्हणजे मुख्य रजिस्टर जे Server कडे असते.
  • Browser ती पावती (Cookie) दाखवतो आणि Server रजिस्टर (Session) मधून ओळखतो की हा कोण आहे.

Q. Cookie Options (httpOnly, secure, sameSite, maxAge)

Concept:
  • When the server sets a cookie, it can attach options (flags) that control security and lifetime.
  • These flags are the main defence for cookies that hold sensitive data such as a Session ID or JWT.
  • Simple example: a locker key with rules written on it: "only the bank can use it", "only inside the building", "valid for 1 day".
  • httpOnly: true → JavaScript in the browser cannot read the cookie (document.cookie won't show it). Protects against XSS token theft. Always use it for session/auth cookies.
  • secure: true → the cookie is sent only over HTTPS, never plain HTTP. Use in production. (Set to false on http://localhost, or the cookie won't be sent.)
  • sameSite → controls whether the cookie is sent on requests from other sites. Protects against CSRF.
    • 'strict': only sent from your own site. Most secure.
    • 'lax' (browser default): sent on normal top-level link navigation, not on cross-site forms/fetch. Good default.
    • 'none': sent everywhere (needed when frontend and backend are on different domains). Requires secure: true.
  • maxAge → lifetime in milliseconds from now (e.g. 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000 = 1 day). expires → an exact Date instead.
  • No maxAge/expires → a session cookie, deleted when the browser closes.
  • path / domain → which URLs/domains the cookie is sent to (default: the current domain, path /).
Code Example: Setting, Reading and Clearing Cookies in Express
const express = require('express');
const cookieParser = require('cookie-parser');     // npm install cookie-parser
const app = express();
app.use(cookieParser());                           // fills req.cookies

app.get('/login', (req, res) => {
    res.cookie('token', 'abc123', {
        httpOnly: true,                            // JS can't read it (XSS-safe)
        secure: process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production', // HTTPS only in production
        sameSite: 'lax',                           // CSRF protection
        maxAge: 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000                // 1 day
    });
    res.send('Logged in, cookie set');
});

app.get('/profile', (req, res) => {
    res.send(`Your token: ${req.cookies.token}`);  // read cookie
});

app.get('/logout', (req, res) => {
    res.clearCookie('token');                      // delete cookie
    res.send('Logged out');
});
  • Syntax: res.cookie(name, value, options) — name: cookie name; value: string (or object, JSON-encoded); options: the flags above.
  • Syntax: res.clearCookie(name, options) — deletes the cookie (pass the same path/domain used when setting it).
  • req.cookies: Object of all cookies sent by the browser (needs cookie-parser).
मराठी सारांश (Cookie Options):
  • httpOnly: true → browser मधील JavaScript cookie वाचू शकत नाही. XSS हल्ल्यापासून token सुरक्षित. Login cookies साठी नेहमी लावा.
  • secure: true → cookie फक्त HTTPS वरून जाते. Production मध्ये लावा, localhost (http) वर false ठेवा.
  • sameSite → दुसऱ्या website वरून आलेल्या request सोबत cookie जावी का. CSRF पासून संरक्षण. strict (सर्वात सुरक्षित), lax (default, योग्य), none (वेगळ्या domains साठी, secure आवश्यक).
  • maxAge → किती milliseconds टिकावी. उदा. 1 दिवस = 24*60*60*1000. न दिल्यास browser बंद केल्यावर cookie जाते.
  • Express: res.cookie() set, req.cookies read (cookie-parser), res.clearCookie() delete.
  • उदाहरण: लॉकरच्या चावीवरचे नियम: "फक्त बँकच वापरू शकते (httpOnly)", "फक्त सुरक्षित इमारतीत (secure)", "1 दिवस valid (maxAge)".

Q. How to Use Sessions in Express (express-session)?

Concept:
  • express-session is the standard middleware for server-side sessions in Express.
  • It creates a session for each visitor, stores the session data on the server, and sends only a Session ID to the browser in a cookie (named connect.sid by default).
  • On every request, it reads that cookie, finds the matching data and puts it on req.session.
  • Simple example: a coat-check counter. You get a token number (Session ID); your coat (data) stays with the counter (server).
Code Example: Login / Profile / Logout with express-session
const express = require('express');
const session = require('express-session');      // npm install express-session
const app = express();
app.use(express.json());

app.use(session({
    secret: process.env.SESSION_SECRET,           // signs the session ID cookie
    resave: false,                                // don't re-save unchanged sessions
    saveUninitialized: false,                     // don't create empty sessions
    cookie: {
        httpOnly: true,
        secure: process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production',
        sameSite: 'lax',
        maxAge: 60 * 60 * 1000                    // 1 hour
    }
    // store: new RedisStore({ client })          // production: use Redis/DB, not memory
}));

app.post('/login', (req, res) => {
    // ...check username/password in DB first...
    req.session.user = { id: 101, name: 'Vishal' };   // save data in session
    res.json({ message: 'Logged in' });
});

app.get('/profile', (req, res) => {
    if (!req.session.user) return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Please login' });
    res.json(req.session.user);                       // data comes back on every request
});

app.post('/logout', (req, res) => {
    req.session.destroy(() => {                       // delete session on server
        res.clearCookie('connect.sid');               // delete cookie in browser
        res.json({ message: 'Logged out' });
    });
});
Syntax & Options: app.use(session(options))
  • secret: Secret key used to sign the session ID cookie so it can't be forged. Keep it in .env.
  • resave: false → don't save the session back to the store if nothing changed.
  • saveUninitialized: false → don't create a session until something is stored in it (saves storage, helps with privacy laws).
  • cookie: Options for the Session ID cookie (httpOnly, secure, sameSite, maxAge).
  • store: Where session data lives. Default is memory, which leaks memory and is lost on restart, so it's for development only. Use Redis (connect-redis) or MongoDB (connect-mongo) in production.
  • req.session: Object where you read/write the user's data. req.session.destroy(cb): Deletes the session.
मराठी सारांश (express-session):
  • express-session हा Express मध्ये sessions साठीचा middleware आहे.
  • User चा data server वर राहतो. Browser ला फक्त Session ID cookie (connect.sid) मिळते.
  • प्रत्येक request ला तो ID वाचून data req.session मध्ये देतो.
  • Login: req.session.user = {...}. Check: if (!req.session.user) → 401. Logout: req.session.destroy() + res.clearCookie().
  • Options: secret (ID sign करायला, .env मध्ये), resave: false, saveUninitialized: false, cookie (httpOnly, secure, maxAge).
  • Production: default memory store वापरू नका (restart ला data जातो, memory leak). Redis किंवा MongoDB store वापरा.
  • उदाहरण: हॉटेलचा coat-check. तुम्हाला token नंबर (Session ID), कोट (data) counter (server) कडे.

Q. LocalStorage vs SessionStorage

  • LocalStorage: Stores data in the browser persistently. Data stays even if you close the browser and restart your computer. (Max size ~5MB).
  • SessionStorage: Temporary storage. Data is completely cleared the moment you close the browser tab. (Max size ~5MB).
  • Neither is sent to the server automatically (unlike cookies). Both are read with JavaScript: localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark').
  • ⚠️ Don't store sensitive tokens here: any JavaScript on the page (including injected XSS scripts) can read them.
PointCookieLocalStorageSessionStorage
Size~4 KB~5 MB~5 MB
LifetimeUntil expiry dateForever (until cleared)Until the tab closes
Sent to server✅ With every request❌ No❌ No
Can be hidden from JS✅ With HttpOnly❌ No❌ No
मराठी सारांश (Cookie, LocalStorage, SessionStorage):
  • Cookie: browser मध्ये, ~4KB. प्रत्येक request सोबत server ला आपोआप जाते. Session ID, preferences साठी. HttpOnly लावल्यास JS वाचू शकत नाही.
  • LocalStorage: ~5MB, कायमचे (browser बंद केले तरी राहते). Server ला आपोआप जात नाही. उदा. dark mode setting.
  • SessionStorage: ~5MB, फक्त tab चालू असेपर्यंत. Tab बंद केला की संपले. उदा. form चा तात्पुरता data.
  • Security: passwords / sensitive tokens LocalStorage मध्ये ठेवू नका. XSS script ते चोरू शकते.

Chapter 22: MVC Architecture

Q. What is MVC?

Definition:
  • MVC stands for Model – View – Controller.
  • It is a popular architectural pattern that structures an application into three separate parts, each with one responsibility (separation of concerns).
  • Simple example: a restaurant. The kitchen (Model) prepares food, the plate presentation (View) is what the customer sees, and the waiter (Controller) takes the order, gets it from the kitchen and serves it.
  • 1. Model (Data): Represents the database structure and business logic. It handles saving, fetching, and updating data. (e.g., models/product.js).
  • 2. View (UI): Represents what the user sees. It displays the data sent by the controller in HTML/EJS/HBS format. (e.g., views/index.ejs).
  • 3. Controller (Brain): The middleman. It receives requests from the user, asks the Model for data, and then passes that data to the View to be rendered. (e.g., controllers/shop.js).
Diagram: MVC Flow
मराठी सारांश:
  • MVC म्हणजे कोडला ३ भागात विभागणे.
  • Model म्हणजे Database चे काम (उदा. Data सेव्ह करणे, आणणे, बदलणे). File: models/product.js.
  • View म्हणजे डिझाईन आणि HTML (user ला दिसणारे). File: views/index.hbs.
  • आणि Controller म्हणजे मॅनेजर, जो Model कडून Data घेतो आणि View ला देतो. File: controllers/shop.js.
  • Flow: Browser → Route → Controller → Model (DB) → Controller → View → Browser.
  • उदाहरण: हॉटेल. Kitchen (Model), ताटातील सजावट (View), waiter (Controller).
  • यामुळे कोड खूप स्वच्छ आणि वाचायला सोपा होतो, आणि वेगवेगळे लोक एकाच वेळी वेगवेगळ्या भागांवर काम करू शकतात.
Code Example: MVC Folder Structure (Express)
project/
├── models/product.js        → data + DB logic
├── views/products.hbs       → HTML template
├── controllers/product.js   → getProducts(req, res) { const list = Product.findAll(); res.render('products', { list }); }
├── routes/product.js        → router.get('/products', getProducts)
└── app.js                   → app.use(productRoutes)

Interview Questions (Detailed)

Q1. Why are Sessions more secure than Cookies?
  • Because Cookies store data openly in the user's browser, meaning the user can view or alter the data.
  • Sessions store data securely on the server.
  • The user only gets a random "Session ID" which is useless without the server's backend database.
Q2. What is the main benefit of using MVC?
  • Separation of Concerns. A UI designer can work on the Views, a database expert can work on the Models, and a backend dev can write Controllers, all without breaking each other's code.
मराठी सारांश (Interview: Sessions आणि MVC):
  • Q1 (Session cookie पेक्षा सुरक्षित का?): Cookie चा data browser मध्ये उघडा असतो, user तो बघू / बदलू शकतो. Session चा data server वर. User कडे फक्त random Session ID, जो server शिवाय निरुपयोगी.
  • Q2 (MVC चा फायदा): Separation of Concerns. Designer Views वर, DB तज्ञ Models वर, backend dev Controllers वर, एकमेकांचा code न मोडता काम करतात.

Quick Revision Summary

Concept Location / Role
Cookie Client-side storage, sent with every request (~4KB).
Session Server-side storage, mapped via Session ID.
Model Database queries and logic.
View HTML / EJS templates for the User Interface.
Controller Handles routes, gets data from Model, sends to View.
SECTION 8: ESSENTIAL SECURITY & CONFIGURATION

Chapter 23: Environment Variables (dotenv)

Q. What are Environment Variables and `.env` files?

Definition:
  • Environment variables are key-value settings given to a process from outside the code (by the OS, the hosting platform or a .env file).
  • In Node.js, a .env file stores sensitive or environment-specific data: API keys, database passwords, ports.
  • We should never hardcode these in the main code.
  • Node.js 20.6+ can also load a file natively: node --env-file=.env app.js (no package needed).
  • Why? If you hardcode a database password in app.js and upload it to GitHub, hackers can steal your data.
  • How? We use a package called dotenv (npm install dotenv).
  • Usage: Create a .env file. Add values like PORT=3000. Then in code, use require('dotenv').config() and access it via process.env.PORT.
  • Security Rule: ALWAYS add .env to your .gitignore file so it never goes to GitHub.
मराठी सारांश:
  • आपण कधीही Password किंवा API Key थेट कोडमध्ये लिहीत नाही, कारण ते GitHub वर public होऊ शकतात.
  • आपण ते .env फाईलमध्ये ठेवतो आणि process.env वापरून वाचतो.
  • Steps: npm install dotenv → .env मध्ये PORT=3000 → code च्या सुरुवातीला require('dotenv').config() → process.env.PORT.
  • Rule: .env नेहमी .gitignore मध्ये टाका. Team साठी values शिवाय .env.example commit करा.
  • सगळ्या values string असतात. Number हवा असेल तर Number(process.env.PORT).
  • फायदा: एकच code, पण development आणि production मध्ये वेगळ्या settings (वेगळा DB).
Code Example: Using .env
# .env  (never commit this file)
PORT=3000
DB_URL=mongodb://localhost:27017/shop
JWT_SECRET=superSecretKey

// app.js
require('dotenv').config();                     // loads .env into process.env
const port = Number(process.env.PORT) || 3000;
console.log(process.env.DB_URL);

Chapter 24: CORS (Cross-Origin Resource Sharing)

Q. What is CORS and why does the browser block requests?

Concept:
  • Browsers have a security feature called the Same-Origin Policy (SOP).
  • Origin = protocol + domain + port. http://localhost:3000 and http://localhost:5000 are different origins.
  • SOP stops a frontend on one origin from reading responses from an API on another origin.
  • CORS (Cross-Origin Resource Sharing) is how the backend says "I allow this origin", using response headers like Access-Control-Allow-Origin.
  • CORS is enforced by the browser only. Postman or server-to-server calls are not blocked.
  • To fix CORS errors in Express, we install the cors package.
  • npm install cors
  • Usage: const cors = require('cors'); app.use(cors());
  • You can also configure it to only allow specific domains (e.g., app.use(cors({ origin: 'http://my-frontend.com' }))).
  • Preflight: For non-simple requests (PUT/DELETE, custom headers, JSON body), the browser first sends an OPTIONS request to ask permission. The cors package answers it automatically.
Code Example: CORS Configuration
const cors = require('cors');

app.use(cors({
    origin: ['http://localhost:3000', 'https://myapp.com'],  // allowed frontends
    methods: ['GET', 'POST', 'PUT', 'DELETE'],
    credentials: true                                         // allow cookies
}));
मराठी सारांश (CORS):
  • Same-Origin Policy: browser चा security नियम. एका origin वरचे page दुसऱ्या origin च्या API चा response वाचू शकत नाही.
  • Origin = protocol + domain + port. localhost:3000 आणि localhost:5000 वेगळे origins आहेत.
  • CORS: backend headers ने सांगतो, "या origin ला परवानगी आहे" (Access-Control-Allow-Origin).
  • Express मध्ये: npm install cors → app.use(cors()). Production मध्ये फक्त ठराविक origins द्या.
  • Preflight: PUT / DELETE किंवा custom headers असतील तर browser आधी OPTIONS request पाठवून परवानगी विचारतो.
  • CORS फक्त browser लागू करतो. Postman ला हा error येत नाही.
  • उदाहरण: सोसायटीचा watchman (browser). मालकाने (backend) यादीत नाव लिहिले असेल तरच पाहुण्याला आत सोडतो.

Chapter 25: Authentication & Password Hashing

Q. Why do we hash passwords (bcrypt)?

  • Never store plain-text passwords in the database (e.g., password: "12345"). If the database is hacked, user accounts are compromised.
  • We use hashing (like the bcrypt package). It turns "12345" into a random string like $2b$10$xyz....
  • Hashing is one-way. You cannot decrypt it back to "12345". During login, bcrypt simply hashes the entered password again and checks if it matches the stored hash.
Code Example: bcrypt Signup and Login
const bcrypt = require('bcrypt');

// Signup: hash before saving
const hash = await bcrypt.hash('12345', 10);      // 10 = salt rounds (cost)
// save hash in DB → "$2b$10$Xyz..."

// Login: compare entered password with stored hash
const ok = await bcrypt.compare('12345', hash);   // true / false
  • bcrypt.hash(password, saltRounds): password = plain text; saltRounds = how many times to process (10–12 is common; higher = slower = safer). Returns a Promise with the hash (salt is stored inside it).
  • bcrypt.compare(plain, hash): Returns a Promise of true or false.

Q. What is JWT (JSON Web Token)?

Definition:
  • JWT is a standard format for access tokens.
  • Instead of keeping user state on the server (like sessions), the server gives the client a signed JWT after a successful login.
  • The client sends this token in a header (Authorization: Bearer <token>) with future requests.
  • It has 3 parts separated by dots: Header.Payload.Signature. The payload is only Base64-encoded (readable by anyone), so never put passwords in it. The signature proves it wasn't changed.
Code Example: Creating and Verifying a JWT
const jwt = require('jsonwebtoken');

// After login:
const token = jwt.sign({ userId: 101, role: 'admin' }, process.env.JWT_SECRET, { expiresIn: '1h' });

// Middleware for protected routes:
function auth(req, res, next) {
    const token = req.headers.authorization?.split(' ')[1];   // "Bearer xxx" → "xxx"
    try {
        req.user = jwt.verify(token, process.env.JWT_SECRET);   // throws if invalid/expired
        next();
    } catch {
        res.status(401).json({ error: 'Unauthorized' });
    }
}
मराठी सारांश:
  • Bcrypt ने आपण पासवर्ड असा बदलतो की तो परत मूळ रूपात वाचता येत नाही (one-way hash).
  • Signup: bcrypt.hash(password, 10) ने hash बनवून DB मध्ये ठेवा. Login: bcrypt.compare(entered, hash) → true / false.
  • Plain password DB मध्ये कधीच ठेवू नका. DB hack झाला तरी hash मधून password मिळत नाही.
  • आणि JWT म्हणजे एक 'Digital Entry Pass' जो Server ब्राउझरला देतो.
  • ब्राउझर प्रत्येक वेळी तो पास दाखवून सांगतो की "मी आधीच Login केले आहे". (Authorization: Bearer <token>)
  • JWT चे 3 भाग: Header . Payload (user माहिती) . Signature (बदलला नाही याचा पुरावा).
  • Payload कोणीही वाचू शकतो (फक्त Base64), म्हणून त्यात password ठेवू नका. Secret key .env मध्ये ठेवा, आणि expiresIn नक्की द्या.
  • Session vs JWT: Session = माहिती server वर. JWT = माहिती token मध्ये, server stateless राहतो.

Q. Authentication vs Authorization

Concept:
  • Authentication (AuthN) = "Who are you?" Verifying identity: login with email + password, OTP, Google login. Result: you get a session or token.
  • Authorization (AuthZ) = "What are you allowed to do?" Checking permissions after identity is known: admin vs user, owner of a resource or not.
  • Authentication always comes first; authorization depends on it.
  • Failure codes: authentication fails → 401 Unauthorized; authorization fails → 403 Forbidden.
  • Simple example: at an office, the security guard checks your ID card at the gate (authentication). Your card then opens only the floors you're allowed on (authorization).
PointAuthenticationAuthorization
QuestionWho are you?What can you do?
HappensFirstAfter authentication
Based onCredentials (password, OTP, token)Roles / permissions / ownership
ExampleLogin, verify JWTOnly admin can delete users
Failure code401403
Toolsbcrypt, JWT, sessions, OAuth (Google)Role checks, RBAC, ownership checks
Code Example: authenticate + authorize Middleware (RBAC)
const jwt = require('jsonwebtoken');

// 1. AUTHENTICATION: who are you?
function authenticate(req, res, next) {
    const token = req.headers.authorization?.split(' ')[1];
    if (!token) return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Login required' });
    try {
        req.user = jwt.verify(token, process.env.JWT_SECRET);   // { id, role }
        next();
    } catch {
        res.status(401).json({ error: 'Invalid or expired token' });
    }
}

// 2. AUTHORIZATION: are you allowed? (Role-Based Access Control)
const authorize = (...allowedRoles) => (req, res, next) => {
    if (!allowedRoles.includes(req.user.role)) {
        return res.status(403).json({ error: 'You do not have permission' });
    }
    next();
};

app.get('/api/profile', authenticate, (req, res) => res.json(req.user));          // any logged-in user
app.delete('/api/users/:id', authenticate, authorize('admin'), deleteUser);          // admin only
app.put('/api/posts/:id', authenticate, authorize('admin', 'editor'), updatePost); // two roles

// Ownership check (user can edit only THEIR OWN post)
async function updatePost(req, res) {
    const post = await Post.findById(req.params.id);
    if (!post) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Not found' });
    if (post.authorId !== req.user.id && req.user.role !== 'admin') {
        return res.status(403).json({ error: 'Not your post' });
    }
    // ...update...
}

Q. Access Token vs Refresh Token

Concept:
  • Access token: a short-lived JWT (e.g. 15 minutes) sent with every API request (Authorization: Bearer ...). If it's stolen, it stops working soon.
  • Refresh token: a long-lived token (e.g. 7 days), used only to get a new access token from /auth/refresh. Stored safely in an httpOnly cookie, and also saved/tracked on the server so it can be revoked.
  • Why two tokens? Security (short exposure if an access token leaks) plus good user experience (no login every 15 minutes).
  • Logout / ban: delete or blacklist the refresh token on the server; the access token expires on its own shortly after.
  • Refresh token rotation: issue a new refresh token on every refresh and invalidate the old one; if an old one is reused, assume theft and log the user out everywhere.
  • Simple example: a movie-hall wristband (access token, valid for this show only) and a membership card (refresh token) you show at the counter to get a new wristband for the next show.
Diagram: Access + Refresh Token Flow
Code Example: Login and Refresh Endpoints
const signAccess  = (u) => jwt.sign({ id: u.id, role: u.role }, process.env.ACCESS_SECRET,  { expiresIn: '15m' });
const signRefresh = (u) => jwt.sign({ id: u.id },               process.env.REFRESH_SECRET, { expiresIn: '7d' });
const cookieOpts = { httpOnly: true, secure: true, sameSite: 'strict', path: '/auth/refresh', maxAge: 7 * 24 * 3600 * 1000 };

app.post('/auth/login', async (req, res) => {
    const user = await checkCredentials(req.body);                 // bcrypt.compare inside
    if (!user) return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Wrong email or password' });
    const refresh = signRefresh(user);
    await saveRefreshToken(user.id, refresh);                      // store (hashed) in DB/Redis
    res.cookie('refreshToken', refresh, cookieOpts);
    res.json({ accessToken: signAccess(user) });
});

app.post('/auth/refresh', async (req, res) => {
    const token = req.cookies.refreshToken;
    if (!token) return res.status(401).json({ error: 'No refresh token' });
    try {
        const { id } = jwt.verify(token, process.env.REFRESH_SECRET);
        if (!(await isRefreshTokenValid(id, token))) {             // revoked / reused?
            await revokeAllTokens(id);
            return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Session expired, login again' });
        }
        const user = await User.findById(id);
        const newRefresh = signRefresh(user);                      // rotation
        await replaceRefreshToken(id, token, newRefresh);
        res.cookie('refreshToken', newRefresh, cookieOpts);
        res.json({ accessToken: signAccess(user) });
    } catch {
        res.status(401).json({ error: 'Invalid refresh token' });
    }
});

app.post('/auth/logout', async (req, res) => {
    await deleteRefreshToken(req.cookies.refreshToken);
    res.clearCookie('refreshToken', { path: '/auth/refresh' });
    res.sendStatus(204);
});
PointAccess TokenRefresh Token
LifetimeShort (5–15 min)Long (days / weeks)
Used forCalling APIsGetting a new access token
SentWith every request (Authorization header)Only to /auth/refresh
Stored (browser)In memory (JS variable)httpOnly, secure cookie
Stored on server?No (stateless)Yes (so it can be revoked)
If stolenUseful only until it expiresDangerous; mitigated by rotation + revocation
मराठी सारांश (AuthN vs AuthZ, Access vs Refresh Token):
  • Authentication = "तू कोण आहेस?" (login, password / OTP / token तपासणे). Fail झाल्यास 401.
  • Authorization = "तुला काय करण्याची परवानगी आहे?" (admin / user, स्वतःचा post आहे का). नेहमी authentication नंतर. Fail झाल्यास 403.
  • Express मध्ये: authenticate middleware token verify करून req.user देतो. authorize('admin') role तपासतो (RBAC). Ownership check: post.authorId === req.user.id.
  • उदाहरण: office gate वर ID card तपासणे (authentication). Card ने फक्त परवानगी असलेले मजलेच उघडणे (authorization).
  • Access token: कमी वेळ टिकतो (15 मिनिटे), प्रत्येक API request सोबत. चोरीला गेला तरी लवकर बंद होतो.
  • Refresh token: जास्त वेळ (7 दिवस), फक्त नवीन access token घेण्यासाठी. httpOnly cookie मध्ये, आणि server वर साठवलेला, म्हणजे रद्द (revoke) करता येतो.
  • का दोन? सुरक्षा (छोटा access token) + सोय (दर 15 मिनिटांनी login नको).
  • Rotation: प्रत्येक refresh ला नवीन refresh token, जुना रद्द. जुना पुन्हा वापरला गेला तर चोरी समजून सगळीकडून logout.
  • उदाहरण: सिनेमा हॉलचा wristband (access, फक्त या show साठी) आणि membership card (refresh, counter वर दाखवून नवीन wristband).
SECTION 9: ADVANCED EXPRESS & PERFORMANCE

Chapter 26: File Uploads (Multer)

Q. How do we upload files/images in Node.js?

  • When sending files from a frontend form, the encoding type must be multipart/form-data.
  • Standard body-parsers cannot read files. We must use a package like Multer.
  • Multer processes the incoming files and saves them to a destination folder (e.g., multer({ dest: 'uploads/' })).
  • After Multer runs, file info is in req.file (single) or req.files (multiple); text fields are in req.body.
Code Example: Single Image Upload with Multer
// HTML: <form action="/upload" method="POST" enctype="multipart/form-data">
//         <input type="file" name="avatar">
const multer = require('multer');
const upload = multer({
    dest: 'uploads/',
    limits: { fileSize: 2 * 1024 * 1024 }      // max 2 MB
});

app.post('/upload', upload.single('avatar'), (req, res) => {
    console.log(req.file);   // { originalname, mimetype, size, path, ... }
    res.json({ message: 'Uploaded!', file: req.file.filename });
});
  • multer(options): dest = folder to save into; limits = size limits; fileFilter = function to accept only certain types (for example, images).
  • upload.single(fieldName): One file from the input with that name. upload.array(fieldName, max): Several files.
मराठी सारांश (File Upload - Multer):
  • Form मधून file पाठवताना enctype="multipart/form-data" लागते.
  • express.json() सारखे parsers files वाचू शकत नाहीत. त्यासाठी Multer package.
  • multer({ dest: 'uploads/' }) ने files uploads folder मध्ये save होतात.
  • upload.single('avatar') = एक file. File ची माहिती req.file मध्ये, बाकी fields req.body मध्ये.
  • Security: file size limit आणि file type (फक्त images) तपासा.

Q. Multer diskStorage and fileFilter (Custom Filename & Allowed Types)

Concept:
  • With just { dest: 'uploads/' }, Multer saves files with a random name and no extension (e.g. uploads/3f9a1c...), so you can't open them easily.
  • multer.diskStorage() gives full control over where the file is saved and what name it gets.
  • fileFilter decides which files are accepted (e.g. only JPG/PNG images), and rejects the rest before they are saved.
  • Simple example: a library's receiving desk. diskStorage decides which shelf and what label each book gets; fileFilter is the guard who only accepts books, not magazines.
Code Example: diskStorage + fileFilter + Error Handling
const express = require('express');
const multer = require('multer');
const path = require('path');
const app = express();

// 1. WHERE and WHAT NAME
const storage = multer.diskStorage({
    destination: (req, file, cb) => {
        cb(null, 'uploads/');                              // folder must exist
    },
    filename: (req, file, cb) => {
        const unique = Date.now() + '-' + Math.round(Math.random() * 1e9);
        cb(null, unique + path.extname(file.originalname)); // e.g. 1712345678-123456789.png
    }
});

// 2. WHICH FILES are allowed
const fileFilter = (req, file, cb) => {
    const allowed = ['image/jpeg', 'image/png', 'image/webp'];
    if (allowed.includes(file.mimetype)) {
        cb(null, true);                                    // accept
    } else {
        cb(new Error('Only JPG, PNG and WEBP images are allowed'), false); // reject
    }
};

const upload = multer({
    storage,
    fileFilter,
    limits: { fileSize: 2 * 1024 * 1024 }                  // max 2 MB
});

// 3. Use it
app.post('/upload', upload.single('avatar'), (req, res) => {
    res.json({ message: 'Uploaded!', file: req.file.filename, size: req.file.size });
});

app.post('/gallery', upload.array('photos', 5), (req, res) => {   // up to 5 files
    res.json({ count: req.files.length });
});

// 4. Handle Multer / filter errors nicely
app.use((err, req, res, next) => {
    if (err instanceof multer.MulterError) {               // e.g. LIMIT_FILE_SIZE
        return res.status(400).json({ error: err.message });
    }
    res.status(400).json({ error: err.message });           // our fileFilter error
});
Syntax & Arguments:
  • multer.diskStorage({ destination, filename }):
    • destination(req, file, cb): call cb(null, 'folder/') with the save folder. (If you pass a string instead of a function, Multer creates the folder for you.)
    • filename(req, file, cb): call cb(null, 'name.ext') with the final file name. Add a timestamp/random part so two uploads named photo.png don't overwrite each other.
  • fileFilter(req, file, cb): cb(null, true) = accept; cb(null, false) = silently skip; cb(new Error('...')) = reject with an error.
  • file object fields: originalname (user's file name), mimetype (e.g. image/png), size (bytes, after upload), filename, path.
  • limits: fileSize (bytes), files (max number of files).
  • upload.single(name) → req.file; upload.array(name, max) → req.files; upload.fields([...]) → several named fields.
  • ⚠️ mimetype comes from the client and can be faked. For strong security, also check the real file content (e.g. the file-type package) and never trust originalname as a path.
मराठी सारांश (diskStorage आणि fileFilter):
  • फक्त dest: 'uploads/' दिल्यास file random नावाने, extension शिवाय save होते. उघडता येत नाही.
  • diskStorage: file कुठे (destination) आणि कोणत्या नावाने (filename) save करायची ते आपण ठरवतो. नावात timestamp जोडा, म्हणजे same नावाच्या files एकमेकांवर overwrite होत नाहीत.
  • fileFilter: कोणत्या files चालतील ते ठरवतो. उदा. फक्त JPG / PNG. cb(null, true) = स्वीकार, cb(new Error()) = नकार.
  • limits.fileSize: जास्तीत जास्त size (उदा. 2 MB). जास्त असल्यास MulterError.
  • single → req.file, array → req.files.
  • Errors एका error-handling middleware मध्ये पकडा आणि 400 response द्या.
  • Security: mimetype client कडून येतो, बदलता येतो. जास्त सुरक्षिततेसाठी file चा खरा content पण तपासा.
  • उदाहरण: library चा counter. diskStorage = कोणत्या कपाटात, कोणते label. fileFilter = फक्त पुस्तके स्वीकारणारा रखवालदार.

Chapter 27: Global Error Handling Middleware

Q. How do we handle errors cleanly in Express?

  • If an error occurs deep inside a route, Node.js might crash or the user might get a blank screen.
  • Express has a special Error-Handling Middleware. It must have exactly 4 arguments: (err, req, res, next).
  • Whenever you call next(error) in a route, Express skips all normal routes and jumps straight to this error-handling middleware.
  • It should be placed at the very end of your app.js file.
Code Example: Global Error Handler
app.get('/users/:id', async (req, res, next) => {
    try {
        const user = await User.findById(req.params.id);
        if (!user) {
            const err = new Error('User not found');
            err.status = 404;
            return next(err);                 // jump to error handler
        }
        res.json(user);
    } catch (err) {
        next(err);                            // DB errors go to the handler too
    }
});

// MUST have 4 arguments and be LAST
app.use((err, req, res, next) => {
    console.error(err.stack);
    res.status(err.status || 500).json({ error: err.message || 'Server Error' });
});
